# BE-06 Replacement Specification 52B

**API-owned work-level incremental distribution jobs — `thoth-pub/thoth#848`**

Programme: Publisher Services and Distribution Configuration. Task `BE-06`. Repository `thoth-pub/thoth`. Risk class **CRITICAL**. Read-only specification compilation.

Supersedes Replacement Specification 52A in full — the reviewed artifact of 2,955 lines, 303,360 bytes, SHA-256 `b4949a9c6afe3f9b1c16d02f63dfb6c4912b5518ebc4b036cd75f6286df1d7da` — and through it R52 and R51.

**Release/topology revision (2026-09-10).** This candidate supersedes the R52B candidate of 4,053 lines, 406,244 bytes, SHA-256 `0a019984e315299d3ec56e49fb646d80f3a77b0a53973b11e60793dfbf53c9d7`. It applies only two things: the release-line, migration-identity and branch-topology correction recorded in #903 (`PS-BRANCH-01`) and #848 comment `5619637336`, and the source re-verification that correction requires (§1.3). The platform-neutral functional architecture frozen in #848 comment `5532527833` is unchanged, and every technical correction of that candidate is carried verbatim.

**Diesel-version reservation correction (2026-09-10).** This candidate supersedes the release/topology candidate of 4,173 lines, 425,040 bytes, SHA-256 `79c0abf1b85d9f668f91f6417f870d37f97d4e8748feecf8844517bde444852d`. It applies only the repository-wide reservation of the Diesel migration versions `20260910` and `20260911` for BE-06, recorded in #903 comment `5621256877`, #766 comment `5621260046` and #848 comment `5621263642` (§1.2, §23.1), with the wording precision §1.3 lists. The operative migration directories, the release line, the topology and every technical correction are unchanged.

**Review-correction pass (2026-09-10).** This candidate supersedes the Diesel-version reservation candidate of 4,206 lines, 438,404 bytes, SHA-256 `67683f28b663310eb9da2c95a4932e1433e09c045937ecbe794a4527cad9ed1a`, which the independent CRITICAL review of #848 comment `5622203229` returned `CHANGES REQUIRED`. It closes that review's six findings, R52B-23 to R52B-28, and its one editorial correction (§1.3, §32). The frozen architecture, the release line, the topology, the two migration identities and their Diesel-version reservation are unchanged.

**Second review-correction pass (2026-09-10).** This candidate supersedes the review-correction candidate of 4,390 lines, 478,313 bytes, SHA-256 `4010dce1bf325b71230c7ff91213976ba1cfba573a06be4839bb7a5c362c657d`, which the fresh independent CRITICAL review of #848 comment `5624224185` returned `CHANGES REQUIRED`. It closes that review's three findings, R52B-29 to R52B-31, and carries unchanged the corrections of R52B-23 to R52B-28 that the review accepted (§1.3, §32). The frozen architecture, the release line, the topology, the two migration identities and their Diesel-version reservation are unchanged.

**Third review-correction pass (2026-09-11).** This candidate supersedes the second review-correction candidate of 4,481 lines, 517,696 bytes, SHA-256 `37e44ce3e747607554c154272ea4ba4d0dea8b63fe0aeb4beaa0e8eea3256b62`, which the independent CRITICAL review of #848 comment `5632920797` returned `CHANGES REQUIRED`. That review closed R52B-29 and R52B-30 at specification level, kept R52B-31 open and raised R52B-32. This pass corrects both. The bootstrap seed now writes generation state only, and the census, admission and the asynchronous materializer complete the two-stage bootstrap (R52B-31). `setWorkUpsertExecution` and every operation that consumes `execution_enabled = true` now synchronise on a profile-scoped execution gate, so the operational pause is a closed first-effect barrier (R52B-32). R52B-23 to R52B-30 and E-1 are carried unchanged (§1.3, §32). The frozen architecture, the release line, the topology, the two migration identities and their Diesel-version reservation are unchanged.

**Fourth review-correction pass (2026-09-11).** This candidate supersedes the third review-correction candidate of 4,617 lines, 570,169 bytes, SHA-256 `a17b535b67db26cebc4cc6c00586b4075c0c8609e848937984b393babed6154f`, which the independent CRITICAL review of #848 comment `5635363920` returned `CHANGES REQUIRED`. That review closed R52B-31 and R52B-32, kept R52B-23 to R52B-30 accepted, and raised R52B-33: the release image runs `init`, which applies pending migrations before it serves, and the production GraphQL service does not override that command, so "deploy the backend inert, then migrate" was not executable. This pass corrects R52B-33 with the order the review preferred, after first trying to falsify it: production migration is authorized (gate 9), then executed by the `v1.10.0` release artifact's own `thoth migrate` while the backend still serves the pre-BE-06 release (gate 10), and only then is the backend deployed (gate 11), whose `init` finds nothing pending. The window between migration and deployment is proved by execution, not argued (§23.6, §25.21). Executing Migration 2 against the released schema also exposed four defects in this document's own text, each corrected: a SQL literal naming a label the released `relation_type` enum does not have, a step order PostgreSQL cannot execute, a target-set trigger that refused every released back-catalogue job, and a capture statement that refused every released Work deletion. It exposed one false lock-order claim too, now stated as an executed residual (§10.6). R52B-23 to R52B-32 and E-1 are carried unchanged (§1.3, §32). The frozen architecture, the release line, the topology, the two migration identities and their Diesel-version reservation are unchanged.

**Fifth review-correction pass (2026-09-12), incorporating the CTO's Alternative A architecture decision.** This candidate supersedes the fourth review-correction candidate of 4,743 lines, 623,083 bytes, SHA-256 `21dd00bda832dc29a29206f3b8685475ef56b4bd3fed0ef2506323983d1f9bda`, which the independent CRITICAL review of #848 comment `5637606889` returned `CHANGES REQUIRED`. That review kept R52B-33 open and raised R52B-34, HIGH and blocking: that candidate's capture advanced a propagated parent's generation row inside each editorial statement, and took `FOR KEY SHARE` on the parent's Work row to prove it still existed, which deadlocked against a concurrent deletion of that parent (T303); its `INV-TLO` nevertheless claimed a deterministic total order it did not have; and the mitigation it offered — that the client retries `40P01` — does not exist in the released server or in `thoth-app`. The investigation that answered the review reproduced T303 and found **four further BE-06-introduced `40P01` classes** in that candidate, every one of them on a released editorial or deletion operation that the released schema commits (§25.22).

The CTO resolved the architecture question in #848 comment `5646180203`: **Alternative A — commit-time capture with one transaction-wide ascending generation flush — is approved in principle**, as a narrow amendment to the frozen capture topology, with Alternative E not selected and no implementation authorized. This pass incorporates it. Capture's sixteen row triggers now resolve an event's owners and **record** them; they take no generation row, no Work row and no other lock. One deferred flush applies the transaction's obligations at transaction completion: it ends the generation of every Work the transaction deleted before it advances anything, resolves root existence by MVCC, and upserts every surviving root once, ascending by `work_id`, in one statement. The generation row's foreign key is replaced by that lifecycle, with the containment and orphan guarantees stated and tested. A caller cannot force the flush early: `SET CONSTRAINTS … IMMEDIATE` is detected and the flush restores its own deferral (§8.6). Every `40P01` class the investigation found is eliminated, and each is carried as a regression (§8.4, §8.6, §10.1, §10.5, §10.6, §13.4, §13.5, §18.3, §23.3, §23.6, §25.22, §25.23, §26). The false retry claims are removed. R52B-23 to R52B-32 and E-1 are carried unchanged (§1.3, §32). The frozen architecture is otherwise unchanged, as are the release line, the topology, the two migration identities and their Diesel-version reservation.

**An implementation agent needs none of R52A, R52, R51, R50, the Revision 5 / 5A / 5B convergence documents, or prior conversation. R52B restates every surviving normative requirement required for implementation.** Where an earlier requirement was withdrawn, §6.3 names the item and its replacement. No section refers the reader to a superseded document for behaviour.

## 0. Terminology

| Term | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **BE-04 substrate** | The released `distribution_job` / `distribution_job_target` / `distribution_job_attempt` triple with claim, lease, attempt-token fencing and retry budget, created by `thoth-api/migrations/20260814_v1.7.0/up.sql`. |
| **`WORK_UPSERT`** | The BE-06 `distribution_job_kind` member: one source Work, one execution profile, one generation. |
| **Execution profile** | A programme-local, code-owned unit of work-level execution semantics, keyed by a `DistributionPlatform` value and owning an explicit, non-empty target set. Crossref is the only implemented profile. |
| **Source generation** | `work_upsert_generation.source_generation`: a monotone per-`(work, execution_profile)` counter advanced once, at transaction completion, for every root of a transaction carrying at least one committed profile-relevant editorial event (§8.6). |
| **Residue** | A committed `(work, profile)` state in which the source generation exceeds the resolution generation, or is `0`, and no actionable job exists. Residue is durable and is drained later. |
| **Generation-covered** | An eligible Work whose `work_upsert_generation` row exists with `source_generation > 0`. The admission census counts every eligible Work that is not: one with no row, and one whose row is still `0` (§21.3). |
| **Capture queue** | `work_upsert_capture_queue`: the transaction-local record of a transaction's capture obligations — the owners each fired row resolved, the Works it deleted, and the row that arms the flush. Every row a transaction writes there is removed by its own flush, and no row survives its transaction (§8.6, §18.3a). |
| **Flush** | The single deferred act that applies a transaction's capture obligations, at transaction completion: end of generation for the Works the transaction deleted, then one ascending upsert of its surviving roots. Exactly one flush applies a transaction's obligations, and no generation row is acquired by an editorial transaction before it (§8.6). |
| **Materialization** | The asynchronous, worker-triggered conversion of residue and stale bindings into BE-04 `WORK_UPSERT` jobs. It is a drain mechanism, never the authority for whether work exists. |
| **Root work** | The Work whose Crossref `book`/`book_series_metadata` element a change affects. A chapter change propagates to itself **and** to every `HAS_CHILD` parent. |
| **Binding** | The exact `(publisher_id, activation_id, execution_profile)` triple a concrete job is bound to. |
| **Held publisher** | The `publisher` row a work-level path locks `FOR SHARE` before `W` and holds until it commits: the binding publisher for the materializer and the seed, the job's `publisher_id` for T2 completion and the job-linked reservations, and the permit's immutable `publisher_identity` for finalisation. A path relies on a publisher's assignment, activation, admission, control or eligibility state only when that publisher is its held publisher **and** the binding it re-resolves under `W` equals it (§10.1). |
| **Fence** | The nine-clause proof of §11.1 that a claimed attempt is still authorised. For Crossref it is evaluated inside **finalisation**, and `fenced_at` is written only in the transaction that authorises (§16.6). |
| **Released public route** | `GET /specifications/{specification_id}/work/{work_id}` on `thoth-export-server`: Redis-cached, 14-digit Crossref `<timestamp>`. It remains available for non-provider consumers and is **never** a provider-writing source after the permit cutover (§15.6). |
| **Prepared route** | `GET /specifications/doideposit::crossref/work/{work_id}/prepared/{crossref_timestamp}`: uncached, emits exactly the supplied 17-digit calendar-valid timestamp (§15.3). The only source of provider-writing Crossref bytes after the cutover. |
| **Artifact** | The exact byte sequence returned by the prepared route for one reservation. Only an artifact may be POSTed. |
| **Registration membership** | The canonical DOIs of the `doi_data/doi` elements that are direct children of the three registration containers of the artifact (§16.11). No other DOI-shaped value counts. |
| **Reservation** | A `RESERVED` permit row. It fixes membership, timestamp and batch id, blocks its DOIs, and **authorises no provider write whatsoever**. |
| **Authorization** | The `RESERVED → AUTHORIZED` transition performed inside `finaliseCrossrefWrite`. The **external-write-authorized point**: before its commit no byte may reach Crossref; after it exactly the bytes named by `payload_digest` may. |
| **Void** | The `RESERVED → VOIDED` transition. Performed only by a Crossref-owned operation: the route-owner void, the superuser void, or a finalisation that refuses its own reservation (§16.7). **Never by a generic substrate path.** |
| **Outcome report / reconciliation / closure** | Three distinct acts (§16.7, §16.8): the reservation holder records what happened to its own authorised write; a superuser records an outcome as still unresolved, resolves it, or confirms a terminal truth — each reconciliation act with its own write-once authority reference and time; `closed_at` is set on entry to any terminal state. |
| **Manifest** | The immutable fields an `AUTHORIZED` permit carries: route, binding, root work identity, generation, membership, deposit timestamp, batch id and payload digest. |
| **Generation witness** | `source_generation_witness`: the root Work's `source_generation` read under `G FOR UPDATE` at reservation, after creating a `0` row when none exists (§16.4). Equality at finalisation proves the artifact was built from unchanged source. |
| **Finalisation outcome** | Exactly one of `AUTHORIZED`, `VOIDED_RETRYABLE` or `VOIDED_JOB_RETIRED`, or a refusal error that mutated nothing (§16.6). |
| **`gen(job)`** | The generation a job represents: the greatest `claimed_generation` among its attempts, else its `created_generation`. |
| **`D` / `H`** | Resolution generations derived from BE-04 history: `D` over `SUCCEEDED` jobs, `H` over `FAILED` and administratively `CANCELLED` jobs. |
| **Write permit** | The universal Crossref provider-write authorisation covering all four routes. Provider truth lives here, never on the job. |
| **Recognition / validation** | Deciding that a GitHub comment is a ledger member (sentinel line only, parse-independent) versus deciding that a recognised member is well-formed. |
| **Authority snapshot** | The immutable, digest-pinned authorization register a G-6 attempt is bound to (§22.3). |
| **G-6 / G-7** | The production lock-graph rehearsal gate and the version-floor advance gate. |
| **LO-5** | The generic non-editorial work-level lock order `P FOR SHARE → W FOR SHARE → G FOR UPDATE → J → A`. |
| **TLO** | The one total lock order over every lock class a **BE-06-owned** transaction takes: `P → I → W → G → J → A → Q → K → F → X → M` (§10.1). Every BE-06-owned path takes a subsequence of it, in order; an acquisition that cannot wait — a `SKIP LOCKED` row lock, or the insert of a key no concurrent transaction can insert — is outside that rule (§10.1 rule 6). It does not describe, and does not claim to order, the locks a **released** editorial or deletion statement takes; §10.1 rule 7 states what BE-06 adds to such a transaction, and §10.5 proves that what it adds closes no cycle. |
| **LO-C** | The Crossref suffix of the TLO: `→ K (DOI advisory keys, ascending actual key) → F (floor FOR SHARE) → X (permit row) → M (membership)`. The two `WORK_UPSERT` paths that compose it, reservation and finalisation, take the generic execution gate `Q` immediately before it (§16.5). |
| **Execution gate (`Q`)** | The profile-scoped, transaction-scoped advisory lock `(1948572002, hashtext('be06:work_upsert:execution:' || profile))`. Every operation that may act on `execution_enabled = true` for `WORK_UPSERT` — the claim, the `WORK_UPSERT` reservation and `WORK_UPSERT` finalisation — holds it in SHARE mode from before its read of the control row until its commit; `setWorkUpsertExecution` holds it EXCLUSIVE from before its update until its commit. It makes the operational pause a closed barrier (§21.1). |
| **Release line** | `v1.10.0`: the Publisher Services Thoth release identity that owns BE-06 and its migrations (#903). A release identity only; it authorizes no merge, release, migration, deployment or activation. |
| **Programme integration branch** | `feature/publisher-services-v1-10`: the long-lived, repository-local integration line for remaining Publisher Services work in `thoth-pub/thoth` (#903). Slices branch from it and target it. It reaches `develop` only through the separately gated final integration of §27.3. |
| **Task branch** | `feature/publisher-services-v1-10--be-06`: the intended BE-06 slice, a sibling of the integration branch and targeting it. Its creation is **not** authorized (§1.5, §27.1). |
| **Diesel migration version** | The part of a migration directory's name before the first underscore, with `-` removed — the only identity Diesel's migration runner uses (§23.1). BE-06 owns versions `20260910` and `20260911`, reserved repository-wide by #903 comment `5621256877`, #766 comment `5621260046` and #848 comment `5621263642`. |
| **Compatibility window** | The interval between gate 10, which applies Migration 1 and Migration 2 while the pre-BE-06 release still serves, and gate 11, which deploys the `v1.10.0` backend. The pre-BE-06 release serves on the migrated schema throughout it; §23.6 proves that it may. |

Task-branch creation: not authorized.** Disposition: see §33.

**The live merged `master` inspected by R52B is `4fa7eaa9`,** not `b582e617` as the handoff premise stated. `b582e617`'s own delta is exactly the one-file `noop` addition the handoff described, byte-verified as the single byte `x`. A further commit `4fa7eaa9` then removed that file. The tree of `4fa7eaa9` is **byte-identical** to the tree of `9ae1e567` (both `7ee27360…`). No BE-06-relevant source fact can therefore have changed, and every citation below is valid at `4fa7eaa9`. The R52A review record on `#848` itself already names `master = 4fa7eaa9` and records the same equivalence. The handoff's premise was stale, and the discrepancy is recorded here rather than silently absorbed.

**Repository state is not deployment state.** Nothing here asserts that `b582e617`, `4fa7eaa9` or any other commit is deployed. The runtime and deployment authority is separate: the private `thoth-pub/infrastructure` repository. Until the fourth review-correction pass no BE-06 conclusion depended on it. R52B-33's does, so that pass made one bounded, read-only inspection of it, limited to the production stack's service parameters that decide which command the GraphQL API image runs (§23.6); no other field was recorded or reported.

**`develop` moved by documentation only.** PR #901 (CTRL-BRANCH-RELEASE-01) changed 15 files, all under `docs/` plus `CHANGELOG.md`. No migration, source or generated contract changed. It merged **ADR-0011** (APPROVED 2026-09-09; repository-authoritative now that it is reachable from `develop`), which standardises the *role* of a repository's release branch rather than its spelling. Two BE-06 consequences follow, both in §27 and §28. `thoth`'s flow stays `develop → master`, and `thoth-dissemination`'s established release branch is **`main`**.

**The integration branch is not `master`'s tree.** `feature/publisher-services-v1-10` (= `develop` `395cc16a`) is 33 commits ahead of `master` and 2 behind; the two are `master`'s net-zero `noop` commits.

It required the next correction to attempt elimination of the cycle inside the frozen architecture, to re-establish one truthful concurrency contract, and to stop and return to the CTO if no architecture-preserving design could do so.
* **The CTO architecture decision.** #848 comment `5646180203` records `APPROVED IN PRINCIPLE - ALTERNATIVE A`, on the evidence of the decision paper `733350cc…5989` (452 lines, 44,173 bytes). It selects commit-time capture with one transaction-wide ascending generation flush, does not select Alternative E, approves the generation foreign key's replacement **only together with** the deferred end-of-generation lifecycle and explicit containment and orphan guarantees, requires the removal of the retry-based compatibility claims, and states that it approves no SQL byte-for-byte, no specification and no implementation. It lists the falsifications this candidate must carry, savepoint/subtransaction behaviour and `SET CONSTRAINTS … IMMEDIATE` among them, and requires a stop and return to the CTO rather than a weakened contract if the selected mechanism cannot guarantee them. R52B-33 remains open; the R52B-34 architecture choice is resolved; the R52B-34 specification correction remains open until this candidate is independently reviewed.
* **The fourth independent R52B review.** #848 comment `5635363920` returned the third review-correction candidate (`a17b535b…6154f`) `CHANGES REQUIRED`. It prefers migration before deployment, requires that order's old-binary/new-schema window to be proved, or the pass to stop, and records that R52B-33 does not reopen the frozen architecture by itself, that the Diesel versions `20260910` and `20260911` remain reserved, and that Metrics has priority and is isolated.

**Preflight, read-only, 2026-09-10 — run for the release/topology revision, again for the Diesel-version reservation correction, again for the review-correction pass, again for the second review-correction pass, again for the third review-correction pass, and again for the fourth review-correction pass, with the same result.**
* Both operative paths are absent from `master` (`4fa7eaa9`), `develop` (`395cc16a`), `feature/metrics` (`79c13845`; `9feddcee` at the fourth review-correction pass) and `feature/publisher-services-v1-10` (`395cc16a`).
* Because Diesel keys a migration on its date alone (§23.1), the scan covered every name with those dates, not only the two paths. Across all 75 live remote heads, no directory named `20260910_*` or `20260911_*` exists. The re-run also derived every directory's Diesel version as §23.1 defines it: no head has either version, and no head holds two directories with one version. The second review-correction pass repeated both scans across all 76 live remote heads, with the same result. The third review-correction pass repeated them across all 76 live remote heads on 2026-09-11, with the same result, and found no directory with MET-WP1-13's reserved version `20260912` either. The fourth review-correction pass repeated them across all 76 live remote heads on 2026-09-11, `feature/metrics` at `9feddcee` among them, with the same result.
* The newest dated migration on any head is `20260909_v1.9.0` (Metrics), so both operative versions currently sort after every live line's migrations. MET-WP1-13's reserved `20260912` (#766 `5624024830`) exists on no head yet; once created it will sort after them, which §23.1's out-of-order rule covers.
* No `v1.9.0` or `v1.10.0` tag or release exists.

**The operative identity is the Diesel version pair.** Diesel's version is the directory name up to the first underscore, so BE-06 owns versions `20260910` and `20260911`, and the `_v1.10.0` suffix is ledger-neutral. The two withdrawn `v1.9.0` names alias those versions: `20260910_v1.9.0` and `20260910_v1.10.0` are both version `20260910`. Diesel does not reject a duplicate version. Executed against the locked crates, it ran only one of the two, and the one it dropped was BE-06's (§23.1).

**The reservation is controlling authority**, not a recommendation (#903 `5621256877`; #766 `5621260046`; #848 `5621263642`):
* the Diesel versions `20260910` and `20260911` belong to BE-06, repository-wide;
* no other programme or branch — Metrics included — may create any directory whose name begins `20260910_` or `20260911_`, whatever its suffix, or any other directory with either derived version, so `20260910_v1.9.0` and `20260911_v1.9.0` are prohibited, not available;
* uniqueness is judged on the derived Diesel version, never merely on the directory path;
* preflight, and every gate §23.1 lists, fails closed if either version is found anywhere but BE-06's two directories — including, before BE-06's migrations run, the production Diesel ledger (§23.1, §27.3).

Metrics does not wait for BE-06, and receives no constraint beyond these two version keys (§27.1).

### 1.4 Authority order applied

1. Live merged source, migrations and generated contracts at `master 4fa7eaa9`, re-verified for the release/topology revision at `feature/publisher-services-v1-10` `395cc16a` (§1.1), a SHA unchanged at the Diesel-version reservation correction.
2. Approved repository-authoritative ADRs and designs (ADR-0004, ADR-0008, ADR-0011; BE-02/BE-03/BE-04 task specifications and implementation reports).
3. `#848`, including comment `5532527833`, the durable **APPROVED / FROZEN** platform-neutral architecture boundary.
4. **#903 (`PS-BRANCH-01`) and #848 comment `5619637336`**, controlling on release line, migration identities and implementation topology. #765 comment `5619631944` and #766 comment `5619634367` record the same decision. Together they supersede #848 comment `5618814472` and #766 comment `5618817876` on those questions only. **#903 comment `5621256877`, #766 comment `5621260046` and #848 comment `5621263642`** correct them on one point: the Diesel migration versions `20260910` and `20260911` are reserved repository-wide for BE-06, superseding the release of the two `v1.9.0` names in the #903 issue text, #848 `5619637336` and #766 `5619634367`.
5. **#848 comment `5646180203`**, the CTO architecture decision selecting Alternative A. It is controlling on the capture topology question it decides, and on nothing else; it amends the frozen boundary of item 3 only as §6.1a states.
6. The independent review records `5572823979` (R52A), and `5622203229`, `5624224185`, `5632920797`, `5635363920` and `5637606889` (R52B), and the R52B handoff findings R52B-0 … R52B-10.
7. Programme references, including the read-only state of `thoth-dissemination` at `9a0c252c` / `0be4dfba`.

Where a prior document conflicts with the frozen record, the frozen record wins. On release line, migration identity and branch topology, item 4 wins over every earlier record, this document's earlier candidates included. Where any document conflicts with live merged source, live source wins.

### 1.5 What this document does not authorize

Nothing. Producing R52B authorizes no repository edit, new file, branch, commit, push, PR, issue or comment mutation, CI dispatch, migration execution, release, tag, merge, deployment, runtime or provider mutation, Crossref request, durable-job mutation, secret access, activation or legacy-schedule retirement, no `thoth-dissemination` change and no Metrics change of any kind.

In particular, it authorizes none of the following:
* creation of `feature/publisher-services-v1-10--be-06`;
* any commit to `feature/publisher-services-v1-10`;
* any `feature/publisher-services-v1-10 → develop` integration;
* any mutation of `feature/metrics`.

The existence of `feature/publisher-services-v1-10` is not an implementation authorization, and its current SHA is not a BE-06 base. The Diesel-version reservation authorizes nothing either: no migration creation, no branch or ref mutation, and no Metrics mutation.

### 1.6 Relationship to the independent review

The independent review of R52A returned `CHANGES REQUIRED — R52A NOT APPROVED — FROZEN #848 ARCHITECTURE UNCHANGED`. R52B closes R52B-0 … R52B-10, closes the three review-record findings the handoff list did not restate (§32 rows R52B-11 … R52B-13), and repairs nine further defects found by the mandatory integration pass (§32 rows R52B-14 … R52B-22). The release/topology revision adds one row (§32, PS-BRANCH-01) and resolves the migration-identity row; the Diesel-version reservation correction adds another (§32, PS-BRANCH-02). Neither changes any technical finding's closure. The independent review of that candidate, #848 comment `5622203229`, returned `CHANGES REQUIRED` with six findings; this pass closes them (§32 rows R52B-23 … R52B-28) and the review's editorial correction (§32 row E-1). R52B-23 withdraws the evidence R52B-12's closure rested on and re-closes it against the corrected text; no other earlier closure changes. The fresh independent review of the corrected candidate, #848 comment `5624224185`, accepted those corrections and returned `CHANGES REQUIRED` with three further findings; the second review-correction pass closed them (§32 rows R52B-29 … R52B-31) and changed no earlier closure. The independent review of that pass's candidate, #848 comment `5632920797`, closed R52B-29 and R52B-30 at specification level, kept R52B-31 open and raised R52B-32. The third review-correction pass re-closed R52B-31 and closed R52B-32 (§32 rows R52B-31, R52B-32 and RC-3), withdrew the second pass's closure of R52B-31, and changed no other closure. The independent review of its candidate, #848 comment `5635363920`, closed R52B-31 and R52B-32 and raised R52B-33; the fourth review-correction pass corrected R52B-33's gate order (§32 rows R52B-33 and RC-4) and changed no other closure. The independent review of that candidate, #848 comment `5637606889`, kept R52B-33 open and raised R52B-34. This pass corrects R52B-34 with the architecture the CTO selected in #848 comment `5646180203`, and re-establishes the compatibility proof R52B-33 requires (§32 rows R52B-33, R52B-34 and RC-5). It changes no earlier closure.

**One correction required an architecture decision, and has one.** Every other correction in this document, this pass included, was made inside the frozen `#848` architecture. R52B-34 could not be: no design that advances a propagated parent's generation row inside an editorial statement removes the class of `40P01` the review found (§25.22). The CTO decision `5646180203` amends the frozen capture topology in exactly one respect, stated in §6.1a; the twenty-four frozen rules themselves are unchanged and are restated in §6.1 verbatim.

---

## 2. Objective

Make Crossref incremental distribution **API-owned durable work**, so that a delayed, dropped, duplicated or permanently absent GitHub Actions run may increase latency arbitrarily but can never lose an eligible Crossref update, and so that no Crossref provider write can occur without a durable, queryable authorisation that names **the exact bytes** it authorises and survives the job, the Work and the operational rows it documents.

The mechanism is deliberately **reusable at the boundary and provider-specific in the middle**: a platform-neutral work-level substrate over released BE-04, with Crossref as its only implemented execution profile.

---

## 3. Released baseline

Facts this specification builds on, all verified at `master 4fa7eaa9`, whose tree is byte-identical to `9ae1e567`, and unchanged at `feature/publisher-services-v1-10` `395cc16a` except where §1.1 says otherwise.

Throughout, "a `v1.8.0` client" or worker means any client or worker built against a release that predates BE-06. BE-06 ships in `v1.10.0`, after Metrics `v1.9.0`, so gate 6b of §27.3 re-verifies that no intervening release changed the distribution-job contract BE-06 extends.

### 3.1 BE-04 durable jobs

`distribution_job` (19 columns), `distribution_job_target`, `distribution_job_attempt`, created by `20260814_v1.7.0/up.sql`. Salient released facts:

* `kind public.distribution_job_kind NOT NULL`, enum currently `('PUBLISHER_BACK_CATALOGUE')` (`:11-13`).
* `publisher_id uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES publisher ON DELETE CASCADE` (`:59-61`).
* `work_id uuid REFERENCES work(work_id) ON DELETE CASCADE` (`:63-65`) — **nullable, and never populated by BE-04**.
* `claim_token uuid` is stored in plaintext on the job row (`:45-48`); BE-06's `reservation_token` follows the same released pattern (§18.6).
* `deduplication_key text NOT NULL` with `UNIQUE` (`:67-68`) and a kind-conditional formula CHECK (`:70-75`).
* `distribution_job_back_catalogue_work_check`: `kind <> 'PUBLISHER_BACK_CATALOGUE' OR work_id IS NULL` (`:80-81`).
* `attempt_count` bounded to 5 (`:83-84`); claim-state, completed-at, cancellation-reason, last-error CHECKs (`:86-127`).
* `distribution_job_cancellation_reason` enum `('ADMINISTRATIVE','ASSIGNMENT_DISABLED')` (`:30-33`); `distribution_job_attempt_result` enum `('SUCCEEDED','FAILED','CANCELLED','ABANDONED')` (`:23-28`); `distribution_job_status` enum (`:15-21`).
* Claim: lease recovery first, then one atomic statement with `FOR UPDATE OF j SKIP LOCKED`, batch clamp 50, lease clamp 60…3600, `attempt_count < 5`, and a target-enablement predicate requiring every target enabled under exactly `j.activation_id` (`distribution_job/crud.rs:220-360`).
* `recover_expired_leases`: `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, closes the attempt `ABANDONED`, returns the job to `PENDING` or `FAILED` by budget (`crud.rs:368-415`).
* `complete_distribution_job`, `fail_distribution_job`: one `UPDATE … WHERE id AND status='RUNNING' AND claim_token` then the attempt (`crud.rs:418-540`).
* `cancel_distribution_job`: `FOR UPDATE` on the job, closes the open attempt `CANCELLED`, clears claim fields, **"cannot undo an external delivery already performed"** (`crud.rs:542-600`).
* `cancel_pending_jobs_for_disabled_group_on`: one `UPDATE … WHERE status='PENDING' AND EXISTS(target in disabled set)` (`crud.rs:164-194`).
* GraphQL: `claimDistributionJobs(data: ClaimDistributionJobsInput!)` with `limit = 10`, `leaseSeconds = 900`, `kinds = []` where **an empty list claims every kind** (`model/distribution_job/mod.rs:484-509`); `completeDistributionJob`, `failDistributionJob`, `cancelDistributionJob` (`graphql/mutation.rs:198-240`). Role `DISSEMINATION_WORKER`.
* **BE-04 explicitly deferred the `work_id` cascade decision to this task**: "Because no BE-04 row ever sets it, the `ON DELETE CASCADE` behaviour is unobservable in BE-04; a future work-level job task must revisit that choice explicitly rather than inherit it silently" (`docs/engineering/ai-delivery/tasks/BE-04.md:1000-1005`, repeated at `implementation-reports/BE-04-implementation-report.md:2936-2938`).

### 3.2 BE-02 / BE-03 configuration

* `publisher_distribution_platform(publisher_id, platform, enabled, activation_id, enabled_at, disabled_at)`; `DistributionPlatform` is a closed 17-member enum including `CROSSREF` (`model/publisher_distribution_platform/mod.rs:40-136`); `linked_members()` groups OAPEN/DOAB for **assignment** only.
* `lock_publisher` is `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` on the publisher row (`publisher_distribution_platform/crud.rs:319-328`) and is the coordinator's first statement (`publisher_service_configuration/crud.rs:99`).
* The coordinator's order is: publisher `FOR UPDATE` → assignment writes → back-catalogue job insert → PENDING-job cancellation → **one** publisher UPDATE → the released `AFTER UPDATE` cascade, which locks **every work row of the publisher** in one set-based statement (`crud.rs:99-296`; trigger `20250000_v1.0.0/up.sql:4884`; the coordinator's own comment at `crud.rs:233-236` states the lock footprint).

### 3.3 Released authorization gates

* `require_superuser()` (`policy.rs:201`) and `require_dissemination_worker()` (`policy.rs:226`).
* The released doc comment on `require_dissemination_worker` states the rule §19.4 depends on: **`SUPERUSER` does not satisfy it**, it confers no publisher scope, and *"a principal that must genuinely act as both is granted both roles explicitly, and may then exercise exactly the operations each is independently allowed"*. This specification therefore never asks one principal to satisfy two role gates in one operation (§19.4).

### 3.4 Released identifier grammar

* `Doi::from_str` (`model/mod.rs:138-168`) accepts
  `^(?i:(?:https?://)?(?:www\.)?(?:dx\.)?doi\.org/)?(10\.\d{4,9}/[-._;()\/:a-zA-Z0-9<>+\[\]]+$)`.
  The `(?i:)` group scopes case-insensitivity to the **prefix only**; the identifier class is **closed** and the identifier's case is preserved in the stored value `https://doi.org/<identifier>`. There is no trimming: a leading or trailing space is a parse error.
* `work_doi_check` (`20250000_v1.0.0/up.sql:3266`) is the case-sensitive, anchored `^https://doi\.org/10\.\d{4,9}/[-._;()/:a-zA-Z0-9<>+[\]]+$`.
* `doi_uniq_idx` (`:3974`) is `UNIQUE (lower(doi))` **on `work`** — case-insensitive Work-DOI uniqueness is already released. §16.3 depends on this.
* `work_relation/crud.rs:128-132` takes the **one-argument** `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext($1))`. PostgreSQL keeps one-argument (`bigint`) and two-argument (`int4, int4`) advisory locks in **structurally distinct lock spaces**, so a two-argument BE-06 namespace can never collide with this released lock whatever the hash values. §16.5 uses the two-argument form for exactly that reason.

### 3.5 Released freshness, export and the serializer seam

* `work.updated_at_with_relations` is maintained by per-table triggers; `work_updated_at_with_relations()` bumps only `OLD.work_id`/`NEW.work_id`, and `work_relation_work_updated_at_with_relations()` bumps both endpoints smaller-id-first (`20250000_v1.0.0/up.sql:2276-2351`, triggers at `:4744-4919`).
* Routes (`thoth-export-server/src/specification/mod.rs:7-20`): `/specifications/`, `/{specification_id}`, `/{specification_id}/work/{work_id}`, `/{specification_id}/publisher/{publisher_id}`. Path parameters only.
* `handler::by_work` (`specification/handler.rs:42-59`) parses the specification id, calls `thoth.get_work_last_updated(work_id)`, builds `SpecificationQuery::by_work(thoth, work_id, specification)` and delegates to `MetadataRecord::load_or_generate`.
* `MetadataRecord::load_or_generate` (`record.rs:184-213`) serves a Redis hit whenever `cached_timestamp >= last_updated`, otherwise calls `specification_query.run()` (`specification_query.rs:59`) and `self.generate(data)`, and writes the cache back (`update_cache`, `record.rs:215-233`). Keys `doideposit::crossref:<work_id>`, `…:timestamp`, `…:error` (`record.rs:172-182`); **no TTL** (`thoth-api/src/redis.rs:20-23`).
* `MetadataRecord::generate` (`record.rs:235-277`) calls `doideposit_crossref.generate(&data, None)` for Crossref (`:264-266`).
* `XmlSpecification::generate(&self, works, doctype: Option<&str>)` (`thoth-export-server/src/xml/mod.rs:48-62`) writes `crate::record::XML_DECLARATION` plus the optional doctype, builds `EmitterConfig::new().write_document_declaration(false).perform_indent(true)`, and calls the trait method `Self::handle_event(w, works)`. **The second parameter is a DOCTYPE, not a timestamp, and `handle_event` is a trait method shared by every XML exporter.** There is no existing seam that carries a timestamp.
* `DoiDepositCrossref::handle_event` (`doideposit_crossref.rs:43-83`) stamps `let timestamp = Utc::now().format("%Y%m%d%H%M%S")` at `:50` — **14 digits** — and writes `doi_batch_id = {work_id}_{timestamp}` and `<timestamp>`. Because generation happens only on a cache miss, these values are **frozen at first generation and replayed on every cache hit**.
* Crossref schema **5.4.0**: default namespace `http://www.crossref.org/schema/5.4.0`, plus `ai`, `jats`, `fr` and `xlink` prefixes (`:24-35`).
* Crate versions: `juniper` 0.16.2 (lock; 0.16.1 declared), `diesel` 2.3.10, `diesel_migrations` 2.3.2 (lock; 2.2.0 declared), `chrono` 0.4.45, `deadpool-redis` 0.20.0 over `redis` 0.29.5, `actix-web` 4.14.0, `paperclip` 0.9.7 (lock; 0.9.5 declared) wrapping the export server's routes. Every locked version is identical at `feature/publisher-services-v1-10` `395cc16a`. `thoth-export-server` requires `THOTH_EXPORT_API` at **compile** time (`env!` in `data.rs:13-19`).

### 3.6 Registration structure of the released Crossref document

Established from the serializer source and executed against it (§25.17, T243):

* `doi_batch/body/book[@book_type]` contains exactly one metadata container, `book_series_metadata` when `work.issues` is non-empty and `book_metadata` otherwise (`:108-114`).
* The **root** `doi_data` is written by `write_doi_collection` (`:702-705`) as a **direct child of the metadata container**, and **only when `work.doi` and `work.landing_page` are both present**. A root DOI without a landing page produces no root `doi_data` at all (released test at `:2384-2387`).
* Each `HAS_CHILD` relation whose related work has a DOI produces one `content_item[@component_type="chapter"]` as a **direct child of `book`** (`:142-153`, `:976-996`); its `doi_data` is a direct child of the `content_item` (`:778-790`). A chapter with a DOI but no landing page makes generation fail with `IncompleteMetadataRecord` (`:853-857`), so no artifact exists.
* **DOI-shaped values that are not registration:** `crossmark/crossmark_policy` (the imprint's crossmark DOI, `:589-592`); `crossmark/updates/update` (a superseded or withdrawn work's DOI, `:623-633`); `fr:assertion[@name="funder_identifier"]` (a funder DOI written **with** its `https://doi.org/` prefix, `:1192-1210`); `citation_list/citation/doi` (reference DOIs, root and chapter, `:1232-1238`); `institution_id[@type="ror"]` (a ROR URL, `:1160`, `:1180`). Every one can appear in the same bytes as the registration DOIs.
* The `doi` element text is `Doi::to_string()`, the bare `10.…/…` identifier with its case preserved.

### 3.7 `thoth-dissemination` Crossref write paths, read-only at `9a0c252c` / `0be4dfba`

Scheduled workflows run from the default branch, `main`. Its files equal `develop`'s.

| # | Entry point | Selection | Execution |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `.github/workflows/cr_bulk_disseminate.yml` — `schedule: '45 * * * *'` and `workflow_dispatch` | `bulk_disseminate.yml` runs `obtain_new_ids.py --platform Crossref`; `CrossrefIDFinder` (`obtain_new_ids.py:676-716`) selects `[ACTIVE, FORTHCOMING]` works with `updatedAtWithRelations > now − 1.25 h`, dropping `FORTHCOMING` works lacking a DOI or publication date | a matrix of `disseminate.yml` → `disseminator.py --work W --platform Crossref` → `CrossrefUploader` |
| 2 | `.github/workflows/manual_disseminate.yml` — `workflow_dispatch` with `workIds` (JSON array) and `platform` | the operator's list | `disseminate.yml` → `disseminator.py` → `CrossrefUploader` |
| 3 | `.github/workflows/distribution_job_worker.yml` — `schedule: '20 * * * *'`, gated by `vars.DISTRIBUTION_JOB_WORKER_ENABLED`, environment `disseminate`, `THOTH_WORKER_TOKEN` | claims `kinds: [PUBLISHER_BACK_CATALOGUE]` (`distribution_job_worker.py:365`), refuses to run after an `ABANDONED` predecessor (`:453-520`) | one `crossref_worker_runner.py` subprocess per unit → `CrossrefUploader` |

**All three converge on one method,** `crossrefuploader.py:71-163 CrossrefUploader.upload_to_platform()`. It fetches the **released public route** at `:90` through `uploader.py:222-226 Uploader.get_formatted_metadata`, which builds `{export_url}/specifications/{format}/work/{work_id}` (default export URL `https://export.thoth.pub`, `disseminator.py:60-63`, `crossref_worker_runner.py:34`). It checks the DOI prefix at `:92-126`, POSTs to `https://doi.crossref.org/servlet/deposit` at `:132-143`, and treats `Your batch submission was successfully received.` as acceptance at `:153-158`. The per-work legacy path (`disseminate.yml`) carries **no** Thoth worker credential for Crossref (`THOTH_PAT` is used only for location writes, `:123-124`).

Entry point 2 is a Crossref provider-write path that R52A did not account for. §28 binds it.

### 3.8 Three pre-existing defects this specification must not inherit

1. **Parent payloads go stale.** Editing a chapter bumps the chapter's `updated_at_with_relations` but not the parent's, while the parent's Crossref payload embeds chapter data throughout (`doideposit_crossref.rs:142-923`). The parent's cached payload is therefore served indefinitely with stale child metadata. BE-06 does not repair the public cache and does not depend on it (§15).
2. **A replayed payload cannot supersede at Crossref.** `<timestamp>` is Crossref's deposit version; a cache hit replays the original value. BE-06's prepared path never uses the cache (§15).
3. **A released document can register nothing.** The serializer's gate (`:87-97`) passes when the root has a DOI, but the root `doi_data` also requires a landing page. A root with a DOI, no landing page and no DOI-bearing chapter therefore produces a well-formed document with **no** registration DOI. This was observed in the released test output (§25.17, T243). BE-06 refuses such a reservation (`CROSSREF_PERMIT_EMPTY_DOI_SET`) before any artifact is fetched.

All three are recorded as pre-existing findings, not as BE-06 work.

---

## 4. Scope

In scope: the platform-neutral work-level substrate over BE-04; the Crossref execution profile; the two-phase reservation/authorization write-permit model across all four provider-writing routes; the exact deposit-timestamp, calendar-successor and version-floor contract; the bootstrap/admission model; work/imprint/publisher deletion semantics for work-level jobs and provider evidence; operational reporting; the G-6 rehearsal gate and its ledger, and the G-7 version-floor gate; migration, rollout, rollback and the cross-repository contract the later `thoth-dissemination` task consumes; one `thoth-export-server` change required to prepare a deposit payload safely.

Out of scope, explicitly: any Metrics change; any `thoth-dissemination` implementation; any non-Crossref work-level execution; any Crossref contact during G-6; any provider write performed by this specification; any change to released BE-02/BE-03/BE-04 behaviour beyond the enumerated additive extensions of §18.

---

## 5. Non-goals

1. No generic cross-programme queue, scheduler, job framework or outbox library. The generation ledger is `WORK_UPSERT`-scoped, exposes no enqueue/claim API and serves no other kind or programme.
2. No generic provider-effect enum, lattice or aggregate field on any generic table.
3. No work-level execution for Internet Archive, OAPEN/DOAB, Project MUSE, JSTOR, EBSCO, ProQuest, Google Play, BKCI, Zenodo, Figshare, ScienceOpen, OCLC KB, Ex Libris KB, JISC NBK or Cambridge University Library. Pull-feed and manual destinations are never work-level executable.
4. No reinterpretation of `PUBLISHER_BACK_CATALOGUE`.
5. No mutation of `updated_at_with_relations` to manufacture selection or export freshness.
6. No repair of the released public export cache (§3.8).
7. No change to released `distribution_job.publisher_id ON DELETE CASCADE` (§13.6).
8. No generic, publicly exposed operation that clears a profile's replay block (§11.7, §19.4).
9. **No generic substrate path mutates a Crossref permit** (INV-GENERIC-PERMIT, §16.7). Cancellation, deletion, binding supersession, assignment disablement, lease recovery and generic completion or failure never write a permit row.
10. No change to the released public Crossref route, its cache or its 14-digit timestamp, and no global serializer default change (§15.6).
11. No `thoth-dissemination` change, and no implementation, activation, migration execution or provider contact.

---

## 6. Frozen architecture

### 6.1 The twenty-four frozen rules

Recorded durably on `#848` comment `5532527833` (APPROVED / FROZEN, 2026-09-03). R52B compiles this boundary and does not reopen it.

1. BE-04 `distribution_job*` remains the sole authority for actionable job, target, attempt, claim, lease, retry, cancellation and outcome state; BE-06 extends it and creates no generic cross-programme queue.
2. Incremental work uses the new kind `WORK_UPSERT`; `PUBLISHER_BACK_CATALOGUE` keeps its released publisher-level meaning.
3. `execution_profile: DistributionPlatform` is the programme-local profile key; Crossref is the only implemented profile and owns exactly `{CROSSREF}`; future profiles require separate implementation, review and replay/reconciliation proof.
4. Profile target sets are explicit, non-empty and pairwise disjoint; `linked_members()` is assignment grouping and never determines execution ownership.
5. `work_upsert_generation` records a monotone source generation per `(work, execution_profile)` and contains no claim/lease/retry lifecycle state; editorial commits capture source changes durably even when no worker runs.
6. A delayed, missed or absent wake-up may increase latency arbitrarily but cannot lose the obligation. No correctness invariant uses a 75-minute lookback, a worker cadence or workflow timing.
7. Editorial transactions do not insert `distribution_job` rows; durable generation/residue is later materialized asynchronously. This two-stage shape is required by the released BE-03/BE-04 lock topology and does not replace BE-04's job lifecycle.
8. Generic non-editorial work-level paths use `P FOR SHARE → W FOR SHARE → G FOR UPDATE → J → A`; editorial capture is `W → G`; assignment, admission and control rows are not row-locked by the generic fence/materializer path.
9. Jobs bind exact publisher and activation; obsolete PENDING rows are unclaimable and the asynchronous materializer retires and rebinds them; RUNNING stale work is not generically rewritten.
10. No external effect may begin until the API transaction proves current job, token, open attempt, work existence, publisher binding, exact activation/target enablement, admission, execution control and profile eligibility/identity. The fence freezes ownership through first-effect authorization.
11. INV-GEN, INV-BIND, INV-FENCE and INV-RECOVER are generic; each registered profile is proved replay-safe or replay-blocked; no generic provider-effect enum exists.
12. Crossref is replay-blocked and composes its permit, DOI-membership, timestamp, version-floor and reconciliation semantics immediately after the generic fence prefix; Crossref state never leaks into the generic substrate.
13. Capture is monotone once enabled; legal control states are `(false,false) → (true,false) ⇄ (true,true)`; `(false,true)` is illegal; operational pause disables execution only.
14. Admission is minimal programme-local gating state for an exact `(profile, publisher, activation)`, inserted only after the profile's bootstrap/census proof; it is operational state, not permanent provider or audit evidence.
15. Resolution is derived from BE-04 job/attempt history, never written back into the generation row; a terminal job resolves only the generation it represents; higher source generations remain residue and can be materialized without another edit.
16. Administrative cancellation is job-instance cancellation, not a work mute; once the current attempt is fenced it is refused and provider/profile reporting or reconciliation owns the next transition.
17. There are exactly three generic `WORK_UPSERT` creation sites — asynchronous materializer, fence binding-refusal replacement, T2 completion successor — all calling one helper with one ordinal/deduplication/target-set algorithm under the generation lock; static source containment is the authoritative guarantee and any creator token is instrumentation, not a security boundary.
18. The deferred `work_id` cascade is not inherited: `distribution_job.work_id` becomes `ON DELETE SET NULL` and work-level jobs carry an immutable non-referential `work_identity`, so ordinary Work deletion preserves job/target/attempt/lineage history while the source generation/residue ends with the Work.
19. PENDING and RUNNING-unfenced work-level jobs are retired with explicit `WORK_DELETED` evidence before deletion; a RUNNING fenced attempt makes Work/Imprint/Publisher deletion fail closed until the outcome is reported or lease recovery closes the attempt; terminal history never prevents ordinary Work deletion.
20. Explicit Work/Imprint/Publisher deletion paths acquire the affected publisher set before the entity/work locks, revalidate under lock and restart on pre-lock binding drift.
21. Released `distribution_job.publisher_id ON DELETE CASCADE` is not reopened; deleting the owning Publisher can still remove its job rows including work-level history.
22. After `work_id` becomes NULL, `work_identity` is the canonical stable identifier for reporting, reconciliation and post-deletion deduplication validation.
23. BE-06 does not mutate `updated_at_with_relations`; parent/related-work correctness is handled by capture/propagation rules.
24. Pull-feed and manual destinations remain excluded; other push destinations remain NOT IMPLEMENTED as work-level profiles.

### 6.1a The one amendment: the capture topology (CTO decision `5646180203`)

The twenty-four rules above are unchanged and are restated verbatim. One thing they did not settle, and the fourth review-correction candidate settled wrongly, is **when inside an editorial transaction capture takes the generation row**. #848 comment `5646180203` decides it:

> Alternative A — commit-time capture with one transaction-wide ascending generation flush — is approved in principle.

**What the amendment permits, exactly:**

1. capture's row triggers may resolve and **record** an event's owners, and must add no waiting Work-row acquisition to a released editorial statement (R1 is withdrawn);
2. a transaction's propagated generation rows are acquired **once, at its completion**, never across its statements;
3. that one flush resolves the transaction's complete root set and upserts each surviving root once, ascending by `work_id`, in one statement;
4. end of generation for Works the transaction deleted happens **before** that upsert, and a deleted Work is never recreated as residue;
5. `work_upsert_generation.work_id` may lose its foreign key **only together with** that lifecycle and with explicit containment and orphan guarantees;
6. root existence is resolved by MVCC, without `FOR KEY SHARE` or any other BE-06-added Work-row lock, with a defence cleanup for a root that vanished;
7. every other frozen semantic property stands: editorial commits still record the obligation durably, editorial transactions still create no `distribution_job` row, the direction is still `W → G`, and the scheduler-independent, asynchronous, exactly-bound, fenced and Crossref-profile contracts are untouched;
8. no compatibility claim may rest on a client or server retry of `40P01`.

**What it does not permit.** It approves no SQL byte-for-byte, no specification and no implementation, and it does not select Alternative E. Nothing else in the frozen boundary is reopened, and no other section of this document treats it as reopened.

**Why an amendment was needed at all.** Any capture that advances a propagated parent's generation row *inside* an editorial statement leaves that transaction holding the parent's generation row while released code in the same transaction goes on to take another Work row. A concurrent editor holding that Work row, whose own capture then needs the same parent, closes a cycle. The fourth review-correction candidate did this, and five distinct `40P01` classes follow from it, four of them beyond the T303 the review named; all five are reproduced, with released-schema controls, in §25.22. No design that keeps the advance inside the statement removes them: not a deferrable foreign key, not a pre-lock of the children, not a `BEFORE` trigger, and not `SKIP LOCKED`, which loses the obligation (§25.22, H1–H5).

### 6.2 Generic invariants, stated exactly

* **INV-GEN.** A `SUCCEEDED` `WORK_UPSERT` attempt discharges exactly its `claimed_generation`, and no relevant committed event counted at or below that generation may be absent from the source representation the profile executed.
* **INV-BIND.** The external operation may use only a source representation whose execution-authority-sensitive identity — publisher, activation-relevant destination state, and the profile's provider identity — is compatible with the binding the fence proved.
* **INV-FENCE.** No externally effective profile operation may begin before a passed fence; a fenced attempt is never cancelled, deleted or replayed by generic code; no `SUCCEEDED` work-level attempt exists without `fenced_at`. For Crossref, `fenced_at` is written in the transaction that authorises, and in no other (§11.1).
* **INV-RECOVER.** A profile may be registered as work-level executable, and its control row may leave `(false,false)`, only with a reviewed proof that it is exactly `ReplaySafe` or `ReplayBlocked` for a fenced attempt whose worker disappears.
* **INV-RESIDUE.** Source generations and residue are durable in PostgreSQL independently of any worker. The residue predicate is a pure function of committed rows containing no timestamp. Materialization is a drain, never the authority for whether work exists.
* **INV-SUCC (final form).** For every `(w, profile)` in every committed state exactly one of: `S ≤ max(D, H)`; an actionable job exists; `(w, profile)` is residue; or the Work has been deleted and its obligation intentionally ended. Decidable by one query, `workUpsertResolution` (§20, report 1). No clause mentions time.
* **INV-DELETE-EVIDENCE.** Ordinary deletion of a Work does not destroy the historical BE-04 lifecycle evidence of a materialized `WORK_UPSERT` job: job identity and generation, target set, attempts with claim/fence/terminal evidence, and predecessor/successor lineage.
* **INV-DELETE-SOURCE.** Once a Work is deleted its generation row and residue end. The deleting transaction's own flush removes every generation row of every Work it deleted, **before** it advances any root, and the deleted Work is not upserted again, because the flush resolves root existence in the same snapshot that no longer contains it. A generation row a concurrent transaction writes for a root whose Work has since been deleted is removed by that writer's own flush (§8.6 step 3). No successor or replacement job may be created for a deleted Work; no materializer may resurrect it.
* **INV-PERMIT.** No Crossref provider write occurs, on any of the four routes, without a permit that is **`AUTHORIZED` at the moment of the write** and whose immutable manifest names exactly: the route; the publisher and, where applicable, job, attempt and generation binding; the root work identity; the **registration membership of the authorised bytes**; the **deposit timestamp those bytes contain**; the batch id those bytes contain; and the **SHA-256 of those exact bytes**. A `RESERVED` permit authorises nothing. The permit outlives the job, the attempt, the Work and the Publisher, and cannot be deleted.
* **INV-ARTIFACT.** No byte sequence capable of reaching Crossref may contain a registration DOI outside its permit's membership, a timestamp other than its permit's `crossref_timestamp`, or a batch id other than its permit's `doi_batch_id`. Worker-supplied values are cross-checks and never the authority.
* **INV-GENERIC-PERMIT.** Generic substrate paths never mutate a Crossref permit row. The only writers of a permit row are the Crossref-owned operations of §16, and the referential `SET NULL` of an operational link on Publisher, job or attempt deletion, which changes nothing else (§16.2 rule S1).
* **INV-TLO**, in three parts, each mechanically checkable:
  * **(a) BE-06-owned transactions.** Every transaction BE-06 owns — materializer, seed, claim, completion, failure, cancellation, lease recovery, the three deletion units, reservation, finalisation, the two voids, the outcome report, reconciliation, G-7, admission and the control transitions — acquires its own row and advisory locks as a subsequence of `P → I → W → G → J → A → Q → K → F → X → M`, in that order, with a deterministic order inside each class. An acquisition that cannot wait is outside the order (§10.1 rules 1, 2 and 6).
  * **(b) Released transactions.** Inside a released editorial or deleting transaction, BE-06 acquires **nothing** before that transaction's completion but rows of `work_upsert_capture_queue` whose keys no concurrent transaction can hold, and at completion it acquires **generation rows only**: first the end of generation for the Works the transaction deleted, then one ascending upsert of its surviving roots (§10.1 rule 7).
  * **(c) What BE-06 does not claim.** The Work, relation and child rows a released statement locks — through its own `UPDATE`, its `updated_at_with_relations` triggers or its referential cascades — are released behaviour in the order the released code takes them. BE-06 adds no Work-row acquisition to any released statement and claims no order over those it already takes (§10.1 rule 8). §10.5 proves that no wait-for cycle can contain a lock BE-06 adds.
* **INV-FLUSH.** Exactly one flush applies a transaction's capture obligations, at that transaction's completion and never before it. A caller cannot force it earlier: a forced evaluation of deferrable constraints is detected, and the flush restores its own deferral and re-arms rather than advancing anything (§8.6). The flush's generation acquisitions are ascending by `work_id` within one statement, it leaves no queue row and no orphaned generation row, and obligations accumulated in a subtransaction that rolls back are discarded with it while those of a released subtransaction survive.
* **INV-PAUSE.** After `setWorkUpsertExecution(profile, false)` has returned, no `WORK_UPSERT` claim of that profile returns an attempt opened on an `execution_enabled = true` read, no `WORK_UPSERT` reservation commits on one, and no attempt becomes fenced or `AUTHORIZED` on one. An attempt fenced before the transition still finishes and reports, and capture, generation advancement and materialization continue (§21.1).
* **INV-CUTOVER.** From the Crossref execution-enable gate onward (§27, gate 16), every provider-writing path uses the prepared route with a permit's 17-digit timestamp. The released 14-digit public route is never a provider-writing source (§15.6).

### 6.4 Material preserved unchanged

Preserved in full and restated here: the complete standalone durable-work specification; the three-class dependency analysis and conservative whole-table capture (§8); canonical title/abstract selection treatment and `OLD ∪ NEW` ownership handling (§8); the exact Case-A HOLD → restoration → reconciliation closure, outcome-16 and Case-B semantics (§22.11); parse-independent sentinel recognition (§22.2); RFC 8785/JCS closed ledger schemas (§22.6–§22.9); immutable attempt-closing records; observation terminal before a successful attempt terminal; digest-pinned immutable authorization snapshots (§22.3); native UUID audit identities; PostgreSQL exact-microsecond evidence; gate timestamps distinct from audit timestamps; the closed-world root-independent census (§22.11); no reconciliation kind making G-7 eligible; the accepted no-deadlock G-7 chain and its `census → Class 2 → Class 1B → Class 1A → mutation` order (§22.12); one version-floor advance per attempt; the argument-free canonical blocking count and DRAIN (§16.9); `RESERVED`/`AUTHORIZED`/unreconciled-`INDETERMINATE` blocking; work-level-only superuser reporting (§20); the two-migration enum split (§23); the conservative whole-table capture matrix of §8.2, unchanged by the Alternative A amendment, which changes when a generation row is advanced and not which events advance it; the initially inert state, dated since the fourth review-correction pass from Migration 2's commit rather than from deployment (§21.1); release isolation — since #903, the Publisher Services `v1.10.0` programme integration line, isolated from the Metrics `v1.9.0` line (§27); no Metrics mutation; no `thoth-dissemination` implementation inside BE-06; no Crossref contact during G-6.

Every direction the R52A review accepted is preserved, item for item: `WORK_UPSERT`; execution profiles; Crossref as the only implemented profile; generation → residue → asynchronous materialization; LO-5; stale-PENDING rebinding; derived `D`/`H` resolution; exactly three generic creators; job-instance cancellation; fenced cancellation and deletion refusal; `work_id ON DELETE SET NULL` with `work_identity`; publisher-first deletion; the retained publisher cascade limitation; two-phase `RESERVED → AUTHORIZED` permits; immutable membership; the generation witness; actual advisory-key sorting; calendar-safe Crossref timestamps; route-specific reservation entry points; internal-only generic recovery clearance; the prepared uncached artifact route; permanent legacy-claim isolation; per-unit back-catalogue permits; the admission/bootstrap model; G-6/G-7 governance; and isolated release, as corrected by #903, and cross-repository ordering.

## 7. Work-level execution profile registry

### 7.1 Concept

A work-level execution profile is a **programme-local, code-owned, statically registered** unit of execution semantics. It is distinct from `DistributionPlatformGroup` (assignment normalisation), `BackCatalogueBehaviour` (back-catalogue classification) and the internal `DistributionAdapterProfile` (mechanism, never exposed).

A profile declares exactly:

1. a stable durable **execution key**, a `DistributionPlatform` value;
2. an explicit **target set** of `DistributionPlatform` values, non-empty and containing the key;
3. its **dependency matrix** and cross-work root rule (trigger DDL owned by its migration);
4. its **eligibility predicate**, claim/complete/fail guards and admission predicate;
5. its **fenced-recovery class**, exactly `ReplaySafe` or `ReplayBlocked`, with a proof reference.

### 7.2 Registry, in Rust

One static per implemented profile and one exhaustive `match` over all 17 `DistributionPlatform` values with **no wildcard arm**, returning `Some(&CROSSREF_PROFILE)` for `Crossref` and `None` for every other value. "This platform has a work-level execution profile" is represented only there and in the per-profile database arms of §11.4. It is never inferred from `BackCatalogueBehaviour::AutomaticPush`, from `DistributionAdapterProfile`, or from a linked group.

```rust
pub struct WorkLevelExecutionProfile {
    pub key: DistributionPlatform,
    pub targets: &'static [DistributionPlatform],   // non-empty, contains key, sorted, deduplicated
    pub fenced_recovery: FencedRecovery,            // ReplaySafe | ReplayBlocked
    pub recovery_proof_reference: &'static str,     // non-empty
}
pub enum FencedRecovery { ReplaySafe, ReplayBlocked }
```

### 7.3 The BE-06 instance

```
key                      = CROSSREF
targets                  = [CROSSREF]                       (exactly one)
fenced_recovery          = ReplayBlocked
recovery_proof_reference = "R52B §11.7"
```

### 7.4 Registry invariants, enforced by test

1. Every registered profile's target set is non-empty, sorted, duplicate-free and contains its key.
2. The target sets of all registered profiles are **pairwise disjoint**.
3. Every registered profile declares a `fenced_recovery` class and a non-empty proof reference.
4. The exhaustive `match` has no wildcard arm, so adding a `DistributionPlatform` member fails compilation until the new value is classified.
5. **Registry/database drift test**: for each registered profile, a job carrying that key with exactly its declared target set commits, and one with a missing, extra or duplicate target is refused at COMMIT; for every unregistered key, any `WORK_UPSERT` row is refused at COMMIT with `WORK_UPSERT_PROFILE_NOT_IMPLEMENTED`.

A future multi-target profile adds its own database arm and must pass 1–5. Overlapping ownership requires a separate architecture decision proving no duplicate or conflicting provider work.

---
## 8. Source-generation capture and the dependency matrix

### 8.1 Method

A **payload dependency** is any column affecting (1) a serialized value, (2) selection or order, or (3) ownership/root resolution. All three classes are captured. Emission alone is not the test: a column that never appears in the XML but decides *which* row is emitted is a dependency.

The matrix below was re-derived from the live serializer at `9ae1e567`, not carried forward. It corrects R51 in three places, each confirmed by line evidence:

| Correction | Evidence |
|---|---|
| **`abstract.locale_code` is a serialized value** (root-work path only) | read at `doideposit_crossref.rs:261`, transformed at `:452`, emitted as `xml:lang` at `:463`; the chapter path (`:268-291` → `:424-444`) drops it |
| **`reference.publication_date` is a serialized value** (the citation `cYear`) | `:1339-1344`, `date.format("%Y")` |
| **`location.location_platform` is a selection dependency** | `LocationOrderBy` defaults to `location_platform ASC` (`model/location/mod.rs:253-260`) with no tie-break, so it decides which canonical location `find(|l| l.canonical)` (`:720`, `:802`) returns when more than one exists |

**Conservative-capture rule.** Over-capture is acceptable; a missed Crossref payload mutation is not. A narrow `UPDATE OF` list is used only where every dependency of all three classes is mechanically proven and the row's owner is immutable. Where ownership is mutable or the dependency set is complex, a **whole-table `UPDATE`** trigger is used. Six tables take whole-table UPDATE triggers on that ground: `title`, `abstract`, `contribution`, `affiliation`, `reference`, `work_relation`.

**Physical table names.** Diesel exposes two of these under aliases — `work_title` for physical `title` (`schema.rs:966-967`; DDL `20250000_v1.0.0/up.sql:3190`) and `work_abstract` for physical `abstract` (`schema.rs:125-126`; DDL `:2362`). **Triggers are written against the physical names.**

### 8.2 Normative capture matrix

All triggers are `AFTER … FOR EACH ROW`, non-deferred, and call the capture function of §8.4, which records the event's owners for the transaction's one flush (§8.6) and advances nothing itself. "Root propagation" is always: capture the resolved work **and** every `HAS_CHILD` parent of it.

| # | Physical table | Serialized-value columns | Selection/order columns | Ownership/root columns | Trigger form | OLD owner | NEW owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `work` | `work_type` `:98-104`, `work_status` `:574-583`, `doi` `:87,:616,:703`, `edition` `:479`, `publication_date` `:489,:596`, `withdrawn_date` `:615`, `place` `:559`, `license` `:638,:686`, `landing_page` `:704`, `first_page` `:917`, `last_page` `:923` | `work_status`, `doi`, `publication_date`, `withdrawn_date`, `place`, `license`, `landing_page`, `first_page`, `last_page` (each gates its own element or block) | `imprint_id` (selects publisher name `:556` and `crossmark_doi` `:573`) | `INSERT OR DELETE OR UPDATE OF` those 12 | `OLD.work_id` | `NEW.work_id` |
| 2 | `title` | `title` `:233`, `subtitle` `:235` | `canonical` (order `Canonical DESC`, no tie-break, decides `titles[0]` `:212`) | `work_id` (mutable, `model/title/mod.rs:85`) | `INSERT OR DELETE OR UPDATE` (**whole-table**) | `OLD.work_id` | `NEW.work_id` |
| 3 | `abstract` | `content` `:440,:466`, `abstract_type` `:437,:462`, **`locale_code` `:463`** | `canonical`, `abstract_type` (chapter path filters on both `:276,:285`), array order | `work_id` (mutable, `model/abstract/mod.rs:112`) | `INSERT OR DELETE OR UPDATE` (**whole-table**) | `OLD.work_id` | `NEW.work_id` |
| 4 | `contribution` | `contribution_type` `:1028-1045`, `first_name` `:1057`, `last_name` `:1062` | `contribution_type` (AUTHOR/EDITOR/TRANSLATOR filter `:167-171`), `contribution_ordinal` (query order and `1 → "first"` `:1046-1049`) | `work_id`, `contributor_id` (both mutable, `model/contribution/mod.rs:162-163`) | `INSERT OR DELETE OR UPDATE` (**whole-table**) | `OLD.work_id` | `NEW.work_id` |
| 5 | `contributor` | `orcid` `:1076-1079` | — | — (join target) | `INSERT OR DELETE OR UPDATE OF orcid` | fan out via `contribution` | same |
| 6 | `affiliation` | — (row existence drives `<affiliations>` `:1065`) | `affiliation_ordinal` (query order `Q:132,:242`) | `contribution_id`, `institution_id` (both mutable, `model/affiliation/mod.rs:60-61`) | `INSERT OR DELETE OR UPDATE` (**whole-table**) | `contribution(OLD.contribution_id).work_id` | `contribution(NEW.contribution_id).work_id` |
| 7 | `institution` | `institution_name` `:1156,:1198`, `ror` `:1159-1161`, `institution_doi` `:1199-1205` | `ror`, `institution_doi` (each gates its element) | — | `INSERT OR DELETE OR UPDATE OF institution_name, ror, institution_doi` | fan out via `affiliation` and `funding` | same |
| 8 | `publication` | `isbn` `:1005-1016` | `publication_type` (ISBN media type `:1006-1011`; PDF selection `:718,:801`; default sort key), `isbn` presence (`:536`, hard error at `:545`) | `work_id` (mutable, `model/publication/mod.rs:307`) | `INSERT OR DELETE OR UPDATE OF publication_type, isbn, work_id` | `OLD.work_id` | `NEW.work_id` |
| 9 | `location` | `full_text_url` `:741,:764,:823,:845` | `canonical` `:720,:802`, **`location_platform`** (default order decides which canonical location wins) | `publication_id` (mutable, `model/location/mod.rs:214`) | `INSERT OR DELETE OR UPDATE OF canonical, full_text_url, location_platform, publication_id` | `publication(OLD.publication_id).work_id` | `publication(NEW.publication_id).work_id` |
| 10 | `funding` | `grant_number` `:1213-1219` | row existence gates `<fr:program>` `:638,:670` | `work_id`, `institution_id` (both mutable, `model/funding/mod.rs:65-66`) | `INSERT OR DELETE OR UPDATE OF grant_number, work_id, institution_id` | `OLD.work_id` | `NEW.work_id` |
| 11 | `issue` | `issue_number` → `<volume>` `:119-129` | row existence renames the root element `book_metadata` ⇄ `book_series_metadata` `:108-112`; `issue_ordinal` decides `.first()` `:119`; `series_id` selects the series | `work_id` (mutable, `model/issue/mod.rs:60`), `series_id` (mutable, `:59`) | `INSERT OR DELETE OR UPDATE OF issue_number, issue_ordinal, series_id, work_id` | `OLD.work_id` | `NEW.work_id` |
| 12 | `series` | `series_name` `:941`, `issn_print` `:945-951`, `issn_digital` `:956-962` | ISSN presence gates the whole `<series_metadata>` block `:937` | — | `INSERT OR DELETE OR UPDATE OF series_name, issn_print, issn_digital` | fan out via `issue` | same |
| 13 | `imprint` | `crossmark_doi` `:590` | `crossmark_doi` presence switches the entire `<crossmark>` branch and relocates `<fr:program>`/`<ai:program>` into `<custom_metadata>` `:573,:638-646` | `publisher_id` (mutable, `model/imprint/mod.rs:85`) | `INSERT OR DELETE OR UPDATE OF crossmark_doi, publisher_id` | fan out via `work.imprint_id` | same |
| 14 | `publisher` | `publisher_name` `:556` | — | — | `UPDATE OF publisher_name` | fan out via `imprint` | same |
| 15 | `reference` | `doi`, `unstructured_citation`, `issn`, `isbn`, `journal_title`, `article_title`, `series_title`, `volume_title`, `edition`, `author`, `volume`, `issue`, `first_page`, `component_number`, `standard_designator`, `standards_body_name`, `standards_body_acronym` `:1233-1333`, **`publication_date` → `cYear` `:1339-1344`** | `reference_ordinal` (citation `key` `:1231-1232`, query order), row existence gates `<citation_list>` `:879` | `work_id` (mutable, `model/reference/mod.rs:118`) | `INSERT OR DELETE OR UPDATE` (**whole-table**) | `OLD.work_id` | `NEW.work_id` |
| 16 | `work_relation` | `relation_ordinal` → `<component_number>` `:894-896` | `relation_type` (`HAS_CHILD` selects chapters `:147`; `REPLACES` selects the crossmark update `:599`), `relation_ordinal` (sort `:144`) | `relator_work_id`, `related_work_id` (both mutable, `model/work_relation/mod.rs:143-144`) | `INSERT OR DELETE OR UPDATE` (**whole-table**) | both `OLD` endpoints | both `NEW` endpoints |

**16 capture triggers.** Every mutable ownership foreign key either appears in an `UPDATE OF` list (`work.imprint_id`, `publication.work_id`, `location.publication_id`, `funding.work_id`/`institution_id`, `issue.work_id`/`series_id`, `imprint.publisher_id`) or is covered by a deliberate whole-table trigger (`title.work_id`, `abstract.work_id`, `contribution.work_id`/`contributor_id`, `affiliation.contribution_id`/`institution_id`, `reference.work_id`, both `work_relation` endpoints). No mutable ownership key is unwatched.

### 8.3 Deliberate exclusions, with evidence

No capture trigger exists for: `subject`, `language`, `price`, `award`, `endorsement`, `biography`, `book_review`, `additional_resource`, `contact`, `work_featured_video`, `file`, `file_upload`, `location_checksum`; nor for the columns `affiliation.position` (`Q:133`, fetched, never emitted), `contributor.website` (`Q:131`), `institution.country_code` (`Q:139`), `reference.retrieval_date` (`Q:21`), `series.imprint_id` (`model/series/mod.rs:117`; the serializer never resolves an imprint from a series), `work.copyright_holder`, `general_note`, `bibliography_note`, `page_count`, `page_breakdown`, `page_interval`, `image_count`, `table_count`, `audio_count`, `video_count`, `toc`, `lccn`, `oclc`, `cover_url`, `cover_caption`, `reference` (`Q:41`), and every dimension, weight, price and accessibility column of `publication` (`Q:151-165`).

Evidence classes: `languages` and `subjects` are requested with limit `0` (`thoth-client/src/parameters.rs:179`, `:183-233`), so no row is even returned; `biographies` are fetched (`Q:120-126`, `Q:230-236`) and never read; `affiliation.position` is fetched and never emitted while affiliation *membership* and *order* are already captured by row 6; the serializer contains no `subjects` reference at all and its only `language` occurrences are the literal attribute `("language","en")` at `:115` and a test fixture at `:1494`.

A field is classified non-dependent only after selection/order **and** ownership effects have been excluded, not merely emission.

**Serializer-drift guard.** A build-time assertion pairs this matrix against the serializer's actually-read field set and fails CI on divergence, so a future serializer change that starts reading an excluded column cannot silently break capture completeness. The guard is **blocking in CI** (§26 AC-13).

### 8.4 The capture function and its lock rule

Capture runs inside the editorial transaction, in the sixteen `AFTER … FOR EACH ROW` triggers of §8.2, after the caller's own writes. Under the Alternative A amendment (§6.1a) it performs **exactly two things**, and takes **no lock on any released row**:

```
R1  resolve the event's owners:   $owners = OLD ∪ NEW over the fired row, deduplicated, ascending
R2  record them for the transaction's flush, and arm that flush once:
      -- a work DELETE, cascaded deletions included, also records the deleted identity
      INSERT INTO work_upsert_capture_queue (entry_kind, work_ids) VALUES ('DELETED', ARRAY[OLD.work_id]);
      INSERT INTO work_upsert_capture_queue (entry_kind, work_ids) VALUES ('OWNERS', $owners);
      -- once per transaction, the first time capture fires
      INSERT INTO work_upsert_capture_queue (entry_kind)           VALUES ('ARM');
```

The function, exactly as Migration 2 creates it:

```sql
CREATE FUNCTION public.work_upsert_capture() RETURNS trigger LANGUAGE plpgsql AS $$
DECLARE owners uuid[] := ARRAY[]::uuid[];
BEGIN
    IF TG_TABLE_NAME IN ('work', 'title', 'abstract', 'contribution', 'publication', 'funding', 'issue', 'reference') THEN
        IF TG_OP <> 'INSERT' THEN owners := owners || OLD.work_id; END IF;
        IF TG_OP <> 'DELETE' THEN owners := owners || NEW.work_id; END IF;
    ELSIF TG_TABLE_NAME = 'work_relation' THEN
        IF TG_OP <> 'INSERT' THEN owners := owners || OLD.relator_work_id || OLD.related_work_id; END IF;
```
