```sql
        IF TG_OP <> 'DELETE' THEN owners := owners || NEW.relator_work_id || NEW.related_work_id; END IF;
    ELSIF TG_TABLE_NAME = 'affiliation' THEN
        owners := ARRAY(SELECT c.work_id FROM public.contribution c WHERE c.contribution_id IN (
                        CASE WHEN TG_OP <> 'INSERT' THEN OLD.contribution_id END, CASE WHEN TG_OP <> 'DELETE' THEN NEW.contribution_id END));
    ELSIF TG_TABLE_NAME = 'location' THEN
        owners := ARRAY(SELECT p.work_id FROM public.publication p WHERE p.publication_id IN (
                        CASE WHEN TG_OP <> 'INSERT' THEN OLD.publication_id END, CASE WHEN TG_OP <> 'DELETE' THEN NEW.publication_id END));
    ELSIF TG_TABLE_NAME = 'contributor' THEN
        owners := ARRAY(SELECT c.work_id FROM public.contribution c WHERE c.contributor_id IN (
                        CASE WHEN TG_OP <> 'INSERT' THEN OLD.contributor_id END, CASE WHEN TG_OP <> 'DELETE' THEN NEW.contributor_id END));
    ELSIF TG_TABLE_NAME = 'institution' THEN
        owners := ARRAY(SELECT c.work_id FROM public.affiliation a JOIN public.contribution c ON c.contribution_id = a.contribution_id
                         WHERE a.institution_id IN (CASE WHEN TG_OP <> 'INSERT' THEN OLD.institution_id END,
                                                    CASE WHEN TG_OP <> 'DELETE' THEN NEW.institution_id END)
                        UNION
                        SELECT f.work_id FROM public.funding f
                         WHERE f.institution_id IN (CASE WHEN TG_OP <> 'INSERT' THEN OLD.institution_id END,
                                                    CASE WHEN TG_OP <> 'DELETE' THEN NEW.institution_id END));
    ELSIF TG_TABLE_NAME = 'series' THEN
        owners := ARRAY(SELECT i.work_id FROM public.issue i WHERE i.series_id IN (
                        CASE WHEN TG_OP <> 'INSERT' THEN OLD.series_id END, CASE WHEN TG_OP <> 'DELETE' THEN NEW.series_id END));
    ELSIF TG_TABLE_NAME = 'imprint' THEN
        owners := ARRAY(SELECT w.work_id FROM public.work w WHERE w.imprint_id IN (
                        CASE WHEN TG_OP <> 'INSERT' THEN OLD.imprint_id END, CASE WHEN TG_OP <> 'DELETE' THEN NEW.imprint_id END));
    ELSIF TG_TABLE_NAME = 'publisher' THEN
        owners := ARRAY(SELECT w.work_id FROM public.work w JOIN public.imprint i ON i.imprint_id = w.imprint_id
                         WHERE i.publisher_id = NEW.publisher_id);
    END IF;
    owners := ARRAY(SELECT DISTINCT o FROM unnest(owners) o WHERE o IS NOT NULL ORDER BY o);
    IF TG_TABLE_NAME = 'work' AND TG_OP = 'DELETE' THEN
        INSERT INTO public.work_upsert_capture_queue (entry_kind, work_ids) VALUES ('DELETED', ARRAY[OLD.work_id]);
    ELSIF cardinality(owners) = 0 THEN
        RETURN NULL;
    END IF;
    IF cardinality(owners) > 0 THEN
        INSERT INTO public.work_upsert_capture_queue (entry_kind, work_ids) VALUES ('OWNERS', owners);
    END IF;
    IF coalesce(current_setting('be06.capture_armed', true), '') <> 'on' THEN
        INSERT INTO public.work_upsert_capture_queue (entry_kind) VALUES ('ARM');
        PERFORM set_config('be06.capture_armed', 'on', true);
    END IF;
    RETURN NULL;
END $$;
```

and its sixteen triggers, one per row of §8.2, `AFTER … FOR EACH ROW` and **not** deferred:

```sql
CREATE TRIGGER work_upsert_capture AFTER INSERT OR DELETE OR UPDATE OF work_type, work_status, doi, edition, publication_date, withdrawn_date, place, license, landing_page, first_page, last_page, imprint_id ON public.work FOR EACH ROW EXECUTE FUNCTION public.work_upsert_capture();
CREATE TRIGGER work_upsert_capture AFTER INSERT OR DELETE OR UPDATE ON public.title FOR EACH ROW EXECUTE FUNCTION public.work_upsert_capture();
CREATE TRIGGER work_upsert_capture AFTER INSERT OR DELETE OR UPDATE ON public.abstract FOR EACH ROW EXECUTE FUNCTION public.work_upsert_capture();
CREATE TRIGGER work_upsert_capture AFTER INSERT OR DELETE OR UPDATE ON public.contribution FOR EACH ROW EXECUTE FUNCTION public.work_upsert_capture();
CREATE TRIGGER work_upsert_capture AFTER INSERT OR DELETE OR UPDATE OF orcid ON public.contributor FOR EACH ROW EXECUTE FUNCTION public.work_upsert_capture();
CREATE TRIGGER work_upsert_capture AFTER INSERT OR DELETE OR UPDATE ON public.affiliation FOR EACH ROW EXECUTE FUNCTION public.work_upsert_capture();
CREATE TRIGGER work_upsert_capture AFTER INSERT OR DELETE OR UPDATE OF institution_name, ror, institution_doi ON public.institution FOR EACH ROW EXECUTE FUNCTION public.work_upsert_capture();
CREATE TRIGGER work_upsert_capture AFTER INSERT OR DELETE OR UPDATE OF publication_type, isbn, work_id ON public.publication FOR EACH ROW EXECUTE FUNCTION public.work_upsert_capture();
CREATE TRIGGER work_upsert_capture AFTER INSERT OR DELETE OR UPDATE OF canonical, full_text_url, location_platform, publication_id ON public.location FOR EACH ROW EXECUTE FUNCTION public.work_upsert_capture();
CREATE TRIGGER work_upsert_capture AFTER INSERT OR DELETE OR UPDATE OF grant_number, work_id, institution_id ON public.funding FOR EACH ROW EXECUTE FUNCTION public.work_upsert_capture();
CREATE TRIGGER work_upsert_capture AFTER INSERT OR DELETE OR UPDATE OF issue_number, issue_ordinal, series_id, work_id ON public.issue FOR EACH ROW EXECUTE FUNCTION public.work_upsert_capture();
CREATE TRIGGER work_upsert_capture AFTER INSERT OR DELETE OR UPDATE OF series_name, issn_print, issn_digital ON public.series FOR EACH ROW EXECUTE FUNCTION public.work_upsert_capture();
CREATE TRIGGER work_upsert_capture AFTER INSERT OR DELETE OR UPDATE OF crossmark_doi, publisher_id ON public.imprint FOR EACH ROW EXECUTE FUNCTION public.work_upsert_capture();
CREATE TRIGGER work_upsert_capture AFTER UPDATE OF publisher_name ON public.publisher FOR EACH ROW EXECUTE FUNCTION public.work_upsert_capture();
CREATE TRIGGER work_upsert_capture AFTER INSERT OR DELETE OR UPDATE ON public.reference FOR EACH ROW EXECUTE FUNCTION public.work_upsert_capture();
CREATE TRIGGER work_upsert_capture AFTER INSERT OR DELETE OR UPDATE ON public.work_relation FOR EACH ROW EXECUTE FUNCTION public.work_upsert_capture();
```

Rules, all normative:

1. `$owners` is `OLD ∪ NEW` over the fired row, deduplicated and ascending, resolved by the owner columns §8.2 gives for that table. For the five fan-out tables it is the join §8.2 names, evaluated in the capture statement's own snapshot.
2. **Roots are not resolved here.** The flush resolves them at transaction completion (§8.6), by `roots(x) = {x} ∪ { p : ∃ work_relation(relator_work_id = p, related_work_id = x, relation_type = 'has-child') }`. `has-child` is the label the released `relation_type` enum stores (`20250000_v1.0.0/up.sql`); `HAS_CHILD`, the name this document uses in prose, is its GraphQL name, and as a SQL literal it fails at `CREATE FUNCTION` with `invalid input value for enum relation_type` (§25.21, T294).
3. **Capture acquires no Work row and no generation row.** It takes no `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, no `FOR KEY SHARE` and no `FOR UPDATE` on `work`, and writes no `work_upsert_generation` row. The fourth review-correction candidate's R1 — an explicit `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` on the fired row's own works — is withdrawn (§6.3): on a `work_relation` `UPDATE` it took a Work row the released statement never locks, and deadlocked against a concurrent relation create (§25.22, K5).
4. **The only rows capture writes are its own queue rows.** Their key is a sequence value no concurrent transaction can hold, so the write cannot wait (§10.1 rule 6). Nothing else in the database can observe them: the flush of the writing transaction deletes every one of them, and a transaction that aborts leaves none (§8.7, T322).
5. A `work` `DELETE` records a `DELETED` entry as well as its owners. Every deletion of a Work fires the trigger, including the rows an `imprint` or `publisher` cascade deletes, whose events the cascade queues for the end of the outer statement (§13.4, §25.23).
6. **The arming row is written once per transaction**, the first time capture fires, guarded by a transaction-local setting. Both the setting and the row roll back with a subtransaction, so an edit inside a rolled-back savepoint leaves neither an obligation nor an arming row, and a later edit arms again (§8.7, T317).
7. Capture reads no control row, touches no `distribution_job`, `distribution_job_target`, `distribution_job_attempt`, `publisher_distribution_platform`, `work_upsert_control` or `work_upsert_admission` row, inserts nothing referencing `publisher`, and takes no advisory lock.
8. Capture is unconditional on control state: obligations are recorded, and the flush advances generations, even when `capture_enabled` is false (§21.1). Materialization, not capture, is gated.

### 8.5 Root propagation is Crossref-owned

`roots()` is the Crossref profile's rule, evaluated by the flush (§8.6 step 3) and installed by the Crossref migration, because it exists only because `WorkRelations::xml_element` is reached for `HAS_CHILD` children (`:143-152`) and the chapter's metadata is emitted **inside the parent's `book` element**. A future profile declares its own root rule; the generic substrate has none.

### 8.6 The flush: one transaction-wide, ascending generation act

**What it is.** One `DEFERRABLE INITIALLY DEFERRED` constraint trigger on `work_upsert_capture_queue`, firing on the arming row. PostgreSQL fires a deferred constraint trigger at transaction completion, so the flush runs after every statement of the transaction has taken every lock it takes.

```sql
CREATE FUNCTION public.work_upsert_capture_flush() RETURNS trigger LANGUAGE plpgsql AS $$
DECLARE me xid8 := pg_current_xact_id(); owners uuid[]; deleted uuid[]; roots uuid[]; wrote uuid[];
BEGIN
    IF NEW.entry_kind = 'PROBE' THEN
        PERFORM set_config('be06.capture_probe', 'fired', true);
        DELETE FROM public.work_upsert_capture_queue WHERE entry_id = NEW.entry_id;
        RETURN NULL;
    END IF;
    PERFORM set_config('be06.capture_probe', '', true);
    INSERT INTO public.work_upsert_capture_queue (entry_kind) VALUES ('PROBE');
    IF coalesce(current_setting('be06.capture_probe', true), '') = 'fired' THEN
        -- a caller has made deferrable constraints IMMEDIATE: this firing is not transaction completion. Restore this
        -- constraint's deferral, consume this arming row and arm again, so the flush still happens at completion.
        SET CONSTRAINTS public.work_upsert_capture_flush DEFERRED;
        DELETE FROM public.work_upsert_capture_queue WHERE entry_id = NEW.entry_id;
        INSERT INTO public.work_upsert_capture_queue (entry_kind) VALUES ('ARM');
        RETURN NULL;
    END IF;
    SELECT coalesce(array_agg(DISTINCT u ORDER BY u), ARRAY[]::uuid[]) INTO deleted
      FROM public.work_upsert_capture_queue q, unnest(q.work_ids) u WHERE q.txid = me AND q.entry_kind = 'DELETED';
    SELECT coalesce(array_agg(DISTINCT u ORDER BY u), ARRAY[]::uuid[]) INTO owners
      FROM public.work_upsert_capture_queue q, unnest(q.work_ids) u WHERE q.txid = me AND q.entry_kind = 'OWNERS';
    -- 1. end of generation, before anything is advanced: the generation of every Work this transaction deleted ends
    --    with it, taken ascending
    PERFORM 1 FROM public.work_upsert_generation WHERE work_id = ANY (deleted)
        ORDER BY work_id, execution_profile FOR UPDATE;
    DELETE FROM public.work_upsert_generation WHERE work_id = ANY (deleted);
    -- 2. the transaction's root set: the owners and their HAS_CHILD parents, resolved by MVCC, and the surviving roots
    --    upserted once each, ascending by work_id, in one statement
    roots := ARRAY(SELECT DISTINCT r FROM unnest(owners) o, unnest(public.crossref_roots(o)) r ORDER BY r);
    WITH written AS (
        INSERT INTO public.work_upsert_generation (work_id, execution_profile, source_generation)
        SELECT w.work_id, 'CROSSREF', 1 FROM public.work w WHERE w.work_id = ANY (roots) ORDER BY w.work_id
        ON CONFLICT (work_id, execution_profile)
        DO UPDATE SET source_generation = public.work_upsert_generation.source_generation + 1, updated_at = current_timestamp
        RETURNING work_id)
    SELECT coalesce(array_agg(work_id), ARRAY[]::uuid[]) INTO wrote FROM written;
    -- 3. a root whose Work vanished while the upsert waited keeps no generation row (a fresh READ COMMITTED snapshot)
    DELETE FROM public.work_upsert_generation g WHERE g.work_id = ANY (wrote) AND g.execution_profile = 'CROSSREF'
       AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM public.work w WHERE w.work_id = g.work_id);
    DELETE FROM public.work_upsert_capture_queue WHERE txid = me;
    PERFORM set_config('be06.capture_armed', '', true);
    RETURN NULL;
END $$;
CREATE CONSTRAINT TRIGGER work_upsert_capture_flush AFTER INSERT ON public.work_upsert_capture_queue
    DEFERRABLE INITIALLY DEFERRED FOR EACH ROW WHEN (NEW.entry_kind IN ('ARM', 'PROBE'))
    EXECUTE FUNCTION public.work_upsert_capture_flush();
```

**The four steps, in this order.**

1. **Is this firing the transaction's completion?** A deferred constraint trigger can fire early in exactly one way: a caller sets its constraint `IMMEDIATE`. The flush establishes which case it is in by inserting a `PROBE` row and observing whether that row's own event fired synchronously. It can only have fired synchronously if this constraint is currently `IMMEDIATE`, which is exactly the case in which this firing is not the transaction's completion. In that case the flush **advances nothing**: it restores its own constraint's deferral with `SET CONSTRAINTS public.work_upsert_capture_flush DEFERRED`, consumes the arming row, arms again, and returns. The obligations stay in the queue and are applied at completion (§25.23, T318 and T319, with their negative control).
2. **End of generation, before anything is advanced.** The generation rows of every Work this transaction deleted are taken `FOR UPDATE` ascending by `(work_id, execution_profile)` and deleted. This is the replacement for the withdrawn foreign-key cascade, and it happens after every `W` lock the deleting statement took, not inside that statement (§13.4, §13.5).
3. **The root set, resolved by MVCC, upserted once each, ascending.** The flush unions `crossref_roots()` over the transaction's owners, keeps the roots whose `work` row exists **in its own snapshot** — no `FOR KEY SHARE`, no other Work-row lock — and upserts them in one statement, `ORDER BY w.work_id`. A Work this transaction deleted is invisible in that snapshot, so INV-DELETE-SOURCE needs no special case.
4. **The vanished-root cleanup, then the queue.** A root the upsert waited for, whose Work a concurrent transaction deleted and committed while it waited, keeps no generation row: a fresh `READ COMMITTED` statement removes any row this flush wrote whose Work is gone. The flush then deletes this transaction's queue rows and clears the arming setting.

**The PostgreSQL semantics this rests on**, each verified on PostgreSQL 17.10 (§25.23):

| Fact | Consequence for the flush |
|---|---|
| An `AFTER … FOR EACH ROW` trigger's events fire at the end of the statement; a `DEFERRABLE INITIALLY DEFERRED` constraint trigger's events fire at transaction completion | capture records inside the statement; the flush applies at completion |
| `SET CONSTRAINTS ALL IMMEDIATE` fires the pending events of every deferrable constraint at that point, and makes later events of those constraints fire at the end of their statement | step 1 must exist; without it the flush would run early and hold generation rows across later statements — reproduced as T319's negative control |
| `SET CONSTRAINTS` names a constraint trigger by its trigger name, and may be executed inside a `plpgsql` function | step 1 can restore its own deferral |
| A `SET` (`set_config(…, is_local := true)` included) and an inserted row are both undone by a subtransaction abort, and both survive `RELEASE SAVEPOINT` | the arming flag and the queue rows follow the obligations they represent (T317) |
| Each statement of a `VOLATILE plpgsql` function takes a fresh `READ COMMITTED` snapshot | step 4's cleanup sees a deletion that committed during step 3 |
| Referential actions are never deferred, even on a deferrable foreign key | a foreign key could not have given step 2's ordering — the reason the key is replaced rather than deferred |
| `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` waits for a concurrent transaction that has deleted the conflicting row, then re-checks | step 3 can be made to wait, so step 4 is required |
| Deferred triggers fire at `PREPARE TRANSACTION` in a two-phase commit, and a prepared transaction accepts no further command | the flush is still the transaction's last act. `max_prepared_transactions` is `0` by default and the released stack uses no two-phase commit (§25.23, T323) |

**What the flush is not.** It is not a queue a worker drains, it creates no `distribution_job` row, it reads no control row, and it takes no advisory lock, no publisher row, no Work row, no job row and no permit row. It is the editorial transaction's own last act, and the obligation it records is durable at that transaction's commit exactly as the frozen rule 5 requires.

### 8.7 What the flush guarantees, and the one thing it asks of the source

**Guaranteed by the database, and executed (§25.23):**

1. **One application per transaction, at completion.** T317, T318, T320.
2. **No forced early flush.** T318 and T319, each with the negative control in which the defence is removed.
3. **No generation row outlives its Work through any supported path.** Step 2 removes the rows of every Work the transaction deleted — `deleteWork`, `deleteImprint`, `deletePublisher` and every cascaded Work among them — and step 3 cannot recreate them. T320, T321, T325 and T328.
4. **No orphan from the vanished-root race.** T321 and its negative control, in which a writer that reaches the flush while a deleting transaction sits inside its own flush leaves exactly one orphaned row when step 4 is removed.
5. **No residue.** No queue row survives commit, rollback or a statement error; every queue insert is matched by its own transaction's delete (T322, and the compatibility instrumentation of §23.6).
6. **Ascending acquisition.** Step 2 and step 3 each take their generation rows ascending; two concurrent flushes cannot invert (§10.5; §25.22 K6s and K6c on the withdrawn topology, §25.23 T326 on this one).

**The one thing it asks of the source — the containment statement the removed foreign key created.** With the foreign key gone, the database no longer refuses a generation row for a Work that does not exist. What replaces it is:

* every BE-06 writer of a generation row is one of five paths — the flush, the bootstrap seed, the materialization unit, T2 completion, and the four reservation routes — and each of the last four holds `W FOR SHARE` on the Work before it touches `G`, so the Work exists and cannot be deleted concurrently (§10.4);
* static source containment: `work_upsert_generation` is written from exactly those five places, which the write budget of §24 and the containment test of §25.5 (T203, implementation) pin;
* the flush's own step 4 for the one race a BE-06 writer can lose;
* a **direct** `INSERT` of a generation row for a nonexistent Work — by a superuser, a migration or a future path outside the five — is **accepted by the database**. It was accepted before this amendment too, whenever the Work existed at insert time and was deleted by a path with triggers disabled. T321's direct-writer record notes the behaviour rather than claiming otherwise, and §25.23 requires the implementation drift test that fails CI if a sixth writer appears.
* Two boundaries, stated and executed rather than assumed: **`TRUNCATE work CASCADE` is refused** in ordinary operation, because the released permit guard refuses truncation of `crossref_write_permit`, which the cascade reaches (T324); and under `session_replication_role = replica`, where no trigger fires at all, a truncation of `work` would leave generation rows behind, where the withdrawn foreign key would have cascaded. The released test harness's own reset truncates every public table, so it leaves none (T324). No production path sets `replica`.

---

## 9. Materialization and residue

### 9.1 Derived resolution

For a live `(w, profile)`:

```
gen(job)  = COALESCE( max(attempt.claimed_generation) over the job's attempts , job.created_generation )
D(w,p)    = COALESCE( max gen(j) over jobs with status = 'SUCCEEDED' , 0 )
H(w,p)    = COALESCE( max gen(j) over jobs with status = 'FAILED'
                         OR (status = 'CANCELLED' AND cancellation_reason = 'ADMINISTRATIVE') , 0 )
resolution(w,p) = GREATEST(D, H)
```

`BINDING_SUPERSEDED`, `ASSIGNMENT_DISABLED` and `WORK_DELETED` cancellations contribute to neither: they withdraw a job without resolving a generation. All four aggregates are computed over rows whose `work_id` is non-NULL, so a deleted Work contributes no resolution to anything.

### 9.2 The residue and stale-binding predicate

```sql
-- residue: nothing actionable and something outstanding
SELECT g.work_id, g.execution_profile, 'RESIDUE'
FROM work_upsert_generation g
WHERE NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM distribution_job j
                  WHERE j.kind = 'WORK_UPSERT' AND j.work_id = g.work_id
                    AND j.execution_profile = g.execution_profile
                    AND j.status IN ('PENDING','RUNNING'))
  AND (g.source_generation = 0 OR g.source_generation > resolution(g.work_id, g.execution_profile))
UNION
-- stale binding: an unclaimable PENDING row that must be retired and replaced
SELECT j.work_id, j.execution_profile, 'STALE_PENDING'
FROM distribution_job j
WHERE j.kind = 'WORK_UPSERT' AND j.status = 'PENDING' AND j.work_id IS NOT NULL
  AND ( (SELECT i.publisher_id FROM work w JOIN imprint i USING (imprint_id) WHERE w.work_id = j.work_id)
          IS DISTINCT FROM j.publisher_id
        OR EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM distribution_job_target t
                   WHERE t.distribution_job_id = j.distribution_job_id
                     AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM publisher_distribution_platform p
                                     WHERE p.publisher_id = j.publisher_id AND p.platform = t.platform
                                       AND p.enabled AND p.activation_id = j.activation_id)) );
```

The predicate contains **no timestamp, no interval and no cadence**. A candidate that is currently ineligible or unadmitted remains a candidate until it becomes eligible and admitted. `work_upsert_generation.updated_at` is informational only and appears in no correctness predicate.

**Generation rows created at `0` by a Crossref reservation (§16.4) are residue by this predicate, exactly like a never-seeded Work.** That is intentional. A `0` row records that no captured event has ever been counted for the Work. The materializer creates nothing for it unless the Work is eligible and admitted and capture is enabled (§9.3 steps 5 and 10), and the bootstrap seed turns it into `1` exactly once (§21.2). For an admitted publisher every eligible Work already carries a generation row of at least `1`, which the admission census of §21.3 guarantees. A reservation's `0` row therefore never makes an admitted Work redeposit.

### 9.3 The materialization unit

`work_upsert_materialize(work_id, execution_profile, force boolean DEFAULT false)` — one `(work, profile)` per transaction, under LO-5:

1. Read the current binding by MVCC: `work → imprint → publisher`, and the profile's activation from `publisher_distribution_platform`.
2. `SELECT 1 FROM publisher WHERE publisher_id = $binding_publisher FOR SHARE`.
3. `SELECT 1 FROM work WHERE work_id = $w FOR SHARE`. If absent, end with `NO_WORK` and write nothing.
4. Re-read the binding in a fresh statement under the held `W`. If the publisher differs from the one locked in step 2, end with `BINDING_MOVED_RETRY_LATER`; the drain revisits.
5. Read `work_upsert_control` by MVCC. If `capture_enabled` is false, end with `CAPTURE_DISABLED`.
6. `INSERT INTO work_upsert_generation … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, then `SELECT … FOR UPDATE`.
7. `SELECT … FROM distribution_job WHERE kind='WORK_UPSERT' AND work_id=$w AND execution_profile=$p AND status IN ('PENDING','RUNNING') FOR UPDATE` and apply §9.4.
8. If `source_generation = 0`, set it to 1 exactly once (the seed event).
9. If not `force` and `source_generation ≤ resolution`, end `RESOLVED`.
10. If the profile's eligibility predicate fails, end `INELIGIBLE`; if no admission row exists for the current binding, end `RESIDUE_NOT_ADMITTED`. Both leave durable residue.
11. Otherwise create the job through the single creation helper of §11.2.

**Idempotence.** Running the unit twice with no intervening event writes nothing the second time: step 7 finds the actionable row and returns `PENDING_CURRENT`. The unit never advances `source_generation` except by the one-time seed of step 8, so **no replay of the seed can spuriously increment a generation**.

### 9.4 Stale-binding rebinding, owned by the materializer

| Actionable row | Rule |
|---|---|
| `RUNNING` | never rewritten or cancelled generically; the fence and the profile own the in-flight case; residue above the attempt's `claimed_generation` stays durable and is discharged by the T2 successor or a later drain |
| `PENDING`, publisher = current publisher **and** every declared target enabled under exactly the job's activation | ordinary coalesced job; **no write** — the claim reads the generation, so no bump is needed |
| `PENDING`, obsolete binding | atomically cancelled in the same transaction: `BINDING_SUPERSEDED` when the current publisher or the current activation differs, `ASSIGNMENT_DISABLED` when the profile's target is not enabled at all; history retained; the unit then continues to step 8 and creates the replacement under the current admitted binding, or leaves durable residue |

The claim predicate independently refuses an obsolete binding, so a stale row is never executed; the drain guarantees it is retired and replaced. **Actionable uniqueness can therefore never turn an unclaimable stale row into a permanent blocker.**

Covered cases, each a required test (§25): work moves P1→P2; imprint moves P1→P2; disable then re-enable A1→A2 with and without a released T8 cancellation; current publisher not admitted; destination disabled; concurrent claim versus rebinding.

### 9.5 The drain

`materializeWorkUpsertJobs(executionProfiles, limit)` iterates §9.2's candidate set in ascending `work_id`, one transaction per unit, and reports counts by outcome. It is a wake-up mechanism. It holds no authority: deleting the drain, disabling its schedule or never running it changes latency only.

### 9.6 Where the worker calls it

The worker loop is

```
materialize → claim → reserve → fetch prepared artifact → extract manifest → finalise
   ├─ AUTHORIZED          → recheck digest → POST → report → complete / fail
   ├─ VOIDED_RETRYABLE    → discard the bytes → failDistributionJob(retryable = true)
   └─ VOIDED_JOB_RETIRED  → discard the bytes → nothing further
```

(§16.6, §28.2–§28.4). Materialization is exposed to the worker rather than run by a database timer because the API has no scheduler and because the drain must be observable and rate-limited by the same operator control that runs the worker.

---

## 10. Generic job lifecycle and lock order

### 10.1 LO-5, and the total lock order that contains it

**Generic order for every non-editorial BE-06 work-level path (LO-5):**

```
publisher (FOR SHARE) → work (FOR SHARE) → work_upsert_generation (FOR UPDATE) → distribution_job (FOR UPDATE) → distribution_job_attempt
```

**The one total lock order (TLO) over every lock class any BE-06 transaction takes:**

| Class | Object | Modes used |
|---|---|---|
| `P` | `publisher` row(s) | `FOR SHARE`; the deletion unit's own publisher `FOR UPDATE`; RI `FOR KEY SHARE` |
| `I` | `imprint` row(s) | `FOR UPDATE` (Imprint and Publisher deletion only) |
| `W` | `work` row(s) | `FOR SHARE`; editorial `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`; deletion `FOR UPDATE` |
| `G` | `work_upsert_generation` row(s) | `FOR UPDATE`; editorial and witness `INSERT … ON CONFLICT` |
| `J` | `distribution_job` row(s) | `FOR UPDATE`; released `UPDATE`; claim `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` |
| `A` | `distribution_job_attempt` row(s) | `FOR UPDATE`; released `UPDATE` |
| `Q` | the execution gate of an execution profile | `pg_advisory_xact_lock_shared(1948572002, hashtext('be06:work_upsert:execution:' || profile))` by the claim, the `WORK_UPSERT` reservation and `WORK_UPSERT` finalisation; `pg_advisory_xact_lock(1948572002, …)`, EXCLUSIVE, by `setWorkUpsertExecution` only (§21.1) |
| `K` | Crossref DOI advisory keys | `pg_advisory_xact_lock(1948572001, key)` |
| `F` | `work_crossref_version_floor` row | reservation `FOR SHARE`; G-7 `FOR UPDATE` |
| `X` | `crossref_write_permit` row | `FOR UPDATE`; reservation `INSERT`; RI `SET NULL` |
| `M` | `crossref_write_permit_doi` rows | `INSERT` only, inside the reservation that inserted `X` |

Two BE-06 tables are deliberately **not** lock classes. `work_upsert_capture_queue` rows are written only by the transaction that owns them, under a sequence-generated key no other transaction can hold, and are deleted by that transaction's own flush; they can neither wait nor be waited for (rule 6). `work_upsert_control` and `work_upsert_admission` are read by MVCC and row-locked only by their own writers (rule 3).

Rules, all normative:

1. Every BE-06 transaction acquires locks as a **subsequence** of `P → I → W → G → J → A → Q → K → F → X → M`, in that order. Skipping a class is permitted; acquiring an earlier class after a later one is not, except as rule 6 allows.
2. Inside a class the order is deterministic: publishers ascending by id, in one pass; works ascending by id; generation rows ascending by `work_id` in one statement; job rows ascending by id; execution gates, when a claim names several profiles, and DOI advisory keys, each **deduplicated and ascending as signed `integer`** (§10.7, §16.5).
3. `publisher_distribution_platform`, `work_upsert_admission` and `work_upsert_control` rows are read by MVCC and are **never row-locked** by any BE-06 path but their own writers: admission inserts its row, and the two control mutations update the control row, `setWorkUpsertExecution` only after taking `Q` EXCLUSIVE. The execution gate is an advisory lock, not a lock on the control row: every reader still reads the control row by MVCC, and `Q` only orders that read against a transition (§21.1). Guards that only read `crossref_write_permit` (claim, completion, failure, G-7 DRAIN) read it by MVCC and do not lock it.
4. Editorial capture is `W → G` and nothing else, with the `W` being the locks the released statement itself takes and the `G` being the flush's, at transaction completion (§8.4, §8.6). It is a subsequence.
5. Referential-integrity actions caused by a statement lock rows at the position of the class they touch. A Publisher deletion's cascade to `J` and `A`, and its `SET NULL` of permit links, happen after its own `P`, `I` and `W` locks, and are therefore in order.
6. **An acquisition that cannot wait is outside rule 1**: a row lock taken `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, and the insert of a key no concurrent transaction can insert — a fresh `uuid`, or a `WORK_UPSERT` job row whose generation row the inserter holds `FOR UPDATE`, the lock every creator holds (§11.2). Such an acquisition adds no wait-for edge. Three paths rely on it: the claim, which after `Q` takes job rows `SKIP LOCKED` and inserts new attempts (§10.7); T2, whose successor insert follows `A`; and finalisation's binding-refusal replacement, whose insert follows `X`.

7. **What BE-06 adds to a released editorial or deleting transaction, and nothing more.** Before that transaction's completion: the insert of its own `work_upsert_capture_queue` rows, which cannot wait (rule 6), and no other acquisition whatsoever — no Work row, no generation row, no publisher row, no job row, no advisory lock. At its completion, in this order: the generation rows of the Works it deleted, `FOR UPDATE`, ascending by `(work_id, execution_profile)`, then deleted; then one statement upserting its surviving roots ascending by `work_id`; then the cleanup of §8.6 step 4 over rows that statement wrote. Nothing follows the flush but the commit itself.
8. **What BE-06 does not order.** The Work, relation and child rows a released statement locks — by its own `UPDATE` or `DELETE`, by the released `updated_at_with_relations` triggers, or by a referential cascade — are taken in the order the released code takes them: `LEAST` then `GREATEST` in the relation trigger, `OLD.work_id OR NEW.work_id` in the child-table trigger, scan order in a cascade, ordinal order in the `Reorder` loop. BE-06 neither changes that order nor claims one. Rule 1 governs BE-06-owned transactions (rule 7's first sentence and §10.4); §10.5 proves separately that no cycle can contain a lock BE-06 adds.

The released BE-03 coordinator holds the publisher `FOR UPDATE` for its whole transaction, so reading a publisher's assignment, admission and control rows by MVCC while holding **that** publisher `FOR SHARE` is exact: its coordinator cannot be mid-flight, and cannot commit a change before the reader does.

**The held-publisher rule.** That guarantee covers only the publisher a path holds. No editorial ownership move takes a publisher lock that `FOR SHARE` excludes — moving a Work takes `FOR KEY SHARE` on its new imprint, and moving an imprint `FOR KEY SHARE` on its new publisher — so a Work can move into or out of a publisher between any MVCC read and the moment a path locks `W`. A work-level path therefore relies on a publisher's assignment, activation, admission, control or eligibility state only when that publisher is its held publisher (§0) **and** the binding it re-resolves under `W`, in a statement after `W` is held, equals it. When they differ, the path acts on no configuration and takes no further publisher lock: the materializer and the seed end `BINDING_MOVED_RETRY_LATER`, a reservation refuses `CROSSREF_BINDING_MOVED_RETRY`, finalisation refuses `BINDING_SUPERSEDED` (§16.6), and T2 creates no successor (§10.8). Executed with a Work moving in both directions and the coordinator of either publisher concurrent (§25.19, T270–T275).

### 10.2 Why `FOR SHARE` on the work row

Requirements for the non-editorial work lock: (i) exclude every UPDATE of the row — including the ownership move, which is an ordinary `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` because `work.imprint_id` is not a key column; (ii) exclude DELETE; (iii) allow concurrent non-editorial units on the same work to coexist and serialise at `G`; (iv) allow editorial INSERTs of child rows referencing the work, which take `FOR KEY SHARE`.

`FOR KEY SHARE` fails (i). `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` and `FOR UPDATE` fail (iii). **`FOR SHARE` satisfies all four** and is the mode used by the fence, T2 completion and the materialization unit. Together with the held-publisher rule of §10.1, it is what makes "the binding proved by the fence is still the binding at first-effect authorization" true rather than hopeful.

### 10.3 Implicit referential-integrity locks are part of the graph

Every RI lock a BE-06 path takes falls on a row it already holds in a compatible or stronger mode, so no RI check is ever a wait:

| Insert | RI lock taken | Already held |
|---|---|---|
| `work_upsert_generation` row | none: `work_upsert_generation.work_id` carries no foreign key (§18.3) | — (the writer holds `W FOR SHARE`, or is the flush, §8.6) |
| `distribution_job` row | `FOR KEY SHARE` on `publisher` and on `work` | yes, `FOR SHARE` on both |
| `distribution_job_target` / `distribution_job_attempt` row | `FOR KEY SHARE` on `distribution_job` | yes, `FOR UPDATE` |
| `work_upsert_admission` row | `FOR KEY SHARE` on `publisher` | yes, `FOR SHARE` |
| `crossref_write_permit` row | `FOR KEY SHARE` on `publisher`, `distribution_job`, `distribution_job_attempt` | yes |

Capture is **no** exception any more. It takes no Work row (§8.4 rule 3), and the generation row it used to take inside the statement is now taken by the flush at completion (§8.6). The `FOR KEY SHARE` the withdrawn foreign key took on a propagated parent is gone with the key (§6.3, §18.3).

No BE-06 **non-editorial** path may be described as "not locking the work row": the FK from `distribution_job.work_id` to `work` guarantees otherwise, and LO-5 takes `W FOR SHARE` explicitly before `G`.

### 10.4 Exact locks per path

Every row is a subsequence of the TLO. The permit row, where taken, is always the last row lock.

| Path | Acquisition order and mode |
|---|---|
| Editorial edit of one work | child rows (statement) → `W` `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` (released trigger) → queue rows (cannot wait) → **at completion:** `G` rows of the transaction's roots, one statement, ascending |
| Editorial ownership move `work.imprint_id` | `W` `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` → RI `FOR KEY SHARE` on the new imprint → queue rows → **at completion:** `G` rows ascending |
| Editorial imprint move `imprint.publisher_id` | imprint row → RI `FOR KEY SHARE` on the new publisher → cascade `W` rows (released order) → queue rows → **at completion:** `G` rows ascending |
| Editorial fan-out (publisher/imprint non-key edit) | that row → all `W` of the scope (one released statement) → one queue row → **at completion:** all `G` rows, one statement, ascending |
| `work_relation` write | `W(min)` then `W(max)` `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` (released trigger) → queue rows → **at completion:** `G` rows of all endpoints and their parents, one statement, ascending |
| Editorial multi-statement transaction (`Reorder::change_ordinal`, `WorkRelation::create`/`update`/`delete`) | every statement's released locks, in released order → queue rows → **at completion:** one `G` statement for the whole transaction. No generation row is held across any statement of it (§10.6) |
| Materialization unit | `P` share (the binding publisher) → `W` share → binding re-resolved under `W` → `G` update → actionable `J` update → `J` insert → target insert |
| Seed unit | `P` share (the binding publisher) → `W` share → binding re-resolved under `W` → `G` insert-0 then update. **No `J`, no target, no attempt** (§21.2) |
| Claim | released lease recovery (`J` `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` → `A` update → `J` update) → `Q` SHARE for each requested registered profile, ascending → the atomic claim statement: `J` `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` → `A` insert (rule 6). Guard reads of the permit and of the control row are MVCC, in that statement, after `Q` is granted. **Never waits on a row**; its one added wait is `Q`, only while a control transition holds or awaits it |
| Complete (T2) | `P` share (the job's `publisher_id`) → `W` share → binding re-resolved under `W` → `G` update → `J` (released `UPDATE`) → `A` (released `UPDATE`) → optional successor insert, only when the re-resolved binding is the held `P`. The accepted-permit guard reads the permit by MVCC; `ACCEPTED` is terminal, so the read is stable |
| Fail | released `J` → `A`. The permit guard reads the permit by MVCC |
| Administrative cancel | released `J` `FOR UPDATE` → `A` update. Reads `A.fenced_at`. **Touches no permit** |
| Lease recovery | released `J` `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` → `A` update → `J` update. **Touches no permit** |
| Deletion unit, Work | `P` set (sorted, share) → `W` `FOR UPDATE` → `J` rows → `A` → `DELETE` (released cascades: `J.work_id` `SET NULL`, every released child cascade, and the released relation trigger's `W` of each related Work) → queue rows → **at completion:** the deleted Work's `G` rows `FOR UPDATE` ascending, deleted, then the flush's upsert of the surviving roots. **Touches no permit** |
| Deletion unit, Imprint | `P` set → `I` `FOR UPDATE` → `W` rows ascending → `J` → `A` → `DELETE` → queue rows → **at completion:** as above, over every Work the cascade deleted. **Touches no permit** |
| Deletion unit, Publisher | `P` (own `FOR UPDATE`, others share, one sorted pass) → `I` → `W` → `J` → `A` → `DELETE`; the cascade deletes `J` and `A` and sets permit links `NULL` (`X`, last) → queue rows → **at completion:** as above |
| Admission insert | `P` share → census read → admission insert. **No `J`** |
| Control transition, `setWorkUpsertExecution` | `Q` EXCLUSIVE (the profile's gate) → control row `UPDATE`; `Q` held until commit |
| Control transition, `enableWorkUpsertCapture` | the capture flag's `UPDATE` of the control row, and nothing else; it changes no execution state and takes no `Q` |
| **Reserve, `WORK_UPSERT`** | `P` share → `W` share → `G` update → `J` update → `A` update → `Q` SHARE → execution read → `K` ascending → `F` share → `X` insert → `M` insert |
| **Reserve, `PUBLISHER_BACK_CATALOGUE`** | `P` share (outer job's publisher) → `W` share (the unit's Work) → `G` insert-0 then update → `J` update (outer job) → `A` update (outer attempt) → `K` → `F` share → `X` insert → `M` insert |
| **Reserve, `LEGACY_SCHEDULED` / `MANUAL_RECOVERY`** | `P` share → `W` share → `G` insert-0 then update → `K` → `F` share → `X` insert → `M` insert |
| **Finalise** | MVCC read of the permit's immutable metadata → request authorization, taking no lock → `P` share on the permit's `publisher_identity` → `W` share → binding re-resolved under `W` → `G` update → [`J` update → `A` update, job-linked routes only] → [`Q` SHARE, `WORK_UPSERT` only, held through the commit] → `X` `FOR UPDATE` → checks, fence, transition |
| **Route-owner void, superuser void** | request authorization, taking no lock → `X` `FOR UPDATE` only |
| **Outcome report** | request authorization, taking no lock → `X` `FOR UPDATE` only |
| **Reconciliation** | MVCC read of `X.attempt_identity` → `A` `FOR UPDATE` (when the permit has an attempt) → `X` `FOR UPDATE` → transition → internal clearance writes the `A` it already holds |
| **G-7 floor advance** | `F` `FOR UPDATE` → then DRAIN (MVCC read of permits) and every other precondition → floor `UPDATE` → audit insert |
| Released BE-03 coordinator (unchanged) | `P` `FOR UPDATE` → assignment rows → back-catalogue `J` insert → PENDING `J` rows → `P` UPDATE → **all `W` of the publisher** |

### 10.5 Acyclicity

**The TLO argument.** A wait-for cycle needs some transaction to request a lock of a class no later than one it already holds, while another transaction holds that lock and waits, directly or transitively, for the first. By rule 1 no BE-06 transaction ever requests a class earlier than one it holds, and by rule 2 every transaction orders the instances inside a class the same way. A cycle could therefore only arise through a transaction outside the TLO. There are exactly two:

* **The released BE-03 coordinator** takes `P FOR UPDATE` first, then PENDING `J`, then all `W` of its publisher — `J` before `W`. Every BE-06 path that takes `W` or `J` takes `P` first, `FOR SHARE` or, for deletion, `FOR UPDATE`, on a publisher the coordinator could hold. That conflicts with the coordinator's `P FOR UPDATE`, so the two serialise at `P` before either reaches `J` or `W`. A coordinator on a publisher outside the path's publisher set holds nothing the path needs. Its cascade covers only its own works, so it can want a `W` the path holds only when that Work has moved into its publisher after the path chose its `P`; the path then finds, re-resolving the binding under `W`, that its held publisher is not the Work's, and ends or refuses without taking another publisher lock (§10.1). The coordinator waits on `W` behind a transaction that waits for nothing it holds. Executed: the coordinator of the publisher a root had moved to waited on `W(root)` behind finalisation, which refused `BINDING_SUPERSEDED` and committed (§25.19, T274). The unreleased MIG-01 backfill on the integration branch (`publisher_service_configuration/migration_backfill.rs`, §1.1) commits only through the canonical coordinator `replace_publisher_service_configuration`, so it belongs to this class and adds no other.
* **The released multi-row `W` cascades and multi-statement editorial transactions** — the pre-existing class of §10.6, which exists without BE-06. **BE-06 no longer extends it at all.** Under the Alternative A amendment BE-06 adds no Work-row acquisition to any released transaction and holds no generation row across any released statement, so it adds no edge to that class; §10.6 proves it and §25.22–§25.23 execute every schedule that falsified the withdrawn topology.

**The flush adds no edge against the order.** The only lock BE-06 adds to a released transaction is a generation row, at that transaction's completion (rule 7). Take any transaction `T` waiting there for a `G(x)` another transaction `U` holds.
* If `U` is a BE-06 non-editorial path, it holds `W(x)` `FOR SHARE` and, after `G`, waits only for its own `J`, `A`, `Q`, `K`, `F`, `X` or `M` (LO-5 and rule 1). `T` holds one of those only if `T` deletes `x`, and then `T` holds `W(x)` `FOR UPDATE`, which `U`'s `FOR SHARE` excludes. No cycle.
* If `U` is itself at completion, it is acquiring generation rows only, and both transactions' acquisitions are ascending within one statement, so two upserts cannot invert. The one cross-step case is a deleter `D` whose end-of-generation step holds `G(x)`: `U` can need `G(x)` only if `x` is one of its roots, so `x` is an owner of `U` — whose Work row the released statement locked — or a `has-child` parent of one. `D`'s `DELETE` locked `W(x)` `FOR UPDATE`, and, through the released relation trigger, the `W` of every Work related to `x`. Whichever of `D` and `U` took that Work row second waited for the first's commit **before** reaching its own completion, so they are never both at completion. No cycle.
* A transaction at completion requests nothing else: after the flush comes the commit. So no edge leaves a flush except to another generation row, and the two bullets above exhaust those.

**The two released facts this rests on**, executed here and guarded against drift by required implementation tests (§25.23, §26 AC-141):
1. the released `work_relation` trigger updates both endpoints of every relation row it fires on — `LEAST` then `GREATEST` (`work_relation_work_updated_at_with_relations`);
2. every capture owner is a Work whose row the released statement that fired the capture has already locked — directly, through the released `updated_at_with_relations` trigger of the child table, or through the `ON DELETE CASCADE` of the owner foreign key (§4 of the trace in §25.22).

**The permit row adds no edge against the order.**
* Every path that takes `X` takes it as its last row lock.
* The paths that take `X` alone — route-owner void, superuser void, outcome report — are **leaves**: holding `X`, they wait for nothing.
* Reconciliation takes `A` before `X` and writes only the `A` it already holds.
* No path takes `X` and then any of `P`, `W`, `G`, `J` or `A`, so **`X → A` never appears alongside `A → X`**.
* Completion, failure, claim and G-7 read permits by MVCC and never lock them.

**The execution gate adds no edge against the order.**
* The SHARE holders of `Q` are the claim, after lease recovery, and the `WORK_UPSERT` reservation and finalisation, after `A`. After `Q` they request only `K`, `F`, `X` and `M`, or acquisitions that cannot wait (rule 6). None requests `P`, `I`, `W`, `G`, `J` or `A` after `Q`.
* The one EXCLUSIVE holder, `setWorkUpsertExecution`, holds nothing before `Q` and requests only its own control row after it. No BE-06 path row-locks a control row, and the row's other writer, `enableWorkUpsertCapture`, takes no `Q` and requests nothing after the row.
* PostgreSQL grants a new SHARE request only when it conflicts with no holder and no waiter queued ahead of it, so a transition waiting for `Q` holds back later consumers. That adds only soft edges: from a queued consumer to the queued transition, and from the transition to the SHARE holders ahead of it. Those holders wait only for `K`, `F`, `X` or `M`, whose holders never wait for `Q`. Every wait out of a `Q` holder therefore goes to a later class, and every wait into `Q` comes from a transaction that holds only earlier classes.
* A claim waiting at `Q` holds only job rows it took `SKIP LOCKED` in lease recovery and the attempt rows that recovery updated. A transaction waiting for one of those rows — a finalisation at `J`, a reconciliation at `A` — holds no `Q`: finalisation takes `Q` only after `A`, and reconciliation never.

Executed: the pause against the claim, the reservation and finalisation, in both orders; enable in both orders; a repeated pause with a claim queued behind it; and the gate queued behind a SHARE holder waiting on a DOI key, the floor row or the permit row, with the BE-03 coordinator, Work and Publisher deletion, reconciliation and the route-owner void concurrent. No `40P01` (§25.20, T280–T288).

**Why finalisation takes no `K` and no `F`.** It needs neither.
* `K` protects a reservation's blocking check and history read. A `RESERVED` permit is itself blocking from the instant it commits, so no overlapping reservation can commit while it awaits finalisation.
* `F` protects the floor comparison. G-7 refuses to advance while any `RESERVED` permit exists (DRAIN), and every 17-digit value exceeds the only non-zero floor value.

The authorization trigger nevertheless re-proves blocking, history and floor at the transition (§16.2 rule A), so even a bypass-inserted row cannot be authorised in violation. Leaving `K` and `F` out of finalisation removes edges rather than adding them.

**Mechanical evidence.** I ran 39 two-session interleavings on PostgreSQL 17.10, against a harness reproducing the exact released BE-04 statement shapes (§25.17, T205–T223). They covered every path pair the handoff names, in both orders, plus the cross-route pairs.
- Exactly one produced `40P01`: C14b, the withdrawn R52 string-sorted advisory order, kept deliberately as the negative control.
- In every other interleaving, either the two sessions never conflicted, or the second waited on the holder's transaction and then ended in the specified state.
- The second review-correction pass re-ran all 39 against the corrected finalisation, completion, report and void orderings, with identical outcomes and waits and the same single `40P01`, and added 19 interleavings for the held publisher and the seed, two of them with three sessions. None deadlocked (§25.19).
- The third review-correction pass re-ran the 39, and the 15 held-publisher interleavings of §25.19, against the gated claim, reservation and finalisation, with identical outcomes and waits and the same single `40P01`. It added 33 interleavings for the execution gate and the bootstrap, 12 of them with three sessions. None deadlocked (§25.20, T293).

### 10.6 Cross-work propagation, proved

Propagation is what makes a chapter's edit advance its parent's generation (§8.5). Under the withdrawn topology it was also the one place BE-06 reached outside the statement's own rows, and five distinct `40P01` classes followed. Under the amendment (§6.1a) propagation happens in the flush, at transaction completion, and the classes are gone. This section states the proof and names the executed schedules; §25.22 records every one of them on the withdrawn topology, with released-schema controls, and §25.23 records them on this one.

**The five classes, and why each is now impossible.** In each, `P` is a book, `C` a chapter of it, and every schedule is expressed in the released code's own statement shapes.

| # | The schedule | Why it deadlocked under the withdrawn topology | Why it cannot now |
|---|---|---|---|
| T303 | a deletion of `P` (`deleteWork`, or an `Imprint`/`Publisher` deletion whose cascade removes it) against an edit of `C` | the deletion held `W(P)` `FOR UPDATE` and `G(P)`, which its foreign-key cascade had already deleted, and then waited for `W(C)` through the released relation trigger; the edit held `W(C)` and its capture then requested `W(P)` `FOR KEY SHARE` and `G(P)` | the edit's capture requests neither: it records and the flush applies at completion. The deletion's `G(P)` is taken at **its** completion, after `W(C)`, so the deletion never holds a generation row while it waits for a Work row |
| K1, K2 | `moveWorkRelation` on `P`'s chapters — one `UPDATE` per sibling in one transaction — against an edit of the chapter its next `UPDATE` moves, in both orders | the reorder held `G(P)` from its first statement and then waited for `W(C₂)`; the edit held `W(C₂)` and needed `G(P)` | no generation row is held across a statement: the reorder's `G(P)` is taken once, at completion |
| K4 | deleting a Work related to two chapters of one surviving book, against an edit of one of them | the deletion's cascade captured per relation row: the first event took `G(Q)`, and a later event's released trigger then waited for `W(B)`, which the edit held while needing `G(Q)` | one flush, after every cascaded event, holds nothing during the statement |
| K5 | a relation re-pointed from `C_OLD` to `C_NEW`, against creating a relation on `C_OLD` | the released trigger locks only `NEW`'s endpoints on an `UPDATE`; R1 then requested `W(C_OLD)`, which the creation held while waiting for `W(C_NEW)` | R1 is withdrawn: capture takes no Work row (§8.4 rule 3) |
| K6s | two deletions whose cascades reach the chapters of two books in opposite orders, sharing no Work row | each capture event upserted its own roots, so the per-event order was not the transaction's: one deletion took `G(Q₁)` then `G(Q₂)`, the other the reverse | each transaction takes its generation rows once, in one ascending statement |

**The general statement.** A wait-for cycle needs some transaction to hold a lock another wants while wanting one that other holds. BE-06 adds exactly one kind of lock to a released transaction — a generation row, at that transaction's completion (§10.1 rule 7) — and a transaction at completion requests nothing afterwards but its commit. §10.5 discharges the two cases in which a flush can wait at all. Every remaining edge in an editorial or deletion schedule is a released edge, present without BE-06, and the released schema is the control: where it commits both transactions, this schema commits both.

**Enumeration of the released multi-work editorial transactions.** The enumeration below is kept because the drift guard of §8.3 and the test of §25.5 assert its completeness; what changed is the last column.

| Transaction | Shape | Where its generation rows are taken |
|---|---|---|
| `WorkRelation::create` (`work_relation/crud.rs:121-172`) | advisory lock, the inverse INSERT, then the forward INSERT | once, at completion, over both endpoints and their parents |
| `WorkRelation::update` / `delete` | the inverse row, then the forward row (`crud.rs:174-222`) | once, at completion, over `OLD ∪ NEW` endpoints and their parents |
| `Reorder::change_ordinal` (`model/mod.rs:781-835`) | `SET CONSTRAINTS … DEFERRED`, then one `UPDATE` per sibling in the moved range, then the moved row, then the history row | once, at completion, over every touched relation's endpoints and their parents |
| `imprint.publisher_id` move, `publisher_name` edit | one released set-based statement | once, at completion, ascending |
| Work, Imprint or Publisher deletion | the deleted Works `FOR UPDATE`, the released cascades, and — through the released `work_relation` trigger, whose events the cascade queues for the end of the same statement — the `W` of every related Work | once, at completion: the deleted Works' rows removed first, then the surviving roots upserted |
| Any single-entity mutation | one statement | once, at completion |

Separate GraphQL root mutation fields do **not** share a transaction: each resolver opens its own connection and transaction, so a client cannot compose two disjoint multi-work edits into one transaction.

**No residual is claimed, and none was found.** Every schedule above, in both orders, plus the thirteen non-deadlock interleavings of §25.22 (two chapter edits under one parent; parent edit against chapter edit; relation create against chapter edit; chapter deletion against parent edit; relation edit against parent deletion; the BE-03 coordinator; an Imprint deletion; a rolled-back deletion), plus the chapter Work edit held **before** its own row triggers (D2b), plus the three `v1.10.0` deletion units, **committed on the Alternative A schema**, exactly as on the released schema (§25.23, T325–T328). The earlier candidate's own statement that "if a future mutation touches two *disjoint* work sets across multiple statements in one transaction, PostgreSQL may abort one with `40P01`" is superseded: such a transaction now takes its generation rows in one ascending statement at completion, so BE-06 contributes no such abort. A released cycle over released rows remains possible exactly as it is today, without BE-06, and the drift guard of §8.3 still fails CI when a new multi-work path appears.

### 10.7 Claim

The released statement (`distribution_job/crud.rs:220-360`) is reused unchanged in structure. The work-level claim is a **separate mutation** (§19.2) that binds `kind = 'WORK_UPSERT'` and adds these predicate clauses, all evaluated by MVCC inside the one atomic statement:

1. `j.execution_profile = ANY($executionProfiles)` — the caller's explicit, non-empty list.
2. The work's current publisher equals `j.publisher_id` (`work → imprint`).
3. Every `distribution_job_target` row is enabled in `publisher_distribution_platform` under **exactly** `j.activation_id` (released clause, unchanged).
4. A `work_upsert_admission` row exists for `(j.execution_profile, j.publisher_id, j.activation_id)`.
5. `work_upsert_control.execution_enabled` is true for `j.execution_profile`, read in that statement after the claim transaction holds the profile's execution gate `Q` in SHARE mode (below).
6. No uncleared fenced abandonment exists for the `(work_identity, execution_profile)` pair — that is, no attempt of any job of that pair has `result = 'ABANDONED' AND fenced_at IS NOT NULL AND recovery_cleared_at IS NULL` — unless the profile's registry entry is `ReplaySafe`.
7. The profile's own claim guard (§14.4).

`attempt_generation` is replaced by `distribution_job_attempt.claimed_generation`, projected in the same statement from `work_upsert_generation.source_generation` for the claimed row's `(work_id, execution_profile)`. Reading it in the claim statement gives it the claim's snapshot, which is what makes INV-GEN an ordering property rather than an assumption.

Unchanged: lease recovery runs first; `ORDER BY available_at ASC, distribution_job_id ASC`; `FOR UPDATE OF j SKIP LOCKED`; batch clamp 50; lease clamp 60…3600; `attempt_count < 5`; zero claims is an empty result, not an error.

**The execution gate.** Between the released lease recovery and the claim statement, the claim transaction takes `pg_advisory_xact_lock_shared(1948572002, hashtext('be06:work_upsert:execution:' || p))` for each requested registered profile `p`, deduplicated and ascending by key, and holds it until it commits. The statement's snapshot is taken after the gate is granted, so clause 5 reads the control row as committed at that moment. A `setWorkUpsertExecution` holding the gate EXCLUSIVE has either committed before the grant, and the claim reads its value, or waits for the claim's commit. A claim therefore never returns an attempt opened on a value that a returned pause has replaced. A pause that commits first leaves the claim returning `[]` with no attempt opened, and a claim that holds the gate first commits before the pause returns (§21.1). After the gate the statement takes job rows `SKIP LOCKED` and inserts new attempts, neither of which can wait (§10.1 rule 6).

Taking the candidate job rows first and the gate after them, and then re-checking execution, was considered and not adopted. It would split the released atomic statement into a locking statement and a re-check that must release rows already claimed, and it would hold those rows while queued behind a transition. Both placements keep the order acyclic; the adopted one leaves the released statement unchanged (§25.20, T281).

### 10.8 Completion, failure, retry and lease recovery

| # | Situation | Job status | Attempt result | Same row → PENDING | Successor | Permit consequence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Successful completion (T2) | `SUCCEEDED` | `SUCCEEDED` | no | only if `source_generation > claimed_generation` **and** the binding re-resolved under the held `W` equals the held publisher — the job's `publisher_id` — and is eligible and admitted | requires an `ACCEPTED` permit on the attempt at `claimed_generation` |
| 2 | Terminal failure, provider definitely not attempted | `FAILED` | `FAILED` | no | no | outcome report `NONE_ATTEMPTED`; closed |
| 3 | Terminal failure, provider ambiguous | `FAILED` | `FAILED` | no | no | `INDETERMINATE`, **blocking** until reconciled |
| 4 | Retryable failure, provider definitely not attempted | `PENDING` | `FAILED` | yes | no | `NONE_ATTEMPTED`; closed |
| 4a | Retryable failure after finalisation returned `VOIDED_RETRYABLE` (group A) | `PENDING` | `FAILED` | yes | no | already `VOIDED` by that finalisation; the next claim takes the **current** source generation (§16.6) |
| 5 | Retryable failure, provider ambiguous | `PENDING` but unclaimable | `FAILED` | yes, blocked | no | `INDETERMINATE`, blocking. `failDistributionJob` is **refused** while a permit on the attempt is `RESERVED` (`ATTEMPT_HAS_OPEN_RESERVATION`) or `AUTHORIZED` (`ATTEMPT_HAS_AUTHORIZED_PERMIT`) |
| 6 | Lease expiry, no permit | `PENDING`, or `FAILED` at budget | `ABANDONED` | yes | no | none |
| 6a | Lease expiry, permit `RESERVED` | as row 6 | `ABANDONED`, **unfenced** | yes | no | the reservation survives and blocks its DOIs until the route-owner or superuser void (§16.7). It authorised nothing; the attempt is not a fenced abandonment; the claim guard keeps the job unclaimable only while the reservation blocks |
| 7 | Lease expiry, permit `AUTHORIZED` | as row 6 | `ABANDONED`, **fenced** | yes | no | genuinely ambiguous: blocking, and the fenced-abandonment clause blocks the claim until reconciliation resolves the permit and clears the attempt (§16.8) |
| 8 | Lease expiry, permit `INDETERMINATE` | `PENDING`, unclaimable | `ABANDONED`, fenced | yes, blocked | no | blocking until reconciled; clearance follows |
| 9 | Lease expiry after the worker reported `ACCEPTED` but before it completed | `PENDING`, unclaimable | `ABANDONED`, fenced | yes, blocked | no | reconciliation **confirms** the `ACCEPTED` permit and clears the attempt; the job is then claimed again and redeposits at a strictly later timestamp, which Crossref treats as an ordinary update. No operation turns an abandoned job `SUCCEEDED` without a claim |
| 10 | `work_status` became `WITHDRAWN` | continues to completion; the deposit is the crossmark withdrawal | as rows 1–3 | — | as rows 1–3 | as rows 1–3 |
| 11 | Administrative cancellation, attempt **unfenced** or job PENDING | `CANCELLED` / `ADMINISTRATIVE` | `CANCELLED` | no | no | **no permit is touched**: a `RESERVED` permit of the attempt survives, byte-identical and blocking, until voided |
| 12 | Administrative cancellation, attempt **fenced** | **refused** (§12.3) | unchanged | no | no | unchanged |
| 13 | Binding superseded by the materializer | `CANCELLED` / `BINDING_SUPERSEDED` | `CANCELLED` if open and unfenced | no | replacement under the current binding | **no permit is touched**; a surviving `RESERVED` permit blocks the replacement's reservation until voided |
| 13a | Binding superseded, detected inside finalisation (group B) | `CANCELLED` / `BINDING_SUPERSEDED` or `ASSIGNMENT_DISABLED` | `CANCELLED` | no | replacement when eligible and admitted | the finalisation voids **its own** reservation, in the Crossref-owned transaction |
| 14 | Work deleted (§13.3) | `CANCELLED` / `WORK_DELETED` | `CANCELLED` if open and unfenced | no | no | **no permit is touched**; permits survive with immutable identities |

Rows 3, 5, 6a, 7, 8, 11, 13 and 14 are the safety core. A job may terminalize, be cancelled, be superseded, lose its lease or lose its Work, and **none of that erases an in-flight provider ambiguity or releases a reservation**. No generic transition moves a permit (INV-GENERIC-PERMIT). A worker's outcome report closes an `AUTHORIZED` permit, reconciliation resolves or confirms an ambiguous one, and only a Crossref-owned void releases a `RESERVED` one.

T2 reads `source_generation` under the `G` lock it already holds and creates the successor through the single creation helper (§11.2). It locks the job's `publisher_id` `FOR SHARE` before `W`, and re-resolves the binding in a statement after `W` is held. When the Work has moved to another publisher, T2 creates no successor, and the generation above `D` stays residue that the drain materializes under the current binding (§25.19, T275). A `FAILED` job creates no successor; INV-SUCC is discharged by the residue clause, and the work reappears in `workUpsertResidue` only when a later source event raises `S` above `H` (§12.2).

### 10.9 Released BE-04 behaviour: changed versus unchanged

**Unchanged, byte-for-byte:** the claim statement's predicate, ordering, locking, clamps and empty-`kinds` behaviour for `claimDistributionJobs`; `recover_expired_leases`; `complete_distribution_job` and `fail_distribution_job` statements for `PUBLISHER_BACK_CATALOGUE`; `cancel_pending_jobs_for_disabled_group_on`; every released constraint on all three tables except the one FK named below; the retry budget, back-off constants and lease clamps; `DISSEMINATION_WORKER`.

**Changed, exactly one released object:** `distribution_job_work_id_fkey` becomes `ON DELETE SET NULL` (§13.2), which is the decision BE-04 explicitly deferred to this task.

**Added:** the columns, enum values, constraints, indexes and triggers of §18, plus guards that apply only when `kind = 'WORK_UPSERT'`.

A back-catalogue worker built against `v1.8.0` continues to work without modification, subject only to the operational rule of §19.1. Executed at the API over the migrated schema: the released claim, completion, failure, cancellation and recovery paths, including a claim taken before Migration 2 and completed after it with its original token (§25.21, T296, T299).

---

## 11. Fence, creation containment and recovery

### 11.1 INV-FENCE, stated operationally

No `WORK_UPSERT` attempt may begin a profile operation capable of an externally visible or irreversible provider effect until one transaction has proved, under LO-5, **all** of:

1. the job is `RUNNING`;
2. the presented `claim_token` is the job's current token;
3. the attempt bound to that token is open (`finished_at IS NULL`);
4. the `work` row exists;
5. the work's current publisher equals `job.publisher_id`;
6. every `distribution_job_target` row of the job is enabled in `publisher_distribution_platform` under exactly `job.activation_id`;
7. a `work_upsert_admission` row exists for `(execution_profile, publisher_id, activation_id)`;
8. `work_upsert_control.execution_enabled` is true for the profile, read while the transaction holds the profile's execution gate `Q` in SHARE mode, which it keeps until it commits (§21.1);
9. the profile's eligibility and provider-identity predicate holds now.

**Where the fence sits for Crossref.**

* **Reservation** evaluates the nine clauses as a precondition, clause 8 under `Q`, and **writes nothing to the attempt**.
* **Finalisation** evaluates them again, inside its check groups B and A (§16.6), and writes `fenced_at` **only on success** — in the same transaction, at the same moment, as the `RESERVED → AUTHORIZED` transition. A refusal writes no `fenced_at`. It evaluates them holding the permit's `publisher_identity` `FOR SHARE` from before `W` is locked, and reads the configuration clauses 6–9 depend on only once the binding re-resolved under `W` equals that publisher (clause 5), so the publisher whose configuration authorizes the provider write is the one held through the authorization commit (§10.1). It reads clause 8 holding `Q` SHARE, taken after `A` and before `X`, and holds `Q` through the same commit, so `setWorkUpsertExecution(false)` cannot commit between that read and the commit of `fenced_at` (§21.1).

An attempt whose permit is only `RESERVED` is therefore **unfenced**. That is why administrative cancellation, Work deletion and lease recovery treat it as unfenced, and why, by INV-GENERIC-PERMIT, they leave its reservation untouched.

### 11.2 The single creation helper

`work_upsert_create_job(publisher_id, work_id, activation_id, execution_profile, generation, predecessor_job_id) RETURNS uuid`.

Preconditions by contract, asserted in code: the caller holds `P FOR SHARE`, `W FOR SHARE` and `G FOR UPDATE`.

It performs, and is the only place that performs:

1. `targets := registry(execution_profile).targets`; an unregistered key raises `WORK_UPSERT_PROFILE_NOT_IMPLEMENTED`.
2. `job_ordinal := 1 + (count of existing WORK_UPSERT jobs for (work_identity, execution_profile))`, computed under the held `G` lock.
3. `deduplication_key := 'WORK_UPSERT:' || publisher_id || ':' || work_identity || ':' || execution_profile || ':' || activation_id || ':' || generation || ':' || job_ordinal`.
4. `INSERT INTO distribution_job (…, work_id, work_identity, created_generation, job_ordinal, deduplication_key, predecessor_job_id)` with `work_id = work_identity = $work_id`.
5. `UPDATE distribution_job SET superseded_by_job_id = <new> WHERE distribution_job_id = $predecessor` when a predecessor was given.
6. `INSERT INTO distribution_job_target` one row per declared target.

### 11.3 Exactly three creation sites

1. **materialization unit** (§9.3 step 11), including superuser forced materialization — the sole *asynchronous* residue and stale-binding creator, and the creator of every job the bootstrap leads to, after admission (§21.2);
2. **fence binding-refusal replacement** (§11.5);
3. **T2 completion successor** (§10.8 row 1).

Both 2 and 3 are bounded lifecycle fast paths that create only under the publisher they already hold — the permit's `publisher_identity` for 2, the job's `publisher_id` for 3 — only when the binding re-resolved under `W` equals that publisher and is eligible and admitted, and only through the helper. **No fourth creator exists.** Editorial capture is not one: it inserts or advances generation rows, and creates no job (§8.4, frozen rule 7). Neither the bootstrap seed nor admission is one: the seed writes generation state only, admission inserts its own row, and neither calls the helper (§21.2, §21.3). If implementation discovers a genuine fourth site, that is a specification change, not an implementation decision.

**Containment guarantees, in decreasing authority:**

* **Static source containment is authoritative.** A test asserts that the only `INSERT` into `distribution_job` with `kind = 'WORK_UPSERT'` lies inside the helper, and that its call sites are exactly the three above — not the capture function, the seed or admission — the BE-03 write-path containment pattern (`publisher_service_configuration/crud.rs:277`).
* **Row correctness is enforced by the database against any writer**: the actionable-uniqueness index, the deduplication-key UNIQUE and formula CHECK, the per-profile deferred target-set trigger, the `work_identity` and actionable-work CHECKs and their guard trigger.
* **A transaction-local creator marker may be added as defence in depth and test instrumentation only.** It is **not** an authorization or security boundary: the API runs every statement through one pool as a single database role (`thoth-api/src/db.rs:12-17`), so any statement in that role can set the same marker. No security-definer or privilege-separation framework is introduced to rescue the claim.

### 11.4 The per-profile target-set invariant

One deferred constraint-trigger function, armed on **both** `distribution_job` and `distribution_job_target`, evaluated at COMMIT:

```
read the affected job's kind and execution_profile, at COMMIT
IF the job no longer exists, or its kind is not 'WORK_UPSERT' THEN no check
CASE execution_profile
  WHEN 'CROSSREF' THEN  the job's target rows are exactly {CROSSREF}: count = 1 AND bool_and(platform = 'CROSSREF')
  ELSE  RAISE 'WORK_UPSERT_PROFILE_NOT_IMPLEMENTED'  USING ERRCODE = 'check_violation'
END
```

It fires for every relevant change on either table, so a missing, extra or duplicate target row is refused whichever table the change arrived through. It governs `WORK_UPSERT` jobs only: a `PUBLISHER_BACK_CATALOGUE` job, whose `execution_profile` §18.1's CHECK keeps NULL, keeps its released target set and is outside it, and so is a job the transaction has deleted. Executed without that guard, every back-catalogue job the released coordinator created reached `ELSE` and was refused at COMMIT: 93 released tests that pass over the corrected schema failed (§25.21, T297). **Trigger count: 2** (one function, two constraint triggers). A future profile's own reviewed migration replaces the function with one more arm carrying that profile's exact declared set. There is no SQL mirror of `linked_members()` and no descriptor table.

### 11.5 The fence, its outcomes and its exposure

`work_upsert_execution_fence(distribution_job_id, claim_token)` is a substrate function with two parts: **evaluation**, which returns an outcome and writes nothing, and **commit**, which writes `fenced_at` once. For a `ReplayBlocked` profile both are callable only composed into that profile's first-effect-authorising transaction. For Crossref that is `finaliseCrossrefWrite` (§16.6), which evaluates first and commits only after every Crossref check has passed. **No standalone generic fence mutation is exposed in BE-06.** A future `ReplaySafe` profile may expose `fenceWorkUpsertExecution` under its own review.

| Evaluation outcome | Effect inside finalisation |
|---|---|
| **PASS** | continue to the Crossref checks; on their success `fenced_at` is written and the permit becomes `AUTHORIZED` |
| **PASS_REPEAT** | not reachable through finalisation. A permit that is no longer `RESERVED` never reaches evaluation, and an identical retry of an authorised finalisation, once it has passed request authorization and the token check afresh, replays its recorded result without re-evaluating groups B and A (§16.6) |
| **REFUSE, binding superseded** (clauses 4–6) | resolved atomically: the open attempt closes `CANCELLED`; the job becomes `CANCELLED` with `BINDING_SUPERSEDED` (publisher or activation differs) or `ASSIGNMENT_DISABLED` (target not enabled); and, only when the binding re-resolved under `W` equals the held publisher — the permit's `publisher_identity` — and the current binding is eligible and admitted and `source_generation > resolution`, a replacement job is created through §11.2 under that publisher. A publisher change therefore never yields a replacement here: it is `BINDING_SUPERSEDED`, and the drain materializes the residue under the new publisher. An activation or target change under the same publisher can (§25.19, T273). The finalisation then voids **its own** reservation in the same transaction and returns `VOIDED_JOB_RETIRED` |
| **REFUSE, not permitted now** (clauses 7–9) | **no job or attempt state change**; the finalisation voids its own reservation and returns `VOIDED_RETRYABLE`; the worker reports `failDistributionJob(retryable = true)` |
| **NO_WORK** | for a job-linked route, unreachable: the deletion protocol retires the job first (§13.3), so finalisation refuses earlier on the stale claim. For a job-less route, the finalisation voids its reservation with reason `NO_WORK` and returns `VOIDED_RETRYABLE` |

A void performed inside finalisation is **not** a generic path mutating a permit. It is the Crossref-owned finalisation transaction refusing its own reservation, because no external effect was authorised (INV-GENERIC-PERMIT).

`completeDistributionJob` for a `WORK_UPSERT` attempt is **refused unless `fenced_at IS NOT NULL`** (`WORK_UPSERT_COMPLETION_REQUIRES_FENCE`). Failures may be reported without a fence.

### 11.6 Binding change: who owns which case

| Case | Owner | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| binding changes **before** finalisation | generic substrate, inside the Crossref finalisation | finalisation group B refuses: the job is retired and rebound, the finalisation voids its own reservation (§16.6), no provider operation begins, and the current-binding obligation is preserved |
| binding changes **after** finalisation, before or during the external operation | execution profile | the effect was authorised at finalisation; the permit's recorded binding and the profile's reconciliation rules decide; generic code never cancels, deletes or replays a fenced attempt |
| binding changes **after** a durable provider outcome | generic substrate | the outcome is recorded under the claim token; an ownership move is a captured event that raises the generation and yields a successor under the current binding; an activation change creates no new obligation |

### 11.7 INV-RECOVER, the fenced-abandonment fence, and internal clearance

Lease recovery is unchanged: it marks the attempt `ABANDONED` and returns the job to `PENDING` or `FAILED` by budget. The protection lives at claim and fence, not in recovery:

* Registry: every profile declares `ReplaySafe` or `ReplayBlocked` with a proof reference (§7.2).
* `distribution_job_attempt` gains `recovery_cleared_at timestamptz` and `recovery_clearance_reference text`.
* **Default fail-closed** (§10.7 item 6), evaluated per `(work_identity, execution_profile)` so a *new* job for the same work created after the abandonment is equally blocked.
* The substrate invents no `RESERVED`/`AUTHORIZED`/`INDETERMINATE`/`ACCEPTED` state, no ambiguity policy and no remote identifier; `recovery_cleared_at` is a binary clearance.

**Clearance is internal, profile-owned and derived — never an independent operator act.** `work_upsert_clear_fenced_abandonment(attempt_id, reference)` is a substrate function with **no GraphQL exposure of any kind**. It is called from exactly one place: inside `reconcileCrossrefWritePermit` (§16.8), after the reconciliation's own permit write, and only when both hold in that same transaction:

1. the attempt satisfies `result = 'ABANDONED' AND fenced_at IS NOT NULL AND recovery_cleared_at IS NULL`; and
2. no permit whose `attempt_identity` equals that attempt satisfies `crossref_is_blocking_write_permit(state, reconciliation_state)`.

```
Crossref reconciliation truth  ⟹  internal generic recovery clearance
```

**Every fenced abandonment has an exit.** A fenced attempt has exactly one permit (`crossref_write_permit_one_per_work_upsert_attempt_idx`), in one of four states. Reconciliation handles each of them:

* **`AUTHORIZED` or `INDETERMINATE`:** reconciliation resolves it to `ACCEPTED` or `NONE_ATTEMPTED`, whether or not it was first annotated `RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED` or `RECONCILIATION_IMPOSSIBLE` (§16.8). An annotation never clears the attempt: the permit still blocks.
* **`ACCEPTED` or `NONE_ATTEMPTED` already reported by the worker before it crashed:** reconciliation **confirms** it.

In every case clearance follows in the same transaction. The race against lease recovery was executed in both orders; when reconciliation ran first, the later confirmation cleared the attempt (§25.17, T214). A generic operator cannot clear a ReplayBlocked state, because no operation does so.

**Crossref is `ReplayBlocked`.**
* A fenced attempt that vanishes leaves an `AUTHORIZED` or `INDETERMINATE` permit. That permit blocks its DOIs independently, and the generic clause blocks the claim until reconciliation clears it.
