* An attempt that vanishes holding only a `RESERVED` permit is **unfenced**, so the generic clause does not apply to it. The profile's claim guard refuses any job whose DOI set intersects a blocking permit, so it keeps the job unclaimable while the reservation stands. The route-owner or superuser void (§16.7) is the exit.

**Required lifecycle test for every implemented profile:** `finalise AUTHORIZED → lease expires → recover → claim` returns nothing for a `ReplayBlocked` profile until reconciliation clears the attempt, and may return the job for a `ReplaySafe` profile.

---

## 12. Cancellation and terminal resolution

### 12.1 What a terminal job resolves

A terminal job resolves **only the generation it represents**, `gen(job)` (§9.1). Higher source generations remain durable residue and are materialized by the next drain **without requiring another source edit**. Successful completion additionally requires an `ACCEPTED` Crossref permit at the attempt's `claimed_generation` (§17.7), which is why `SUCCEEDED` needs no provider-effect column.

### 12.2 Administrative cancellation is job-instance cancellation

Administrative cancellation of a `WORK_UPSERT` job means **stop this durable job instance**. It never discards a later source generation, including one that committed before the operator pressed Cancel.

* PENDING, never claimed: resolves `created_generation`.
* RUNNING, current attempt unfenced: resolves the attempt's `claimed_generation`.
* Generations above `gen(job)` remain residue; a later drain creates a replacement with no new edit. **This is expected behaviour, not a retry loop**: each terminal job resolves its own generation, so a permanently failing work costs one budgeted job per outstanding generation, never an unbounded loop.
* `ADMINISTRATIVE` remains a job-lifecycle fact.

**No BE-06 operation mutes a Work.** A work-level suppression control, if ever wanted, must be separately named, specified with its own explicit generation snapshot and authorization, and approved. It is not part of BE-06.

Worked cases, each a required test:

| Case | Setup | Result |
|---|---|---|
| C1 | PENDING created at g1, edits advance `S` to g5, cancel before any claim | `H = 1`, residue `(1,5]`, next drain creates a job at g5 |
| C2 | RUNNING **unfenced** claimed at g4, `S` advances to g5, cancel | `H = 4`, residue `(4,5]`, next drain creates a job at g5 |
| C3 | PENDING created at g1, cancel while `S` is still 1 | `H = 1`, residue none, state `RESOLVED`, no rematerialization |
| C4 | FAILED at claimed g4, an edit during the final attempt produced g5 | `H = 4`, residue `(4,5]`, next drain creates a job at g5 |
| C5 | RUNNING **fenced** claimed at g4, cancel | **refused**; job stays `RUNNING`; attempt stays open and fenced; claim token stays valid; `H` unchanged; the worker still reports the outcome truthfully |

**Cancellation never touches a permit.** In C1 and C2 a `RESERVED` permit of the cancelled attempt, where one exists, survives byte-identical and keeps blocking its DOIs until a Crossref-owned void releases it (§16.7). Executed: after administrative cancellation the permit row's fingerprint was unchanged and an overlapping reservation was refused `CROSSREF_PERMIT_BLOCKED` (§25.17, T197).

### 12.3 The fenced-cancellation rule

For `kind = 'WORK_UPSERT'`, `cancelDistributionJob` takes the job row `FOR UPDATE` (released shape) and then:

* PENDING → allowed;
* RUNNING with the current attempt `fenced_at IS NULL` → allowed;
* RUNNING with the current attempt `fenced_at IS NOT NULL` → **refused** with `WORK_UPSERT_CANCELLATION_REFUSED_FENCED_ATTEMPT`; nothing is written; the profile's reporting or reconciliation owns the next transition.

No `ReplaySafe`/`ReplayBlocked` classification changes this: past the fence an external effect may have begun, and generic cancellation would destroy truthful outcome reporting. `PUBLISHER_BACK_CATALOGUE` cancellation keeps released behaviour, constrained only by the universal permit rule that an outer attempt may not terminally close over an open unit permit (§18.10).

**Cancel/fence race.** The two paths serialise on the job row; the cancel path waits on nothing the fence holds, so there is no cycle and exactly one wins: cancel first while unfenced makes the later finalisation refuse `CROSSREF_PERMIT_CLAIM_STALE`, so no effect can be authorised (§25.17, T207); fence first makes the cancel refuse. **No state permits "fenced, then generically cancelled".**

The fenced state and the `AUTHORIZED` permit are created in the same transaction (§11.1), so this refusal applies exactly when an authorization exists, and a job holding only a `RESERVED` permit is cancellable without any permit write.

### 12.4 Terminal suspension is derived, never written

There is no `suspended_generation` column and no `J → G` write of any kind. `D` and `H` are functions of BE-04 rows, so released `failDistributionJob`, `cancelDistributionJob` and `recover_expired_leases` keep their `J → A` shapes with no generation-row lock, and attempt-budget exhaustion through lease recovery produces exactly the same `H` as an explicit terminal failure.

---

## 13. Deletion semantics

### 13.1 The seam

BE-04 chose `ON DELETE CASCADE` for a column it never populates and explicitly deferred the decision to this task (§3.1). BE-06 is the first task to populate `work_id`, so inheriting the cascade would mean an ordinary editorial `deleteWork` silently destroys the API's own record of what was distributed for that Work.

### 13.2 The work-reference decision

**`distribution_job.work_id` becomes `REFERENCES work(work_id) ON DELETE SET NULL`, and every work-level job carries an immutable, non-referential `work_identity uuid`.**

| Option | Verdict |
|---|---|
| retain `ON DELETE CASCADE` | rejected: destroys terminal work-level lifecycle evidence (INV-DELETE-EVIDENCE) |
| **`ON DELETE SET NULL` + immutable `work_identity`** | **adopted**: smallest design meeting every invariant |
| drop the FK and keep a plain UUID, enforcing existence only while actionable | rejected as larger and weaker: it discards the referential guarantee that a job can only be created for an existing Work and replaces it with a trigger that must reproduce it |
| `SET NULL` with the identity recovered from `deduplication_key` | rejected: reporting and reconciliation would parse a text key, and the key's own formula CHECK would lose its stable source once `work_id` is nulled |
| `RESTRICT` | rejected: terminal history would make a Work permanently undeletable |

Consequences: a deleted Work leaves its job rows intact with `work_id` NULL and `work_identity` preserved, so history is queryable by identity; `D`/`H` are computed over live `work_id` rows only, so a deleted Work has no resolution state and can pass none to any future row; and the backstop CHECK of §18.1 turns any deletion path that bypasses the retirement protocol into a hard failure rather than a silent orphaned obligation.

### 13.3 Active-job deletion lifecycle

| State at deletion | Semantics |
|---|---|
| PENDING | atomically retired: `CANCELLED` with `cancellation_reason = 'WORK_DELETED'`, `completed_at` set; job, targets and attempt history preserved |
| RUNNING, current attempt unfenced | atomically retired the same way; the open attempt closes `CANCELLED` and the claim fields clear, invalidating the token immediately. No external effect can follow: the only gate to one is finalisation, which now refuses on the stale claim. A `RESERVED` permit of the attempt **is not touched**: it survives with its immutable identities and blocks its DOIs until a route-owner or superuser void |
| RUNNING, current attempt `fenced_at IS NOT NULL` | **deletion fails closed** with `WORK_DELETE_BLOCKED_BY_FENCED_ATTEMPT` from all three deletion entry points; nothing is retired, cancelled or erased; the outcome must first be reported, or the lease must expire so recovery closes the attempt `ABANDONED` |
| terminal `SUCCEEDED` / `FAILED` / `CANCELLED` | no action; deletion succeeds and the rows survive with `work_id` NULL |

`WORK_DELETED` is necessary rather than decorative: it separates retirement-by-deletion from `ADMINISTRATIVE` (which resolves a generation), from `BINDING_SUPERSEDED` and from `ASSIGNMENT_DISABLED`, and it contributes to neither `D` nor `H`, which is correct because after deletion there is no generation row and therefore no resolution question. No permanent undeletability arises: the fenced block is bounded by the lease, and terminal history never blocks.

### 13.4 The deletion protocol, exactly

Let `bound(W) = { j.publisher_id : j ∈ distribution_job, kind = 'WORK_UPSERT', status = 'PENDING', j.work_id ∈ W }`, read by MVCC. `bound()` covers PENDING only, because the released coordinator locks PENDING job rows only and every BE-06 non-editorial path takes `W` before `J`.

**`deleteWork(x)`** — implemented as a per-entity `Crud::delete` override:

1. MVCC: `cur` := x's current publisher via `work → imprint`.
2. Lock `sorted(cur ∪ bound({x}))`, each `publisher … FOR SHARE`, ascending by id, in one pass.
3. `SELECT 1 FROM work WHERE work_id = x FOR UPDATE`; absent ⇒ done, nothing to delete.
4. Revalidate under the work lock: recompute `cur′ ∪ bound({x})`; it must be a subset of the locked set. It can only shrink, because a new PENDING row for x requires `W FOR SHARE` and an ownership change requires `W FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, both refused while the work row is held `FOR UPDATE`.
5. Otherwise raise a `serialization_failure`-class error; the mutation rolls back and restarts the whole unit. A rollback is required because row locks cannot be released inside a transaction and holding `W` while acquiring a new publisher share would reintroduce the cycle. **Bound: 3 attempts**; on exhaustion return `WORK_DELETE_BINDING_DRIFT_UNRESOLVED`.
6. Retire actionable work-level jobs of x (§13.3), refusing on a fenced attempt. No permit row is written (INV-GENERIC-PERMIT).
7. `DELETE FROM work WHERE work_id = x`.
8. Released cascades run: `distribution_job.work_id` is set to NULL on every work-level row; every released child cascade is unchanged; and the released `work_relation` trigger updates `updated_at_with_relations` on every related Work, taking its row. The Work's generation rows are **not** touched here — `work_upsert_generation.work_id` carries no foreign key (§18.3).
9. At the transaction's completion the flush runs (§8.6): the generation rows of every Work this transaction deleted are taken `FOR UPDATE` ascending and deleted, and only then are the transaction's surviving roots upserted. So the deleted Work's obligation ends with it (INV-DELETE-SOURCE), and the Works its removal affects — its parents, and the chapters whose relation rows the cascade removed — are advanced once, after every Work row this transaction takes has been taken.
10. Error mapping: §21.

**`deleteImprint(i)`** — identical shape with `W` := the imprint's works; step 3 locks the **imprint** row `FOR UPDATE`, which also blocks moves *into* the imprint because that RI check takes `FOR KEY SHARE`; step 6 also locks the imprint's work rows ascending before retiring.

**`deletePublisher(p)`** — identical shape with `W` := the works of p's imprints; step 2 is one ascending pass in which `p` is taken `FOR UPDATE` (the released mode) and every other publisher `FOR SHARE`; step 3 locks p's imprints `FOR UPDATE` ascending, after which no work can move into p while works may still leave, which only shrinks the cascade.

### 13.5 Why no BE-06 `40P01` cycle remains: the two rules that make it true

Deletion is where BE-06 twice introduced a deadlock class, and each was removed by a different rule. Both rules are stated here, and both are executed.

**Rule 1 — the publisher set first (frozen rule 20).** A plain `DELETE` deadlocks against the released BE-03 coordinator, because `SET NULL` updates the job rows and the retirement step locks them, while the coordinator holds PENDING job rows and then wants the publisher's work rows. The publisher-set-first rule removes that cycle: any coordinator that can hold a PENDING row of an affected work holds `P FOR UPDATE` on a publisher in the deletion's lock set, so the two are mutually excluded before the deletion touches any work row. A coordinator on a publisher outside the set holds nothing the deletion needs and wants no work row the deletion holds, because its cascade set is its own works in its own snapshot. All publisher locks are taken in one sorted pass before any work or imprint lock, so two deletion units cannot invert, share/share is compatible, and two cross-bound publisher deletions serialise at the smaller id.

The set must include the **bound** publishers, not merely the current one: a work whose PENDING job is still bound to a former publisher is reachable for an unbounded time under asynchronous rebinding, and a current-publisher-only wrapper deadlocks in exactly that case.

**Rule 2 — the generation rows at completion (the Alternative A amendment, §6.1a).** The fourth review-correction candidate deleted the Work's generation row **inside** the deleting statement, through the withdrawn foreign key's cascade, and then waited — in that same statement — for the Work rows of every related Work, through the released `work_relation` trigger. A concurrent chapter edit held one of those rows and its capture then wanted the parent's generation row. That is T303, and K1, K2, K4 and K6s are the same inversion in four other shapes (§10.6). Under the amendment the deleting transaction touches no generation row until its flush, which runs after every one of its Work-row waits, and the editing transaction touches none until its own. Neither can hold one while waiting for the other.

**Executed.** On the Alternative A schema, with the released binary's exact statement shapes: T303's four schedules, the rolled-back deletion, K1, K2, K4, K5, K6s, K6c, D2b, the thirteen interleavings S01–S13 and the three `v1.10.0` deletion units of §13.4 **all commit, exactly as on the released schema** (§25.23, T325–T328). On the withdrawn topology the same harness produced `40P01` in each of the five classes, and the released schema in none (§25.22).

### 13.6 Publisher deletion, stated honestly

Released `distribution_job.publisher_id ON DELETE CASCADE` is **not reopened**. Deleting a Publisher therefore still removes that publisher's job rows, including work-level history. This specification does not claim BE-06 job lifecycle history survives Publisher deletion. Provider-write evidence is a separate concern and **does** survive (§17.8). Changing the publisher cascade requires its own architecture decision and migration.

The cascade sets the permits' operational links to `NULL` through their `ON DELETE SET NULL` foreign keys. §16.2 rule S1 permits exactly that same-state write and nothing else. Executed with permits in five states: all five survived, every link `NULL`, every identity intact (§25.17, T204).

### 13.7 Deletion outcomes, verified

| Test | Result |
|---|---|
| terminal history survives `deleteWork` | work row and generation row gone; job row remains with `work_id` NULL, identity, generation, ordinal, its target row and its attempt (`fenced`, `SUCCEEDED`) |
| PENDING deletion | retired `CANCELLED/WORK_DELETED`; evidence retained; no candidates; materialize → `NO_WORK`; zero resolution rows |
| unfenced RUNNING deletion | retired, attempt closed `CANCELLED`, token cleared; a stale worker's finalisation → `CROSSREF_PERMIT_CLAIM_STALE` (§25.17, T208), complete → `NO_WORK`, fail → terminal-state refusal |
| fenced RUNNING deletion | all three entry points refuse; job, attempt and evidence unchanged; the worker then reports; deletion afterwards succeeds and preserves the history |
| lease recovery then deletion | after recovery closes the fenced attempt `ABANDONED`, deletion proceeds and the abandoned-fenced evidence survives |
| historical identity | staff reporting by `work_identity` returns the deleted Work's UUID with status, reason, targets and attempts |
| no resurrection | the deleted Work never reappears in candidates, generation rows or a drain; actionable rows with NULL `work_id` remain zero |
| raw unsafe path | a `DELETE` bypassing the protocol is rejected by the backstop CHECK, not silently accepted |
| the deleted Work's generation | removed by the deleting transaction's own flush, before it advances any root, and never recreated: the flush resolves roots in a snapshot that no longer contains the Work (§8.6; T320, T325) |
| a concurrent writer's row for the deleted Work | removed by that writer's own flush (§8.6 step 4): the vanished-root cleanup, with its negative control (T321) |
| the capture queue | empty after the deletion commits, and after one that rolls back (T322) |
| `RESERVED` permit on an attempt retired by deletion | survives byte-identical and still blocking; an overlapping reservation is refused (§25.17, T198) |
| Publisher deletion with permits in five states | all five survive; links `NULL`; identities intact (§25.17, T204) |

---

## 14. The Crossref execution profile

### 14.1 Identity

```
key                 CROSSREF
targets             {CROSSREF}                (exactly one target row per job, enforced at COMMIT, §11.4)
granularity         one root Work × one CROSSREF target × one generation; never a batch, never multi-work
fenced_recovery     ReplayBlocked
```

### 14.2 Eligibility

A Work `w` is eligible for Crossref work-level execution iff **all** of the following hold. Every clause fails closed; unknown, inactive, manual, pull and feed routes yield `false`.

| # | Predicate | Rule and evidence |
|---|---|---|
| E1 | Automatic-push route | the publisher's service configuration declares Crossref, and `DistributionPlatform::Crossref.descriptor().back_catalogue_behaviour == AutomaticPush`. Manual, pull and feed routes are not eligible. |
| E2 | Covered publisher, current activation | a `publisher_distribution_platform` row with `platform = 'CROSSREF'`, `enabled = true`, whose `activation_id` is the publisher's current activation. |
| E3 | Assignment state | that assignment is enabled and not withdrawn. |
| E4 | Depositable identity | `w.doi IS NOT NULL` **or** `w` has a `HAS_CHILD` relation to a Work with a non-NULL DOI — the serializer's own gate (`doideposit_crossref.rs:87-97`). |
| E5 | Work status | `work_status ∈ {ACTIVE, WITHDRAWN}`. `FORTHCOMING` qualifies only once `publication_date IS NOT NULL`. `SUPERSEDED`, `POSTPONED_INDEFINITELY` and `CANCELLED` are not eligible. |
| E6 | Serializer preconditions | `publication_date IS NOT NULL` (`:493`); at least one `publication` with a non-NULL `isbn` (`:545`); `landing_page IS NOT NULL` when `doi IS NOT NULL`, because `landing_page` gates the whole `<doi_data>` block (`:704`); **at least one `title` row for the Work and for every emitted chapter**, because `titles[0]` (`:212`, `:220`) panics otherwise; every emitted chapter has a `landing_page` (`:856`) and no `edition` (`:905`); every emitted abstract normalises under `normalise_crossref_abstract_jats` (`:429`, `:453`). A Work failing any of these is **reported, not enqueued**. |
| E7 | Control | `work_upsert_control` row for `CROSSREF` has `capture_enabled = true` for materialization and `execution_enabled = true` for claiming. |
| E8 | Admission | a `work_upsert_admission` row exists for `('CROSSREF', publisher_id, activation_id)`. |

**First registration versus maintenance.** Both use this predicate. There is no separate first-deposit path: Crossref deposit is idempotent by DOI at the provider, and the version floor plus the per-DOI strictly-increasing timestamp order revisions. The distinction is recorded only in reporting.

**ACTIVE → WITHDRAWN** is a `work` UPDATE on a watched column (§8.2 row 1), so it captures; the resulting deposit carries the crossmark `withdrawal` update type (`:575-577`), which is why withdrawal is a deposit and not a cancellation. Note the released structural fact that a withdrawn Work whose imprint has no `crossmark_doi` emits no withdrawal signal at all (`:573-583`); that is a serializer property, reported by §20 and not repaired here.

**Parent with a DOI-bearing child** qualifies through E4's second disjunct.

### 14.3 DOI membership of a deposit is API-derived and serializer-exact

For a Crossref deposit the membership is exactly the set of DOIs the serializer registers:

* the root Work's DOI, **when the root has both a DOI and a landing page** — the only case in which the serializer emits root `doi_data` (§3.6); and
* the DOI of every `HAS_CHILD` related Work that has a DOI — each yields one `content_item` with its own `doi_data`.

```sql
CREATE FUNCTION public.crossref_deposit_membership(root uuid) RETURNS text[] LANGUAGE sql STABLE AS $$
    SELECT coalesce(array_agg(d ORDER BY d COLLATE "C"), ARRAY[]::text[]) FROM (
        SELECT public.crossref_canonical_doi(w.doi) AS d
          FROM public.work w
         WHERE w.work_id = root AND w.doi IS NOT NULL AND w.landing_page IS NOT NULL
        UNION
        SELECT public.crossref_canonical_doi(c.doi)
          FROM public.work_relation r JOIN public.work c ON c.work_id = r.related_work_id
         WHERE r.relator_work_id = root AND r.relation_type = 'has-child' AND c.doi IS NOT NULL
    ) s WHERE d IS NOT NULL
$$;
```

* `'has-child'` is the label the released `relation_type` enum stores; `HAS_CHILD` is its GraphQL name (§8.4 rule 2).
* **`COLLATE "C"` is normative** wherever an ordering feeds the digest or a comparison with the worker, because it orders by code point independent of the database's default collation. Executed: `en-US-x-icu` orders `…/a_b`, `…/a-b`, `…/ab`, whereas `C` and Python's `sorted()` both order `…/a-b`, `…/a_b`, `…/ab` (§25.17, T245).
* A chapter with a DOI but no landing page is a member. The serializer then fails with `IncompleteMetadataRecord` (§3.6), so no artifact exists and no finalisation can succeed.
* An empty membership refuses the reservation with `CROSSREF_PERMIT_EMPTY_DOI_SET` before any artifact is fetched. This is what neutralises the pre-existing document-that-registers-nothing defect (§3.8 item 3).

**The set is computed by the API from the database, and is never parsed out of the XML or taken from the caller.** No reservation mutation accepts DOI values. At finalisation the worker submits the registration membership it extracted under §16.11, **only** as a cross-check against this set (§16.6 check A3).

**Parity, executed.** The corrected rule equals the reference extractor's output on the same shapes — `book_metadata`, `book_series_metadata`, root without a landing page, root without a DOI, mixed-case identifiers (§25.17, T244). R52A's rule, which counted a root DOI whether or not it is emitted, **disagreed** on the root-without-landing-page shape, and would have voided every legacy deposit of such a Work forever.

### 14.4 Profile guard seams

The Crossref profile supplies four guards to the generic substrate, and no others. Each reads permits by MVCC and locks none:

* **claim guard** — refuse a job whose API-derived DOI set intersects a blocking permit;
* **first-effect guard** — the composed finalisation of §16.6, which contains the generic fence evaluation and commit and the INV-BIND identity comparison;
* **completion guard** — `completeDistributionJob` requires an `ACCEPTED` permit on the attempt at its `claimed_generation` (`WORK_UPSERT_COMPLETION_REQUIRES_ACCEPTED_PERMIT`);
* **failure guard** — `failDistributionJob` is refused while a permit on the attempt is `RESERVED` (`ATTEMPT_HAS_OPEN_RESERVATION`) or `AUTHORIZED` (`ATTEMPT_HAS_AUTHORIZED_PERMIT`), so a worker must void its reservation, or report its provider truth, before it gives the attempt up.

---

## 15. Crossref payload freshness, the prepared artifact and the cache

### 15.1 The requirement

**A Crossref attempt that discharges generation `g` must operate on an artifact containing every Crossref-relevant committed event captured through `g`, and that artifact must be exactly the bytes its permit authorises.** Generation propagation alone is not sufficient, because the released export path can serve a payload built before those events.

### 15.2 What the released path does, and why it cannot be used

`GET /specifications/{specification_id}/work/{work_id}` returns a Redis-cached payload whenever `cached_timestamp >= work.updatedAtWithRelations` (`record.rs:184-212`). Three released facts make it unusable for a deposit:

1. The freshness token is the Work's own `updated_at_with_relations`, which a chapter edit does not move, while the parent's payload embeds chapter data throughout. A parent deposit could be built from stale child metadata.
2. The cache has no TTL and no per-work invalidation; the only tool is an interactive, TTY-bound, per-specification bulk delete (`src/bin/commands/cache.rs:21-42`).
3. `<timestamp>` and `doi_batch_id` are frozen at first generation and replayed on every hit (`:50-62`). A replayed deposit **cannot supersede its predecessor at Crossref**, because `<timestamp>` is the deposit version.

BE-06 must not mutate `updated_at_with_relations` (frozen rule 23), so the cache cannot be fixed by moving the token.

### 15.3 The prepared-deposit route

**The BE-06 prepared path never reads and never writes the export cache, and the deposit `<timestamp>` is supplied by the API, not generated by the serializer.**

```
GET /specifications/{specification_id}/work/{work_id}/prepared/{crossref_timestamp}
```

**The exact seam.** Only three files in `thoth-export-server` change; `record.rs`, `xml/mod.rs` and every other exporter are untouched.

1. **`src/xml/doideposit_crossref.rs`.**
   * The body of `DoiDepositCrossref::handle_event` moves into a private `fn write_deposit<W: Write>(w, works, timestamp: &str)`. `handle_event` calls it with `&Utc::now().format("%Y%m%d%H%M%S").to_string()`, so the released 14-digit behaviour is preserved exactly.
   * A new `pub(crate) fn generate_prepared(&self, works: &[Work], deposit_timestamp: &str) -> ThothResult<String>`:
     - refuses any value rejected by `is_valid_crossref_timestamp` (§17.2);
     - writes `crate::record::XML_DECLARATION` with no doctype;
     - builds the identical `EmitterConfig::new().write_document_declaration(false).perform_indent(true)`;
     - calls `write_deposit` with the supplied value.
   * `is_valid_crossref_timestamp` lives in the same file.
2. **`src/specification/handler.rs`.** A new `by_work_prepared(path: web::Path<(String, Uuid, String)>, thoth_client)`:
   * refuses any specification other than `doideposit::crossref` with the released "not a valid metadata specification" error;
   * validates the timestamp **before any query**, answering HTTP 400 with the stable body `CROSSREF_TIMESTAMP_NOT_DECODABLE`;
   * builds `SpecificationQuery::by_work(thoth, work_id, specification)` and awaits `run()` directly;
   * returns `DoiDepositCrossref {}.generate_prepared(&works, ts)` with the released XML content type.

   It never calls `get_work_last_updated`, never constructs a `MetadataRecord`, and never touches `cache_key`, `cache_timestamp_key` or `cache_error_key`.
3. **`src/specification/mod.rs`.** One more resource, `/{specification_id}/work/{work_id}/prepared/{crossref_timestamp}`, routed to `by_work_prepared`. `paperclip` regenerates the OpenAPI artifact with exactly one new path.

**The released route keeps every byte of its behaviour**, including its cache, its 14-digit timestamp and its `Content-Disposition`.

**Executed against the released tree.** The seam was applied to a scratch copy of `master 4fa7eaa9`'s export server (§25.17, T246–T247):

* all 8 released `doideposit_crossref` tests still pass;
* for a complete Work, the prepared document equals the released document byte-for-byte, except that the 14-digit timestamp is replaced by the supplied 17-digit value in both `<timestamp>` and the `doi_batch_id` suffix;
* `generate_prepared` refuses second 60, hour 24, 29 February in a non-leap year, a leading-zero year, a 16-digit value and a non-digit value.

**Considered and rejected:**

* A generation-aware cache key — it adds a cache system to a payload that must be provably fresh, and still replays a stale `<timestamp>`.
* A cache-bypass query parameter — the crate reads no query string today, and a bypass without a caller-supplied timestamp still cannot supersede.
* Invalidating the parent's cache entry from BE-06 — this needs either a watermark mutation, which is forbidden, or a Redis dependency in `thoth-api`'s editorial path.
* Having `thoth-api` fetch the artifact itself — this puts a synchronous cross-service call inside a transaction holding `P`, `W`, `G`, `J` and `A`.
* A timestamp parameter on `XmlSpecification::generate` or `handle_event` — both belong to a trait shared by every XML exporter.

### 15.4 The artifact protocol

Preparation has **no external effect**. Its position is fixed by a circular dependency that must be broken explicitly:

```
DOI membership → timestamp allocation → artifact bytes → payload digest → authorization
        ↑                                                                        │
        └──────────── the artifact must contain exactly this membership ─────────┘
```

R52B breaks the cycle with a **reservation / finalisation** protocol that is identical on all four routes apart from the route prefix and credential:

| Step | Actor | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | worker or operator | obtains the route's authority: a `WORK_UPSERT` claim; a back-catalogue claim plus the unit's root Work; a legacy selection; or an operator authorization reference |
| 2 | API | **reserve** (§16.4). Checks the route's preconditions without writing any attempt state; derives membership (§14.3); creates the `0` generation row if absent and reads the witness under `G FOR UPDATE`; takes `K` then `F`; refuses on any blocking permit; allocates `crossref_timestamp` once (§17.3) and `doi_batch_id`; inserts a `RESERVED` permit and its immutable membership. **Blocking from this instant; authorising nothing** |
| 3 | worker | fetches the artifact from the prepared route with **exactly that timestamp**. Generated, never cached |
| 4 | worker | computes `payload_digest` (lower-case hex SHA-256 of the exact response bytes) and extracts, from those same bytes, the registration membership (§16.11), the `<timestamp>` and the `doi_batch_id` |
| 5 | API | **finalise** (§16.6). Runs checks C, then B, then A. On success it writes `fenced_at` (job-linked routes), binds `payload_digest` and moves the permit to `AUTHORIZED`. Otherwise it voids its own reservation and returns the discriminant, or refuses and mutates nothing |
| 6 | worker | recomputes the digest of the bytes it is about to send and compares it to the bound value. A mismatch means: abort without POSTing, and report `NONE_ATTEMPTED` |
| 7 | worker | POSTs **exactly those bytes**, then reports (§16.7) |

**The external-write-authorized point is the commit of step 5.** Before it, no byte may reach Crossref. After it, exactly the byte sequence whose SHA-256 equals `payload_digest` may.

### 15.5 Why this satisfies INV-GEN, INV-BIND and INV-ARTIFACT

* **INV-GEN.** The artifact is generated at step 3, strictly after the claim froze `claimed_generation` at step 1, from current database state with no cache. Every event counted at or below `claimed_generation` was committed before the claim and is therefore in the artifact.
* **INV-BIND.** The fence runs inside step 5 under `W FOR SHARE` and a share lock on the permit's own publisher, whose configuration it reads only once the binding re-resolved under `W` equals it (§10.1). The binding is therefore proved at the instant authorization begins, not earlier, and the configuration that proved it cannot change before the authorization commits. Between step 5's commit and the POST, ownership can change; the worker therefore compares the artifact's publisher identity and DOI prefix against the binding recorded in the `AUTHORIZED` permit (§16.6) immediately before the POST (§28.2, step D) and refuses if they differ, reporting `NONE_ATTEMPTED`.
* **INV-ARTIFACT.** The generation witness is the load-bearing mechanism. `source_generation` is bumped by every captured Crossref-relevant event on the root **and on every chapter, propagated to the parent** (§8.4 rule 2), so `witness_at_finalisation = witness_at_reservation` means no payload-relevant event committed during preparation, and therefore the artifact fetched at step 3 is byte-equivalent to one fetched at step 2 — whose membership is exactly the permit's. The three artifact-derived equality checks (DOI set, batch id, timestamp) are independent cross-checks over the same property.

**Why membership equality alone is insufficient**, and why the witness cannot be dropped: a chapter may be attached and then detached during preparation. The artifact then contains a DOI the database no longer yields, while the re-derived membership equals the reserved membership exactly. Falsified in §25.16 (T177): membership comparison passes, the witness catches it, and the finalisation voids its own reservation.

### 15.6 Legacy scheduling and the 17-digit cutover

**The legacy selector and the legacy provider write are different things, and R52B keeps only the first.**

| | Before the cutover | After the cutover (gate 16 onward) |
|---|---|---|
| **Legacy selector** — `cr_bulk_disseminate.yml` (hourly at `:45`) → `CrossrefIDFinder` | chooses root Works | **unchanged**, until separately retired |
| **Legacy provider write** — `CrossrefUploader.upload_to_platform()` | GET the released public route (14-digit, cached) → POST | `reserveLegacyScheduledCrossrefWrite(rootWorkId)` → prepared route with the permit's 17-digit timestamp → extract → finalise → recheck → POST **exactly that artifact** → report |
| **Released public route** | serves consumers, and feeds the legacy POST | serves non-provider consumers only, still 14-digit and cached; **never** a provider-writing source |
| **`WORK_UPSERT`** | not executable (`execution_enabled = false`) | reserve → prepared 17-digit artifact → finalise → POST |
| **Back-catalogue unit** | runner → released route → POST | per-unit reservation → prepared 17-digit artifact → finalise → POST |
| **Manual dispatch** — `manual_disseminate.yml` | any operator, released route → POST | `reserveManualRecoveryCrossrefWrite` with a superuser credential and an authorization reference, or refused (§28.6) |

Consequences:

1. **No global serializer default changes.** The released route calls `generate(&data, None)` and keeps `Utc::now().format("%Y%m%d%H%M%S")` byte-for-byte. Only the prepared route emits a supplied 17-digit value.
2. **No atomic cross-repository deployment is assumed.**
   1. The API ships first, inert: migrated at gates 9 and 10, then deployed at gate 11 (§23.6).
   2. The dissemination release that moves every Crossref write path onto the permit protocol ships next.
   3. Crossref work-level execution is enabled only after that release is live (gate 16).
3. **Both provider routes obey one rule set.** Legacy and `WORK_UPSERT` permits share one DOI history, one blocking predicate, one lock order and one timestamp allocator.
   * Executed, blocking: a `WORK_UPSERT` reservation for a root whose legacy permit is `AUTHORIZED` is refused `CROSSREF_PERMIT_BLOCKED`.
   * Executed, ordering: once the legacy permit is `ACCEPTED`, a `WORK_UPSERT` reservation made with a clock behind the legacy deposit receives exactly that timestamp's calendar successor (§25.17, T255).
4. **Legacy retirement** remains a later, separate authorization. It needs all four:
   * `crossrefDrained` is true;
   * every covered publisher is admitted;
   * the G-7 floor advance has committed and been reconciled;
   * one full observation window has passed with no unresolved permit and no residue growth.

**Can a 14-digit provider write occur after universal permits are mandatory? No.**

* **Mechanically**, after the cutover no API operation yields an authorization for anything but a prepared 17-digit artifact:
  - the permit carries a 17-digit `crossref_timestamp` (CHECK);
  - finalisation refuses an artifact whose `<timestamp>` differs from it;
  - the prepared route refuses every non-17-digit or calendar-invalid value.
* **By gate**, Crossref execution is not enabled until the conformance evidence of §28.7 shows every dissemination write path — scheduled, manual and back-catalogue — reaching Crossref only through the permit protocol, and none of them using the released route.
* **At the provider**, as defence in depth only: Crossref's documented deposit rule refuses a submission whose `<timestamp>` is not greater than the one it already holds for that DOI. So once a DOI has a 17-digit deposit, a stray 14-digit submission cannot supersede it. Nothing in R52B relies on this rule for safety.

The API cannot observe a POST that bypasses it entirely. That residual is the contractual half of §16.10, and gate 16 exists to close it before activation.

---

## 16. Crossref write permits: states, membership, ordering and authorization

### 16.1 Purpose and universality

Every Crossref provider write, from **all four routes**, must hold an `AUTHORIZED` permit at the moment of the write. The permit — not the job — is the durable record of what may have reached the provider, and it outlives the job, the attempt, the Work and the Publisher.

Routes (closed enum `crossref_write_route`): `WORK_UPSERT`, `PUBLISHER_BACK_CATALOGUE`, `LEGACY_SCHEDULED`, `MANUAL_RECOVERY`.

### 16.2 The state machine

Six states. Exactly one of them authorises a provider write.

| State | Authorises a POST? | Blocking? | Terminal? | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `RESERVED` | **no** | **yes** | no | membership, timestamp and batch id are fixed and the DOIs are held; no artifact is authorised |
| `AUTHORIZED` | **yes** | yes | no | the manifest is complete and immutable; exactly the bytes named by `payload_digest` may be POSTed |
| `INDETERMINATE` | no | yes | no | a write was attempted and the outcome is unknown |
| `ACCEPTED` | no | no | **yes** | the provider acknowledged receipt of the submission |
| `NONE_ATTEMPTED` | no | no | **yes** | proven that no write reached the provider |
| `VOIDED` | no | **no** | **yes** | a reservation was released before it ever authorised anything |

**The complete transition relation.** Seven state-changing edges, and three same-state writes. Nothing else exists, in either direction.

| From | To | Actor and operation |
|---|---|---|
| `RESERVED` | `AUTHORIZED` | `finaliseCrossrefWrite`, which is also the fence (§16.6) |
| `RESERVED` | `VOIDED` | route-owner void, superuser void, or a finalisation voiding its own reservation (§16.7) |
| `AUTHORIZED` | `ACCEPTED`, `INDETERMINATE`, `NONE_ATTEMPTED` | outcome report (§16.7) or reconciliation (§16.8) |
| `INDETERMINATE` | `ACCEPTED`, `NONE_ATTEMPTED` | reconciliation only |
| same state, rule S1 | — | referential `SET NULL` of an operational link on Publisher, job or attempt deletion |
| same state, rule S2 | `INDETERMINATE` | the annotation act, once: `reconciliation_state` from `NULL` to `RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED` or `RECONCILIATION_IMPOSSIBLE`, with its reference and time (§16.8) |
| same state, rule S3 | `ACCEPTED`, `NONE_ATTEMPTED` | reconciliation confirmation: `reconciliation_state` from `NULL` to `RECONCILED`, with its reference and time |

**Database-enforced rules.** A `BEFORE INSERT` row trigger, a `BEFORE UPDATE OR DELETE` row trigger and a `BEFORE TRUNCATE` statement trigger on `crossref_write_permit` enforce every rule below against any statement in the API's single database role.

* **I1 — insertion.** A permit can only be inserted as a clean `RESERVED` row: `state = 'RESERVED'`, with `payload_digest`, `authorized_at`, `provider_reported_at`, `closed_at`, `reconciliation_state`, `reconciliation_annotation_reference`, `reconciliation_annotated_at`, `reconciliation_authorization_reference`, `reconciled_at`, `void_reason`, `void_detail` and `void_authorization_reference` all `NULL` (`CROSSREF_PERMIT_INITIAL_STATE_INVALID`). Executed: a direct `INSERT` into each of the other five states, and a `RESERVED` insert already carrying a digest, were all refused (§25.17, T237); re-run against the corrected trigger, with a report time, an annotation reference and a reconciled time added to the refused shapes (§25.18, T269).
* **U1 — immutable for life.** `permit_id`, `route`, `scope`, `reservation_token`, `publisher_identity`, `root_work_identity`, `job_identity`, `attempt_identity`, `permit_generation`, `source_generation_witness`, `crossref_timestamp`, `doi_batch_id`, `doi_set_digest`, `doi_set_cardinality`, `issued_at` and the issuance-time `operator_authorization_reference` never change (`CROSSREF_PERMIT_EVIDENCE_IMMUTABLE`).
* **U2 — operational links.** `publisher_id`, `distribution_job_id` and `distribution_job_attempt_id` may only change to `NULL`, and are never set or restored (`CROSSREF_PERMIT_LINK_NOT_RESTORABLE`).
* **U3 — write-once fields.** Once non-NULL, these never change: `payload_digest`, `authorized_at`, `provider_reported_at`, `closed_at`, `reconciliation_annotation_reference`, `reconciliation_annotated_at`, `reconciliation_authorization_reference`, `reconciled_at`, `void_reason`, `void_detail`, `void_authorization_reference`. Each may move from `NULL` to a value only inside the act that owns it:
  * `payload_digest` and `authorized_at` only on `RESERVED → AUTHORIZED`;
  * the three void fields only on `RESERVED → VOIDED`;
  * `provider_reported_at` only in the outcome report, on `AUTHORIZED → ACCEPTED | INDETERMINATE | NONE_ATTEMPTED` (§16.7);
  * `reconciliation_annotation_reference` and `reconciliation_annotated_at` only in the annotation act: `AUTHORIZED → INDETERMINATE`, or rule S2 (§16.8);
  * `reconciliation_authorization_reference` and `reconciled_at` only in the act that sets `reconciliation_state = RECONCILED`: a resolution to `ACCEPTED` or `NONE_ATTEMPTED`, or rule S3;
  * `closed_at` only on entry to `ACCEPTED`, `NONE_ATTEMPTED` or `VOIDED`.

  Any other write is refused `CROSSREF_PERMIT_WRITE_ONCE_FIELD`. An edge out of `AUTHORIZED` is exactly one act: the outcome report, which writes `provider_reported_at` and no reconciliation field, or a reconciliation, which writes its reconciliation fields and not `provider_reported_at`. A write that is both, or neither, is `CROSSREF_PERMIT_ILLEGAL_TRANSITION`.

* **S1, S2, S3 — the only same-state writes**, as tabled above. Any other update that leaves `state` unchanged is `CROSSREF_PERMIT_ILLEGAL_TRANSITION`.
* **T — the edge relation.** Any state change not in the table is `CROSSREF_PERMIT_ILLEGAL_TRANSITION`. Executed over all 30 ordered pairs of distinct states: exactly the seven specified edges were permitted, and 23 were refused (§25.17, T239).
* **A — authorization preconditions, re-proved at `RESERVED → AUTHORIZED`:**
  * `payload_digest` and `authorized_at` are set (`CROSSREF_PERMIT_AUTHORIZATION_REQUIRES_MANIFEST`);
  * `work_upsert_generation.source_generation` for the root equals `source_generation_witness` (`CROSSREF_ARTIFACT_SOURCE_CHANGED`);
  * for `WORK_UPSERT`, the permit's attempt is open and `fenced_at IS NOT NULL` (`CROSSREF_PERMIT_AUTHORIZATION_REQUIRES_FENCE`);
  * no **other** blocking permit overlaps the membership (`CROSSREF_PERMIT_BLOCKED`);
  * `crossref_timestamp` exceeds the floor and every **other** non-`VOIDED` overlapping permit's timestamp (`CROSSREF_TIMESTAMP_NOT_INCREASING`).

  **History at authorization excludes the permit being authorised.** That permit overlaps its own membership and carries its own timestamp, so a history that included it could never be strictly exceeded and no permit could ever be authorised. Every other non-`VOIDED` overlapping permit still counts, and the comparison stays strict. The two queries, exactly as the trigger evaluates them:

```sql
-- rule A, inside crossref_write_permit_fsm, on RESERVED -> AUTHORIZED.
-- NEW is the permit being authorised; it overlaps its own membership and carries its own
-- timestamp, so both queries exclude it by identity.
IF EXISTS (SELECT 1
             FROM public.crossref_write_permit_doi mine
             JOIN public.crossref_write_permit_doi o ON o.doi = mine.doi
             JOIN public.crossref_write_permit p ON p.permit_id = o.permit_id
            WHERE mine.permit_id = NEW.permit_id
              AND p.permit_id <> NEW.permit_id
              AND public.crossref_is_blocking_write_permit(p.state, p.reconciliation_state)) THEN
    RAISE EXCEPTION 'CROSSREF_PERMIT_BLOCKED';
END IF;
SELECT max(p.crossref_timestamp) INTO history
  FROM public.crossref_write_permit_doi mine
  JOIN public.crossref_write_permit_doi o ON o.doi = mine.doi
  JOIN public.crossref_write_permit p ON p.permit_id = o.permit_id
 WHERE mine.permit_id = NEW.permit_id
   AND p.permit_id <> NEW.permit_id
   AND p.state <> 'VOIDED';
SELECT floor_value INTO floor_now FROM public.work_crossref_version_floor;
IF NEW.crossref_timestamp <= floor_now
   OR (history IS NOT NULL AND NEW.crossref_timestamp <= history) THEN
    RAISE EXCEPTION 'CROSSREF_TIMESTAMP_NOT_INCREASING';
END IF;
```

  Executed: a bypass-inserted `RESERVED` row cannot be raw-updated into `AUTHORIZED` when it overlaps an authorised permit, lacks a fence, carries a false witness, or sits below history (§25.17, T238). Against the text above, a clean `RESERVED` permit authorises; an older overlapping `ACCEPTED` or `NONE_ATTEMPTED` permit at or above its timestamp refuses it; an older `VOIDED` one does not constrain it. Read without the self-exclusion, the same trigger refused every clean authorization (§25.18, T265).
* **D — permanence.** `DELETE` of a permit is refused (`CROSSREF_PERMIT_DELETE_REFUSED`). `TRUNCATE` of `crossref_write_permit` or `crossref_write_permit_doi` is refused by statement-level triggers. No foreign key cascades a delete into either table.

**What the database cannot enforce, stated plainly.**
* It checks that `fenced_at` is set, but it cannot prove *how* it was set.
* It cannot check the worker-reported artifact cross-checks, because those values are never stored.

Both are finalisation's obligations. Static source containment protects them: exactly one code path issues `RESERVED → AUTHORIZED`, and that path is `finaliseCrossrefWrite`. This is the same standard §11.3 applies to the creator helper. It is not a security boundary against arbitrary SQL in the API's own role, and it is not presented as one.

`ACCEPTED` means **Crossref acknowledged the submission, not that registration completed**. Crossref registration is asynchronous; no code path treats `ACCEPTED` as "the DOI is live".

`crossref_reconciliation_state` is `NULL` (no reconciliation act yet), `RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED`, `RECONCILIATION_IMPOSSIBLE` or `RECONCILED`. **The two annotations are not terminal.** Each records a superuser's assessment of an `INDETERMINATE` permit: `RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED`, the outcome is unknown and must be established; `RECONCILIATION_IMPOSSIBLE`, the evidence available when it was recorded cannot establish it. Both keep the permit blocking, and either is superseded by a resolution to `ACCEPTED` or `NONE_ATTEMPTED` once evidence establishes the truth (§16.8). Only `RECONCILED` is final. A permit that no evidence ever resolves stays blocking — its DOIs unwritable, its fenced attempt uncleared, DRAIN false and G-7 ineligible — but it always keeps an exit, which §11.7 requires of every fenced abandonment.

### 16.3 Canonical DOI identity (R52A-6)

Membership is persisted exactly; `doi_set_digest` alone is never an overlap index.

```sql
CREATE TABLE public.crossref_write_permit_doi (
    permit_id uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES public.crossref_write_permit(permit_id),
    doi       text NOT NULL,
    PRIMARY KEY (permit_id, doi),
    CONSTRAINT crossref_write_permit_doi_canonical_check CHECK (
        doi ~ '^https://doi\.org/10\.[0-9]{4,9}/[-._;()/:a-zA-Z0-9<>+\[\]]+$' AND doi = lower(doi))
);
CREATE INDEX crossref_write_permit_doi_lookup_idx
    ON public.crossref_write_permit_doi (doi, permit_id);
```

**The canonicalisation function, total for every value live Thoth accepts:**

```sql
CREATE FUNCTION public.crossref_canonical_doi(raw text) RETURNS text
LANGUAGE plpgsql IMMUTABLE AS $$
DECLARE ident text;
BEGIN
    IF raw IS NULL OR raw = '' THEN RETURN NULL; END IF;
    -- Strip exactly the optional prefix Doi::from_str accepts, case-insensitively.
    -- The 'i' flag scopes case-insensitivity to the prefix, exactly as the live (?i:) group does.
    ident := regexp_replace(raw, '^(https?://)?(www\.)?(dx\.)?doi\.org/', '', 'i');
    -- Accept exactly the live identifier class -- a CLOSED class, not "any non-whitespace".
    IF ident !~ '^10\.[0-9]{4,9}/[-._;()/:a-zA-Z0-9<>+\[\]]+$' THEN RETURN NULL; END IF;
    RETURN 'https://doi.org/' || lower(ident);
END $$;
```

Three properties, each mechanically established (§25.10; the 40-value corpus):

1. **Totality.** Every value `Doi::from_str` accepts canonicalises: 22 accepted inputs, **0 totality gaps**. This includes `www.`, which R52 did not strip and would have refused.
2. **No over-acceptance.** Every value live Thoth rejects is refused: **0 over-acceptances**. R52's `[^[:space:]]+` suffix admitted `10.12345/a%20b`, `10.12345/a#b` and non-ASCII identifiers, none of which live Thoth accepts.
3. **Exactness.** `crossref_canonical_doi(v) = lower(live_stored_form(v))` for all 22, so the canonical identity is precisely the lower-cased form of what the released parser would store.

**Lower-casing is deliberate, and compatible with released semantics.** The permit identity lower-cases the identifier even though the released `Doi` display form preserves its case. This is safe and, in fact, required for consistency, because `doi_uniq_idx` is `UNIQUE (lower(doi))` **on `work`** (`20250000_v1.0.0/up.sql:3974`): two live Works whose DOIs differ only by case cannot exist. Lower-casing can therefore never merge two distinct Works' DOIs into one permit identity — demonstrated by inserting the second Work and observing the released unique index reject it (§25.10). Conversely a case-sensitive permit identity would treat two spellings the database already considers the same DOI as different identities, which would let a second permit be issued for a DOI already held. Reference and institution DOIs never enter a deposit's membership (§14.3 reads only `work.doi`), so no other table's case semantics are implicated.

**Total order.** DOIs are sorted ascending by Unicode code point. `doi_set_digest` is the lower-case hex SHA-256 of the UTF-8 concatenation of the canonical DOIs, each followed by `\n`. `doi_set_cardinality` is the count.

**Membership immutability (R52A-2), by two independent rules:**

* a `BEFORE UPDATE OR DELETE` row trigger on `crossref_write_permit_doi` refuses every change (`CROSSREF_PERMIT_MEMBERSHIP_IMMUTABLE`) — from the instant the reservation commits, not merely once the permit leaves `RESERVED`;
* a **deferred** constraint trigger armed on **both** tables recomputes the digest and cardinality from membership at COMMIT and refuses disagreement (`CROSSREF_PERMIT_MEMBERSHIP_CARDINALITY_MISMATCH`). Because the digest and cardinality are themselves immutable, this closes the one hole the row trigger leaves: a post-hoc **INSERT** of an extra DOI row into an existing permit. Verified refused from `RESERVED`, `AUTHORIZED`, `ACCEPTED` and `VOIDED` (§25.16, T185).

The agreement trigger **must be `DEFERRABLE INITIALLY DEFERRED` and must never be forced immediate inside a reservation**: the reservation legitimately inserts the permit row before its membership rows, and an immediate check would reject its own valid intermediate state. This is a normative implementation constraint, not an implementation preference.

**Overlap detection** is an exact query over the membership index, and it must detect all six shapes: identical, subset, superset, one-member overlap, multi-member overlap, and disjoint (the only permitted case). The first five are refused with `CROSSREF_PERMIT_BLOCKED`; disjoint sets are admitted and do not serialise with each other.

**Digest ordering.** Every ordering that feeds `doi_set_digest` or a comparison with worker input uses `COLLATE "C"` (§14.3), so the digest is the SHA-256 of the canonical DOIs sorted by code point regardless of the database's default collation.

### 16.4 Reservation

`RESERVED` is created by exactly four route-specific mutations (§19.4). Each assigns its own `route` server-side; `route` is never an input. Membership is API-derived (§14.3); **no reservation accepts DOI values**.

**Common checks, every route:**
* the root Work exists (`CROSSREF_ROOT_WORK_NOT_FOUND`);
* its current publisher has an enabled `CROSSREF` assignment (`CROSSREF_PUBLISHER_NOT_COVERED`);
* the binding re-read under `W` equals the binding read before the lock (`CROSSREF_BINDING_MOVED_RETRY`, no mutation);
* membership is non-empty (`CROSSREF_PERMIT_EMPTY_DOI_SET`), and every stored DOI canonicalises (`CROSSREF_DOI_NOT_CANONICALISABLE`);
* **no blocking permit overlaps the membership** (`CROSSREF_PERMIT_BLOCKED`).

| Route | Mutation | Role | Credential presented | Prefix | Additional checks |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `WORK_UPSERT` | `reserveWorkUpsertCrossrefWrite` | `DISSEMINATION_WORKER` | job id + claim token | `P → W → G → J → A → Q` | the nine fence clauses of §11.1 hold **as a precondition**, clause 8 read under `Q` SHARE (§21.1), writing nothing; `permit_generation := attempt.claimed_generation`; `root_work_identity := job.work_identity`; **one permit per attempt, ever** (`crossref_write_permit_one_per_work_upsert_attempt_idx`) |
| `PUBLISHER_BACK_CATALOGUE` | `reserveBackCatalogueCrossrefWrite` | `DISSEMINATION_WORKER` | outer job id + claim token + unit root Work | `P → W → G → J → A` | outer job is `PUBLISHER_BACK_CATALOGUE` and `RUNNING`, attempt open; the unit's publisher equals the outer job's; the unit has **no `ACCEPTED` permit in this outer job** (`CROSSREF_UNIT_ALREADY_DEPOSITED_IN_JOB`) |
| `LEGACY_SCHEDULED` | `reserveLegacyScheduledCrossrefWrite` | `DISSEMINATION_WORKER` | root Work | `P → W → G` | none further; no job, attempt or generation exists |
| `MANUAL_RECOVERY` | `reserveManualRecoveryCrossrefWrite` | `SUPERUSER` | root Work + non-blank authorization reference | `P → W → G` | a blank reference is refused `CROSSREF_MANUAL_RECOVERY_REQUIRES_REFERENCE`; the reference is recorded as `operator_authorization_reference` |

Every reservation then completes LO-C (§16.5), the `WORK_UPSERT` route after taking `Q`: `K` ascending → `F FOR SHARE` → blocking check → history read → **one** clock read and allocation (§17.3) → `doi_batch_id := <root_work_identity>_<crossref_timestamp>` → `X` insert → `M` insert.

**The zero-row witness rule (R52B-5), common to all four routes.** Under the route's `W` lock:

```sql
INSERT INTO public.work_upsert_generation (work_id, execution_profile, source_generation)
VALUES ($root, 'CROSSREF', 0)
ON CONFLICT (work_id, execution_profile) DO NOTHING;

SELECT source_generation INTO witness
  FROM public.work_upsert_generation
 WHERE work_id = $root AND execution_profile = 'CROSSREF'
   FOR UPDATE;
```

It creates durable generation state when none exists, manufactures **no** source event, and records witness `0`. Its interactions, each executed (§25.17):

| Interaction | Outcome |
|---|---|
| concurrent capture of the **root** | capture's `W FOR NO KEY UPDATE` waits for the reservation's `W FOR SHARE`, or vice versa; the witness is the value committed before the reservation's read (T219) |
| concurrent capture of a **chapter**, propagating to the root without `W(root)` | whichever takes `G(root)` second waits; a capture committing after the reservation raises `G(root)` to `1`, and the later finalisation voids `SOURCE_CHANGED_DURING_PREPARATION` (T219, C15a) |
| bootstrap seed | finds the `0` row and raises it to `1` **exactly once** (T232) |
| materialization | lists the `0` row as residue (§9.2) and creates nothing unless the Work is eligible, admitted and capture is enabled (T232) |
| manual recovery with capture disabled | creates the `0` row and records witness `0`; the capture flag gates materialization only (T233) |
| legacy and back-catalogue routes | create the `0` row on an untouched Work; witness `0` (T231, T233) |
| `WORK_UPSERT` | the row already exists (the job's creation required it); read unchanged, never bumped (T233) |
| Work deletion | the `0` row cascades away with the Work; a surviving reservation keeps blocking its DOIs until voided (T220, C16a); deletion first ⇒ `CROSSREF_ROOT_WORK_NOT_FOUND`, no row created (C16b) |

**Reservation token — a per-permit bearer capability.** The database generates `reservation_token uuid` when the reservation inserts the permit, and rule U1 keeps it immutable. The reservation mutation returns it once, as `CrossrefWriteReservation.reservationToken`, and no other field of any type ever returns it: not `CrossrefWritePermit`, not `CrossrefFinalisationResult`, and no report or query (§19.6, §20). For that permit, and no other, it authenticates every later operation the permit's state still admits. It authenticates; it never authorizes: each of those operations also requires the caller's route-derived role, checked first, on every invocation (§19.4 rule 3):
* `finaliseCrossrefWrite` while the permit is `RESERVED`, and the replay of a finalisation that already authorised it or voided its own reservation (§16.6 C2);
* `reportCrossrefWrite` while it is `AUTHORIZED`, and a repeat after a lost response, which is then refused `CROSSREF_PERMIT_ILLEGAL_TRANSITION` because the first report landed (§28.2);
* the route-owner void while it is `RESERVED`, and a repeat after a lost response.

It is **not consumed by use**: the state machine, not the token, bounds what each call can do, so the token confers nothing once the permit is terminal. Reconciliation and the superuser void do not use it. It is the **only** credential available to `LEGACY_SCHEDULED`. It is stored in plaintext on the row, exactly as BE-04 stores `claim_token` (`20260814_v1.7.0/up.sql:45`). A worker **should** persist `(permitId, reservationToken)` durably before fetching the artifact, so that a restarted worker can void or report; a lost reservation response leaves no way to recover the token, and the superuser void (§16.7) is the fallback that must always exist.

### 16.5 LO-C — the Crossref suffix of the total lock order

Every Crossref path composes, after its route prefix, exactly this suffix of the TLO (§10.1):

```
… P → W → G [→ J → A]                            (route prefix)
  [→ Q  execution gate, SHARE        (WORK_UPSERT reservation and finalisation only; generic, not LO-C, §21.1)]
  → K   DOI advisory keys, deduplicated, ascending signed int4        (reservation only)
  → F   work_crossref_version_floor FOR SHARE                         (reservation only)
  → X   crossref_write_permit  (INSERT on reservation, FOR UPDATE otherwise)
  → M   crossref_write_permit_doi  (INSERT on reservation only)
```

| Path | Classes taken |
|---|---|
| reservation, four routes | route prefix → [`Q`, `WORK_UPSERT` only] → `K` → `F` → `X` → `M` |
| finalisation | route prefix → [`Q`, `WORK_UPSERT` only] → `X` |
| route-owner void, superuser void, outcome report | `X` |
| reconciliation | `A` (when the permit has an attempt) → `X` |
| G-7 advance | `F` (`FOR UPDATE`) |

**The advisory key, exactly.**

```sql
pg_advisory_xact_lock(1948572001, hashtext('be06:crossref:doi:' || <canonical doi>))
```

* The first argument is a **fixed literal namespace**.
* The two-argument `(int4, int4)` form occupies a **structurally distinct lock space** from the one-argument `bigint` form used by the released `work_relation` ordinal lock (`work_relation/crud.rs:132`). A BE-06 DOI lock can therefore never collide with that lock.
* `hashtext` returns a **signed 32-bit `integer`**. Derive the key for each canonical DOI, **deduplicate equal keys**, sort ascending under PostgreSQL's `integer` ordering, and acquire in that order.
* The execution gate `Q` uses the same two-argument space with a different fixed literal, `1948572002` (§10.1, §21.1). Two-argument keys with different first arguments are different locks whatever their second arguments, and `pg_locks` shows a one-argument key with `objsubid = 1` against `2` for a two-argument one, so the gate aliases neither a DOI key nor the `work_relation` lock. Executed: while one backend held the `CROSSREF` gate EXCLUSIVE, another took, at the same moment, the DOI namespace with the same second key, a one-argument key with the same two 32-bit halves, the one-argument `hashtext` form and another profile's gate, and was refused the gate itself in both modes (§25.20, T289).

**Sorting DOI strings is not sufficient.** With a real collision, `hashtext` of `https://doi.org/10.12345/138621` and of `https://doi.org/10.12345/258883` are both `-2098135504`. Add a third DOI that sorts between them by string. Two issuers holding `{X, Y}` and `{Y, Z}` then each sort their own set correctly and still acquire the two distinct keys in opposite orders. Executed: `40P01` under string sorting, and no deadlock under key sorting (§25.17, T218).

**Why the floor follows the keys, and the permit row follows the floor.** Taking `F` after `K` makes the floor row a sink for reservations: an issuer holding the floor share has finished acquiring. Measured earlier, the G-7 advance blocked 36 ms under this order against 2410 ms with the floor first. Placing `X` last and making the `X`-only paths leaves removes every possible `X → A` edge (§10.5).

### 16.6 Finalisation — the external-write-authorized point

```
finaliseCrossrefWrite(permitId, reservationToken, claimToken,
                      observedDois, observedDoiBatchId, observedCrossrefTimestamp, payloadDigest)
  → CrossrefFinalisationResult { outcome, voidReason, permit }     or a refusal error
```

`claimToken` is required exactly when the permit has a job, and must be `NULL` otherwise. The required role is **derived from the permit's persisted `route`** (§19.4). One transaction:

1. **MVCC read** of the permit's immutable metadata: route, `publisher_identity`, root, job and attempt identities. A principal holding neither `DISSEMINATION_WORKER` nor `SUPERUSER` is refused the released `Unauthorised` before this read (§19.4 rule 3). No such permit → `CROSSREF_PERMIT_NOT_FOUND`, with no lock taken.
2. **C0 — request authorization, on every invocation.** The caller must hold the role derived from the persisted `route`; otherwise the released `Unauthorised`. C0 is evaluated before any lock and before any credential, state or replay check, so a caller without the role takes no lock, changes nothing, and receives nothing about the permit's state, outcome, digest or void reason. **Authorization is never replayed:** a retry is authorised afresh, against the roles its own request carries.
3. **Locks**, in TLO order, starting from the permit's own publisher: `P FOR SHARE` on `publisher_identity` → `W FOR SHARE` → **the binding re-resolved under `W`** (`work → imprint → publisher`, in a statement after `W` is held) → `G FOR UPDATE` → [`J FOR UPDATE` → `A FOR UPDATE`, job-linked routes] → [`Q` SHARE, `WORK_UPSERT` only] → `X FOR UPDATE`. `Q` is held until the transaction ends, so it spans the read of fence clause 8 at A0 and the commit of `fenced_at`. `publisher_identity` is immutable (rule U1) and, on the job-linked routes, is the job's `publisher_id` (§16.4). No MVCC read of the Work's publisher chooses the lock, so the held publisher is always the publisher the permit is bound to.
4. **Group C — refusals and replays that change nothing.** No permit, job or attempt row changes.
   * **C1** the reservation token does not match → `CROSSREF_PERMIT_REQUIRES_RESERVATION_TOKEN`.
   * **C2 replay.** If the permit is `AUTHORIZED` with `payload_digest` equal to the one presented, return `AUTHORIZED` again, without re-evaluating groups B and A. If it is `VOIDED` by a finalisation, return the recorded outcome and reason. A replay returns the operation's recorded result, never a recorded authorization: it is reached only after C0 and C1 have passed in this invocation. A lost response is therefore safe to retry for a caller that still holds the role and the token.
   * **C3** any other state than `RESERVED` → `CROSSREF_PERMIT_ILLEGAL_TRANSITION`. This includes `AUTHORIZED` with a *different* digest, and a reservation voided by an owner or operator.
   * **C4** a job-linked route whose claim token is absent or stale, whose job is not `RUNNING`, whose attempt is closed, or whose attempt is not the permit's; or a job-less route that was sent a claim token → `CROSSREF_PERMIT_CLAIM_STALE`.
5. **Group B — binding, against the held publisher.** Evaluated **before** source and artifact, because an ownership move is also a captured event and would otherwise always surface as `SOURCE_CHANGED`.
   * **B1** root Work absent → `NO_WORK`.
   * **B2** the binding re-resolved under `W` differs from the held `publisher_identity` → `BINDING_SUPERSEDED`.
   * **B3** for `WORK_UPSERT`, a job target not enabled under exactly the job's activation; for the other routes, the publisher's `CROSSREF` assignment not enabled → `ASSIGNMENT_DISABLED`.

   **No assignment, activation, admission, control or eligibility row is read until B1 and B2 have passed.** B3 and A0 then read the held publisher's rows, which its coordinator cannot change before this transaction commits (§10.1). The publisher row whose service configuration authorizes the provider write is therefore the publisher row held from before `W` through the authorization commit. Executed: a BE-03 writer of that publisher waited for finalisation in every order; under the `4010dce1` candidate's ordering, which locked the publisher read before `W`, it did not (§25.19, T270–T273).

   For `WORK_UPSERT` the fence's binding refusal retires the job (§11.5), the finalisation voids its own reservation, and the outcome is **`VOIDED_JOB_RETIRED`**. For the other routes the reservation is voided and the outcome is `VOIDED_RETRYABLE`.
6. **Group A — retryable invalidation.** The finalisation voids its own reservation and returns **`VOIDED_RETRYABLE`**.
   * **A0** for `WORK_UPSERT`, fence clause 7, 8 or 9 fails → `PROFILE_NOT_ADMITTED`, `EXECUTION_NOT_PERMITTED` or `INELIGIBLE`. Clause 8 is read in a statement after `Q` was granted: a pause that committed first is seen, and a pause that has not committed is waiting for this transaction (§21.1).
   * **A1** the root's `source_generation`, read under the held `G`, differs from the witness → `SOURCE_CHANGED_DURING_PREPARATION`.
   * **A2** re-derived membership (§14.3) differs from the reserved membership → `DOI_MEMBERSHIP_CHANGED`.
   * **A3** canonicalised `observedDois` differs from the reserved membership → `ARTIFACT_DOI_SET_MISMATCH`.
   * **A4** `observedDoiBatchId` differs from `doi_batch_id` → `ARTIFACT_BATCH_ID_MISMATCH`.
   * **A5** `observedCrossrefTimestamp` differs from `crossref_timestamp` → `ARTIFACT_TIMESTAMP_MISMATCH`.
7. **Success.** For `WORK_UPSERT`, write `fenced_at` on the attempt. Bind `payload_digest`, set `authorized_at`, and move the permit to `AUTHORIZED`; trigger rule A re-proves the durable preconditions. The outcome is **`AUTHORIZED`**, and its commit is the moment a provider write becomes permissible.

Checks A1 and A2 are database-side and cannot be influenced by the worker. Checks A3–A5 compare worker-reported values against server-held truth. They are **cross-checks, never the authority**. The generation witness is the load-bearing check: a chapter attached and then detached during preparation leaves re-derived membership **equal** to the reservation, while the artifact still contains the extra DOI. Only A1 catches it.

**The discriminant, and exactly what the caller does next:**

| Outcome | Route | Caller action |
|---|---|---|
| `AUTHORIZED` | any | recheck the digest of the bytes about to be sent; POST exactly those bytes; `reportCrossrefWrite`; then, for job-linked routes, complete or fail the job |
| `VOIDED_RETRYABLE` | `WORK_UPSERT` | discard the bytes; `failDistributionJob(retryable = true)`; the job returns to `PENDING` under released retry semantics, and the **next claim projects the current `source_generation`**. The materializer creates no second actionable row, because the original remains actionable |
| `VOIDED_RETRYABLE` | `PUBLISHER_BACK_CATALOGUE` | discard the bytes; skip the unit in this attempt, and record it in the worker's attempt report; the outer attempt continues |
| `VOIDED_RETRYABLE` | `LEGACY_SCHEDULED` | discard the bytes; do nothing further; the legacy selector reconsiders the Work on its own schedule |
| `VOIDED_RETRYABLE` | `MANUAL_RECOVERY` | discard the bytes; the operator decides whether to reserve again |
| `VOIDED_JOB_RETIRED` | `WORK_UPSERT` only | discard the bytes; **no job call** — the job and attempt are already `CANCELLED` and the token is dead; `failDistributionJob` would be refused |
| refusal error (group C) | any | nothing was mutated. **Never reinterpret it as an ordinary retry.** A stale claim means the lifecycle has already resolved the attempt; the worker voids its reservation with the reservation token if it still holds one, and stops. `ILLEGAL_TRANSITION` after a lost response is resolved by retrying with the identical digest, which replays. `Unauthorised` (C0) means the credential lacks the route's role: nothing was locked or changed and nothing about the permit was returned, and no retry succeeds until the role is restored |

**The three groups, executed as complete transcripts** (§25.17):

* **A (T224–T225).** reserve → capture on the root → finalise → `VOIDED_RETRYABLE / SOURCE_CHANGED_DURING_PREPARATION`, with the job still `RUNNING` and the attempt open and unfenced → `failDistributionJob(retryable = true)` → `PENDING` → the next claim's `claimed_generation` is `2`, the current generation. Also with execution paused at finalisation → `VOIDED_RETRYABLE / EXECUTION_NOT_PERMITTED`.
* **B (T226).** reserve → ownership moved and captured → finalise → `VOIDED_JOB_RETIRED / BINDING_SUPERSEDED`, binding winning over the concurrent source change → job `CANCELLED / BINDING_SUPERSEDED`, attempt `CANCELLED` → a worker `failDistributionJob` is refused.
* **C (T227–T228).** wrong reservation token → refused, permit row fingerprint unchanged, job untouched. Claim went stale through cancellation → `CROSSREF_PERMIT_CLAIM_STALE`, fingerprint unchanged, permit still `RESERVED`.
* **Replay (T229).** An identical retry of an `AUTHORIZED` finalisation returns `AUTHORIZED`; a retry with a different digest is refused; a retry of a voided finalisation returns its recorded outcome.
* **Request authorization (T277, specification-harness evidence).** On every route, a caller without the route-derived role, presenting the correct token, was refused `Unauthorised` on the first call, on an identical-digest replay of an `AUTHORIZED` permit and on a replay of a finalisation-voided one, and after the role was removed from the principal that had authorised; the permit, job and attempt were unchanged. The role and the token together received the recorded result. Under the `4010dce1` candidate's order, C2 before C5, the same wrong-principal calls received the replay and void results (§25.19).
* **One permit per attempt (T230).** A second reservation on the same `WORK_UPSERT` attempt is refused `23505`.
* **The pause against finalisation (T280).** Order A: finalisation held `Q` SHARE and the pause waited for `Q` EXCLUSIVE; finalisation read `true`, authorised and committed, and the pause was granted `Q` only after finalisation's last in-transaction event, so the authorization was historical when the pause returned. Order B: the pause held `Q`; finalisation waited at `Q` holding its `P`, `W`, `G`, `J` and `A`, then read `false` and returned `VOIDED_RETRYABLE / EXECUTION_NOT_PERMITTED`, unfenced. Negative control, the `37e44ce3` candidate's ungated finalisation in the review's schedule: it read `true`, the pause committed and returned without waiting, and it then committed `AUTHORIZED` and fenced.

**Every race, and its outcome.** Executed where marked (§25.17, T205–T223):

| Race | Outcome |
|---|---|
| source edit before the reservation | included in the derived membership and in the witness; ordinary |
| source edit between reservation and artifact fetch, during generation, or after the fetch | the witness differs at A1 → `VOIDED_RETRYABLE`; nothing authorised |
| DOI set grew, or changed and changed back | the witness catches both; membership equality alone would miss the second |
| artifact changed while the DOI set did not | `VOIDED_RETRYABLE / SOURCE_CHANGED_DURING_PREPARATION` |
| timestamp or history changed by a concurrent overlapping issuer | impossible: that issuer is refused `CROSSREF_PERMIT_BLOCKED` while this reservation blocks (C13, C19) |
| binding changed | group B (T226). Finalisation holds the permit's `publisher_identity` from before `W`: a Work that moves back into that publisher before `W` is re-resolved as its own, with the publisher's configuration writers waiting; a Work that ends elsewhere is refused `BINDING_SUPERSEDED` before any configuration is read (T270–T272) |
| a configuration change of the permit's publisher (the BE-03 coordinator) | serialised at `P`: finalisation first, the coordinator waits and commits after the authorization; the coordinator first, finalisation waits and then refuses on B3 (T273) |
| `setWorkUpsertExecution(false)`, concurrent | serialised at `Q`: finalisation first — the pause waits, and commits after the authorization, which is therefore historical when it returns; the pause first — finalisation waits at `Q`, then reads `false` and returns `VOIDED_RETRYABLE / EXECUTION_NOT_PERMITTED` (T280) |
| concurrent route-owner or superuser void | serialised on `X`: exactly one wins; the loser is refused (C01, C02) |
| concurrent administrative cancellation, Work or Publisher deletion, lease recovery | serialised at `J` or `P`; if the generic path wins, finalisation refuses `CLAIM_STALE` and the **permit is untouched** (C03b, C04b, C05b, C17b); if finalisation wins, the fenced attempt makes cancellation and deletion refuse (C03a, C04a, C17a) and recovery skips the locked job (C05a) |
| concurrent outcome report or reconciliation | serialised on `X` (and `A`): the report or reconciliation then applies to the authorised permit, or is refused while it is still `RESERVED` (C06, C07) |
| two concurrent finalisations of one permit, with different digests | serialised on `X`: the first authorises, and the second is refused `CROSSREF_PERMIT_ILLEGAL_TRANSITION` (C12). With the identical digest the second replays |
| worker crash before finalisation | permit stays `RESERVED`: authorised nothing, blocks its DOIs, released by the route-owner or superuser void |
| worker crash after finalisation, before POST | permit `AUTHORIZED`: genuinely ambiguous; blocking; reconciliation resolves it and clears the attempt |
| lost finalisation response | an identical retry, authorised afresh, replays the recorded result |

### 16.7 Outcome reporting, voiding and closure

Three distinct acts replace R52's single word "close". Each has one actor and one transition set.

* **Outcome report.** `reportCrossrefWrite(permitId, reservationToken, outcome)` moves `AUTHORIZED → ACCEPTED | INDETERMINATE | NONE_ATTEMPTED`.
  * The required role is derived from `route`, and is checked first, on every invocation, before the permit row is locked and before the token or the state (§19.4 rule 3). The **reservation token** authenticates the call; **no claim token is required**, so a worker whose lease lapsed can still record provider truth.
  * Every report writes `provider_reported_at` from the server clock: the API's receipt of the report, never a time the caller supplies (§17.1). `ACCEPTED` and `NONE_ATTEMPTED` also set `closed_at`. A repeat after a lost response is refused `CROSSREF_PERMIT_ILLEGAL_TRANSITION`, which tells a caller holding the role and the token that the first report landed; a caller without the role is refused `Unauthorised` first, and learns nothing.
  * The report takes `X` only.
* **Reconciliation.** A superuser records an outcome as still unresolved, resolves it, or confirms a terminal truth (§16.8).
* **Closure.** Setting `closed_at` on entry to `ACCEPTED`, `NONE_ATTEMPTED` or `VOIDED`, whoever caused it. Closure is a consequence, not an operation.

**Voiding — exactly three authorities, all Crossref-owned:**

| Void | Operation | Role | Credential | Records |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **route owner** | `voidCrossrefWriteReservation(permitId, reservationToken, detail)` | derived from `route` | reservation token; **no claim token**, so it remains usable after lease expiry, cancellation or deletion | `void_reason = OWNER_ABANDONED`, `void_detail` |
| **superuser cleanup** | `voidCrossrefWriteReservationAsSuperuser(permitId, detail, authorizationReference)` | `SUPERUSER` | a **non-blank authorization reference**, persisted write-once in `void_authorization_reference`; no reservation token needed | `void_reason = OPERATOR_CLEANUP`, `void_detail`, `void_authorization_reference` |
| **finalisation** | inside `finaliseCrossrefWrite`, groups A and B | as §16.6 | as §16.6 | one of the eleven finalisation reasons |

Both explicit voids require a non-blank `detail` (`CROSSREF_PERMIT_VOID_REQUIRES_DETAIL`). The superuser void also requires the reference (`CROSSREF_VOID_REQUIRES_AUTHORIZATION_REFERENCE`). Both check their role first, on every invocation, before the permit row is locked and before the token or the state: the route owner's role derived from `route`, the superuser's `SUPERUSER` (§19.4 rule 3). A repeated route-owner void is refused `CROSSREF_PERMIT_VOID_REQUIRES_RESERVED` only for a caller holding the role and the token (§25.19, T278).

Neither explicit void acts on `AUTHORIZED`, `INDETERMINATE` or any terminal state (`CROSSREF_PERMIT_VOID_REQUIRES_RESERVED`). A superuser cleanup is **not reconciliation**: no write was ever authorised, and it writes no `reconciliation_state`. Every void takes `X` only.

**INV-GENERIC-PERMIT, enforced and executed.** No generic substrate path writes a permit row. For each of six generic paths, the permit row's fingerprint was identical before and after, and an overlapping reservation was still refused `CROSSREF_PERMIT_BLOCKED` (§25.17, T197–T202). The six paths are: administrative cancellation, `WORK_DELETED` retirement, lease recovery, binding supersession by the materializer, assignment disablement, and `failDistributionJob` (which is refused while the reservation stands). The one permitted exception is a finalisation voiding its own reservation.

**The abandoned reservation, end to end** (§25.17, T234–T236):

1. A worker reserves and crashes.
2. The lease expires; recovery leaves the attempt `ABANDONED` and **unfenced**.
3. The job is cancelled; the reservation remains `RESERVED` and blocking.
4. The route owner voids it with the reservation token, and no claim token → `VOIDED`, and a new overlapping reservation succeeds.

In a second run, a superuser voids it without the token, carrying the reference `INC-42`, which is persisted. A superuser void without a reference is refused. Neither void can touch an `AUTHORIZED` permit.

### 16.8 Reconciliation, and the internal recovery clearance
