`reconcileCrossrefWritePermit(permitId, outcome, reconciliationState, authorizationReference)` is `SUPERUSER`-only and requires a non-blank reference (`CROSSREF_RECONCILIATION_REQUIRES_REFERENCE`). **Each reconciliation act records its own authority, and no act overwrites another's.** A permit carries at most two reconciliation acts, each written once (rule U3):
* the **annotation act** records that the outcome is still unresolved — `RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED`, it must be established, or `RECONCILIATION_IMPOSSIBLE`, the evidence available when it was recorded cannot establish it — and writes `reconciliation_annotation_reference` and `reconciliation_annotated_at`;
* the **reconciled act**, a resolution or a confirmation, writes `reconciliation_authorization_reference` and `reconciled_at`.

| From | Presented | Result |
|---|---|---|
| `AUTHORIZED` | `ACCEPTED` or `NONE_ATTEMPTED` | moves there; `RECONCILED`; the reconciled act's reference and time; closed |
| `AUTHORIZED` | `INDETERMINATE` with `RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED` or `RECONCILIATION_IMPOSSIBLE` | moves to `INDETERMINATE` with that annotation and the annotation act's reference and time; still blocking |
| `INDETERMINATE`, not annotated | `INDETERMINATE` with an annotation | the annotation act (rule S2); still blocking |
| `INDETERMINATE`, annotated | `INDETERMINATE` with any annotation | refused `CROSSREF_PERMIT_ILLEGAL_TRANSITION`: an assessment is recorded once, and a later one is recorded by the resolution |
| `INDETERMINATE`, annotated or not | `ACCEPTED` or `NONE_ATTEMPTED` | moves there; `RECONCILED`; the reconciled act's reference and time; closed. An annotation — `RECONCILIATION_IMPOSSIBLE` included — is superseded, not erased: its reference and time stay on the row |
| `ACCEPTED` / `NONE_ATTEMPTED` | the same state | **confirmation**: if `reconciliation_state` is `NULL`, it becomes `RECONCILED` with the reconciled act's reference and time (rule S3); otherwise the permit is left unchanged. Clearance is evaluated in either case |
| `RESERVED` / `VOIDED` | anything | refused `CROSSREF_PERMIT_ILLEGAL_TRANSITION`: a reservation is released by a void, never reconciled |

A provider report arriving after the job terminalized is applied to the **permit**, never to the job. Reconciliation locks `A` (when the permit has an attempt), then `X`. After its permit write it evaluates the clearance conditions of §11.7, and when both hold it calls `work_upsert_clear_fenced_abandonment` on the `A` it already holds. **There is no public mutation that clears a fenced abandonment.** Executed in both orders against finalisation, completion and lease recovery (§25.17, T211, T213, T214).

**An unresolved permit, and everything that depends on it.** An `INDETERMINATE` permit — unannotated, `RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED` or `RECONCILIATION_IMPOSSIBLE` — keeps every consequence of blocking until a resolution:
* **Blocking predicate:** it blocks (§16.9), so no reservation for an overlapping DOI set can succeed.
* **Fenced-abandonment clearance:** evaluated after every reconciliation write, and performed only when no permit on the attempt blocks — after the resolution, never after an annotation (§11.7).
* **Later claims:** the profile's claim guard, and for a fenced abandonment the generic clause, keep the job unclaimable until then.
* **Deletion:** unaffected. Work, Imprint and Publisher deletion turn on open fenced attempts (§13.3), not on permit states, and the permit survives every deletion with both acts' references (rule S1, §17.8).
* **Reporting:** `crossrefUnresolvedPermits` lists it with its annotation and both acts' references and times (§20).
* **DRAIN and G-7:** `crossref_is_drained()` is false while it blocks, so the floor cannot advance. Resolving it is the only way forward, and that is intended.
* **Audit:** the annotation act and the reconciled act are each written once, and survive every later act and every deletion.

Executed end to end (§25.18, T267, T268). An `AUTHORIZED` permit on a fenced, abandoned attempt, annotated `RECONCILIATION_IMPOSSIBLE`, still blocked, left the attempt uncleared and the job unclaimable, kept DRAIN false and had G-7 refused. A later resolution to `NONE_ATTEMPTED` under a second reference cleared the attempt in the same transaction, made DRAIN true and let G-7 advance; both references stayed on the row and survived Publisher deletion. A second annotation, and every overwrite of either act's reference or time, was refused.

### 16.9 The canonical blocking predicate, count and DRAIN

```sql
CREATE FUNCTION public.crossref_is_blocking_write_permit(
    state public.crossref_write_permit_state,
    reconciliation_state public.crossref_reconciliation_state)
RETURNS boolean LANGUAGE sql IMMUTABLE CALLED ON NULL INPUT AS $$
    SELECT state IN ('RESERVED','AUTHORIZED','INDETERMINATE')
       AND (reconciliation_state IS DISTINCT FROM 'RECONCILED')
$$;
```

`CALLED ON NULL INPUT` is load-bearing: a `STRICT` variant returns NULL for `AUTHORIZED + NULL` — a permit that authorised a write and was never reported — which is exactly the case that must block. `IS DISTINCT FROM` keeps NULL reconciliation blocking.

Verified over all nine state/reconciliation combinations (§25.10): `RESERVED`, `AUTHORIZED`, `INDETERMINATE` (NULL, `RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED`, `RECONCILIATION_IMPOSSIBLE`) block; `INDETERMINATE + RECONCILED`, `ACCEPTED`, `NONE_ATTEMPTED` and `VOIDED` do not. `INDETERMINATE + RECONCILED` is a value of the function only: `crossref_write_permit_reconciliation_state_check` makes it unrepresentable in the table (§18.6).

`crossref_blocking_write_permit_count()` and `crossref_is_drained()` are **argument-free**: no publisher, route, kind or DOI filter. A parameterised variant is trivially falsifiable by omitting the value that matters and is prohibited. GraphQL exposes both with no arguments (§19.5).

### 16.10 What is mechanical and what is contractual

**Mechanical** — enforced by the database against every statement in the API's single role:
* membership derivation and immutability;
* digest and cardinality agreement at COMMIT;
* the insertion guard;
* the transition relation and the same-state rules;
* write-once and immutable evidence;
* deletion and truncation refusal;
* timestamp allocation, calendar ordering and the floor bound;
* the witness, fence, blocking and history preconditions re-proved at authorization;
* the blocking predicate;
* one permit per `WORK_UPSERT` attempt;
* the prepared route's timestamp validation.

**Contractual** — the worker's obligation, verified by the cross-repository conformance evidence of §28.7 and not by the database:
* fetch only from the prepared route, with exactly the reservation's timestamp;
* extract registration membership exactly as §16.11 specifies;
* POST exactly the bytes whose digest it bound;
* recheck that digest immediately before the POST;
* report truthfully;
* never use the released route for a provider write.

A holder of `DISSEMINATION_WORKER` credentials could POST arbitrary bytes to Crossref without involving the API at all, so no API-side mechanism can make the contractual half mechanical. The mechanical half guarantees two things:
* **an authorization exists only for a byte sequence** whose registration membership, timestamp and batch id the API derived and holds immutably;
* any divergence a conforming worker can observe causes a void rather than an authorization.

### 16.11 The registration-membership extraction contract (R52B-6)

The worker derives `observedDois` from the exact artifact bytes by this rule, and by nothing else.

1. **Refuse the artifact** if the bytes contain `<!DOCTYPE` or `<!ENTITY`, if the document element is not `{http://www.crossref.org/schema/5.4.0}doi_batch`, or if the document does not contain exactly one `body`, exactly one `body/book`, and exactly one of `body/book/book_metadata` or `body/book/book_series_metadata`.
2. With `ns = http://www.crossref.org/schema/5.4.0`, the registration DOIs are **exactly** the texts of the elements at these three paths, each step a **direct child**:

   ```
   /{ns}doi_batch/{ns}body/{ns}book/{ns}book_metadata/{ns}doi_data/{ns}doi
   /{ns}doi_batch/{ns}body/{ns}book/{ns}book_series_metadata/{ns}doi_data/{ns}doi
   /{ns}doi_batch/{ns}body/{ns}book/{ns}content_item/{ns}doi_data/{ns}doi
   ```

3. Each text is taken **exactly as written**, with no trimming, and canonicalised by the §16.3 function. A value that does not canonicalise refuses the artifact.
4. `observedDois` is the set of distinct canonical values, sorted by code point. An empty set refuses the artifact.
5. **Everything else is ignored**, however DOI-shaped: `crossmark/crossmark_policy`, `crossmark/updates/update`, `fr:assertion[@name="funder_identifier"]` (which carries a full `https://doi.org/…` URL), `citation_list/citation/doi`, `institution_id`, `doi_data/collection/item/resource`, and any DOI text inside an abstract or title.

A refused artifact is never finalised. The worker voids its reservation with the reservation token and, on the `WORK_UPSERT` route, fails the attempt retryably.

**Reference implementation**, normative in behaviour, which the dissemination task may port but must not vary:

```python
import re, xml.etree.ElementTree as ET
NS = 'http://www.crossref.org/schema/5.4.0'
Q = lambda t: '{%s}%s' % (NS, t)
PATHS = ('body/book/book_metadata/doi_data/doi',
         'body/book/book_series_metadata/doi_data/doi',
         'body/book/content_item/doi_data/doi')
PREFIX = re.compile(r'^(https?://)?(www\.)?(dx\.)?doi\.org/', re.IGNORECASE)
IDENT = re.compile(r'^10\.[0-9]{4,9}/[-._;()/:a-zA-Z0-9<>+\[\]]+$')

def canonical(raw):
    if raw is None or raw == '': return None
    ident = PREFIX.sub('', raw, count=1)
    return 'https://doi.org/' + ident.lower() if IDENT.match(ident) else None

def observed_dois(xml_bytes):
    if b'<!DOCTYPE' in xml_bytes or b'<!ENTITY' in xml_bytes: raise ValueError('refused: DTD')
    root = ET.fromstring(xml_bytes)
    if root.tag != Q('doi_batch'): raise ValueError('refused: root/namespace')
    q = lambda p: '/'.join(Q(s) for s in p.split('/'))
    metas = root.findall(q('body/book/book_metadata')) + root.findall(q('body/book/book_series_metadata'))
    if len(root.findall(Q('body'))) != 1 or len(root.findall(q('body/book'))) != 1 or len(metas) != 1:
        raise ValueError('refused: container count')
    out = set()
    for p in PATHS:
        for el in root.findall(q(p)):
            c = canonical(el.text)
            if c is None: raise ValueError('refused: registration doi not canonicalisable')
            out.add(c)
    if not out: raise ValueError('refused: no registration doi')
    return sorted(out)
```

**Executed** (§25.17):

* **T242, synthetic fixtures.** Six fixtures that reproduce the serializer's structure with every excluded class present at once — `book_metadata`, `book_series_metadata`, root without a landing page, root without a DOI, a withdrawal whose crossmark update carries the root's own DOI, and mixed case — all returned exactly the registration set. Collecting every DOI-shaped value instead over-collected 3–4 foreign DOIs in each. The three malformed artifacts (DTD, whitespace inside a registration `doi`, wrong schema namespace) were refused.
* **T243, real serializer output.** Ten documents: eight generated by the released serializer from its own test fixtures, plus a released/prepared pair for one complete Work. Nine returned exactly their one registration DOI. On the five released documents that carry references, funders or crossmark, naive collection over-collected 2–5 foreign DOIs. The tenth registers nothing, and was refused (§3.8 item 3).

**A prepared-manifest response header was considered and rejected.** The export server would derive it from the same database state the API already holds, so it would duplicate unverified state rather than prove anything about the bytes. Extraction from the exact bytes is the proof.

---

## 17. Deposit timestamp and the version floor

### 17.1 The deposit version, and the permit's times

| Column | Meaning | Written by | Source | Rule |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `crossref_timestamp bigint` | the **exact 17-digit value in the authorised artifact's `<timestamp>` element**: a version number, not an observation of when anything happened | the reservation's allocation (§17.3) | the server clock through `crossref_ts_now()`; never a caller value | immutable (U1) |
| `issued_at timestamptz` | when the reservation inserted the permit | the reservation | server `clock_timestamp()` | immutable (U1) |
| `authorized_at timestamptz` | when finalisation authorised the write. It is read before that transaction commits, so every POST of the bytes follows it | `finaliseCrossrefWrite`, `RESERVED → AUTHORIZED` | server `clock_timestamp()` | write-once (U3) |
| `provider_reported_at timestamptz` | when the API received and applied the holder's outcome report. It records the report, not the POST: the worker sends its request before it reports, so every request byte was sent after `authorized_at` and before `provider_reported_at` | `reportCrossrefWrite`, `AUTHORIZED → ACCEPTED | INDETERMINATE | NONE_ATTEMPTED`, and nothing else | server `clock_timestamp()`; the report carries no time | write-once (U3). `NULL` when the permit left `AUTHORIZED` by reconciliation; reconciliation never writes or changes it |
| `reconciliation_annotated_at timestamptz` | when a superuser recorded the outcome as still unresolved | the annotation act (§16.8) | server `clock_timestamp()` | write-once (U3) |
| `reconciled_at timestamptz` | when a superuser recorded reconciliation truth: a resolution or a confirmation | the reconciled act (§16.8) | server `clock_timestamp()` | write-once (U3) |
| `closed_at timestamptz` | entry to `ACCEPTED`, `NONE_ATTEMPTED` or `VOIDED` | whichever act caused it: a report, a resolution or a void | server `clock_timestamp()` | write-once (U3) |

**No column records when bytes were physically submitted.** The API never observes that instant, and no call carries it. Crossref's own submission records are keyed by `doi_batch_id`, which the permit carries, and reconciliation works from those (§16.8, §28.2). None of these times orders anything; only `crossref_timestamp` orders deposits. Executed: the report writes `provider_reported_at` once and nothing else can; a reconciliation from `AUTHORIZED` leaves it `NULL`; resolving a reported `INDETERMINATE` permit leaves it unchanged (§25.18, T266).

`crossref_timestamp` carries `CHECK (crossref_timestamp BETWEEN 10000000000000000 AND 99999999999999999)`, which is exactly "17 digits". Format: `YYYYMMDDHH24MISSMS`, UTC, milliseconds zero-padded to three digits. In `chrono` that is `%Y%m%d%H%M%S%3f`, where `%3f` renders exactly three fractional digits with no separator (`chrono` 0.4.45). The API allocates it; the serializer only echoes it (§15.3).

### 17.2 Valid calendar arithmetic (R52A-4)

**A 17-digit deposit timestamp is a positional encoding of a UTC instant, not an integer to be incremented.** `20260904120059999 + 1 = 20260904120060000` is 17 digits and is not the encoding of any instant. Decimal `+1` is therefore withdrawn everywhere it appeared.

Three functions define the arithmetic:

```sql
-- exact rendering of an instant, and the one domain guard: no instant at or after
-- 10000-01-01T00:00:00.000Z has a 17-digit encoding, because PostgreSQL's YYYY renders a fifth digit
CREATE FUNCTION public.crossref_ts_encode(t timestamptz) RETURNS bigint
LANGUAGE plpgsql IMMUTABLE AS $$
BEGIN
    IF t >= TIMESTAMPTZ '10000-01-01 00:00:00+00' THEN
        RAISE EXCEPTION 'CROSSREF_TIMESTAMP_OVERFLOW: %', t;
    END IF;
    RETURN to_char(t AT TIME ZONE 'UTC', 'YYYYMMDDHH24MISSMS')::bigint;
END $$;

-- STRICT decode: a value is valid only if it is the exact rendering of the instant it decodes to.
-- make_timestamptz silently NORMALISES second 60 and hour 24, so leniency would admit values
-- that encode no instant.  Round-trip identity is the validity test.  The round trip runs inside
-- the handler, so a value that normalises past the domain (second 60 or hour 24 on 9999-12-31)
-- makes crossref_ts_encode raise and is refused like every other non-encoding.
CREATE FUNCTION public.crossref_ts_decode(v bigint) RETURNS timestamptz
LANGUAGE plpgsql IMMUTABLE AS $$
DECLARE s text; t timestamptz; BEGIN
    IF v IS NULL OR v < 10000000000000000 OR v > 99999999999999999 THEN RETURN NULL; END IF;
    s := v::text;
    BEGIN
        t := make_timestamptz(substr(s,1,4)::int, substr(s,5,2)::int, substr(s,7,2)::int,
                              substr(s,9,2)::int, substr(s,11,2)::int,
                              substr(s,13,2)::numeric + substr(s,15,3)::numeric/1000, 'UTC');
        IF public.crossref_ts_encode(t) <> v THEN RETURN NULL; END IF;
    EXCEPTION WHEN others THEN RETURN NULL; END;
    RETURN t;
END $$;

-- the calendar successor: exactly one millisecond later.  99991231235959999 has none:
-- crossref_ts_encode raises CROSSREF_TIMESTAMP_OVERFLOW.
CREATE FUNCTION public.crossref_ts_next(v bigint) RETURNS bigint
LANGUAGE plpgsql IMMUTABLE AS $$
DECLARE t timestamptz; BEGIN
    t := public.crossref_ts_decode(v);
    IF t IS NULL THEN RAISE EXCEPTION 'CROSSREF_TIMESTAMP_NOT_DECODABLE: %', v; END IF;
    RETURN public.crossref_ts_encode(t + interval '1 millisecond');
END $$;
```

The strict round-trip rule is normative and is the only correct validity test: `make_timestamptz` accepts second 60 and hour 24 and rolls them forward silently, so a decoder that merely "does not raise" admits non-encodings. Verified refused: second 60, hour 24, month 13, day 32, 29 February in a non-leap year, and the 14-digit floor sentinel (§25.16, T195).

**The domain guard (R52B-23).** PostgreSQL's `timestamp` reaches far past year 9999, and `to_char`'s `YYYY` renders a fifth digit there, so without a guard the successor of the largest 17-digit value is the 18-digit `100000101000000000`. Executed against the `67683f28` candidate's displayed functions, `crossref_ts_next(99991231235959999)` returned exactly that and raised nothing, and allocation returned it too; only the permit column's CHECK would have refused it, with a constraint violation rather than a stable code (§25.18, T259). The guard therefore sits in `crossref_ts_encode`, the one function that renders every 17-digit value. The successor (`crossref_ts_next`) and the clock (`crossref_ts_now`) both reach the domain only through it, so neither can produce a value past `99991231235959999`, and both fail with `CROSSREF_TIMESTAMP_OVERFLOW`; a second guard in `crossref_ts_next` would be unreachable. `crossref_ts_decode` runs its round trip inside its handler, so an input that normalises past the domain — second 60 or hour 24 on 9999-12-31 — is refused like every other non-encoding and never raises. Below the domain nothing moves: a successor is always later, and a clock value before year 1000 fails the allocation rule's decode check.

**The prepared-route validator, mechanically equivalent to `crossref_ts_decode` (R52B-7).** A string is a valid deposit timestamp iff **all** of the following hold:
1. it is exactly 17 ASCII decimal digits, and the first is not `0`;
2. its explicit components satisfy `NaiveDate::from_ymd_opt(y, m, d)` and `and_hms_milli_opt(h, mi, s, ms)`;
3. `s ≤ 59` and `ms ≤ 999`;
4. the resulting value re-renders to the identical string under `%Y%m%d%H%M%S%3f`.

```rust
pub(crate) fn is_valid_crossref_timestamp(s: &str) -> bool {
    use chrono::{NaiveDate, Timelike};
    let b = s.as_bytes();
    if b.len() != 17 || !b.iter().all(|c| c.is_ascii_digit()) || b[0] == b'0' {
        return false;
    }
    let n = |r: std::ops::Range<usize>| s[r].parse::<u32>().unwrap_or(u32::MAX);
    let (y, mo, d, h, mi, se, ms) =
        (n(0..4) as i32, n(4..6), n(6..8), n(8..10), n(10..12), n(12..14), n(14..17));
    if se > 59 || ms > 999 {
        return false; // and_hms_milli_opt would otherwise admit a leap second
    }
    match NaiveDate::from_ymd_opt(y, mo, d).and_then(|dt| dt.and_hms_milli_opt(h, mi, se, ms)) {
        Some(t) => t.nanosecond() < 1_000_000_000 && t.format("%Y%m%d%H%M%S%3f").to_string() == s,
        None => false,
    }
}
```

**The obvious implementation is wrong, and the rule above exists because of it.** `NaiveDateTime::parse_from_str(s, "%Y%m%d%H%M%S%3f")` followed by a round-trip comparison accepts second `60` as a chrono leap second, and accepts leading-zero years. Neither is an encoding PostgreSQL accepts.

**Parity, executed** with `chrono` 0.4.45 — the version `thoth` pins — over a corpus of **360,039** strings (§25.17, T248). The corpus combined:
* 39 targeted vectors;
* 100,000 uniformly random 17-digit strings;
* 100,000 random valid instants across 1000–9999, and their calendar successors;
* 60,000 surgical corruptions (second `60`, hour `24`, day `32`).

The PostgreSQL verdict was `CASE WHEN s ~ '^[1-9][0-9]{16}$' THEN crossref_ts_decode(s::bigint) IS NOT NULL ELSE false END`. The rule above agreed with it on **all 360,039**. The naive `parse_from_str` round-trip disagreed on **20,005**: 20,003 second-`60` leap-second acceptances and 2 leading-zero years. Re-run against the corrected `crossref_ts_decode`, the PostgreSQL verdict equalled the recorded one on every string, so the parity carries over unchanged (§25.18, T261).

### 17.3 The allocation rule, and the one clock primitive (R52B-8)

```sql
-- the only clock: UTC, millisecond, re-evaluated on every call
CREATE FUNCTION public.crossref_ts_now() RETURNS bigint LANGUAGE sql VOLATILE AS $$
    SELECT public.crossref_ts_encode(clock_timestamp())
$$;

-- the pure rule, testable with any clock value
CREATE FUNCTION public.crossref_allocate_timestamp(history bigint, floor_value bigint, now_17 bigint)
RETURNS bigint LANGUAGE plpgsql IMMUTABLE AS $$
DECLARE cand bigint;
BEGIN
    IF floor_value NOT IN (0, 99999999999999) THEN RAISE EXCEPTION 'CROSSREF_VERSION_FLOOR_DOMAIN'; END IF;
    IF public.crossref_ts_decode(now_17) IS NULL THEN RAISE EXCEPTION 'CROSSREF_TIMESTAMP_NOT_DECODABLE'; END IF;
    IF history IS NOT NULL AND public.crossref_ts_decode(history) IS NULL THEN
        RAISE EXCEPTION 'CROSSREF_TIMESTAMP_NOT_DECODABLE'; END IF;
    cand := now_17;
    IF history IS NOT NULL AND cand <= history THEN
        cand := public.crossref_ts_next(history);          -- calendar successor; CROSSREF_TIMESTAMP_OVERFLOW at 99991231235959999
    END IF;
    IF cand <= floor_value OR (history IS NOT NULL AND cand <= history) THEN
        RAISE EXCEPTION 'CROSSREF_TIMESTAMP_NOT_INCREASING'; END IF;
    RETURN cand;
END $$;
```

**Normative requirements, each executed** (§25.17, T249–T253; re-run against the corrected functions in §25.18, T259–T261):

1. **UTC.** `crossref_ts_encode` renders `t AT TIME ZONE 'UTC'`, so the value does not depend on the session's `TimeZone`. Executed under `Pacific/Chatham` (UTC+12:45): the value was UTC, and the local rendering differed by 12 h 45 min.
2. **Millisecond truncation.** `MS` truncates sub-millisecond digits and never rounds into the next second. `12:00:00.999999` encodes as `…120000999`, and `2026-12-31 23:59:59.9999` as `20261231235959999`, not the next year.
3. **`VOLATILE`, and `clock_timestamp()` — never `CURRENT_TIMESTAMP`.** `CURRENT_TIMESTAMP` is frozen at transaction start, so it would predate the lock waits on `K` and `F`. Executed: after a 350 ms wait inside one transaction, `CURRENT_TIMESTAMP` was unchanged while `crossref_ts_now()` had advanced by at least 350 ms. Every other timestamp primitive is `IMMUTABLE`.
4. **Evaluated exactly once per reservation**, after `K` and `F` are held and after the blocking and history reads, by passing its value to the pure rule. The reservation function contains exactly one call site, positioned after the floor share and the advisory loop (static containment, T253).
5. **History** is the maximum `crossref_timestamp` over permits whose membership intersects the set and whose `state <> 'VOIDED'`. A `VOIDED` reservation provably authorised nothing. Every other state — including `NONE_ATTEMPTED`, however it was reached — constrains the next value. Executed: after a `NONE_ATTEMPTED` permit at `…120000999`, a reservation made with the same frozen clock received `…120001000`; after a `VOIDED` one it received the reused `…120000999` (T254).
6. **The successor is calendar-valid, and bounded** (§17.2). `crossref_ts_next(99991231235959998)` is `99991231235959999`. The maximum instant has no successor: `crossref_ts_next(99991231235959999)` raises `CROSSREF_TIMESTAMP_OVERFLOW` from `crossref_ts_encode`'s guard, and so does allocation with that history, before any write (T251, re-run as T259 against this text).
7. **Numeric order is chronological order.** The encoding is fixed-width and positional, so for valid encodings `sign(a − b)` equals the sign of their instants' difference. Executed over 49,999 consecutive random pairs (T252).

**Controlled-clock vectors**, with the floor at `99999999999999` (T250):

| History | Clock | Allocated |
|---|---|---|
| `20260904120000500` | `…120000400` (behind) | `20260904120000501` |
| `20260904120000500` | `…120000500` (equal) | `20260904120000501` |
| `20260904120000500` | `…120000600` (ahead) | `20260904120000600` |
| `20260904120000999` | `…120000100` | `20260904120001000` |
| `20260904235959999` | `…235959000` | `20260905000000000` |
| `20260930235959999` | `20260930000000000` | `20261001000000000` |
| `20261231235959999` | equal | `20270101000000000` |
| `20240229235959999` | `20240229000000000` | `20240301000000000` |
| none | `20260904120000123` | `20260904120000123` |

* **Equality fails before any external write.** The comparison is strict in both directions.
* The authorised artifact contains exactly `candidate`, and the permit stores exactly `candidate`.
* Finalisation check A5 refuses any artifact whose observed timestamp differs.
* Two issuers for **disjoint** sets may legitimately receive the same value; the rule is per DOI.
* No two provider-reaching permits for the same DOI ever share a value.

### 17.4 The version floor, and why it is never incremented

```sql
CREATE TABLE public.work_crossref_version_floor (
    floor_id    boolean PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT true CHECK (floor_id),
    floor_value bigint NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
    updated_at  timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT current_timestamp,
    CONSTRAINT work_crossref_version_floor_domain_check
        CHECK (floor_value = 0 OR floor_value = 99999999999999)
);
```

Single row, inserted by the migration with `floor_value = 0`. Two triggers make it monotone and permanent against every writer, including ad-hoc SQL in the API's role:
* a `BEFORE UPDATE OR DELETE` row trigger raises `CROSSREF_VERSION_FLOOR_NOT_DECREASING` on any attempt to lower the floor, and `CROSSREF_VERSION_FLOOR_PERMANENT` on any `DELETE`;
* a `BEFORE TRUNCATE` statement trigger refuses `TRUNCATE` with `CROSSREF_VERSION_FLOOR_PERMANENT`.

Without the row there is no floor to compare against, so deleting it would be as dangerous as lowering it.

**The floor is a pure numeric lower bound and is never subjected to calendar arithmetic.** Its domain is exactly two values, enforced by CHECK:

* `0` — the initial value, before G-7.
* `99999999999999` — the **exact G-7 target**, the largest 14-digit value, and the only value G-7 may write.

`99999999999999` is deliberately **not** a valid timestamp encoding (14 digits, and `crossref_ts_decode` refuses it). It never needs a successor, because the allocation rule uses it only in the strict comparison `candidate > floor`. Since every 17-digit value is at least `10000000000000000` and the floor is at most `99999999999999`, that comparison is satisfied by construction once the clock is used — and it is still evaluated, and still fails closed, rather than assumed.

The floor is **global**, not per-DOI: its job is to place every future deposit above the entire legacy 14-digit era, which per-DOI history cannot do because the legacy route minted no permits and therefore left no per-DOI history. Once the floor is at the target, every subsequent allocation strictly exceeds every timestamp the legacy route could ever have emitted. This converts "17-digit values happen to be larger" into a durable, checkable guarantee.

**A 17-digit floor is not permitted.** Raising the floor into the 17-digit range would make it a version value with calendar semantics rather than an era boundary, and would require successor rules the era boundary does not need. If that is ever wanted it is a separate specification change with its own migration; the CHECK makes an accidental one impossible. A floor value outside the domain raises `CROSSREF_VERSION_FLOOR_DOMAIN` (§25.16, T196).

### 17.5 `advanceCrossrefVersionFloor` and its ordering

```
1  SELECT floor_value FROM work_crossref_version_floor WHERE floor_id FOR UPDATE   -- LOCK FIRST
2  evaluate, on the post-wait state: closed-world census; Class-2 cross-check; Class-1B consumption;
   Class-1A eligibility, runtime identity revalidation, authorisation against the pinned register,
   DRAIN, blocking-permit absence and floor binding; and targetValue = 99999999999999 exactly
3  UPDATE the floor
4  INSERT exactly one crossref_version_floor_audit row
5  post-commit G7_RECONCILIATION ledger record
```

**Step 1 must precede step 2.** If the drain check runs first, an in-flight uncommitted reservation is invisible and the floor can advance past a permit that will commit a millisecond later, stranding it below the new floor. With the lock first, either the advance waits and then observes the committed permit and refuses `REFUSED_NOT_DRAINED`, or it wins the lock and the reservation — which takes the floor `FOR SHARE` as the last step of LO-C — waits and then allocates above the new floor. Both orderings were exercised in both interleavings; both are safe, with no deadlock (§25.16, T192–T193).

The advance holds exactly one row lock plus its audit insert, so it participates in no cycle. Authorisation is separate from eligibility: eligibility is a property of the records and runtime state, authorisation is an explicit act recorded in `g7_authorization_reference` and checked against the register snapshot pinned at `ATTEMPT_OPEN` (§22.3).

### 17.6 One advance per attempt

```sql
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX crossref_version_floor_audit_one_advance_per_g6_attempt_idx
    ON public.crossref_version_floor_audit (g6_attempt_id)
    WHERE mutation_kind = 'ADVANCE_VERSION_FLOOR';
```

`g6_attempt_id` and `observation_id` are **native `uuid`**, so PostgreSQL normalises alternative textual spellings on input and a respelled identifier collides on the index. Under `text` it would not, and the one-advance guarantee would be evadable. Verified: a second advance for the same attempt raises `unique_violation` even when the UUID is respelled in upper case.

### 17.7 Completion binding

`completeDistributionJob` for a Crossref `WORK_UPSERT` job requires an `ACCEPTED` permit linked to that job with `permit_generation = attempt.claimed_generation`. This is why no `PROVIDER_ACCEPTED` attempt result and no `provider_identity` column exist: a `SUCCEEDED` work-level attempt already implies an accepted permit at the right generation, by construction.

### 17.8 Deletion-proof provider evidence

Permits carry **immutable, non-referential identities** alongside their operational links:

| Column | Kind | On deletion |
|---|---|---|
| `publisher_identity uuid NOT NULL` | immutable identity | survives Publisher deletion |
| `root_work_identity uuid NOT NULL` | immutable identity | survives Work deletion |
| `job_identity uuid`, `attempt_identity uuid` | immutable identities | survive job/attempt deletion |
| `publisher_id` | operational FK | `ON DELETE SET NULL` — rule S1 |
| `distribution_job_id`, `distribution_job_attempt_id` | operational FKs | `ON DELETE SET NULL` — rule S1 |
| `route`, `scope`, `state`, `reconciliation_state`, `crossref_timestamp`, `doi_batch_id`, `payload_digest`, `doi_set_digest`, `doi_set_cardinality`, `source_generation_witness`, the void reference, both reconciliation acts' references and times, membership rows | content | never deleted by any cascade; the permit row itself cannot be deleted or truncated (§16.2 rule D) |

What survives each deletion:

| Deletion | Job/target/attempt history | Permit and membership |
|---|---|---|
| Work | **survives** (`work_id` NULL, `work_identity` kept) | survives, untouched |
| Imprint | survives (its works' rows keep their identities) | survives, untouched |
| **Publisher** | **destroyed** by the retained released cascade (§13.6) | **survives** in every state, with links `NULL` and identities intact (executed, T204) |
| job row destroyed by the publisher cascade | — | survives, `distribution_job_id` NULL, `job_identity` intact |
| attempt row destroyed with its job | — | survives, `distribution_job_attempt_id` NULL, `attempt_identity` intact |

**No operational cascade can remove provider evidence.** Every foreign key on the permit is `SET NULL`, the membership foreign key is `NO ACTION`, and no other table references either with `CASCADE`. A surviving permit reconstructs route, publisher identity, work identity, DOI set, payload digest, Crossref timestamp, job and attempt linkage where it survives, the void reference, both reconciliation acts, and reconciliation truth.

Admission evidence is a separate operational concept and cascades with its publisher, as frozen (§21.3).

### 17.9 No aggregate provider effect

The generic substrate has **no** provider-effect field. For Crossref:

* provider truth is **per permit**;
* multi-unit and back-catalogue reporting is derived from unit permits at read time and is never stored as a second mutable truth;
* mixed outcomes remain mixed: `crossrefWritePermits` and the back-catalogue projection report the unit-level distribution, and an outer attempt that contains one `INDETERMINATE` unit can never present as a single `ACCEPTED`;
* an outer attempt's terminal conditions are defined by §18.10, not by an aggregate value.

---
## 18. Generic data model, and the back-catalogue, legacy and manual routes

### 18.1 `distribution_job` — added columns and constraints

| Column | Type | Null rule |
|---|---|---|
| `execution_profile` | `public.distribution_platform` | NOT NULL iff `kind = 'WORK_UPSERT'` |
| `work_identity` | `uuid` | NOT NULL iff `kind = 'WORK_UPSERT'`; immutable |
| `created_generation` | `bigint` | NOT NULL iff `kind = 'WORK_UPSERT'`; `>= 1` |
| `job_ordinal` | `integer` | NOT NULL iff `kind = 'WORK_UPSERT'`; `>= 1` |
| `predecessor_job_id` | `uuid` | NULL on a first job; set on every replacement and successor |
| `superseded_by_job_id` | `uuid` | NULL unless a successor or replacement was created |

```sql
ALTER TABLE public.distribution_job
  ADD CONSTRAINT distribution_job_work_upsert_profile_check
      CHECK ((kind = 'WORK_UPSERT') = (execution_profile IS NOT NULL)),
  ADD CONSTRAINT distribution_job_work_upsert_identity_check
      CHECK ((kind = 'WORK_UPSERT') = (work_identity IS NOT NULL)),
  ADD CONSTRAINT distribution_job_work_upsert_generation_check
      CHECK ((kind = 'WORK_UPSERT') = (created_generation IS NOT NULL)
             AND (created_generation IS NULL OR created_generation >= 1)),
  ADD CONSTRAINT distribution_job_work_upsert_ordinal_check
      CHECK ((kind = 'WORK_UPSERT') = (job_ordinal IS NOT NULL)
             AND (job_ordinal IS NULL OR job_ordinal >= 1)),
  -- work_id and work_identity agree while both are present
  ADD CONSTRAINT distribution_job_work_identity_agreement_check
      CHECK (work_id IS NULL OR work_identity IS NULL OR work_id = work_identity),
  -- BACKSTOP: an actionable work-level job can never reference a deleted Work
  ADD CONSTRAINT distribution_job_actionable_work_present_check
      CHECK (kind <> 'WORK_UPSERT' OR status NOT IN ('PENDING','RUNNING') OR work_id IS NOT NULL),
  ADD CONSTRAINT distribution_job_work_upsert_dedup_formula_check CHECK (
      kind <> 'WORK_UPSERT'
      OR deduplication_key = 'WORK_UPSERT:' || publisher_id::text
                              || ':' || work_identity::text
                              || ':' || execution_profile::text
                              || ':' || activation_id::text
                              || ':' || created_generation::text
                              || ':' || job_ordinal::text ),
  ADD CONSTRAINT distribution_job_no_self_predecessor_check
      CHECK (predecessor_job_id IS DISTINCT FROM distribution_job_id),
  ADD CONSTRAINT distribution_job_no_self_successor_check
      CHECK (superseded_by_job_id IS DISTINCT FROM distribution_job_id),
  ADD CONSTRAINT distribution_job_predecessor_fkey
      FOREIGN KEY (predecessor_job_id) REFERENCES public.distribution_job(distribution_job_id) ON DELETE SET NULL,
  ADD CONSTRAINT distribution_job_successor_fkey
      FOREIGN KEY (superseded_by_job_id) REFERENCES public.distribution_job(distribution_job_id) ON DELETE SET NULL;

-- the one changed released object (BE-04's explicitly deferred decision)
ALTER TABLE public.distribution_job
  DROP CONSTRAINT distribution_job_work_id_fkey,
  ADD  CONSTRAINT distribution_job_work_id_fkey
       FOREIGN KEY (work_id) REFERENCES public.work(work_id) ON DELETE SET NULL;
```

The deduplication key is built from `work_identity`, never `work_id`, so the formula CHECK stays satisfiable after a Work is deleted and `work_id` becomes NULL. Because `job_ordinal` increments per `(work_identity, execution_profile)` under the `G` lock held by all three creation sites, the key is unique by construction and the released `distribution_job_deduplication_key_key` UNIQUE remains a backstop rather than ordinary race control. **A `unique_violation` on that key is a protocol violation, not a retry signal**: it is never caught, never retried, and a test asserts it is never observed under the concurrency suite.

**Guard trigger** `distribution_job_work_reference_guard` (BEFORE UPDATE, row): refuses any change to `work_identity` (`DISTRIBUTION_JOB_WORK_IDENTITY_IMMUTABLE`) and refuses restoring `work_id` from NULL to non-NULL (`DISTRIBUTION_JOB_WORK_REFERENCE_NOT_RESTORABLE`).

**Actionable uniqueness:**

```sql
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX distribution_job_one_actionable_work_upsert_idx
    ON public.distribution_job (work_id, execution_profile)
    WHERE kind = 'WORK_UPSERT' AND status IN ('PENDING','RUNNING');
```

At most one PENDING-or-RUNNING work-level job per live `(work, execution_profile)`. It is total over actionable rows because the backstop CHECK forbids an actionable row with NULL `work_id`. Terminal rows fall outside the predicate, so history accumulates without blocking new work.

### 18.2 `distribution_job_attempt` — added columns

| Column | Type | Null rule |
|---|---|---|
| `claimed_generation` | `bigint` | NOT NULL when the parent job is `WORK_UPSERT`; `>= 1` |
| `fenced_at` | `timestamptz` | NULL until the fence passes; set once |
| `recovery_cleared_at` | `timestamptz` | NULL until profile reconciliation clears a fenced abandonment |
| `recovery_clearance_reference` | `text` | NOT NULL exactly when `recovery_cleared_at` is set; non-blank |

`CHECK (claimed_generation IS NULL OR claimed_generation >= 1)`; `CHECK ((recovery_cleared_at IS NULL) = (recovery_clearance_reference IS NULL))`; `CHECK (recovery_cleared_at IS NULL OR fenced_at IS NOT NULL)`.

### 18.3 `work_upsert_generation`

```sql
CREATE TABLE public.work_upsert_generation (
    work_id           uuid NOT NULL,
    execution_profile public.distribution_platform NOT NULL,
    source_generation bigint NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 CHECK (source_generation >= 0),
    updated_at        timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT current_timestamp,
    PRIMARY KEY (work_id, execution_profile)
);
CREATE INDEX work_upsert_generation_profile_idx
    ON public.work_upsert_generation (execution_profile, work_id);
```

**`work_id` carries no foreign key, and the lifecycle that replaces it is §8.6's flush.** The CTO decision `5646180203` permits the removal only together with that lifecycle and with explicit containment and orphan guarantees, which §8.7 states and §25.23 executes. The reason the key could not stay is mechanical: a referential action is never deferred, even on a `DEFERRABLE` key, so the cascade always fired inside the deleting statement — before that statement's own waits for the Work rows of related Works — and the key's insert check took `FOR KEY SHARE` on each root; those two acquisitions are the T303 cycle (§10.6, §25.22). What replaces the key:

* the deleting transaction's flush removes the generation rows of every Work it deleted, first, at completion (§8.6 step 2);
* the flush resolves root existence in its own snapshot, so a deleted Work is never re-inserted (step 3);
* a row written for a root deleted concurrently is removed by its writer's own flush (step 4);
* every other BE-06 writer of a generation row holds `W FOR SHARE` first (§10.4), and static source containment keeps the writer set at five (§8.7, §24, §25.5);
* the two boundaries §8.7 names — `TRUNCATE` and `session_replication_role = replica` — are stated and executed rather than assumed.

`updated_at` is informational and appears in no correctness predicate. The table holds **no** claim, lease, retry, status or terminal column, and nothing may add one.

### 18.3a `work_upsert_capture_queue`

```sql
CREATE TABLE public.work_upsert_capture_queue (
    entry_id   bigint GENERATED ALWAYS AS IDENTITY PRIMARY KEY,
    txid       xid8 NOT NULL DEFAULT pg_current_xact_id(),
    entry_kind text NOT NULL CHECK (entry_kind IN ('OWNERS', 'DELETED', 'ARM', 'PROBE')),
    work_ids   uuid[] CHECK ((entry_kind IN ('OWNERS', 'DELETED')) = (work_ids IS NOT NULL))
);
CREATE INDEX work_upsert_capture_queue_txid_idx ON public.work_upsert_capture_queue (txid, entry_kind);
```

The transaction-local record of a transaction's capture obligations (§8.4, §8.6). `OWNERS` rows carry the owners an event resolved, `DELETED` rows the identity of a Work the transaction deleted, `ARM` the row whose deferred constraint trigger is the flush, and `PROBE` the row by which the flush establishes that it is firing at transaction completion. Every row is written by the transaction that owns it, under a sequence-generated key no other transaction can hold, and deleted by that transaction's own flush; none survives its transaction, and an aborted transaction leaves none (§8.7, T322). The table is never read by any other path, appears in no correctness predicate, and holds no state between transactions. `txid` is `pg_current_xact_id()`, so a flush reads exactly its own rows.

### 18.4 `work_upsert_control`

```sql
CREATE TABLE public.work_upsert_control (
    execution_profile public.distribution_platform PRIMARY KEY,
    capture_enabled   boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false,
    execution_enabled boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false,
    updated_at        timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT current_timestamp,
    CONSTRAINT work_upsert_control_execution_requires_capture_check
        CHECK (NOT execution_enabled OR capture_enabled)
);
```

Rows exist only for implemented profiles; the migration inserts exactly one, `('CROSSREF', false, false)`. A BEFORE UPDATE/DELETE trigger enforces the state machine of §21.1.

### 18.5 `work_upsert_admission`

```sql
CREATE TABLE public.work_upsert_admission (
    execution_profile  public.distribution_platform NOT NULL,
    publisher_id       uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES public.publisher(publisher_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    activation_id      uuid NOT NULL,
    evidence_reference text NOT NULL CHECK (evidence_reference ~ '[^[:space:]]'),
    admitted_at        timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT current_timestamp,
    actor              text NOT NULL CHECK (actor ~ '[^[:space:]]'),
    PRIMARY KEY (execution_profile, publisher_id, activation_id)
);
```

Row present ⇔ admitted; absent ⇔ not admitted. There are no admission states. A BEFORE UPDATE/DELETE trigger refuses `UPDATE` outright (`WORK_UPSERT_ADMISSION_IMMUTABLE`) and refuses `DELETE` while the referenced publisher row still exists (`WORK_UPSERT_ADMISSION_DELETE_ONLY_BY_PUBLISHER_CASCADE`); an RI cascade delete runs after the parent row is gone, so the cascade passes and a direct delete does not. `evidence_reference` is the opaque pointer to the profile's own durable proof; it is **operational gating state, not permanent audit history**, and it disappears with its publisher.

### 18.6 Crossref profile tables

`crossref_write_permit`, `crossref_write_permit_doi` (§16.3), `work_crossref_version_floor` (§17.4), `crossref_version_floor_audit` (§17.6, §22.5), `work_crossref_baseline` (§21.3).

```sql
CREATE TABLE public.work_crossref_baseline (
    publisher_id     uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES public.publisher(publisher_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    activation_id    uuid NOT NULL,
    residual_class   public.crossref_baseline_residual_class NOT NULL,
    residual_count   bigint NOT NULL CHECK (residual_count >= 0),
    detail           text,
    recorded_at      timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT current_timestamp,
    actor            text NOT NULL CHECK (actor ~ '[^[:space:]]'),
    PRIMARY KEY (publisher_id, activation_id, residual_class)
);
```

Baseline rows are written once by the admission transaction and are immutable thereafter. A BEFORE UPDATE/DELETE trigger raises `CROSSREF_BASELINE_IMMUTABLE`; the publisher cascade is excepted, exactly as §18.5 excepts it for admission rows. A class with no residual is recorded with `residual_count = 0`, so "complete" always means "residual recorded and frozen", never "no residual".

```sql
CREATE TABLE public.crossref_write_permit (
    permit_id            uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT public.uuid_generate_v4(),
    route                public.crossref_write_route NOT NULL,
    scope                public.crossref_write_scope NOT NULL,
    state                public.crossref_write_permit_state NOT NULL DEFAULT 'RESERVED',
    reconciliation_state public.crossref_reconciliation_state,
    reservation_token    uuid NOT NULL DEFAULT public.uuid_generate_v4(),
    publisher_id         uuid REFERENCES public.publisher(publisher_id) ON DELETE SET NULL,
    publisher_identity   uuid NOT NULL,
    root_work_identity   uuid NOT NULL,
    distribution_job_id  uuid REFERENCES public.distribution_job(distribution_job_id) ON DELETE SET NULL,
    distribution_job_attempt_id uuid
        REFERENCES public.distribution_job_attempt(distribution_job_attempt_id) ON DELETE SET NULL,
    job_identity         uuid,
    attempt_identity     uuid,
    permit_generation    bigint,
    source_generation_witness bigint NOT NULL CHECK (source_generation_witness >= 0),
    doi_set_digest       text NOT NULL CHECK (doi_set_digest ~ '^[0-9a-f]{64}$'),
    doi_set_cardinality  integer NOT NULL CHECK (doi_set_cardinality >= 1),
    crossref_timestamp   bigint NOT NULL
        CHECK (crossref_timestamp BETWEEN 10000000000000000 AND 99999999999999999),
    doi_batch_id         text NOT NULL CHECK (doi_batch_id ~ '[^[:space:]]'),
    payload_digest       text CHECK (payload_digest IS NULL OR payload_digest ~ '^[0-9a-f]{64}$'),
    operator_authorization_reference       text,
    reconciliation_annotation_reference    text,
    reconciliation_authorization_reference text,
    void_reason          public.crossref_void_reason,
    void_detail          text,
    void_authorization_reference text,
    issued_at            timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT clock_timestamp(),
    authorized_at        timestamptz,
    provider_reported_at timestamptz,
    reconciliation_annotated_at timestamptz,
    reconciled_at        timestamptz,
    closed_at            timestamptz,
    CONSTRAINT crossref_write_permit_authorized_manifest_check CHECK (
        state IN ('RESERVED','VOIDED') OR (payload_digest IS NOT NULL AND authorized_at IS NOT NULL)),
    CONSTRAINT crossref_write_permit_reserved_check CHECK (
        state <> 'RESERVED' OR (payload_digest IS NULL AND authorized_at IS NULL AND provider_reported_at IS NULL
                                AND void_reason IS NULL)),
    CONSTRAINT crossref_write_permit_void_check CHECK (
        (state = 'VOIDED') = (void_reason IS NOT NULL)
        AND (state <> 'VOIDED' OR (payload_digest IS NULL AND authorized_at IS NULL AND provider_reported_at IS NULL))),
    CONSTRAINT crossref_write_permit_operator_void_check CHECK (
        (void_reason IS NOT DISTINCT FROM 'OPERATOR_CLEANUP') = (void_authorization_reference IS NOT NULL)
        AND (void_authorization_reference IS NULL OR void_authorization_reference ~ '[^[:space:]]')),
    CONSTRAINT crossref_write_permit_explicit_void_detail_check CHECK (
        void_reason IS NULL OR void_reason NOT IN ('OWNER_ABANDONED','OPERATOR_CLEANUP')
        OR coalesce(void_detail, '') ~ '[^[:space:]]'),
    CONSTRAINT crossref_write_permit_closed_check CHECK (
        (state IN ('ACCEPTED','NONE_ATTEMPTED','VOIDED')) = (closed_at IS NOT NULL)),
    CONSTRAINT crossref_write_permit_report_check CHECK (
        provider_reported_at IS NULL
        OR (payload_digest IS NOT NULL AND state IN ('ACCEPTED','INDETERMINATE','NONE_ATTEMPTED'))),
    CONSTRAINT crossref_write_permit_reconciliation_state_check CHECK (
        (reconciliation_state IS DISTINCT FROM 'RECONCILED' OR state IN ('ACCEPTED','NONE_ATTEMPTED'))
        AND (reconciliation_state IS NULL OR reconciliation_state = 'RECONCILED' OR state = 'INDETERMINATE')),
    CONSTRAINT crossref_write_permit_reconciliation_evidence_check CHECK (
        (reconciliation_state IS NOT DISTINCT FROM 'RECONCILED') = (reconciliation_authorization_reference IS NOT NULL)
        AND (reconciliation_authorization_reference IS NULL) = (reconciled_at IS NULL)
        AND (reconciliation_annotation_reference IS NULL) = (reconciliation_annotated_at IS NULL)
        AND (reconciliation_annotation_reference IS NULL OR reconciliation_state IS NOT NULL)
        AND (reconciliation_state IS NULL OR reconciliation_state = 'RECONCILED'
             OR reconciliation_annotation_reference IS NOT NULL)
        AND coalesce(reconciliation_annotation_reference, 'x') ~ '[^[:space:]]'
        AND coalesce(reconciliation_authorization_reference, 'x') ~ '[^[:space:]]'),
    CONSTRAINT crossref_write_permit_work_route_check CHECK (
        route <> 'WORK_UPSERT'
        OR (permit_generation IS NOT NULL AND job_identity IS NOT NULL
            AND attempt_identity IS NOT NULL AND scope = 'SINGLE_ROOT_WORK')),
    CONSTRAINT crossref_write_permit_unit_route_check CHECK (
        route <> 'PUBLISHER_BACK_CATALOGUE'
        OR (job_identity IS NOT NULL AND attempt_identity IS NOT NULL AND permit_generation IS NULL
            AND scope = 'SINGLE_ROOT_WORK')),
    CONSTRAINT crossref_write_permit_jobless_route_check CHECK (
        route NOT IN ('LEGACY_SCHEDULED','MANUAL_RECOVERY')
        OR (distribution_job_id IS NULL AND job_identity IS NULL AND attempt_identity IS NULL
            AND permit_generation IS NULL)),
    CONSTRAINT crossref_write_permit_manual_route_check CHECK (
        (route = 'MANUAL_RECOVERY') = (operator_authorization_reference IS NOT NULL)
        AND (operator_authorization_reference IS NULL OR operator_authorization_reference ~ '[^[:space:]]'))
);
CREATE INDEX crossref_write_permit_blocking_partial_idx
    ON public.crossref_write_permit (issued_at, permit_id)
    WHERE state IN ('RESERVED','AUTHORIZED','INDETERMINATE');
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX crossref_write_permit_one_per_work_upsert_attempt_idx
    ON public.crossref_write_permit (attempt_identity)
    WHERE route = 'WORK_UPSERT';
CREATE INDEX crossref_write_permit_job_idx     ON public.crossref_write_permit (job_identity);
CREATE INDEX crossref_write_permit_root_idx    ON public.crossref_write_permit (root_work_identity);
CREATE INDEX crossref_write_permit_attempt_idx ON public.crossref_write_permit (attempt_identity);
```

* `root_work_identity` is `NOT NULL` on every route: every deposit this specification permits is a single root Work's deposit.
* `crossref_write_permit_one_per_work_upsert_attempt_idx` makes an attempt's permit unique for all time, so a voided reservation cannot be re-reserved on the same attempt. The attempt must be failed (§16.6 group A), and the next attempt reserves afresh.
* The membership table's foreign key is `REFERENCES public.crossref_write_permit(permit_id)` with the default `NO ACTION`, since a permit can never be deleted.
* `crossref_write_permit_report_check` confines the report time to the three states a report reaches. `crossref_write_permit_reconciliation_state_check` confines `RECONCILED` to the two closed states it can describe, and the two annotations to `INDETERMINATE`. `crossref_write_permit_reconciliation_evidence_check` pairs each reconciliation act's reference with its time and with the state it records, and refuses a blank reference (§16.8, §17.1).

### 18.7 New enum types

Created whole in Migration 2 (creating a type is transaction-safe; only `ADD VALUE` on an existing type is not):

| Type | Members | n |
|---|---|---|
| `crossref_write_route` | `WORK_UPSERT`, `PUBLISHER_BACK_CATALOGUE`, `LEGACY_SCHEDULED`, `MANUAL_RECOVERY` | 4 |
| `crossref_write_permit_state` | `RESERVED`, `AUTHORIZED`, `INDETERMINATE`, `ACCEPTED`, `NONE_ATTEMPTED`, `VOIDED` | 6 |
| `crossref_reconciliation_state` | `RECONCILED`, `RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED`, `RECONCILIATION_IMPOSSIBLE` (NULL means never reconciled) | 3 |
| `crossref_write_scope` | `SINGLE_ROOT_WORK` | 1 |
| `crossref_baseline_residual_class` | `PARSER_FAILURES`, `DELETED_WORK_IDENTITY`, `DESTROYED_RELATION_TOPOLOGY`, `UNKNOWN_UNQUANTIFIED` | 4 |
| `crossref_void_reason` | finalisation: `SOURCE_CHANGED_DURING_PREPARATION`, `DOI_MEMBERSHIP_CHANGED`, `ARTIFACT_DOI_SET_MISMATCH`, `ARTIFACT_BATCH_ID_MISMATCH`, `ARTIFACT_TIMESTAMP_MISMATCH`, `EXECUTION_NOT_PERMITTED`, `PROFILE_NOT_ADMITTED`, `INELIGIBLE`, `BINDING_SUPERSEDED`, `ASSIGNMENT_DISABLED`, `NO_WORK`; explicit: `OWNER_ABANDONED`, `OPERATOR_CLEANUP` | 13 |

`PUBLISHER_CORPUS` and `OPERATOR_SET` are deliberately absent from `crossref_write_scope`. After the frozen per-unit rule (§18.10) and the manual-recovery rule (§18.12), no route writes a multi-work deposit, so a second scope member would be a value with no writer. The type is kept, with one member, because a future multi-work profile would extend it and because the route CHECKs read it.

### 18.8 Enum values added to released types

`distribution_job_kind` += `WORK_UPSERT`; `distribution_job_cancellation_reason` += `BINDING_SUPERSEDED`, `WORK_DELETED`. **Three `ADD VALUE` statements across two released types**, and nothing else (§23.2). `distribution_job_attempt_result` and `distribution_job_status` are untouched.

### 18.9 Trigger, index, table and enum inventory

Recomputed from this document.

**Generic substrate triggers: 22**

| Group | Count | Where |
|---|---|---|
| capture triggers | 16 | §8.2, one per matrix row |
| the capture flush, a deferred constraint trigger on `work_upsert_capture_queue` | 1 | §8.6 |
| per-profile target-set constraint triggers (one function, two triggers) | 2 | §11.4 |
| `distribution_job` work-reference guard | 1 | §18.1 |
| `work_upsert_control` state-machine guard | 1 | §21.1 |
| `work_upsert_admission` immutability guard | 1 | §18.5 |

**Crossref profile triggers: 12**

| # | Trigger | Kind | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `crossref_write_permit_insert_guard` | BEFORE INSERT, row | §16.2 rule I1 |
| 2 | `crossref_write_permit_fsm` | BEFORE UPDATE OR DELETE, row | §16.2 rules U1–U3, S1–S3, T, A, D |
| 3 | `crossref_write_permit_no_truncate` | BEFORE TRUNCATE, statement | §16.2 rule D |
| 4 | `crossref_write_permit_doi_immutable` | BEFORE UPDATE OR DELETE, row | §16.3 |
| 5 | `crossref_write_permit_doi_no_truncate` | BEFORE TRUNCATE, statement | §16.2 rule D |
| 6 | `crossref_permit_membership_agreement_p` | deferred constraint, on `crossref_write_permit` | §16.3 |
| 7 | `crossref_permit_membership_agreement_d` | deferred constraint, on `crossref_write_permit_doi` | §16.3 |
| 8 | `work_crossref_version_floor_guard` | BEFORE UPDATE OR DELETE, row — monotone and permanent | §17.4 |
| 9 | `work_crossref_version_floor_no_truncate` | BEFORE TRUNCATE, statement | §17.4 |
| 10 | `crossref_version_floor_audit_append_only` | BEFORE UPDATE OR DELETE, row | §22.5 |
| 11 | `crossref_version_floor_audit_no_truncate` | BEFORE TRUNCATE, statement | §22.5 |
| 12 | `work_crossref_baseline_immutable` | BEFORE UPDATE OR DELETE, row, publisher cascade excepted | §18.6 |

**Total triggers added: 34** (22 generic + 12 Crossref). Executed: the released schema carries 57 user triggers and the migrated schema 91 (§25.23, T331).

**Indexes added: 10.**
* **Partial: 4.** Baseline at `4fa7eaa9` is 20, so the total becomes **24**. The four are `distribution_job_one_actionable_work_upsert_idx`, `crossref_version_floor_audit_one_advance_per_g6_attempt_idx`, `crossref_write_permit_blocking_partial_idx` (`WHERE state IN ('RESERVED','AUTHORIZED','INDETERMINATE')`) and `crossref_write_permit_one_per_work_upsert_attempt_idx` (`UNIQUE … WHERE route = 'WORK_UPSERT'`).
* **Non-partial, excluding every primary key: 6.** `work_upsert_generation_profile_idx`, `work_upsert_capture_queue_txid_idx`, `crossref_write_permit_doi_lookup_idx`, `crossref_write_permit_job_idx`, `crossref_write_permit_root_idx` and `crossref_write_permit_attempt_idx`.

**New tables: 9.** `work_upsert_generation`, `work_upsert_capture_queue`, `work_upsert_control`, `work_upsert_admission`, `crossref_write_permit`, `crossref_write_permit_doi`, `work_crossref_version_floor`, `crossref_version_floor_audit` and `work_crossref_baseline`. Executed: 60 tables released, 69 migrated (§25.23, T331).

**Foreign keys removed: 1.** `work_upsert_generation.work_id`'s key to `work`, replaced by §8.6's lifecycle under CTO decision `5646180203` (§18.3). **Released objects changed: still exactly one**, `distribution_job_work_id_fkey` (§10.9).

**New enum types: 6** (§18.7). **`ADD VALUE` statements: 3** (§18.8).

### 18.10 Back-catalogue provider writes

`PUBLISHER_BACK_CATALOGUE` keeps its released orchestration exactly. The one universal rule that reaches it is the permit rule. **The outer job and attempt hold no permit of their own; every actual Crossref POST performed inside the attempt obtains its own reservation and its own authorization.** Each of those permits carries:
* `route = 'PUBLISHER_BACK_CATALOGUE'` and `scope = 'SINGLE_ROOT_WORK'`;
* `root_work_identity` = the unit's Work;
* its own API-derived membership and its own `crossref_timestamp`;
* `job_identity` and `attempt_identity` pointing at the outer job and attempt.

| Unit evidence | Meaning | Outer complete / terminal fail | Next attempt |
|---|---|---|---|
| a permit `ACCEPTED` **in this outer job** | unit deposited | allowed | a reservation for the unit in the same outer job is refused `CROSSREF_UNIT_ALREADY_DEPOSITED_IN_JOB` (executed, T256); the worker skips the unit as deposited |
| latest permit `NONE_ATTEMPTED` | proven not attempted | allowed | retried with a new reservation and a strictly later timestamp (history includes `NONE_ATTEMPTED`) |
| latest permit `VOIDED` | proven never authorised | allowed | retried with a new reservation |
| `INDETERMINATE`, unreconciled | ambiguous | **refused** (`OUTER_ATTEMPT_HAS_OPEN_PERMITS`) | reservation refused `CROSSREF_PERMIT_BLOCKED`; the unit is skipped and reported; **never auto-replayed** |
| `AUTHORIZED` | authorised, not yet reported | **refused**; retryable failure also refused (`ATTEMPT_HAS_AUTHORIZED_PERMIT`) | the worker must report the unit first |
| `RESERVED` | reserved, never authorised | **refused** until voided or finalised (`ATTEMPT_HAS_OPEN_RESERVATION`) | the worker must void or finalise it first |
| no permit | never attempted in any attempt | allowed | deposited normally |

* **Crash after N of M units.** The N units keep their permits in their true states; the remaining M−N have none. Lease expiry closes the outer attempt `ABANDONED`; permits survive and keep blocking. The retry reserves only for units with no permit, a `NONE_ATTEMPTED` or a `VOIDED` one. `RESERVED` leftovers are voided by the route owner or a superuser.
* **Late accepted report** is applied to the unit permit, never to the outer job.
* **Zero units attempted** is a legitimate successful outer attempt: no permit exists and nothing is refused.
* **No aggregate may conceal an ambiguous unit** (§17.9): the outer attempt cannot terminally close while any linked unit permit is `RESERVED`, `AUTHORIZED` or blocking `INDETERMINATE`.
* **Outer cancellation touches no unit permit.** Executed in both orders: cancellation after a unit reservation leaves that reservation `RESERVED`; a reservation after cancellation is refused `CROSSREF_PERMIT_CLAIM_STALE` (T222).
* **Compatibility.** A `v1.8.0` back-catalogue worker mints no permits, so none of these refusals can fire, and released behaviour is unchanged until gate 16. Provider writes without a permit are prohibited by the activation contract.

### 18.11 The legacy scheduled route

The legacy **selector** continues to run unchanged until it is separately retired: `cr_bulk_disseminate.yml`, hourly at `:45`, through `CrossrefIDFinder`. Its **provider write** does not continue unchanged. Before Crossref work-level execution is enabled (gate 16), every legacy Crossref POST must be made through this sequence (§15.6, §28.4):

1. `reserveLegacyScheduledCrossrefWrite(rootWorkId)`, with a `DISSEMINATION_WORKER` credential;
2. the prepared route, with the permit's 17-digit timestamp;
3. extraction (§16.11);
4. `finaliseCrossrefWrite`, with no claim token;
5. the digest recheck;
6. the POST of exactly that artifact;
7. `reportCrossrefWrite`.

A `LEGACY_SCHEDULED` permit has no job, attempt or generation (`crossref_write_permit_jobless_route_check`). Its `reservation_token` is its only later credential. The route requires coverage like every other route. Gate 16 therefore also requires every publisher in the legacy selector's publisher set to have an enabled `CROSSREF` assignment; otherwise its legacy writes are refused `CROSSREF_PUBLISHER_NOT_COVERED`, which fails closed. Until gate 16, the two paths are kept apart by the fact that no work-level job can be claimed at all (`execution_enabled = false`).

### 18.12 The manual recovery route

`MANUAL_RECOVERY` permits are reserved by `reserveManualRecoveryCrossrefWrite`, which requires `SUPERUSER` (§19.4) and a non-blank reference recorded as `operator_authorization_reference`. They are subject to every rule above: API-derived canonical membership, the blocking predicate, LO-C, the calendar timestamp allocation, the floor, and finalisation with all checks. They exist so that an operator repairing a deposit by hand cannot bypass the ordering guarantees the automated routes obey.

* **A dissemination worker cannot mint one.** `route` is assigned by the mutation invoked, and that mutation requires `SUPERUSER`.
* **A superuser is never asked for a worker claim token.** A `MANUAL_RECOVERY` permit has no job, so finalisation requires only the reservation token and the route-derived `SUPERUSER` role.
* **The manual dispatch workflow** (`manual_disseminate.yml`, §3.7) is the natural caller. After the cutover, a `platform: Crossref` dispatch must present a superuser credential and an authorization reference and use this route, or be refused before any POST (§28.6).

---

## 19. GraphQL and API contract

### 19.1 Released compatibility is permanent

`claimDistributionJobs(data: ClaimDistributionJobsInput!): [ClaimedDistributionJob!]!` keeps its released signature, defaults (`limit = 10`, `leaseSeconds = 900`, `kinds = []`), clamps and semantics **byte-for-byte**, and is permanently bound to the **legacy-claimable kind set** `{PUBLISHER_BACK_CATALOGUE}`:

* `kinds: []` means "every legacy-claimable kind", which is and remains exactly `PUBLISHER_BACK_CATALOGUE`. A released client that passes an empty list can therefore never begin claiming `WORK_UPSERT` jobs it cannot execute — the hazard is closed by the API contract, not merely by the initially inert state.
* A list naming `WORK_UPSERT` is refused as a whole with `DISTRIBUTION_JOB_KIND_NOT_CLAIMABLE`. It is not silently filtered.
* The allow-list is bound into the SQL predicate per mutation, so for any request a `v1.8.0` client can form, the result set is identical to the released one.

`completeDistributionJob`, `failDistributionJob` and `cancelDistributionJob` keep their released signatures; the new guards (§11.5, §12.3, §14.4, §17.7) apply server-side when `kind = 'WORK_UPSERT'`, and the unit-permit guards of §18.10 apply to a back-catalogue attempt only while it holds unit permits. A `v1.8.0` back-catalogue worker mints no permits, so for it nothing changes.

### 19.2 Work-level worker API

Both require `DISSEMINATION_WORKER` (`policy.rs:226`, `require_dissemination_worker`), which `SUPERUSER` does not satisfy.

```graphql
input MaterializeWorkUpsertJobsInput {
  executionProfiles: [DistributionPlatform!]!     # required, non-empty
  limit: Int = 100                                # <= 0 does nothing; clamped to 500
}
type MaterializeWorkUpsertJobsResult {
  examined: Int!
  created: Int!
  rebound: Int!
  skippedResolved: Int!
  skippedIneligible: Int!
  skippedNotAdmitted: Int!
  remainingCandidates: Int!
}
materializeWorkUpsertJobs(data: MaterializeWorkUpsertJobsInput!): MaterializeWorkUpsertJobsResult!

input ClaimWorkUpsertJobsInput {
  executionProfiles: [DistributionPlatform!]!     # required, non-empty
  limit: Int = 10                                 # clamped to 50; <= 0 claims nothing
  leaseSeconds: Int = 900                         # clamped to 60 … 3600
}
claimWorkUpsertJobs(data: ClaimWorkUpsertJobsInput!): [ClaimedDistributionJob!]!
```

Exact behaviour of `claimWorkUpsertJobs`:

| Condition | Result |
|---|---|
| `executionProfiles` empty | `WORK_UPSERT_EXECUTION_PROFILES_REQUIRED`. There is **no** "empty means all" semantics anywhere in the work-level API. |
| a listed key is not a registered profile | `WORK_UPSERT_PROFILE_NOT_IMPLEMENTED`, naming no other key |
| registered but `execution_enabled = false`, as read under the profile's execution gate (§10.7) | `[]`; no attempt is opened |
| registered, enabled, but no admission row for a job's binding | that job is not returned; no error |
| a job's `(work_identity, profile)` has an uncleared fenced abandonment and the profile is `ReplayBlocked` | that job is not returned; no error |
| otherwise | the released claim behaviour, with `kind` hard-bound to `WORK_UPSERT` |

`ClaimedDistributionJob` keeps its released shape. `DistributionJob` gains `executionProfile: DistributionPlatform`, `workIdentity: Uuid`, `createdGeneration: Generation`, `jobOrdinal: Int`, `predecessorJobId: Uuid`, `supersededByJobId: Uuid`; `DistributionJobAttempt` gains `claimedGeneration: Generation`, `fencedAt: Timestamp`, `recoveryClearedAt: Timestamp`. Every added field is nullable in SDL except where the kind guarantees otherwise, because back-catalogue rows carry NULL.

### 19.3 Generation wire type — one exact lossless contract

```
PostgreSQL  bigint
Rust        i64
GraphQL     scalar Generation      # a decimal string matching 0|[1-9][0-9]*, domain 0 … 9223372036854775807
```

`Generation` is a `GraphQLScalar` newtype over `String`, exactly like the released `Doi`, `Isbn`, `Orcid` and `Ror` scalars (`model/mod.rs:18-82`), with a parse that rejects a sign, a leading zero, whitespace, an empty string, a non-digit and any value outside the `i64` domain. It is **never** `Int`: Juniper's `Int` is `i32` and a `bigint` does not fit. It is never a JSON number in any wire position. The same type is used in SQL, Rust, the SDL, the generated client contract, every input and output type, every report and every test. `graphql_client` maps the custom scalar to `String` in `thoth-client`, matching how the released scalars are handled.

### 19.4 Crossref profile API, with route-coherent authorization

Three rules make the four-route authorization coherent, and none depends on a caller-supplied value:

1. **`route` is never an input.** Four route-specific reservation mutations each assign their own `route` server-side. A caller cannot select a route it is not authorised for.
2. **For every later operation on a permit, the required role is derived from the row's persisted `route`.**
   * `WORK_UPSERT`, `PUBLISHER_BACK_CATALOGUE` and `LEGACY_SCHEDULED` apply `require_dissemination_worker()`.
   * `MANUAL_RECOVERY` applies `require_superuser()`.

   The one exception is the superuser void, which is `SUPERUSER` for every route.
3. **Request authorization is checked on every invocation, and is never replayed.** Replayability applies to an operation's state and result, never to the caller's authorization. For `finaliseCrossrefWrite`, `reportCrossrefWrite` and `voidCrossrefWriteReservation` the precedence is exact:
   1. validation of the call's own arguments alone, such as a blank `detail`, which discloses nothing about the permit;
   2. a principal holding neither `DISSEMINATION_WORKER` nor `SUPERUSER` → the released `Unauthorised`, before any read, because no route's role could admit it;
   3. an MVCC read of the permit's immutable `route`; no such permit → `CROSSREF_PERMIT_NOT_FOUND`;
   4. the caller lacks the role derived from `route` → the released `Unauthorised`, before any lock;
   5. only then the operation's locks, the reservation-token check (`CROSSREF_PERMIT_REQUIRES_RESERVATION_TOKEN`), and its state, replay and claim checks (§16.6, §16.7).

   A caller without the role therefore takes no lock, changes nothing, and receives no replay result, void result, digest or landing signal, whatever token it presents; the not-found and unauthorised answers tell it only whether a permit with that identifier exists. The role is evaluated against the principal of the request itself, exactly as the released gates evaluate it (`policy.rs:201`, `:226`). Nothing records an authorization on the permit or carries one across requests, so a role removed between an original call and its retry refuses the retry. `voidCrossrefWriteReservationAsSuperuser` and `reconcileCrossrefWritePermit` apply `require_superuser()` first, before any read.

No operation requires a role its intended caller cannot hold, and no operation requires two role gates at once. This matters because the released `require_dissemination_worker` explicitly does not accept `SUPERUSER` (§3.3).

**Reservation — four mutations, four fixed routes**

| Mutation | Role | Route assigned | Required identity fields |
|---|---|---|---|
| `reserveWorkUpsertCrossrefWrite(data: {distributionJobId, claimToken})` | `DISSEMINATION_WORKER` | `WORK_UPSERT` | job + claim token + open attempt; nine fence clauses as a precondition; `permit_generation := claimed_generation`; `root_work_identity := job.work_identity` |
| `reserveBackCatalogueCrossrefWrite(data: {distributionJobId, claimToken, rootWorkId})` | `DISSEMINATION_WORKER` | `PUBLISHER_BACK_CATALOGUE` | outer job `PUBLISHER_BACK_CATALOGUE` and `RUNNING` + claim token + open attempt; the unit belongs to the outer job's publisher |
| `reserveLegacyScheduledCrossrefWrite(data: {rootWorkId})` | `DISSEMINATION_WORKER` | `LEGACY_SCHEDULED` | root Work only |
| `reserveManualRecoveryCrossrefWrite(data: {rootWorkId, operatorAuthorizationReference})` | `SUPERUSER` | `MANUAL_RECOVERY` | root Work + non-blank reference |

All four return `CrossrefWriteReservation`: `permitId`, `reservationToken`, `crossrefTimestamp: Generation` (17 digits), `doiBatchId`, the API-derived canonical `dois`, and the binding the reservation checked. **None accepts a `dois` argument.** `CrossrefWriteReservation` is the only output type with a `reservationToken` field; the token then travels only as an input, to `finaliseCrossrefWrite`, `reportCrossrefWrite` and `voidCrossrefWriteReservation` (§16.4).

**Later operations**

| Mutation | Role | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `finaliseCrossrefWrite(data: {permitId, reservationToken, claimToken, observedDois, observedDoiBatchId, observedCrossrefTimestamp, payloadDigest})` | derived from `route` | returns `CrossrefFinalisationResult { outcome: CrossrefFinalisationOutcome!, voidReason: CrossrefVoidReason, permit: CrossrefWritePermit! }`, where `CrossrefFinalisationOutcome = AUTHORIZED | VOIDED_RETRYABLE | VOIDED_JOB_RETIRED` and `voidReason` is NULL iff `AUTHORIZED`. Group C refusals are GraphQL errors carrying their stable code. `claimToken` is required exactly when the permit has a job |
| `reportCrossrefWrite(data: {permitId, reservationToken, outcome})` | derived from `route` | `outcome ∈ {ACCEPTED, INDETERMINATE, NONE_ATTEMPTED}`; requires `AUTHORIZED`; no claim token |
| `voidCrossrefWriteReservation(data: {permitId, reservationToken, detail})` | derived from `route` | `RESERVED → VOIDED` (`OWNER_ABANDONED`); no claim token |
| `voidCrossrefWriteReservationAsSuperuser(data: {permitId, detail, authorizationReference})` | `SUPERUSER` | `RESERVED → VOIDED` (`OPERATOR_CLEANUP`); non-blank reference, persisted; no reservation token; every route |
| `reconcileCrossrefWritePermit(data: {permitId, outcome, reconciliationState, authorizationReference})` | `SUPERUSER` | §16.8; the only operation that can clear a fenced abandonment, and only internally |

**Control and operator mutations**

| Mutation | Role |
|---|---|
| `enableWorkUpsertCapture(executionProfile: DistributionPlatform!)` | `SUPERUSER` — `(false,false) → (true,false)` only |
| `setWorkUpsertExecution(executionProfile: DistributionPlatform!, enabled: Boolean!)` | `SUPERUSER` — `(true,false) ⇄ (true,true)` only. Takes the profile's execution gate `Q` EXCLUSIVE before the control row and holds it until it commits, so it returns only after every claim, reservation and finalisation that read the previous value has committed (§21.1) |
| `seedCrossrefWorkUpsert(data: {publisherId, limit})` | `SUPERUSER` — one bounded batch of the lock-and-inspect seed (§21.2); writes generation state only, and creates no job, target or attempt |
| `admitCrossrefWorkUpsert(data: {publisherId, evidenceReference})` | `SUPERUSER` — census and admission in one transaction; refuses if the census is non-empty; creates no job |
| `advanceCrossrefVersionFloor(data: {targetValue, g6AttemptId, observationId, authorizationReference, authorizationRegisterDigest})` | `SUPERUSER` — §17.5; `targetValue` uses the `Generation` grammar and must be exactly `99999999999999` |
| `materializeWorkUpsertJob(data: {workId, executionProfile, force: Boolean = false})` | `SUPERUSER` — single-unit operator drain; `force` skips only the resolution test |

**Removed, and not replaced by anything public: `clearFencedAbandonment`** (§11.7). No generic fence mutation is exposed either (§11.5).

**New mutations: 17** — 2 work-level worker (§19.2), 4 Crossref reservations, 5 Crossref permit operations, 6 control/operator.

**The authorization matrix is a required implementation test** (§25.17, T257). Each of the 17 mutations is exercised with four principal shapes: worker-only, superuser-only, both roles, and publisher-scoped. The observed accept/refuse matrix must equal this table exactly. It cannot be executed in the database harness, because the gates are GraphQL resolver guards. For finalisation, the outcome report and the route-owner void on each of the four routes, it adds rule 3's cases: an identical-digest replay of an `AUTHORIZED` permit and a replay of a finalisation-voided one, each with the correct token and a principal without the role; the role with a wrong token; the role with the token; the role removed between the original call and the retry, wherever the resolver's principal model can express that; and the lost-response repeats of the report and the void under the same shapes. A principal without the role must receive the released `Unauthorised` and no replay or void result. The precedence itself was modelled in the database harness, as specification-harness evidence, in §25.19 (T277, T278); that is not T257.

### 19.5 Safety fields

Two **argument-free** query fields: `crossrefBlockingWritePermitCount: Int!` and `crossrefDrained: Boolean!`. Neither accepts a publisher, route, kind or DOI filter, in any form.

### 19.6 Generated artifacts

`thoth-client/assets/schema.graphql` is build-generated and gitignored, so `git status` is not a valid "SDL unchanged" check; the review compares built SDL between the base and the candidate. The candidate's SDL diff must contain exactly: the new scalar, the new enums, the new input and output types, the seventeen new mutations, the new queries, and the added fields on `DistributionJob` and `DistributionJobAttempt` — and no change to any released field's type or nullability. `thoth-export-server`'s `paperclip` OpenAPI artifact gains exactly one path (§15.3).

**The token's SDL boundary.** In the built SDL, `reservationToken` is an output field of exactly one type, `CrossrefWriteReservation`, and an input field of exactly three input types: those of `finaliseCrossrefWrite`, `reportCrossrefWrite` and `voidCrossrefWriteReservation`. No other type — no permit, finalisation-result, report or query type — has a field named `reservationToken` or `reservation_token` (T170).

---
## 20. Reporting and observability

All reports are **work-level only** and **superuser only** via `require_superuser()` (`policy.rs:201`), unscoped, following the argument, pagination and ordering conventions of `publisher_service_configurations` (`graphql/query.rs:680`) and `publisher_service_configuration_count` (`:735`). None takes a `kinds` argument; each is hard-bound in the resolver to `kind = 'WORK_UPSERT'`. `workId: Uuid!` is type-safe here only because of `distribution_job_work_upsert_identity_check`; back-catalogue reporting remains a separate future field with its own nullable types.

| # | Field | Content |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `workUpsertResolution(executionProfile!, filter, order, limit, offset)` | per `(work, profile)`: `currentSourceGeneration`, `successResolutionGeneration` (`D`), `terminalJobResolutionGeneration` (`H`), `resolutionGeneration`, `outstandingResidueGenerations` (the half-open interval `(max(D,H), S]` or none), `state ∈ {ACTIONABLE, RESOLVED, RESIDUE}` |
| 2 | `workUpsertResolutionCount` | count for the same filter |
| 3 | `workUpsertResidue(executionProfile!, …)` | candidates with class `MATERIALIZABLE`, `NOT_ADMITTED` or `INELIGIBLE`, plus the failing eligibility clause |
| 4 | `workUpsertStaleBindings(executionProfile!, …)` | PENDING rows whose binding is obsolete, with the reason the drain will use |
| 5 | `workUpsertJobs(executionProfile!, …)` / `workUpsertJob(workId!)` | job rows with lineage |
| 6 | `workUpsertAttempts(workId!)` | attempts with `claimedGeneration`, `fencedAt`, `result`, `recoveryClearedAt` |
| 7 | `workUpsertBlockedByRecovery(executionProfile!)` | `(work_identity, profile)` pairs with an uncleared fenced abandonment |
| 8 | `workUpsertCaptureLag(executionProfile!)` | `max(source_generation − resolution)` over non-resolved rows |
| 9 | `workUpsertAdmissions(executionProfile!)` | admission rows with evidence references |
| 10 | `workUpsertControl` | the control row per profile |
| 11 | `crossrefWritePermits(filter, …)` | permits with state, reconciliation state, route, identities, timestamp, digest, DOI membership, void reason and detail, the void authorization reference, both reconciliation acts' references and times, and the issuance, authorization, report and closure times (§17.1) — never the reservation token; the unit-level projection for a back-catalogue outer attempt (§17.9) |
| 12 | `crossrefUnresolvedPermits` | blocking permits, oldest first, with their DOIs, their annotation with its reference and time if any, and their linked job identity if any |
