| 13 | `crossrefVersionFloor` | the floor value and its audit history |
| 14 | `crossrefBaseline(publisherId)` | the residual classes and counts recorded at admission (§21.2) |
| 15 | `crossrefBlockingWritePermitCount` / `crossrefDrained` | §19.5, argument-free |

Naming discipline: nothing is called a "suppressed generation". The three quantities of report 1 are named separately, because a terminal job's resolution generation, the current source generation and the outstanding residue are three different facts and conflating them was exactly the ambiguity the frozen model removed.

**Operational alarms** are stated as predicates over these reports, never as clocks: residue non-empty and not decreasing across drains; stale bindings non-empty after a drain; blocked-by-recovery non-empty; unresolved permits non-empty; capture lag increasing while execution is enabled.

---

## 21. Control, admission and bootstrap

### 21.1 The control state machine

```
(capture=false, execution=false)  --enableWorkUpsertCapture-->  (true, false)
(true, false)   --setWorkUpsertExecution(true)-->   (true, true)
(true, true)    --setWorkUpsertExecution(false)-->  (true, false)      operational pause
(false, true)   impossible;   capture true -> false impossible;   row DELETE impossible
```

Guards: the CHECK of §18.4 rejects `(false, true)` on INSERT and UPDATE; a BEFORE UPDATE/DELETE trigger raises `WORK_UPSERT_CAPTURE_IS_MONOTONE` on `true → false`, `WORK_UPSERT_CONTROL_ROW_IS_PERMANENT` on DELETE and `WORK_UPSERT_CONTROL_KEY_IMMUTABLE` on a key change. Both mutations refuse an unregistered key.

Semantics:

* **Capture is monotone** once enabled, for the lifetime of the deployed profile. Rollback pauses execution; it never disables capture.
* `capture_enabled = false` gates **materialization only**: the unit ends `CAPTURE_DISABLED` and creates, bumps or rebinds nothing.
* `execution_enabled = false` gates **claiming and fence passage only**, and does so as a **closed barrier**, through the execution gate below. Completion and failure of an already-fenced attempt are always accepted, because a durable outcome must be recordable. T2's successor creation does not depend on it.
* **Generation advancement never depends on either flag**, so durable generations and residue accumulate during a pause and during the shadow phase.

**The execution gate `Q`.** Each registered execution profile has one transaction-scoped advisory lock, in the two-argument lock space under the fixed namespace `1948572002`, distinct from the DOI keys' `1948572001` (§16.5):

```sql
SELECT pg_advisory_xact_lock_shared(1948572002, hashtext('be06:work_upsert:execution:' || $profile));  -- a consumer
SELECT pg_advisory_xact_lock       (1948572002, hashtext('be06:work_upsert:execution:' || $profile));  -- setWorkUpsertExecution
```

* **Consumers** are exactly the operations that may act on `execution_enabled = true` for `WORK_UPSERT`: the claim (§10.7), the `WORK_UPSERT` reservation (§16.4) and `WORK_UPSERT` finalisation (§16.6). Each takes `Q` in SHARE mode after its generic prefix — the claim after lease recovery, the reservation and finalisation after `A`. Each reads `execution_enabled` in a statement executed after the lock is granted, and holds `Q` until its transaction ends.
* **`setWorkUpsertExecution`** takes `Q` in EXCLUSIVE mode first, then updates the control row, and holds `Q` until it commits; the mutation returns only after that commit. A call that finds the value already set writes nothing, but still takes the gate, so it too returns only after every earlier consumer has finished.
* **No other path takes `Q`.** Capture, materialization, the seed, admission, `enableWorkUpsertCapture`, completion, failure, cancellation, lease recovery, the outcome report, both voids, reconciliation, deletion and G-7 never take it, and none of them reads `execution_enabled`.

SHARE and EXCLUSIVE conflict, and both are held until commit, so a consumer's hold and a transition's hold never overlap. A consumer granted `Q` before a transition commits before the transition is granted it. A consumer that requests `Q` while a transition holds it or waits for it is granted `Q` only after the transition commits, because PostgreSQL grants a new SHARE request only when it conflicts with no holder and no waiter queued ahead of it. The consumer's read, taken in a later statement under `READ COMMITTED`, then sees the new value. Hence, once `setWorkUpsertExecution(profile, false)` has returned:

1. no claim returns an attempt opened on a value read before the pause: a claim that read `true` committed before the pause did, and one that did not returns `[]`;
2. no attempt becomes fenced or `AUTHORIZED` on such a value: a finalisation that read `true` committed before the pause did, so its authorization is historical when the pause returns, and one that did not reads `false` and returns `VOIDED_RETRYABLE / EXECUTION_NOT_PERMITTED`;
3. no `WORK_UPSERT` reservation commits on such a value: it is refused `WORK_UPSERT_EXECUTION_NOT_PERMITTED`, as the consequences below promise;
4. an attempt fenced before the barrier finishes and reports: the outcome report, completion, failure and T2's successor take no gate and are never refused by it;
5. capture, generation advancement, materialization and rebinding continue unchanged;
6. nothing is destroyed: the transition writes one column of one control row;
7. no generic path row-locks a control, admission or assignment row — the gate is an advisory lock, and every reader still reads the control row by MVCC;
8. the publisher-first prefix is intact: no path takes `Q` before `P`, and the claim, which takes no `P`, takes `Q` after lease recovery and before its statement.

The same argument holds for `setWorkUpsertExecution(profile, true)`: a consumer that read `false` has committed its refusal before enable returns, and one granted `Q` after enable reads `true`. Executed in both orders for the claim, the reservation and finalisation, and for enable, with the pause statement, `pg_locks`, the waits, the commit order and the final states. The `37e44ce3` candidate's ungated claim, reservation and finalisation were kept as negative controls, and each reproduced the stale-`true` escape (§25.20, T280–T286).

Consequences of a pause, each a required test: PENDING jobs stay PENDING and unclaimable; a RUNNING unfenced attempt's reservation is refused `WORK_UPSERT_EXECUTION_NOT_PERMITTED`, or its finalisation returns `VOIDED_RETRYABLE / EXECUTION_NOT_PERMITTED`, and the worker reports a retryable failure; a claim, reservation or finalisation that read `true` before the pause has committed by the time the pause returns; a RUNNING fenced attempt finishes and reports; T2 still creates the successor; rebinding is unaffected; edits keep advancing generations and the drain keeps creating durable jobs.

**The initially inert state, stated exactly.** From Migration 2's commit until `enableWorkUpsertCapture` — first under the pre-BE-06 release still serving (gate 10, §23.6), then under the deployed `v1.10.0` backend (gate 11) — the only database writes BE-06 causes are: the transient `work_upsert_capture_queue` rows of a committing editorial transaction, every one of them deleted by that transaction's own flush; and the inserts, bumps and deletions of `work_upsert_generation` rows that flush performs at the transaction's completion. No job, target, attempt, admission, permit or floor-audit row is written; no claim of a `WORK_UPSERT` job is possible; no provider is contacted; and, once the `v1.10.0` backend serves, the two GraphQL safety fields report a drained system. Executed under the released binary, over its whole test suite and over the sequence of §23.6: on the Alternative A schema, 2,798 generation inserts and 833 bumps, every one for an existing Work, 7 deletions, every one after its Work was gone, 6,192 queue inserts and 6,192 matching deletes with no transaction unbalanced, no queue row left, no orphaned generation row, and no other BE-06 row of any kind (§25.23, T329, T330).

This statement concerns BE-06's own behaviour. The reservation mutations are callable from the `v1.10.0` deployment (gate 11) onward, but only two callers can exist:
* the dissemination release of gate 15, which follows gate 12;
* a superuser using `MANUAL_RECOVERY`, which §27.4 forbids before gate 16.

A reservation writes the `0` generation row of §16.4 when none exists, and nothing else outside the permit tables.

### 21.2 Crossref bootstrap: the lock-and-inspect seed

A seed that bumped the generation whenever it found a row would not be idempotent — each replay would advance the generation — so the seed is defined as a lock-and-inspect step whose property is exact idempotence. **The seed writes generation state only.** It never inserts a `distribution_job`, `distribution_job_target` or `distribution_job_attempt` row, never calls the creation helper (§11.2), and is not a creation site (§11.3).

For each Work `w` in the eligible population for `(P, A)` (E1–E6 of §14.2, evaluated on current ownership), ascending `work_id`, bounded batches, one transaction per Work, under LO-5's prefix:

1. `P FOR SHARE` on the binding publisher, `W FOR SHARE`, and re-read the binding under `W`; if it is no longer that publisher, end `BINDING_MOVED_RETRY_LATER` and write nothing (§9.3 step 4, §10.1).
2. `INSERT INTO work_upsert_generation … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, then `SELECT … FOR UPDATE`. **Never bump.**
3. If `source_generation = 0`, set it to `1` exactly once — the seed event — and end `SEEDED`.
4. Otherwise write nothing, and end `OBSERVED`: the row already counts at least one event.

A second run finds every row positive and writes nothing.

**The two-stage bootstrap, exactly.**

```
seed            durable generation state for every eligible Work    (this section; no job)
  → census      every eligible Work generation-covered               (§21.3; no job)
  → admission   the exact (profile, publisher, activation)          (§21.3; no job)
  → materializer and its drain — creation site 1                     (§9.3, §9.5)
  → BE-04 WORK_UPSERT jobs, claimed under the execution gate         (§10.7)
```

Before admission nothing in the bootstrap creates a job: the seed creates none, capture creates none (§8.4 rule 5), and the materializer ends `RESIDUE_NOT_ADMITTED` for the unadmitted binding (§9.3 step 10). After admission the drain turns every Work whose generation exceeds its resolution into one job at that generation, and a second drain with no intervening event creates nothing (§9.3). `capture_enabled = true` is the seed's precondition (§21.1), so that drain is not gated off (§9.3 step 5).

**The seed and editorial capture.** A capture records the event's owners and writes nothing else — no job, no target, no attempt (§8.4); the editorial transaction's flush inserts or advances the Work's generation row at that transaction's completion (§8.6). It serialises with the seed on that Work: on `W`, which the seed holds `FOR SHARE` and an edit of the Work itself takes `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` through the released statement, or, for an edit propagated from a chapter, on the root's generation row, which the seed holds `FOR UPDATE` and the flush takes at completion. Whichever commits first, the other observes it:
* **the seed first:** it inserts the row and raises it from `0` to `1`, and the capture then raises it to `2`;
* **the capture first:** it leaves the row at `1` or above, which the seed observes and leaves unchanged.

Either way the Work ends generation-covered, with a generation that counts every committed event, and with no job. After admission the drain creates its one job, at that generation. Executed in both orders, for an edit of the Work and for a propagated chapter edit, each followed by the census, a refused and then a successful admission, and two drains (§25.20, T290). Before admission the seed and the capture created no job in any order, and the census stayed non-empty until the seed completed, and was then 0. After admission the first drain created exactly one `PENDING` job per residue Work, the edited Work's at its captured generation, and the second created nothing. The `37e44ce3` candidate's seed, kept as the negative control, created a `PENDING` job before any admission existed.

**A Work the seed has not yet visited has no generation row**, unless an edit or a reservation inserted one. The census of §21.3 counts a Work with no row, or with a reservation's `0` row, as uncovered, so an interrupted or partial seed can never be admitted.

### 21.3 Census and admission

`admitCrossrefWorkUpsert` runs one transaction for a publisher `P`:
1. `SELECT 1 FROM public.publisher WHERE publisher_id = P FOR SHARE` — the binding publisher's lock, first (LO-5). It holds off every assignment and activation change for `P`, because the released coordinator takes `P FOR UPDATE`.
2. Read `A`, the activation of `P`'s enabled `CROSSREF` assignment (E2).
3. Compute the eligible population of `(P, A)` — E1–E6 of §14.2, on current ownership — with the seed's own eligibility code.
4. Evaluate the census, one statement, exactly:

```sql
-- $1: publisher P, already held FOR SHARE by this transaction.
-- $2: the eligible population of (P, A), uuid[] -- E1-E6 of §14.2 on current ownership,
--     computed by the seed's own eligibility code in this transaction, after P FOR SHARE.
-- Generation coverage: a Work is covered iff it has a generation row with source_generation > 0.
-- A Work with no generation row, or with its row still at 0, is uncovered. No job is consulted.
SELECT count(*) AS uncovered
  FROM unnest($2::uuid[]) AS e(work_id)
  JOIN public.work w    ON w.work_id = e.work_id
  JOIN public.imprint i ON i.imprint_id = w.imprint_id AND i.publisher_id = $1
  LEFT JOIN public.work_upsert_generation g
         ON g.work_id = w.work_id AND g.execution_profile = 'CROSSREF'
 WHERE g.work_id IS NULL
    OR g.source_generation = 0;
```

5. If `uncovered > 0`, refuse `WORK_UPSERT_ADMISSION_CENSUS_NOT_EMPTY`. Otherwise insert the admission row for `('CROSSREF', P, A)` and the baseline rows, and commit. **Admission creates no job, target or attempt**; after it, the materializer drains the admitted binding's residue (§9.3).

**Generation-covered, exactly.** An eligible Work is covered iff it has a generation row with `source_generation > 0`. It is uncovered, and counted, when it has **no generation row at all** — the seed never reached it — or when its row is `0` because only a reservation touched it. The absent row is an explicit disjunct, `g.work_id IS NULL`, over a `LEFT JOIN`: an inner join would drop the unvisited Work. The joins to `work` and `imprint` re-check existence and current ownership, so a Work deleted, or moved to another publisher, after the population was read leaves the census.

**Why no job is consulted.** The census proves what admission needs and nothing more: every eligible Work's source state is durably recorded as a counted generation. A job cannot be a coverage condition, because before admission nothing in the bootstrap may create one (§21.2). A positive row whose generation exceeds its resolution, with no job, is residue: it is durable (INV-RESIDUE) and is drained after admission, so counting it as uncovered would make admission wait for work that only admission can release. The two uncovered cases are exactly the ones the drain cannot repair. A Work with no row is invisible to the residue predicate, which ranges over generation rows (§9.2), and would never be drained. A `0` row records a reservation's witness and no counted event, and the seed must still raise it to `1` exactly once. The `37e44ce3` candidate's job disjunct is withdrawn (§6.3).

Executed under generation coverage (§25.20, T291–T292), from this document's own statement. This supersedes the census evidence of §25.18 (T262–T264), which ran against the withdrawn job-disjunct statement. The census counted all 7 eligible Works before any seed, and 5 after a bounded first batch of 2, so admission was refused. A batch that crashed after visiting 3 kept the one Work it had written (4). An edit's row made its unvisited Work covered. A reservation's `0` row left its Work uncovered, and so did an actionable job on a Work whose row was `0`. A deleted and a moved Work left the census (1). Once the seed completed, the census was 0 and a re-run of the seed wrote nothing. Admission then succeeded, bound to the publisher's activation, and created no job. The drain created exactly one `PENDING` job per covered Work, each at its durable generation, and a second drain created nothing. An edit after resolution left the census at 0 and left residue, which the next drain turned into one job at the captured generation 2. An activation change for `P` waited for the admission transaction; an editorial capture did not; and neither created a job.

If the census is non-empty, **nothing is admitted**; the seed is re-run. There is no partial admission.

Completeness despite: partial batches and process restart — every batch commits independently, re-running is a no-op for positive rows, and a Work no batch has reached is uncovered; concurrent edits — a capture only inserts or advances the Work's generation row, serialised with the seed on that row or its `W` (§21.2), and leaves the Work generation-covered whichever commits first, while an edit that commits after the census's read raises a positive row further, which after admission is residue for the drain (§9.3); Work deletion — the generation row cascades and the Work leaves the population; ownership movement — the population is evaluated on current ownership, a moved Work leaves the census, and stale PENDING rows are rebound by the drain; eligibility transitions — every eligibility input is either a captured column or an assignment change. A captured change leaves the Work's generation row positive and above its resolution: durable residue, which the materializer turns into a job once the Work is eligible and admitted (§9.3). An assignment change is a new activation, whose population is covered only through its own seed and census before its admission. No event, no seed and no admission creates a job: the only creators are the materializer, the fence's binding-refusal replacement and the T2 successor (§11.3).

`work_crossref_baseline` records the immutable residual classes (`PARSER_FAILURES`, `DELETED_WORK_IDENTITY`, `DESTROYED_RELATION_TOPOLOGY`, `UNKNOWN_UNQUANTIFIED`) with counts, frozen at admission. **Recording a residual is not the same as having none**; the admission row's `evidence_reference` names this record.

### 21.4 Coverage is not a BE-06 table

Coverage remains the released `publisher_distribution_platform` with `platform = 'CROSSREF'`, `enabled` and `activation_id`. BE-06 reads it and never writes it: one coverage authority, already released and already used by the claim predicate.

---

## 22. G-6, G-7, the ledger and the authority register

G-6 is the production **lock-graph rehearsal** gate: it proves, in the production topology, that the lock graph this specification defines does not deadlock, and it records that proof in an append-only GitHub-comment ledger. G-7 is the **version-floor advance** gate. **No G-6 step contacts Crossref.** G-7 is separately authorized production work and is not authorized by this specification.

### 22.1 Two disjoint sentinels

| Envelope | First line of the comment body | Enters the ledger census? |
|---|---|---|
| Ledger record | `THOTH-BE06-LEDGER-V1` | **yes** |
| Authority register | `THOTH-BE06-AUTHORITY-REGISTER-V1` | **never** |

The two are disjoint by construction, so an authority-register comment can never be counted as a ledger record and a malformed register can never be classified as a malformed ledger record. An authority failure yields an **authorization-authority HOLD**, never a ledger-malformation classification.

### 22.2 Recognition is parse-independent

The comment body is:

````
THOTH-BE06-LEDGER-V1
```json
{ canonical record JSON }
```
````

**Outer-whitespace rule.** Leading and trailing space, tab, CR and LF of the whole body are ignored. The *first line* is the bytes from the start of the trimmed body to the first LF, with a trailing CR stripped.

**Membership test, in full: the first line is exactly `THOTH-BE06-LEDGER-V1`.** Nothing after it participates. Consequently these are all **recognised members that then fail validation**, each counted and never repaired: missing fence, truncated JSON, malformed JSON, duplicate keys, a scalar instead of an object, missing schema version, unknown record type, no recoverable `g6_attempt_id`. There is no redundant `sentinel` payload member.

**Recognition corpus (9), all required:**

| Body | Member? | Then |
|---|---|---|
| exact sentinel + valid fence | yes | validates |
| exact sentinel + truncated JSON | yes | fails at step 3, counted |
| exact sentinel + no fence at all | yes | fails at step 2, counted |
| exact sentinel + `42` in the fence | yes | fails at step 5, counted |
| exact sentinel + `{"a":1,"a":2}` | yes | fails at step 3 (duplicate key), counted |
| leading and trailing blank lines and spaces | yes | validates after the outer-whitespace rule |
| sentinel on line 2 | no | outside the ledger |
| `THOTH-BE06-LEDGER-V1 extra` on line 1 | no | outside the ledger |
| an ordinary review comment | no | outside the ledger |

The first five are exactly the cases a parse-dependent recognition rule would silently drop from the census.

### 22.3 The authority register

The authority is an **immutable, digest-pinned snapshot**. Registers are append-only documents; a new version is a new document with a new digest naming its predecessor. A register is never edited in place.

**Closed register schema:**

```
register_schema_version      json_nonneg_int, exactly 1
register_id                  uuid_lower
register_version             uint_decimal_string, 1 … 2147483647
programme                    bounded_text(64), exactly "BE-06"
predecessor_register_digest  sha256_hex or null (null only for the first version)
grants                       ordered array of grant objects
```

**Closed grant object:** `authority` `bounded_text(64)` · `grant_id` `bounded_text(128)` · `operation_class` `enum_identifier` · `role_member` `bounded_text(128)` · `scope` `bounded_text(128)` · `validity_start` `postgres_timestamp_exact_us` · `validity_end` `postgres_timestamp_exact_us` or null.

`grants` is ordered by `(role_member, grant_id)` ascending, and the order is digest-significant. One `grant_id` may appear under more than one `role_member` only when it genuinely authorises both.

**Canonicalization, digest, storage.** RFC 8785 JCS; the digest is the lower-case hex SHA-256 of those UTF-8 bytes. Storage is the same GitHub issue thread under the register sentinel; retrieval is by digest, and any store returning the exact bytes is acceptable because the digest is the identity. **A document whose bytes do not hash to the requested digest is not that register and is treated as unfetchable. Missing, unfetchable or corrupt ⇒ HOLD.** Never "assume valid", never "fall back to the current register".

**Binding and propagation.** Every ledger record carries `authorization_register_digest` in its common envelope; `ATTEMPT_OPEN` additionally carries `authorization_register_id` and `authorization_register_version`. Graph check **G12** requires every non-root record's digest to equal its predecessor's, so the digest is invariant along the whole attempt and pinned at `ATTEMPT_OPEN`. Validation step 13 resolves the bound digest and requires every named authorisation member's value to be a `grant_id` bound to **that exact `role_member`** in **that exact snapshot**, with validity covering the record's `emitted_at`.

**A later register version cannot retroactively change whether an old record was valid when emitted**, because validation never consults the current register. Revocation is therefore **prospective only**.

**G-7 dual binding.** `crossref_version_floor_audit.authorization_register_digest` is NOT NULL for floor-advance rows, and `G7_RECONCILIATION` carries it in the envelope. The two must agree; disagreement is a Class-2 condition.

### 22.4 Bounds, timing and session facts

Fixed: **quiescence 900 s, settling 600 s, absolute 1500 s**. Since 900 + 600 = 1500, the settling deadline **is** the absolute deadline. By exact integer-second arithmetic on the recorded start, `quiescence_deadline = started_at + 900 s` and `settling_deadline = started_at + 1500 s`. No rounding enters deadline creation, so all three timestamps carry identical fractional digits — a locally checkable predicate. Runtime enforcement uses a **monotonic** clock (`elapsed_seconds_from_start`), never these wall-clock values; both are recorded and a disagreement is evidence, not something to reconcile away.

Class-1: **7 sessions** default, 5 savepoint. Class-2: exactly one `OBSERVER`.

**Class-1 session identity, ten fields**, each required by the isolation predicate: `session_label` · `backend_pid` · `backend_start` · `datid` · `datname` · `usename` · `application_name` · `track_counts` (a false value makes `pgstat_report_deadlock()` early-return, hiding a deadlock) · `conf_load_time` (per-backend, which is what makes `B1_TARGETED_RELOAD` provable) · `session_replication_role` (must be `origin`; `replica` suppresses triggers, rules and FK enforcement, voiding the entire lock graph).

**Statistics and session proof, six parts:** `track_counts` enabled in every controlled backend; `stats_reset` identical between the initial and final samples; the `deadlocks` delta equals the count of `deadlock_detected`; `sessions` consistent with the declared set; `pgstat_should_report_connstat()` true only for `B_BACKEND`; zero Class-4 backends.

### 22.5 `crossref_version_floor_audit`

```sql
CREATE TABLE public.crossref_version_floor_audit (
    audit_id                      uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT public.uuid_generate_v4(),
    mutation_kind                 text NOT NULL,
    before_value                  bigint,
    after_value                   bigint,
    g6_attempt_id                 uuid,
    observation_id                uuid,
    g7_authorization_reference    text,
    authorization_register_digest text,
    actor                         text NOT NULL,
    occurred_at                   timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT current_timestamp,
    CONSTRAINT crossref_version_floor_audit_shape_check CHECK (
        CASE WHEN mutation_kind = 'ADVANCE_VERSION_FLOOR' THEN
                 before_value IS NOT NULL AND after_value IS NOT NULL
             AND after_value > before_value
             AND g6_attempt_id IS NOT NULL AND observation_id IS NOT NULL
             AND authorization_register_digest ~ '^[0-9a-f]{64}$'
             AND g7_authorization_reference ~ '[^[:space:]]'
             ELSE g6_attempt_id IS NULL AND observation_id IS NULL
              AND g7_authorization_reference IS NULL
              AND authorization_register_digest IS NULL
        END )
);
```

Plus the one-advance-per-attempt partial unique index of §17.6. `g6_attempt_id` and `observation_id` are native `uuid` so respellings collide (§17.6). The shape CHECK is two-directional and total over `mutation_kind`. This table replaces R51's `work_crossref_control_audit`, whose only mutation kind was already the floor advance and whose name referred to a control row that the frozen architecture replaced.

**Append-only and permanent.**
* A `BEFORE UPDATE OR DELETE` row trigger on `crossref_version_floor_audit` raises `CROSSREF_VERSION_FLOOR_AUDIT_APPEND_ONLY`.
* A `BEFORE TRUNCATE` statement trigger refuses `TRUNCATE` with the same code.

An audit row is the only durable record that the floor moved, and G-7's Class-2 detection depends on it (§22.12).

### 22.6 Parsing and canonicality

1. Extract the payload: after the sentinel line the body must be exactly `` ```json `` LF, canonical JSON, LF, `` ``` ``, with at most trailing whitespace.
2. Parse as I-JSON with a parser that **rejects duplicate object keys** — an explicit obligation, not a default: both Python's `json.loads` and Node's `JSON.parse` silently accept `{"a":1,"a":2}` and keep the last value.
3. Payload bytes must be **byte-identical** to their RFC 8785 JCS canonicalisation. No repair, no re-sorting, no re-serialisation.
4. `payload_digest` is the lower-case hex SHA-256 of the JCS canonicalisation with `payload_digest` removed.

### 22.7 Scalar wire types (12)

`bounded_text(n)` · `enum_identifier` · `uuid_lower` · `sha256_hex` · `github_comment_id` (exact decimal string, never a JSON number) · `uint_decimal_string` · `int_decimal_string` (declared; used by no R52 field) · `boolean` · `json_nonneg_int` (exactly three fields) · `postgres_timestamp_exact_us` · `gate_timestamp_exact_us` · `audit_timestamp_ms`.

`uint_decimal_string` is a JSON **string** matching `0|[1-9][0-9]*`, no sign, no leading zero, no whitespace, within a field-specific domain: `backend_pid` 1…2147483647 · `datid` 0…4294967295 · `deadlocks`, `sessions`, `before_value`, `after_value`, `cas_*_generation`, `configuration_generation` 0…9223372036854775807 · `class4_backend_count`, `subscription_row_count`, `census_record_total`, `write_attempt_count`, `sequence_index`, `deadlocks_delta`, `final_reread_matching_start_count`, `authorization_register_version` 0…2147483647 · `attempt_index` 1…16 · `attempt_outcome_index` 1…17 · `observation_outcome_index` 1…6 · `elapsed_seconds_from_start` 0…1500. `"banana"`, `"01"`, `"+1"`, `" 1"`, `"1.0"`, `"-1"`, `""` and the JSON number `1` are schema-invalid for all of them.

### 22.8 Shared sub-objects (6)

`bound_record_ref` (`comment_id`, `payload_digest`, `record_id`, `record_type`) · `class1_session` (the ten fields of §22.4) · `stat_sample` (`checkpoint_label`, `datid`, `datname`, `deadlocks`, `sampled_at`, `sessions`, `stats_reset` nullable, `track_counts`) · `scenario_result` (`case_id`, `deadlock_detected`, `expected_edge_class`, `observed_edge_class`, `result`, `scenario_id`, `victim_session_label` nullable) · `checkpoint_result` (`checkpoint_label`, `elapsed_seconds_from_start` nullable, `reached`, `reached_at` nullable) · `configuration_identity` (`conf_load_time`, `configuration_generation`, `parameter_manifest_digest`, `session_replication_role`, `track_counts`). All closed. `stats_reset` `null` equals only `null`.

| `record_type` | `ATTEMPT_OPEN`, `ATTEMPT_READY`, `START`, `CHECKPOINT`, `OBSERVATION_TERMINAL`, `ATTEMPT_TERMINAL`, `ATTEMPT_HOLD`, `RESTORATION_START`, `RESTORATION_TERMINAL`, `LEDGER_OUTAGE`, `OBSERVATION_RECONCILIATION`, `G7_RECONCILIATION` | 12 |
