| `mutation_kind` | `ADVANCE_VERSION_FLOOR` | 1 |
| `failed_record_type` | the 12 `record_type` values | 12 |
| `operation_class` (register) | `CONFIGURATION_WRITE`, `CONFIGURATION_RELOAD`, `CONFIGURATION_RESTORE`, `PRINCIPAL_LIFECYCLE`, `SESSION_LOCAL_SET`, `DATABASE_READ`, `CANARY_PARAMETER`, `LOCK_GRAPH_OBSERVE`, `TRANSIENT_DML`, `LEDGER_COMMENT_WRITE`, `VERSION_FLOOR_ADVANCE` | 11 |

`session_replication_role` is the only lower-case enum, mirroring PostgreSQL's spelling; the exception is stated, not inferred.

**22.9.1 Attempt outcomes (17; 16 non-success):** 1 `ATTEMPT_COMPLETED_OBSERVATION_RECORDED` · 2 `PREFLIGHT_REVALIDATION_FAILED` · 3 `RUNTIME_LOCK_SCHEMA_DRIFT` · 4 `EXECUTOR_EXPRESSION_DRIFT` · 5 `OBSERVER_EXECUTION_AUDIT_DRIFT` · 6 `PRINCIPAL_STATE_DRIFT` · 7 `SESSION_CONFIGURATION_DRIFT` · 8 `SUBSCRIPTION_EXCLUSION_VIOLATED` · 9 `MODULE_CAPABILITY_VIOLATED` · 10 `MIGRATION_LEDGER_DRIFT` · 11 `CANARY_PARAMETER_MANIFEST_DRIFT` · 12 `QUERY_DEFINITION_DRIFT` · 13 `QUIESCENCE_NOT_REACHED` · 14 `SETTLING_NOT_REACHED` · 15 `ABSOLUTE_DEADLINE_EXCEEDED` · 16 `LEDGER_START_UNCONFIRMED` · 17 `OPERATOR_ABORTED`.
**Observation outcomes (6):** 1 `OBSERVATION_COMPLETE_NO_DEADLOCK` · 2 `OBSERVATION_COMPLETE_DEADLOCK_OBSERVED` · 3 `OBSERVATION_ABORTED_TIMEOUT` · 4 `OBSERVATION_ABORTED_ISOLATION_VIOLATION` · 5 `OBSERVATION_ABORTED_OPERATOR` · 6 `OBSERVATION_INCOMPLETE_UNRECONCILED`.
Both `*_outcome_index` members must equal the index in these tables.

### 22.10 The edge inventory and scenario map

The G-6 rehearsal probes the lock graph **this specification defines**, so the edge inventory is re-derived from §10.4, §13.4 and §16.5 rather than carried forward.

| Edge | Wait-for edge probed |
|---|---|
| E1 | editorial capture: `W (FOR NO KEY UPDATE) → G` |
| E2 | editorial cross-work propagation: holder of `W(child)` → `G(parent)` (§10.6) |
| E3 | materializer: `P (FOR SHARE) → W (FOR SHARE)` |
| E4 | materializer: `W → G (FOR UPDATE)` |
| E5 | materializer: `G → J (FOR UPDATE)` and the job INSERT's RI `FOR KEY SHARE` on the held `P` and `W` |
| E6 | fence: `J → A → Q (SHARE)` for `WORK_UPSERT`, and completion's `fenced_at` precondition |
| E7 | Crossref profile suffix LO-C: `A → [Q (SHARE), WORK_UPSERT] → DOI advisory keys (deduplicated, ascending signed int4) → floor (FOR SHARE) → permit INSERT (X) → membership (M)`; the `X`-only leaves (void, report) and reconciliation's `A → X` (§10.5, §16.5) |
| E8 | two overlapping reservations at their first shared **advisory key**, including a hash-collision pair; the loser is refused `CROSSREF_PERMIT_BLOCKED` (§16.5) |
| E9 | T2 completion: `G → J → A → successor INSERT` |
| E10 | claim: lease recovery → `Q (SHARE)` → `J (FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED)`; the statement must never wait on a row |
| E11 | released coordinator: `P (FOR UPDATE) → assignments → J(PENDING) → all W of the publisher` versus any BE-06 path |
| E12 | deletion unit: publisher set → entity/`W` (FOR UPDATE) → `J` |
| E13 | deletion versus a fenced attempt: refusal without lock escalation |
| E14 | G-7: floor `FOR UPDATE` → DRAIN evaluation (an MVCC read of permits, `RESERVED` included) → floor UPDATE → audit INSERT, versus an in-flight reservation |
| E15 | released `work_relation` cascade: `W(min) → W(max)` |

**The execution gate adds no edge class.** A G-6 attempt takes `Q` only in SHARE mode. No control transition runs during it: `session_label` (§22.9) declares no control-transition session, and the session proof of §22.4 admits no backend outside the declared set. SHARE conflicts with nothing but EXCLUSIVE, so `Q` appears in E6, E7 and E10 as a step that cannot wait, and the closed enum sets, the scenario map and the 23 exercised cases are unchanged. The gate's one waiting edge, SHARE against `setWorkUpsertExecution`'s EXCLUSIVE, is proved by the specification harness (§25.20, T280–T288) and required of the implementation (§25.5). It is not a production-rehearsal edge.

**Scenario map (canonical array order).** Group W probes BE-06 paths, Group B probes released-interaction boundaries; `W_09` carries 8 cases, every other scenario carries `SINGLE`. **Exercised cases: 23 = 15 + 8.** Every observation terminal carries exactly 23 scenario results and 6 checkpoint results.

`W_01→E1`, `W_02→E2`, `W_03→E3`, `W_04→E4`, `W_05→E5`, `W_06→E6`, `W_07→E7` (LO-C: DOI keys, then the floor, then the permit row), `W_08→E9`, `W_09 cases 1–8→E8` (identical, subset, superset, one-member, multi-member, disjoint, **reversed advisory-key order**, and a **real `hashtext` collision pair** whose string order opposes its key order, §16.5), `W_10→E10`; `B_01→E11`, `B_02→E12`, `B_03→E13`, `B_04→E14`, `B_05→E15`, `B_06→E11` under the deletion unit.

Observing an edge is not a deadlock; only `deadlock_detected` counts.

### 22.11 Records, chronology, validation, graph checks and census

**Common envelope:** `authorization_register_digest` · `ledger_schema_version` (exactly 1) · `record_type` · `record_id` · `g6_attempt_id` · `observation_id` · `emitted_at` (`audit_timestamp_ms`) · `writer_identity` · `predecessor_comment_id` (null **iff** `ATTEMPT_OPEN`) · `payload_digest`.

**The twelve record schemas are closed** — no extension point, no `additionalProperties`, no vendor namespace; a future version raises `ledger_schema_version`:

* **ATTEMPT_OPEN** — `authorization_register_id`, `authorization_register_version`, `barrier_shape`, `c1_database_read_authorization_reference`, `c5_github_comment_authorization_reference`, `census_record_total`, `census_scan_complete`, `target_environment`. No `bound_predecessor`.
* **ATTEMPT_READY** — `barrier_shape`, `bound_predecessor`, the ten named digests (`applied_migration_ledger_digest`, `executor_expression_audit_digest`, `g6_canary_parameter_manifest_digest`, `module_capability_allowlist_digest`, `observation_principal_state_digest`, `observer_execution_audit_digest`, `query_definition_digest`, `runtime_lock_schema_digest`, `session_configuration_defaults_digest`, `subscription_exclusion_evidence_digest`), `snapshot_binding_procedure_version`, `subscription_row_count`, and the `c2`/`c3`/`c4` authorisations. **B1-only, all four required and all four forbidden under B2:** `b1_configuration_writer_…`, `b1_reload_admin_…`, `b1_principal_lifecycle_…`, `b1_restoration_authorization_reference`. **B2-only:** `b2_session_local_authorization_reference`. Decided from the record's **own** `barrier_shape`.
* **START** — `barrier_shape`, `bound_predecessor`, `class1_session_shape`, `class1_sessions` (ordered by `session_label` ascending), `class2_observer_session`, `started_at`, `quiescence_deadline`, `settling_deadline`, `snapshot_binding_procedure_version_at_start`, and the ten `_at_start` digests. START revalidates **eleven** identities by name.
* **CHECKPOINT** — `bound_predecessor`, `checkpoint_result`, `stat_sample`, `class4_backend_count`, `sequence_index`.
* **OBSERVATION_TERMINAL** — `bound_predecessor`, `checkpoint_results` (all six labels exactly once, ordered by elapsed then label), `scenario_results` (ordered by `scenario_id` then `case_id`), `deadlocks_delta`, `observation_outcome`, `observation_outcome_index`, `stat_sample_initial`, `stat_sample_final`, `observation_id_permanently_retired` (always true), `terminal_is_immutable` (always true).
* **ATTEMPT_TERMINAL** — `attempt_outcome`, `attempt_outcome_index`, `bound_predecessor`, `terminal_is_immutable`. Conditional: `bound_observation_terminal` **iff** outcome 1 and equal to `bound_predecessor`; `attempted_start_payload_digest`, `final_reread_matching_start_count`, `no_workload_began`, `bound_attempt_open`, `bound_attempt_ready`, `observation_id_permanently_retired` **iff** outcome 16.
* **ATTEMPT_HOLD** — `bound_predecessor`, `hold_cause`, `hold_is_immutable`, `observation_terminal_present`, `other_closing_record_present`, `restoration_required`, `workload_began`, `controlled_sessions_terminated`, `census_record_total`, `census_scan_complete`.
* **RESTORATION_START** — `barrier_shape`, `bound_predecessor`, `restoration_id`, `restoration_cause`, `restoration_authorization_reference`, `configuration_identity_expected`, `configuration_identity_observed`.
* **RESTORATION_TERMINAL** — `bound_predecessor`, `restoration_id`, `restoration_outcome`, `attempt_index`, `cas_expected_generation`, `cas_observed_generation`, `configuration_identity_restored`, `principal_revoked`, `terminal_is_immutable`.
* **LEDGER_OUTAGE** — `bound_predecessor`, `outage_branch`, `failed_record_type`, `irreversible_mutation_performed`, `outage_detected_at`, `outage_resolved_at` (nullable), `write_attempt_count`.
* **OBSERVATION_RECONCILIATION** — `bound_predecessor`, `reconciliation_kind`, `reconciliation_disposition`, `reconciliation_authorization_reference`, `census_record_total`, `census_scan_complete`, `orphan_chain_detected`, `observation_id_permanently_retired`, `bound_restoration_terminal`, `workload_began`, `terminal_is_immutable`. Conditional **iff** Case A: `bound_attempt_hold`. **iff** Case B: `bound_outcome_16_terminal`, `bound_late_start_comment_id`, `bound_late_start_payload_digest`, `attempted_start_payload_digest`.
* **G7_RECONCILIATION** — `bound_predecessor` (must be `RESTORATION_TERMINAL`), `bound_observation_terminal`, `bound_attempt_terminal`, `audit_id`, `mutation_kind`, `before_value`, `after_value`, `g7_authorization_reference`, `g7_class`, `g7_disposition`, `terminal_is_immutable`. No `G7_RECONCILIATION` may be its own predecessor.

**Chronology.**

```
ATTEMPT_OPEN → ATTEMPT_READY → START → CHECKPOINT*
             → OBSERVATION_TERMINAL → ATTEMPT_TERMINAL
             → RESTORATION_START → RESTORATION_TERMINAL → G7_RECONCILIATION
```

For the successful outcome the observation terminal must already be durable and `ATTEMPT_TERMINAL` must bind exactly it, enforced at validation step 8. **Exactly one closing record per attempt** (`ATTEMPT_TERMINAL` or `ATTEMPT_HOLD`, never both), graph check G11.

**Predecessor contract (12 rows):** `ATTEMPT_OPEN` none · `ATTEMPT_READY` ← `ATTEMPT_OPEN` · `START` ← `ATTEMPT_READY` · `CHECKPOINT` ← `START` or `CHECKPOINT` · `OBSERVATION_TERMINAL` ← `START` or `CHECKPOINT` · `ATTEMPT_TERMINAL` ← `OBSERVATION_TERMINAL` iff outcome 1, `ATTEMPT_READY` iff outcome 16, else `START` or `CHECKPOINT` · `ATTEMPT_HOLD` ← `START`, `CHECKPOINT` or `LEDGER_OUTAGE` · `RESTORATION_START` ← `ATTEMPT_TERMINAL`, `ATTEMPT_HOLD` or `LEDGER_OUTAGE` · `RESTORATION_TERMINAL` ← `RESTORATION_START` · `LEDGER_OUTAGE` ← any of the 12 · `OBSERVATION_RECONCILIATION` ← `ATTEMPT_TERMINAL`, `ATTEMPT_HOLD` or `RESTORATION_TERMINAL` · `G7_RECONCILIATION` ← `RESTORATION_TERMINAL` only.

**Case A — the exact closure sequence:**

```
START (or latest CHECKPOINT, or LEDGER_OUTAGE)
  → ATTEMPT_HOLD              hold_cause = INCOMPLETE_OBSERVATION_AFTER_PROCESS_LOSS
  → RESTORATION_START         restoration_cause = ATTEMPT_HELD
  → RESTORATION_TERMINAL      restoration_outcome = RESTORED_VERIFIED
  → OBSERVATION_RECONCILIATION  kind = CASE_A_START_WITHOUT_CLOSING_RECORD
                                disposition = CLOSED_AS_HOLD
```

Every arrow is a predecessor-contract row. The hold binds exact attempt and observation identity, the latest durable predecessor, `workload_began`, `observation_terminal_present = false`, `other_closing_record_present = false`, `controlled_sessions_terminated = true`, `restoration_required = true` and complete census evidence. **If workload may have begun the hold fails closed**: `workload_began = true` is permitted and recorded, forces `restoration_required = true`, and forbids any later success claim. The reconciliation binds the exact hold, the exact same-attempt same-observation `RESTORED_VERIFIED` restoration terminal (which must also be its own predecessor), census evidence that no terminal appeared concurrently, and the permanently retired observation ID. It never fabricates an observation terminal, never creates a successful attempt terminal and **never becomes G-7 eligible**. A resolved Case A permits a **wholly fresh** attempt with a fresh `g6_attempt_id` and a fresh `observation_id`; the old identifiers are never reused. A late observation terminal after closure is a ledger conflict reported by the census, never a silent reopening.

**Case B** — an exact late START discovered after a durable outcome-16 terminal, non-conflicting only if all eleven conjuncts hold: same retired `observation_id`; payload digest equal to the `attempted_start_payload_digest` bound into outcome 16; same `g6_attempt_id`; same OPEN identity and digest; same READY identity and digest; exactly one such START; unedited, fetchable and schema-valid; no observation terminal; restoration complete; no controlled session alive; `workload_began = false`. Then an `OBSERVATION_RECONCILIATION` with `CASE_B_LATE_START_AFTER_OUTCOME_16` / `CLOSED_AS_HOLD` is appended. It never edits outcome 16, never creates outcome 17 retrospectively, never fabricates an observation terminal and never makes G-7 eligible. A mismatching late START is `CONFLICT_UNRECONCILABLE`.

**Outcome 16** is written only after a **mandatory final reread** returns zero matching STARTs, and binds the retired `observation_id`, `attempted_start_payload_digest`, `no_workload_began = true`, `final_reread_matching_start_count = "0"` and both OPEN and READY bindings. If the exact START is visible at the reread, outcome 16 **must not** be written and the hold path is taken. `LATE_START_RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED` is a derived census condition, never a disposition.

**Validation order.** Steps 1–9 are schema-local, 10–13 cross-record; no schema-local step reads an unvalidated predecessor. 1 recognise (sentinel only) · 2 extract the fenced payload · 3 parse as I-JSON rejecting duplicate keys · 4 compare payload bytes to their JCS canonicalisation · 5 validate the common envelope member set · 6 validate `record_type` · 7 validate the record-specific member set and every scalar against type, grammar, domain and enum · 8 evaluate every local cross-field predicate, including B1/B2 from this record's own `barrier_shape` and every semantic array ordering · 9 recompute `payload_digest` · 10 resolve `bound_predecessor` and auxiliary refs, requiring existence, validity and matching identity/digest · 11 require `bound_predecessor.comment_id == predecessor_comment_id` (auxiliary refs exempt) · 12 predecessor contract, graph checks, the eleven named START↔READY revalidations, barrier-shape equality, Case-A/Case-B rules and G-7 eligibility · 13 authority-snapshot cross-check against the pinned register.

**Graph checks (15 + the unassignable rule).** G1 predecessor exists · G2 predecessor valid · G3 contract row holds · G4 `predecessor_comment_id` matches · G5 no cycle · G6 exactly one `ATTEMPT_OPEN` per `g6_attempt_id` · G7 `g6_attempt_id` and `observation_id` constant along a chain · G8 no two records of the same type share a predecessor · G9 `barrier_shape` equal across OPEN→READY→START · G10 terminal immutability · G11 exactly one closing record per attempt · G12 `authorization_register_digest` invariant along the chain and equal to the root's · G13 the eleven named START↔READY revalidations · G14 Case-A closure binds an `ATTEMPT_HOLD` and a same-attempt same-observation `RESTORED_VERIFIED` restoration terminal that is also its own predecessor · G15 G-7 eligibility. **Unassignable rule:** a recognised record that cannot be assigned to any chain is reported as unassignable — counted, never discarded, never attached to the nearest plausible chain.

**Closed-world census.** Enumerates **every recognised record of every one of the twelve types**, not only roots, so deleting a root does not hide its chain. **Twelve derived conditions:** unassignable record · orphaned chain · missing predecessor · digest mismatch · non-canonical body · duplicate `record_id` · fork · durable START with no closing record (Case A) · late START after a durable outcome-16 terminal (Case B) · durable floor-advance audit row with no `G7_RECONCILIATION` (Class 2) · attempt with two closing records · bound authorization-register digest unfetchable or inconsistent along a chain.

### 22.12 G-7

**Eligibility** requires all three exact records: an immutable `OBSERVATION_TERMINAL` with `observation_outcome = OBSERVATION_COMPLETE_NO_DEADLOCK` **and** `deadlocks_delta = "0"`; an immutable `ATTEMPT_TERMINAL` with outcome 1, written after it and binding exactly it; and a `RESTORATION_TERMINAL` with `restoration_outcome ∈ {RESTORED_VERIFIED, RESTORATION_NOT_REQUIRED}`. Nothing else qualifies — not deadlock-observed, timeout, isolation violation, operator abort, incomplete observation, `ATTEMPT_HOLD`, or either reconciliation kind.

**Order** (and note that step 0 is the floor lock of §17.5, which precedes every evaluation):

```
floor FOR UPDATE → census → Class-2 cross-check → Class-1B consumption → Class-1A eligibility → mutation → G7_RECONCILIATION
```

An audit row with no matching ledger record yields **Class 2** and wins immediately. Any prior `ADVANCE_VERSION_FLOOR` row for this `g6_attempt_id` yields **Class 1B** and stops. Only then are eligibility, runtime identity revalidation, authorisation against the pinned register, DRAIN, blocking-permit absence and floor binding evaluated; failures there are **Class 1A**, because the candidate is now proven unconsumed. **A consumed attempt can never be Class 1A**, from the order alone.

* **Class 1A**: proven unconsumed; no mutation; no audit row; the floor after refusal is byte-identical to the floor this invocation observed immediately before refusing. The universal claim "Class 1A floor = the authorisation's old value" stays withdrawn: in the subcase where the failure *is* a floor mismatch, both `observed_floor` and `authorization_bound_old_floor` are recorded and equality is not claimed.
* **Class 1B**: a prior audit row exists; the candidate is **permanently consumed**; no mutation, no new row, floor unchanged; the same `g6_attempt_id` is never retryable or re-authorisable; a fresh G-6 attempt is required. The partial unique index enforces this a second time.
* **Class 2**: an audit row with no `G7_RECONCILIATION`; post-commit; the floor **is** changed; HOLD until reconciled.

### 22.13 Restoration and ledger outage

CAS on `configuration_generation`, bounded retries with a recorded `attempt_index`, and the terminal records the CAS expected/observed pair so a lost update is visible. `RESTORED_VERIFIED` additionally requires CAS agreement and `principal_revoked = true`. The rollback envelope opens **before** any write and before any principal mutation. **Branch A** — ledger unavailable before any irreversible mutation: halt safely, record on recovery. **Branch B** — unavailable after: HOLD with `irreversible_mutation_performed = true` and no further mutation until reconciled. Branch and flag are locked together by a cross-field predicate.

### 22.14 The normative fixture corpus

The corpus is normative. It is carried forward from R51 in **structure, count and meaning**, and must be regenerated by the implementation's harness against this specification's enums before the ledger tests can pass.

**Unchanged and not to be regenerated:** the two authorization register documents and their digests — register **v3** `f99bbc866c7dfce7650318d5bc489861a31626f67c60319c16fc756edab7d4ea` and register **v4** `dcbfd9caf37f44d893f0cc2b1313dff5cf720a46f13bc649a77d0e5b288fe382`. This specification changes no register field, no `role_member` name and no `operation_class` member, so both documents remain byte-valid and both digests remain correct. Regenerating them would be gratuitous.

**Must be regenerated, with the reason:** every ledger fixture in all four lineages, because `edge_class` gains `E14` and `E15` (16 members, was 15), the scenario→edge map is re-derived (§22.10), and `session_label` names the reservation and finalisation paths (`C1_OVERLAPPING_RESERVER`, `C1_PERMIT_FINALISER`). Those enums appear in `scenario_results` (`OBSERVATION_TERMINAL`) and `class1_sessions` (`START`), and a payload change propagates through `payload_digest` into every successor's `bound_predecessor`. No other cause of regeneration exists.

**Required corpus, unchanged in shape:**

| Lineage | Records | What it proves |
|---|---|---|
| L1 successful, G-7 qualified (`B1_TARGETED_RELOAD`, seven-session) | 9 | the full chain through `G7_RECONCILIATION` |
| L2 deadlock observed, G-7 disqualified (`B2_SESSION_LOCAL`, five-session savepoint) | 7 | a deadlock-observed chain never reaches mutation |
| L3 outcome 16 then Case B (`B1_GLOBAL_RELOAD`) | 6 | the mandatory final reread and late-START reconciliation |
| L4 ledger outage, incomplete observation, Case A (`B2_SESSION_LOCAL`) | 8 | hold → restoration → Case-A closure |
| **total** | **30** | all twelve record types covered |

**Four independent evidence classes, reported separately per lineage:** Class 1 BE-06 schema validation (steps 4–9); Class 2 the fifteen graph checks plus the unassignable rule; Class 3 the authority cross-check at step 13 against the pinned register; Class 4 JCS/digest agreement between **two independent RFC 8785 implementations** from different authors (for example `canonicalize` 2.1.0 in JavaScript and `rfc8785` 0.1.4 in Python), each first checked against RFC 8785's own vectors, and one of them fed the same objects with keys reverse-sorted to force independent ordering. **Class 4 is never offered as evidence of Class 1**: RFC 8785 preserves array order, so agreeing implementations say nothing about whether an array satisfies a BE-06 ordering rule.

**Negative corpus: 27**, each a byte-exact mutation of a named positive fixture, each rejected by the stated rule at the stated step, with the digest recomputed wherever the mutation is semantic so the fixture is rejected on its meaning rather than its hash:

| # | Mutation class | Rejected by | Step |
|---|---|---|---|
| N01–N04 | unknown member; deleted required member; sub-object wrapped in an array; numeric field as JSON `null` | closed-schema and wire-type rules | 7 |
| N05 | swap `class1_sessions[0]` and `[1]`, digest recomputed | `class1_sessions` not ordered by `session_label` | 8 |
| N06 | move a `scenario_results` element to index 0, digest recomputed | not ordered by `(scenario_id, case_id)` | 8 |
| N07 | duplicate a `scenario_result` key | duplicate key | 8 |
| N08 | change outcome while leaving `bound_observation_terminal` | closed-schema unknown member | 7 |
| N09 | add a B1-only member to a B2 record | B1 member on a B2 shape | 7 |
| N10 | mismatch `predecessor_comment_id` | `bound_predecessor.comment_id` mismatch | 8 |
| N11 | swap the `c1_database_read_…` and `c5_github_comment_…` values | authority: the cited grant is not for that `role_member` in the bound register | **13** |
| N12 | swap two `_at_start` digests | named revalidation failed | 12 |
| N13 | `"banana"`, `"01"`, `"+1"`, `" 1"`, `"1.0"`, `"-1"`, `""` and JSON `0` in a `uint_decimal_string` | grammar | 7 |
| N14 | rebind a `G7_RECONCILIATION` to the deadlock lineage | G-7 eligibility | 12 |
| N15 | repoint a success terminal's predecessor to a CHECKPOINT | predecessor must be `OBSERVATION_TERMINAL` | 8 |
| N16 | outcome 16 with `final_reread_matching_start_count = "1"` | requires zero | 8 |
| N17 | Case-B late-START digest mismatch | must equal the bound attempted-START digest | 8 |
| N18 | `payload_digest` zeroed, not recomputed | digest mismatch | 9 |
| N19 | register digest changed to v4 mid-chain | G12 drift | 12 |
| N20 | register digest zeroed | unknown/unfetchable ⇒ HOLD | 13 |
| N21 | cite a grant valid only in v4 | not a grant for that role in the bound register | 13 |
| N22 | Case-A binds another attempt's restoration terminal | G14 same-attempt/same-observation | 12 |
| N23 | Case-A `bound_attempt_hold` points at the START | must bind its `ATTEMPT_HOLD` | 8 |
| N24–N27 | hold with `other_closing_record_present`; `controlled_sessions_terminated = false`; `observation_terminal_present = true`; `census_scan_complete = false` | hold cross-field predicates | 8 |

**27 of 27 must be rejected, each by the rule and at the step stated.** A conforming validator reports that rule, not merely "digest mismatch". N05 and N06 are the required demonstration that a syntactically valid, correctly digested, perfectly canonical record can still fail on array ordering; N19–N21 are the authority corpus; N22–N27 are the Case-A and hold corpus.

### 22.15 The complete comment body

One fixture must be reproduced in full in the implementation report and asserted to be exactly four lines: one sentinel, one opening fence, one complete canonical payload, one closing fence — and it must pass recognition, extraction, duplicate-key-safe parse, canonical-byte comparison, schema validation, digest verification and the authority check.

### 22.16 Known governance residual

The ledger lives in GitHub comments and a sufficiently privileged actor can delete them. The census detects partial erasure and orphaned chains; it **cannot** detect erasure of everything, and this specification does not claim otherwise. Accepting that residual is a CTO decision (§31).

---

## 23. Migration

### 23.1 Operative migration identities — `v1.10.0`

The last existing migration directory on `master`, `develop` and `feature/publisher-services-v1-10` is `20260814_v1.7.0`. The released crate version is `1.8.0` (`Cargo.toml:3`), so `v1.8.0` shipped with no new migration. The repository's convention is `YYYYMMDD_vX.Y.Z`: the suffix names the release line the migration ships in, and consecutive dates are used when one change needs more than one directory.

**The two operative directories, fixed by #903 and #848 comment `5619637336`; their Diesel versions are reserved repository-wide by #903 comment `5621256877`, #766 comment `5621260046` and #848 comment `5621263642`:**

```
thoth-api/migrations/20260910_v1.10.0/        Migration 1 (§23.2)
thoth-api/migrations/20260911_v1.10.0/        Migration 2 (§23.3)
```

They are control-plane identities. They are fixed rather than re-derived at implementation time, and the date prefix orders migrations; it does not record when a commit is authored. BE-06 never uses the historical names of §1.2:
* `20260904_v1.9.0` and `20260905_v1.9.0` — rejected, owned by Metrics;
* `20260910_v1.9.0` and `20260911_v1.9.0` — the withdrawn reservation. Their later release to the Metrics/shared namespace is superseded: they alias BE-06's Diesel versions, so no programme may create them.

**The Diesel identity.** Diesel's version for a migration directory is its name up to the first underscore, with `-` removed (`migrations_internals` 2.3.0, `src/lib.rs:71-72`). BE-06's versions are therefore `20260910` and `20260911`, and the `_v1.10.0` suffix is ledger-neutral.
* `thoth-api/src/db.rs` embeds the migrations directory with `embed_migrations!()` and applies it with `run_pending_migrations` (`db.rs:10`, `:22`).
* `MigrationHarness::pending_migrations` in `diesel_migrations` 2.3.2 (`src/migration_harness.rs:111`) collects the embedded migrations into a map keyed by version (`:120`).
* Two directories sharing a version therefore collapse into one entry. One of them is silently never run — on an empty database as much as on a migrated one — and nothing reports the duplicate.
* The `embed_migrations!` macro sorts directories by path and checks only that each name yields a version (`migrations_macros` 2.3.0, `src/embed_migrations.rs:31`, `:44`), so nothing detects the duplicate at compile time either.

**Executed in the release/topology revision**, against exactly the locked crates, on PostgreSQL 17.10 on a disposable instance. The probe embedded `20260910_v1.9.0`, `20260910_v1.10.0` and `20260911_v1.10.0`:
* **On a fresh database,** Diesel listed two pending migrations and ran `20260910_v1.9.0` and `20260911_v1.10.0`. `20260910_v1.10.0` never ran, and nothing reported it. The embedded order is by path, so `v1.10.0` sorts before `v1.9.0`, and the later entry wins the map.
* **With version `20260910` already in the ledger**, as a deployed Metrics `v1.9.0` would leave it, only `20260911_v1.10.0` ran.

For BE-06 the lost migration is Migration 1. Migration 2 would then fail on enum labels Migration 1 never added — an error whose real cause Diesel never reports.

What BE-06 owns is the version pair, not merely the two paths. This executed evidence is why #903 comment `5621256877`, #766 comment `5621260046` and #848 comment `5621263642` reserve the version pair repository-wide. That reservation is controlling authority, not a recommendation.

**Verified unconsumed on 2026-09-10, for the release/topology revision and again for the Diesel-version reservation correction** (§1.2): no directory named `20260910_*` or `20260911_*` exists on any of the 75 live remote heads, and the newest dated migration on any head is `20260909_v1.9.0`.

**Rules:**
* `thoth-api/src/db.rs` must be touched to rebuild `embed_migrations!`.
* **The Diesel versions `20260910` and `20260911` are BE-06's, reserved repository-wide** (#903 `5621256877`; #766 `5621260046`; #848 `5621263642`). While the reservation is current, no other programme or branch in `thoth-pub/thoth` — Metrics included — may create a migration directory whose name begins `20260910_` or `20260911_`, whatever follows the underscore, or any other directory whose derived version is `20260910` or `20260911`. `20260910_v1.9.0` and `20260911_v1.9.0` are therefore prohibited.
* **Uniqueness is judged on the derived Diesel version, never on the path.** Every check derives each directory's version exactly as `migrations_internals::version_from_string` does — the text before the first underscore, with `-` removed — and compares versions, not names. Every directory under `thoth-api/migrations/` has a distinct Diesel version, and a test asserts it (§25.15).
* **Covered lines:** `master`, `develop`, `feature/publisher-services-v1-10`, `feature/metrics`, and every other programme or integration line, with its task branches, whose content can reach the future integration or release.
* **The check fails closed.** At every preflight — this document's and each later authorization's — and at gates 1–8 of §27.3 (every one but the Metrics-owned 6a), a directory other than BE-06's two with version `20260910` or `20260911`, on any covered line or in the tree the gate binds, is the stop state `BLOCKED - RESERVED BE-06 DIESEL MIGRATION VERSION HAS BEEN CONSUMED`. At gates 9 and 10 the same state follows if the production Diesel ledger already records either version before BE-06's migrations run. BE-06 progresses no further, and the breach of the reservation goes to the control plane. It is resolved only by a new control-plane decision — never by a silent rename, and never by an implementer choosing new dates.
* **Metrics** does not wait for BE-06, and receives no constraint from this specification beyond the two reserved version keys (§27.1).
* Diesel applies pending migrations in version order, and does not refuse a version older than one already applied. If another line's migrations dated after `20260911` are applied first, BE-06's two migrations still run, out of order. That is safe only because BE-06's migrations depend on no other programme's schema, and no other programme's migration depends on BE-06's. Gate 6b re-verifies both halves of that condition.

### 23.2 Why two directories

`origin/master` declares `CREATE TYPE public.distribution_job_kind AS ENUM ('PUBLISHER_BACK_CATALOGUE')` (`20260814_v1.7.0/up.sql:11-13`). PostgreSQL 17 forbids using a value added by `ALTER TYPE … ADD VALUE` before that transaction commits. Diesel runs each migration in its own transaction by default, and this repository has **no `metadata.toml` anywhere under `thoth-api/migrations/`**. `run_in_transaction = false` was considered and rejected: it would require a `metadata.toml` the repository has never had, admit statement-level partial-failure states with no transactional rollback, and need bespoke retry semantics — all to avoid one extra directory.

**Migration 1 — `20260910_v1.10.0/up.sql`** (§23.1), enum `ADD VALUE` only, three statements:

```sql
ALTER TYPE public.distribution_job_kind                ADD VALUE 'WORK_UPSERT';
ALTER TYPE public.distribution_job_cancellation_reason ADD VALUE 'BINDING_SUPERSEDED';
ALTER TYPE public.distribution_job_cancellation_reason ADD VALUE 'WORK_DELETED';
```

`down.sql` is a **documented no-op**: PostgreSQL provides no `ALTER TYPE … DROP VALUE`, and the labels are inert until Migration 2 creates anything that uses them. The no-op is written in the file, not implied.

### 23.3 Migration 2 — `20260911_v1.10.0/`

One transaction, in this order:

1. **Functions that read no BE-06 type, column or table**, created first with their invariant assertions executed immediately, before anything depends on them. Every function body schema-qualifies its callees (`public.…`), so behaviour does not depend on `search_path`. Executed: an unqualified callee broke `crossref_ts_now()` under a different `search_path`.
   * `crossref_canonical_doi(text)` (§16.3);
   * `crossref_ts_encode`, `crossref_ts_decode`, `crossref_ts_next`, `crossref_ts_now`, `crossref_allocate_timestamp` (§17.2, §17.3);
   * `crossref_roots(uuid)`, `crossref_deposit_membership(uuid)`, `crossref_doi_set_digest(text[])` (§8.4, §14.3, §16.3).
2. **New enum types**, the six of §18.7.
3. **New columns** on `distribution_job` and `distribution_job_attempt` (§18.1, §18.2).
4. **The FK replacement** on `distribution_job.work_id` (§18.1). It takes `SHARE ROW EXCLUSIVE` on `distribution_job` and on `work` for the duration of the validation scan. No released row sets `work_id`, so the scan is trivial. This is the same lock class BE-04's own migration documented for adding this key.
5. **New CHECK constraints, foreign keys and guard triggers** on the released tables.
6. **New tables**, the nine of §18.9, `work_upsert_capture_queue` among them (§18.3a). `work_upsert_generation.work_id` carries no foreign key (§18.3).
7. **Indexes**, including the four new partial indexes (§18.9); then the functions that read BE-06 types, columns or tables, with their assertions: `crossref_is_blocking_write_permit(...)`, `crossref_blocking_write_permit_count()`, `crossref_is_drained()` (§16.9) and `work_upsert_resolution(uuid, distribution_platform)` (§9.1). None can come before the objects it reads: `crossref_is_blocking_write_permit` takes two of step 2's enum types, and PostgreSQL validates a `LANGUAGE sql` body at `CREATE FUNCTION`, so a function over the new columns or tables cannot precede them. Executed: step 1 as it previously listed them failed at the first, `type public.crossref_write_permit_state does not exist` (§25.21, T294).
8. **The 16 capture triggers** (§8.4), **the capture flush function and its `DEFERRABLE INITIALLY DEFERRED` constraint trigger** on `work_upsert_capture_queue` (§8.6), and the profile's root-propagation function, created **last** among the generic objects. The flush function is created before its trigger, and both after the queue table of step 6 and the generation table they write.
9. **The generic constraint triggers of §11.4** and the twelve Crossref profile triggers of §18.9.
10. **Seed rows**: `work_upsert_control ('CROSSREF', false, false)` and `work_crossref_version_floor (true, 0)`.

`down.sql` reverses 9→2, drops every BE-06 object — the capture triggers, the flush trigger and function, and the capture queue with the generation table among them — restores `distribution_job_work_id_fkey` to `ON DELETE CASCADE`, and **leaves the Migration 1 labels in place**. Dropping the profile tables requires dropping their `TRUNCATE` and permanence guards first, in the same transaction. That is the intended behaviour: `down.sql` is a stage-1 rollback only (§23.5).

**Assertions executed at migration time**, each a hard failure:
* `crossref_canonical_doi` is total and exact over the released grammar corpus of §16.3, the same 40 values asserted in SQL.
* `crossref_ts_next` returns a valid 17-digit instant exactly one millisecond later for all 13 boundary vectors of §17.2.
* `crossref_ts_decode` refuses all six non-encodings of §17.2, and returns NULL, without raising, for `99991231235960000` and `99991231240000000`.
* `crossref_ts_next(99991231235959999)` and `crossref_ts_encode(TIMESTAMPTZ '10000-01-01 00:00:00+00')` both raise `CROSSREF_TIMESTAMP_OVERFLOW`.
* `crossref_is_blocking_write_permit('AUTHORIZED', NULL)` and `('RESERVED', NULL)` are both `true`.
* `work_upsert_control` has exactly one row, `('CROSSREF', false, false)`, and the floor is exactly `0`.

**Data rewrite.** None. Every added column is nullable or has a default that PostgreSQL 17 stores as a catalog default, so no table is rewritten.

**Backfill.** None. Generation rows are created lazily by capture, by the seed and by the zero-row witness rule; no historical row is fabricated.

### 23.4 Migration tests

```
up M1 → up M2 → down M2 → up M2
up all → down newest → up newest
```

Both required green, plus: after `down M2` the three enum labels still exist and no BE-06 object does; a single-transaction variant is demonstrated to fail with PostgreSQL's "unsafe use of new value" error; `SELECT diesel_manage_updated_at` behaviour on `distribution_job` is unchanged; and **the two migration directory names, and the uniqueness of every Diesel version in the tree, are asserted by an exact-path test** (§25.15).

Executed again for this pass, against the released schema, with the Alternative A Migration 2 (§25.23, T331): `up M1 → up M2 → down M2 → up M2 → down M2` green; after each `down M2` the catalog is **exactly** the released baseline — 60 tables, 57 user triggers, 173 indexes, 30 functions, `distribution_job_work_id_fkey` back to `ON DELETE CASCADE` — no BE-06 object remains, and the three Migration 1 labels do. Migrated, the same database carries 69 tables, 91 user triggers, 192 indexes and 57 functions, which is §18.9's inventory.

Executed for the fourth review-correction pass against the released schema (§25.21, T294): `up M1 → up M2 → down M2 → up M2` green; after `down M2` the three labels remain, `distribution_job_work_id_fkey` is `ON DELETE CASCADE` again, and the released 60 tables, 173 indexes and 57 triggers return exactly; the single-transaction variant refused with `unsafe use of new value`. `up all → down newest → up newest` needs Diesel's `revert_last_migration` against the implementation's own tree, and remains an implementation test. **The compatibility window is a migration test too**: §23.6's differential and sequence are required green at gate 9, against the exact release then in production.

### 23.5 Rollback, by the state reached

Neither binary's `thoth migrate --revert` is ever a BE-06 rollback: it is Diesel's `revert_all_migrations`, not a one-step revert. Executed on disposable copies of a migrated database (§25.21, T302): the pre-BE-06 binary refused at once — `Unable to find migration version 20260911 to revert` — and changed nothing; the candidate binary reverted Migration 2, Migration 1 and every released migration after the first, and stopped only when the first's own `down.sql` failed on data. Re-executed for this pass against the Alternative A schema (§25.23, T330): the pre-BE-06 binary again refused and changed nothing, leaving all 69 tables; the candidate binary again reverted down to the first migration and stopped on data, leaving 55 tables — the capture queue is dropped with every other BE-06 object.

| State reached | Rollback |
|---|---|
| the `v1.10.0` release artifact exists; migrations not run (before gate 10) | none needed: nothing ran, and the backend is still the pre-BE-06 release |
| Migration 1 and Migration 2 applied; the pre-BE-06 backend still serving (after gate 10, before gate 11) | **do not deploy.** The old release keeps serving on the migrated schema, which §23.6 proves compatible, so nothing must be undone to keep production correct, and the only BE-06 rows are generation rows. If the schema itself must go, the only means is Migration 2's `down.sql`, run as a separately authorized, targeted DBA act while the old backend serves: it drops every BE-06 object — the generation rows with their table — restores `distribution_job_work_id_fkey` to `ON DELETE CASCADE`, and leaves Migration 1's labels, which no row uses and the released code never reads (§23.2). The act removes `20260911` from the Diesel ledger with it, so that a later `thoth migrate` runs Migration 2 again. Nothing external happened. |
| the `v1.10.0` backend deployed; capture not enabled (after gate 11, before gate 12) | redeploy the previous release. It is safe while no row carries a Migration 1 label, which the released code cannot read (§23.6, T300) and nothing writes before the first admission's drain. This is checked, not assumed, immediately before the redeploy: `SELECT count(*) FROM distribution_job WHERE kind = 'WORK_UPSERT' OR cancellation_reason IN ('BINDING_SUPERSEDED', 'WORK_DELETED')` must return 0. The schema stays, and Migration 2's `down.sql` remains available as in the row above. |
| capture enabled, no job ever created (gates 12 and 13, before the first admission's drain) | the same redeploy, on the same checked precondition. Capture stays on, because it is monotone (§21.1), and generations keep advancing under either binary. `down M2` drops the generation ledger. **This discards durable source obligations**: after a re-`up`, every affected Work must be re-seeded. It is safe for provider state but not free, needs the separate authorization of the second row, and the runbook must say so. |
| jobs and permits exist | **`down.sql` is not a safe rollback.** Permits are provider evidence and job rows are lifecycle evidence; dropping them destroys the record of what may have reached Crossref. The supported rollback is `setWorkUpsertExecution(false)` — an operational pause that keeps every row, and that returns only after every in-flight claim, reservation and finalisation has committed, so no first-effect authorization follows its return (§21.1) — plus reconciliation of any blocking permit and voiding of any abandoned reservation. Redeploying the previous release is no longer a rollback once any `WORK_UPSERT` row exists, because the released code cannot read one (§23.6, T300). A destructive rollback at this stage requires an explicit CTO decision and an evidence export first. |
| after the G-7 floor advance | **no rollback.** The floor is monotone by trigger and its domain admits no lower value; §22.12's ordering is what makes this acceptable. |

Production migration authorization (gate 9), production migration (gate 10) and backend deployment (gate 11) are separately authorized acts (§27.3), and each row above names the gate it follows.

### 23.6 The compatibility window: the pre-BE-06 release over the migrated schema

**Why the window exists.** The release image is `ENTRYPOINT ["/thoth"]` with `CMD ["init"]` (`Dockerfile`, identical at `v1.8.0`, at `395cc16a` and at `feature/metrics` `9feddcee`), and `init` runs `run_migrations()` — every pending migration — and then starts the GraphQL API (`src/bin/thoth.rs`, `src/bin/commands/mod.rs`). The production GraphQL service does not override that command. A bounded, read-only inspection of the production stack in `thoth-pub/infrastructure` at `582379fc6382c9a3a2822ce45ef0f1eb4718ac5e` found its GraphQL API service given an image (`ghcr.io/thoth-pub/thoth`) and a version (`1.8.0`) and no command parameter, while the same stack gives the export API service two command parameters. A `v1.10.0` backend deployed onto an unmigrated database would therefore run Migration 1 and Migration 2 itself, at startup. Executed: the candidate binary's `init`, on a database only the `v1.8.0` binary had migrated, added `20260910` and `20260911` to the ledger before it served (§25.21, T301). Deployment cannot precede a separately authorized migration, so the migration comes first, and from it until the deployment the pre-BE-06 release serves on the migrated schema.

**The mechanism is the existing one.** Gate 10 runs the `v1.10.0` release artifact's own `thoth migrate` — the release image with its command set to `migrate`, the database given by `-D/--database-url` or `DATABASE_URL` — once, as a one-off task against the production database, while the backend service keeps running the previous release. `migrate` calls `run_pending_migrations` and exits; it starts no server. It applies exactly the pending migrations, in version order, each in its own transaction (§23.2), and records each in the Diesel ledger. Nothing else is introduced: no deployment system, migration service, sidecar or bootstrap container. Running a service's image with an overridden command is an existing capability of the production stack, which already overrides the export API's. `thoth migrate --revert` is prohibited (§23.5). After the run, gate 10's exit check records that the ledger holds `20260910` and `20260911`, that `work_upsert_control` is exactly `('CROSSREF', false, false)`, that the floor is `0`, and that no BE-06 row other than a generation row exists. The `v1.10.0` backend deployed at gate 11 then runs `init`, whose `run_migrations()` finds nothing pending. Executed (T295, T299).

**The compatibility claim, exactly.** Over the migrated schema, the pre-BE-06 release behaves as it does over its own schema, with exactly **two** differences:
1. every committed editorial transaction writes its own `work_upsert_capture_queue` rows and, at its completion, its flush inserts, advances or deletes `work_upsert_generation` rows (§8.6, §21.1). No queue row survives the transaction;
2. the released BE-04 tests that assert BE-04's exact catalog — its enum labels, its named constraints, its triggers on `distribution_job`, and `schema.rs`'s column lists — see Migration 1's labels and Migration 2's columns, constraints and triggers, and fail. They test the schema's shape, not the released behaviour, and pass again over the released schema.

**The third difference the fourth review-correction candidate had to claim — a deletion/capture `40P01` residual — is gone.** It was the reason #848 comment `5637606889` kept R52B-33 open. Under the Alternative A amendment no BE-06-introduced `40P01` class remains: every schedule that produced one on that candidate commits here, exactly as on the released schema (§10.6, §25.22, §25.23).

Each part of that claim was executed again for this pass, against the released `v1.8.0` binary and its full test suite, over the **Alternative A** schema migrated by the candidate binary's own `thoth migrate` (§25.23, T329, T330). The fourth review-correction pass's evidence over the withdrawn schema is kept in §25.21 for comparison. What this pass observed:

* **The suite.** Run twice over the Alternative A schema: 1,377 passed / 16 failed / 9 ignored, then **1,378 passed / 15 failed / 9 ignored**. On a released schema, on the same machine under the same load: **1,382 passed / 11 failed / 9 ignored**. The difference between the two schemas is **exactly the four BE-04 catalog-shape tests** of difference 2; the other eleven failures fail on the released schema too and are the local temp-cluster migration tests. The sixteenth is not a schema effect. `graphql::dataloader::failure_tests::explicit_thoth_backend_failure_preserves_extensions_without_serde_clone` compares two GraphQL errors produced by a deliberately unreachable connection pool, and the two differ only in whether `r2d2` had attached libpq's connection-refused detail before its timeout elapsed — a load-dependent race in the released test itself, over a code path that touches no captured table. In isolation it passed six runs of six on the Alternative A schema and six of six on the released schema, and a **second full-suite run over the Alternative A schema gave 1,378 passed and 15 failed**: the withdrawn candidate's fifteen exactly, with no sixteenth.
* **No deadlock and no tripwire.** No `40P01` anywhere in the run, and the instrumentation of §25.23 refused nothing.
* **Generation writes.** 2,798 inserts and 833 advances, every one for an existing Work; 7 deletions, every one after its Work was gone; no orphaned row.
* **Queue writes.** 6,192 inserts and 6,192 deletes, with no transaction leaving one behind and no row left in the table.
* **Nothing else.** No job, target, attempt, permit, membership, admission, baseline, control, floor or audit row, and no BE-06 column of a released row.

The earlier pass's evidence, over its own schema:
* **Startup.** The released `thoth migrate` over the migrated schema exits `0` with the ledger unchanged, and the released `init`, restarted, serves. Diesel's `run_pending_migrations` runs the embedded migrations missing from the ledger and ignores applied versions it does not embed (T295, T299). That covers every restart, scale-out or crash recovery of the old service inside the window.
* **BE-04.** Every added column is nullable and no released statement names it. Every added CHECK is conditional on `kind = 'WORK_UPSERT'` and holds vacuously for `PUBLISHER_BACK_CATALOGUE`. The new unique index is partial on `WORK_UPSERT`, and the target-set trigger ignores every other kind (§11.4). The `work_id` foreign key's change to `ON DELETE SET NULL` acts on no released row, because `distribution_job_back_catalogue_work_check` keeps `work_id` NULL on every back-catalogue job. Executed: creation through the released coordinator, claim, completion, retryable and terminal failure, administrative cancellation, lease expiry and recovery, and every read that deserializes `DistributionJob`, its targets and its attempts — including a job claimed before Migration 2 and completed after it with its original token (T296, T299).
* **Enum labels — the window's hard boundary.** The released code's `DbEnum` mapping refuses a row carrying a label it does not know. Executed: one `WORK_UPSERT` job made its read of `distribution_job` fail with `Unrecognized enum variant: 'WORK_UPSERT'`, one `BINDING_SUPERSEDED` reason likewise, and removing the row restored the read (T300). Nothing writes such a row before the first admission's drain, and no released path writes one at all, so the window holds. The same fact is the precondition of §23.5's redeploy rollback.
* **Capture under released editorial operations.** Every released editorial mutation commits, and writes nothing BE-06 owns but generation rows: no job, target, attempt, permit, membership, admission, baseline, control, floor or audit row, and no BE-06 column of a released row. An instrumentation trigger on each of them refused any such write, and none fired (T296, T299). Direct Work edits and child and relation edits alike: 2,798 generation inserts, 1,025 bumps and 7 cascade deletions over the released suite.
* **Deletion.** Work, Imprint and Publisher deletions commit, their generation rows cascade, and no generation row outlives its Work (T296, T299). Before any `WORK_UPSERT` job exists there is nothing for §13.3's lifecycle to retire or fence, and the `SET NULL` acts on no row. Once such jobs exist — after admission, under the `v1.10.0` backend — deletion runs §13.4's protocol, and the pre-BE-06 release is neither in service nor redeployable (§23.5).
* **No new code before the commit.** Migration 2 requires no application code. It ran to commit with the released server serving throughout, and the released server answered before and after it (T299).

**Re-run at gate 9, not carried forward.** This evidence was taken against `v1.8.0`, the release the production stack names at `582379fc`. By gate 9 production will run a later release — Metrics `v1.9.0` at the least — with its own migrations; at `feature/metrics` `9feddcee` its `Dockerfile` and `thoth-api/src/db.rs` are those of `v1.8.0`, and its CLI is that of `395cc16a`. Gate 9 therefore re-runs the differential and the sequence of T296 and T299 against the exact release then in production and the exact `v1.10.0` release artifact, on a copy of production's schema, and fails closed on any difference beyond the three above.

The three `delete` overrides replace the `delete` method the released `crud_methods!` macro generates for those entities (`work/crud.rs:581`, `imprint/crud.rs:127`, `publisher/crud.rs:126`); every other method of the macro is retained unchanged.

**`thoth-export-server/src/record.rs` and `thoth-export-server/src/xml/mod.rs` are deliberately not in the list.**
* The prepared handler never constructs a `MetadataRecord`.
* `generate_prepared` builds its own emitter and reuses the existing declaration constant, so no trait signature changes.
* The seam was applied to a scratch copy of the released tree with neither file touched. It compiled, and all ten export tests passed (§15.3, §25.17 T246).

If implementation nonetheless finds either file must change, that is a budget amendment requiring review, not an implementation decision.

### 24.3 Prohibited paths

Writing to any of these is a stop condition:
* `thoth-dissemination/**` (any file, any branch).
* Anything on or merged from `feature/metrics`, and any Metrics table, migration, model, query or workflow. This includes the Metrics-owned `20260904_v1.9.0` and `20260905_v1.9.0` directories on that line (§1.2).
* `thoth-app/**`.
* `thoth-client/**` (the generated SDL is build output, not a source write).
* Every existing file under `thoth-api/migrations/`; released migrations are immutable.
* `.github/workflows/**`.
* Any infrastructure repository.
* `thoth-export-server/src/record.rs`, `thoth-export-server/src/xml/mod.rs`, `thoth-api/src/redis.rs`, and every other file under `thoth-export-server/src/xml/` except `doideposit_crossref.rs`.
* `docs/engineering/ai-delivery/tasks/BE-06.md`, before implementation is authorized.

Branch discipline is §27.1's. The BE-06 diff is authored only on `feature/publisher-services-v1-10--be-06`, and merged only into `feature/publisher-services-v1-10`. `develop`, `master` and `feature/metrics` are never its target.

### 24.4 Expected generated artifacts and CI side effects

Build-generated, not source writes: `thoth-client/assets/schema.graphql` (gitignored; compared between base and candidate by building both).

Expected natural CI effects:
* a longer `thoth-api` test run;
* new PostgreSQL-backed concurrency tests requiring the standard test database;
* the `paperclip` OpenAPI document gaining exactly one path.

No new CI workflow, no new job, no new runner and no new secret.

---

## 25. Error taxonomy and required tests

### 25.1 Error taxonomy

Every fail-closed path returns a distinct, stable code.
* Each code becomes a `ThothError` variant with a fixed message that echoes no caller value.
* Each has an explicit `IntoFieldError` arm mapping it to a `"type"` extension, following the released pattern (`thoth-errors/src/lib.rs:191-273` and its `IntoFieldError` impl at `master 4fa7eaa9`; `:191-338`, with the impl at `:298`, on the integration branch at `395cc16a`, where MIG-01 added variants).
* Codes also satisfy the released worker-facing grammar `^[A-Z][A-Z0-9_]*$`, at most 64 characters, so they are usable as `last_error_code`.

| Code | Raised when |
|---|---|
| `WORK_UPSERT_PROFILE_NOT_IMPLEMENTED` | a key is not a registered execution profile, at claim, materialization, control, or at COMMIT by the target-set trigger |
| `WORK_UPSERT_EXECUTION_PROFILES_REQUIRED` | an empty `executionProfiles` list |
| `WORK_UPSERT_PROFILE_NOT_ADMITTED` | an operation requires an admission row for the binding and none exists, including a `WORK_UPSERT` reservation's precondition check of fence clause 7 |
| `WORK_UPSERT_EXECUTION_NOT_PERMITTED` | execution is paused, or the profile's eligibility predicate is false, when a `WORK_UPSERT` reservation checks fence clauses 8 and 9 as preconditions |
| `WORK_UPSERT_COMPLETION_REQUIRES_FENCE` | completion attempted with `fenced_at IS NULL` |
| `WORK_UPSERT_COMPLETION_REQUIRES_ACCEPTED_PERMIT` | completion attempted without an `ACCEPTED` permit at the attempt's generation |
| `WORK_UPSERT_CANCELLATION_REFUSED_FENCED_ATTEMPT` | administrative cancellation of a job whose current attempt is fenced |
| `WORK_UPSERT_RECOVERY_BLOCKED` | a claim or fence is refused by an uncleared fenced abandonment |
| `WORK_UPSERT_CAPTURE_IS_MONOTONE` | an attempt to set `capture_enabled` from true to false |
| `WORK_UPSERT_CONTROL_ROW_IS_PERMANENT` | an attempt to delete a control row |
| `WORK_UPSERT_CONTROL_KEY_IMMUTABLE` | an attempt to change a control row's profile key |
| `WORK_UPSERT_ADMISSION_IMMUTABLE` | any `UPDATE` of an admission row |
| `WORK_UPSERT_ADMISSION_DELETE_ONLY_BY_PUBLISHER_CASCADE` | a direct `DELETE` of an admission row while its publisher exists |
| `WORK_UPSERT_ADMISSION_CENSUS_NOT_EMPTY` | admission attempted while the census still reports uncovered Works |
| `WORK_UPSERT_GENERATION_OVERFLOW` | a generation would exceed `9223372036854775807`; unreachable in practice, and fails closed rather than wrapping |
| `DISTRIBUTION_JOB_KIND_NOT_CLAIMABLE` | `claimDistributionJobs` names `WORK_UPSERT` |
| `DISTRIBUTION_JOB_WORK_IDENTITY_IMMUTABLE` | an attempt to change `work_identity` |
| `DISTRIBUTION_JOB_WORK_REFERENCE_NOT_RESTORABLE` | an attempt to set `work_id` from NULL back to non-NULL |
| `WORK_DELETE_BLOCKED_BY_FENCED_ATTEMPT` | Work, Imprint or Publisher deletion reaching a Work with a fenced open attempt |
| `WORK_DELETE_BINDING_DRIFT` | the deletion unit's revalidation failed; the unit restarts |
| `WORK_DELETE_BINDING_DRIFT_UNRESOLVED` | the deletion unit exhausted its bound of 3 restarts |
| `CROSSREF_ROOT_WORK_NOT_FOUND` | a reservation names a root Work that does not exist |
| `CROSSREF_PUBLISHER_NOT_COVERED` | the root's current publisher has no enabled `CROSSREF` assignment |
| `CROSSREF_BINDING_MOVED_RETRY` | the binding re-read under `W` differs from the one read before the lock; nothing is written |
| `CROSSREF_PERMIT_BLOCKED` | a reservation's membership intersects a blocking permit; or trigger rule A finds one at authorization |
| `CROSSREF_PERMIT_EMPTY_DOI_SET` | the API-derived membership is empty |
| `CROSSREF_DOI_NOT_CANONICALISABLE` | a stored DOI does not canonicalise under §16.3 |
| `CROSSREF_UNIT_ALREADY_DEPOSITED_IN_JOB` | a back-catalogue unit already has an `ACCEPTED` permit in the same outer job |
| `CROSSREF_MANUAL_RECOVERY_REQUIRES_REFERENCE` | a manual-recovery reservation without a non-blank operator authorization reference |
| `CROSSREF_PERMIT_CLAIM_STALE` | on a job-linked reservation or finalisation: the claim token is absent or stale, the job is not `RUNNING`, or the attempt is closed or is not the permit's; or a claim token was sent on a job-less route |
| `CROSSREF_PERMIT_INITIAL_STATE_INVALID` | an `INSERT` of anything but a clean `RESERVED` row (§16.2 rule I1) |
| `CROSSREF_PERMIT_EVIDENCE_IMMUTABLE` | a change to any rule-U1 column |
| `CROSSREF_PERMIT_LINK_NOT_RESTORABLE` | an operational link set or restored, rather than set `NULL` (rule U2) |
| `CROSSREF_PERMIT_WRITE_ONCE_FIELD` | a rule-U3 field changed once set, or set outside the transition that owns it |
| `CROSSREF_PERMIT_ILLEGAL_TRANSITION` | a state change outside §16.2's seven edges, or a same-state write outside rules S1–S3. Includes finalising a non-`RESERVED` permit with a different digest, a second reconciliation annotation, and an edge out of `AUTHORIZED` that is both, or neither, a report and a reconciliation |
| `CROSSREF_PERMIT_AUTHORIZATION_REQUIRES_MANIFEST` | `RESERVED → AUTHORIZED` without `payload_digest` and `authorized_at` |
| `CROSSREF_PERMIT_AUTHORIZATION_REQUIRES_FENCE` | `RESERVED → AUTHORIZED` on `WORK_UPSERT` without an open, fenced attempt |
| `CROSSREF_ARTIFACT_SOURCE_CHANGED` | `RESERVED → AUTHORIZED` while the root's `source_generation` differs from the witness |
| `CROSSREF_PERMIT_DELETE_REFUSED` | any `DELETE` of a permit, or `TRUNCATE` of the permit or membership table |
| `CROSSREF_PERMIT_MEMBERSHIP_IMMUTABLE` | any `UPDATE` or `DELETE` of a membership row |
| `CROSSREF_PERMIT_MEMBERSHIP_CARDINALITY_MISMATCH` | membership, `doi_set_digest` and `doi_set_cardinality` disagree at COMMIT, from either table |
| `CROSSREF_PERMIT_NOT_FOUND` | a finalisation, report, void or reconciliation names no permit |
| `CROSSREF_PERMIT_REQUIRES_RESERVATION_TOKEN` | finalisation, outcome report or route-owner void without the matching reservation token |
| `CROSSREF_PERMIT_VOID_REQUIRES_RESERVED` | either explicit void attempted from any state other than `RESERVED` |
| `CROSSREF_PERMIT_VOID_REQUIRES_DETAIL` | either explicit void without a non-blank `detail` |
| `CROSSREF_VOID_REQUIRES_AUTHORIZATION_REFERENCE` | a superuser void without a non-blank authorization reference |
| `CROSSREF_RECONCILIATION_REQUIRES_REFERENCE` | a reconciliation without a non-blank authorization reference |
| `CROSSREF_TIMESTAMP_NOT_INCREASING` | the candidate timestamp is not strictly above both the floor and the DOI history, at allocation or at authorization |
| `CROSSREF_TIMESTAMP_NOT_DECODABLE` | a 17-digit value is not the exact encoding of an instant (§17.2); also the stable body of the prepared route's HTTP 400 |
| `CROSSREF_TIMESTAMP_OVERFLOW` | an instant at or after `10000-01-01T00:00:00.000Z` would be encoded: the successor of `99991231235959999`, or a clock past year 9999 (§17.2) |
| `CROSSREF_VERSION_FLOOR_NOT_DECREASING` | any attempt to lower the floor |
| `CROSSREF_VERSION_FLOOR_DOMAIN` | a floor value other than `0` or `99999999999999` |
| `CROSSREF_VERSION_FLOOR_NOT_DRAINED` | a floor advance while any blocking permit exists |
| `CROSSREF_VERSION_FLOOR_ALREADY_ADVANCED` | a second advance for the same `g6_attempt_id` |
| `CROSSREF_VERSION_FLOOR_PERMANENT` | a `DELETE` or `TRUNCATE` of the floor |
| `CROSSREF_VERSION_FLOOR_AUDIT_APPEND_ONLY` | an `UPDATE`, `DELETE` or `TRUNCATE` of the floor audit |
| `CROSSREF_BASELINE_IMMUTABLE` | an `UPDATE` of a baseline row, or its `DELETE` while its publisher exists |
| `ATTEMPT_HAS_AUTHORIZED_PERMIT` | a worker failure or terminal transition while a linked permit is `AUTHORIZED` |
| `ATTEMPT_HAS_OPEN_RESERVATION` | a worker failure or terminal transition while a linked permit is `RESERVED` |
| `OUTER_ATTEMPT_HAS_OPEN_PERMITS` | a back-catalogue outer attempt terminalizing over a `RESERVED`, `AUTHORIZED` or blocking `INDETERMINATE` unit permit |

**Error codes: 60** — R52A's 49, less the 8 withdrawn below, plus 19 new.

* No code is reused for two semantically different states.
* `EntityNotFound` keeps its released mapping to `INTERNAL_ERROR`, which is BE-03's recorded pre-existing limitation, inherited rather than widened.
* Refusals that reuse released codes are not counted: `Unauthorised` for a role gate, and the released `STALE_DISTRIBUTION_JOB_CLAIM` / `DISTRIBUTION_JOB_TERMINAL` for a worker call on a job that finalisation already retired (§16.6 group B).
* The worker-set `failDistributionJob.errorCode` values of §28.3 are not API-raised codes and are not counted.

**Withdrawn R52A codes, with their replacements:**

| Withdrawn | Replacement |
|---|---|
| `CROSSREF_PERMIT_PAYLOAD_DIGEST_ONLY_AT_AUTHORIZATION`, `CROSSREF_PERMIT_PAYLOAD_DIGEST_IMMUTABLE` | `CROSSREF_PERMIT_WRITE_ONCE_FIELD`: rule U3 covers every write-once field, not only the digest |
| `CROSSREF_PERMIT_REQUIRES_OPEN_ATTEMPT` | `CROSSREF_PERMIT_CLAIM_STALE` |
| `CROSSREF_ARTIFACT_MEMBERSHIP_CHANGED`, `CROSSREF_ARTIFACT_DOI_SET_MISMATCH`, `CROSSREF_ARTIFACT_BATCH_ID_MISMATCH`, `CROSSREF_ARTIFACT_TIMESTAMP_MISMATCH` | the `crossref_void_reason` members `DOI_MEMBERSHIP_CHANGED`, `ARTIFACT_DOI_SET_MISMATCH`, `ARTIFACT_BATCH_ID_MISMATCH` and `ARTIFACT_TIMESTAMP_MISMATCH`, returned in `CrossrefFinalisationResult.voidReason`. A group-A invalidation is a result, not an error |
| `CROSSREF_RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED` | none: it had no raising path. An operation blocked by an unreconciled permit already returns `CROSSREF_PERMIT_BLOCKED` (reservation) or `CROSSREF_VERSION_FLOOR_NOT_DRAINED` (G-7), or silently omits the job (claim) |

### 25.2 Method

Correctness that depends on PostgreSQL locking is tested with **real two-connection PostgreSQL tests**, following the released pattern at `thoth-api/src/model/publisher_service_configuration/tests.rs:1470`. Every concurrency test states: initial rows; session A's statements; session B's statements; the synchronisation point; which session is expected to wait; the expected final state; and the deadlock expectation. **"No deadlock" without a mechanically synchronised test is not evidence and does not satisfy any criterion here.**

### 25.3 Generation and capture (T1–T40)

T1–T16: one test per §8.2 table asserting that every listed serialized-value, selection/order and ownership column fires capture and that the listed excluded columns do not. T17 `title.canonical` flip. T18 title moved A→B dirties **both** roots in one transaction. T19 abstract moved A→B. T20 **`abstract.locale_code` change fires**. T21 `work.imprint_id` move. T22 contribution moved A→B. T23 `contribution.contributor_id` change altering the ORCID. T24 affiliation institution change. T25 affiliation contribution move. T26 affiliation ordinal reorder. T27 publication work move. T28 location publication move. T29 **`location.location_platform` change fires**. T30 funding work move. T31 issue work and series moves. T32 **`reference.publication_date` change fires**. T33 chapter edit propagates to every `HAS_CHILD` parent and to no other Work. T34 `work_relation` endpoint re-parenting dirties all four endpoints. Every ownership-move test asserts **both** OLD and NEW roots in one transaction.

T35–T37 capture negatives: `subject`; `language`; `price`, `award`, `endorsement`, `biography`, `book_review`, `additional_resource`, `contact`, `work_featured_video`, `affiliation.position`, `contributor.website`, `institution.country_code`, `reference.retrieval_date`, `series.imprint_id`. Each asserts the exclusion only after selection/order and ownership effects were checked.

T38 serializer drift: the guard fails CI when a field is added to the serializer without a matrix row. T39 capture continues while `capture_enabled = false` and while `execution_enabled = false`. T40 **arbitrary worker absence**: commit source events, advance the clock by simulated days, assert the residue predicate still lists them and a later drain materializes them, with no timestamp read anywhere.

### 25.4 Materialization (T41–T54)

Seed from zero; existing residue; resolved state; current PENDING coalescing (no write); obsolete PENDING publisher; obsolete PENDING activation; destination disabled; unadmitted binding; ineligible Work; deleted Work; forced materialization; ordinal and deduplication identity across all three creation sites; **exactly three creation sites** (static containment); replay of the seed advances no generation; the seed creates no job, target or attempt, and a Work it covers is materialized only after admission (§25.20, T290).

### 25.5 Concurrency (T55–T78)

Configuration versus materializer, both orders. Configuration versus fence, both orders. Work edit versus finalisation, both orders. Ownership move versus finalisation, both orders, including a move back into the permit's publisher while finalisation waits for `W` with that publisher's coordinator concurrent, and the coordinator of the publisher a Work moved to (the schedules of §25.19, T270–T275, run against the implementation). Claim versus rebinding, both orders. Deletion versus configuration, both orders **and** the stale-bound variant for work, imprint and publisher. Deletion versus materializer, claim, finalisation, T2 and administrative cancellation, both orders each. Deletion versus ownership movement, both orders. Two cross-bound publisher deletions. Cross-work propagation: two chapter edits under one parent both orders; relation create versus chapter edit; propagation versus materialization on the parent; parent edit versus chapter edit. Reservations with overlapping DOI sets in **opposite** orders; partially overlapping sets; disjoint sets proven not to serialise. G-7 floor advance versus in-flight reservation, both orders. Execution pause versus the claim, the `WORK_UPSERT` reservation and finalisation, both orders; enable versus the claim and finalisation; a repeated pause with a claim queued behind it; the pause against a fenced report, completion and T2 successor; and the gate queued behind a SHARE holder that waits on a DOI key, the floor or the permit row (the schedules of §25.20, T280–T288, run against the implementation). **Every test asserts no new `40P01`**, except the three that deliberately demonstrate the pre-existing class and the plain-delete contrast, which assert that the safe path does not deadlock where the naive one does.

### 25.6 Fence and recovery (T79–T91)

Fence pass inside finalisation; an identical finalisation retry by a caller holding the route-derived role and the token replays its recorded result without re-evaluating the binding, fence or artifact checks, and the same retry without the role is refused `Unauthorised`; binding refusal with replacement, with the finalisation voiding its own reservation (group B); binding refusal without replacement leaving residue; target disabled; execution paused; unadmitted; stale token; completion without a fence refused; fenced crash then lease expiry then claim refused for a `ReplayBlocked` profile; reconciliation clearance then claim admitted; a registry fixture for a hypothetical `ReplaySafe` profile proving the clause is profile-driven; `recovery_cleared_at` requires `fenced_at`.

### 25.7 Cancellation (T92–T97)

C1–C5 of §12.2 exactly; plus the cancel/finalise race in **both** orders asserting that no state permits "fenced then generically cancelled", and that a job carrying only a `RESERVED` permit is cancellable while the reservation survives byte-identical and blocking.

### 25.8 Work deletion (T98–T105)

Terminal history survives; PENDING retirement; RUNNING-unfenced retirement with a stale worker proven unable to finalise or write; RUNNING-fenced refusal from all three entry points; lease recovery then deletion; historical identity reporting after deletion; no resurrection; **a raw `DELETE` bypassing the protocol is rejected by the backstop CHECK**.

### 25.9 Crossref freshness and the artifact (T106–T114)

Direct root change reflected in the prepared artifact; child change affecting the parent reflected in the parent's prepared artifact; an artifact cached before the change is **not** used by the prepared path; the prepared route reads and writes no Redis key (asserted against a Redis test double); the released route's behaviour and cache are byte-unchanged; the emitted `<timestamp>` and `doi_batch_id` equal the reservation's, asserted by parsing the XML; the digest recheck before POST fails closed on mismatch; a 16-digit or 18-digit path parameter, and a calendar-invalid 17-digit one (second 60, hour 24, 29 February in a non-leap year, a leading-zero year), is a 400 `CROSSREF_TIMESTAMP_NOT_DECODABLE` issued before any query; a non-`doideposit::crossref` specification id on the prepared path is the released error.

### 25.10 Permits, membership and the FSM (T115–T130)

Canonicalisation of case, `http`, `https`, `www.`, `dx.doi.org` and mixed-case-prefix variants to one value; the full released-grammar corpus with zero totality gaps and zero over-acceptances; a non-canonicalisable value refused; digest and cardinality agreement enforced at COMMIT from both tables; a post-hoc membership INSERT refused; membership `UPDATE`/`DELETE` refused; permit `DELETE` refused; every manifest column immutable; `payload_digest` write-once and only at authorization; every illegal transition refused; all six overlap shapes; all four routes subject to the same rules; opposite-order concurrent overlapping reservations without deadlock; **no two concurrent provider permits for the same DOI**; `AUTHORIZED + NULL` blocks and a `STRICT` variant demonstrated not to; the argument-free count and DRAIN.

### 25.11 Timestamp and version floor (T131–T144)

Exact 17-digit format; the emitted `<timestamp>` equals the permit's `crossref_timestamp`; two deposits within one second receive different values; **same-millisecond** allocation yields a deterministic strictly-higher value; equality with the floor or with history fails before any write; `RESERVED`, `AUTHORIZED`, `INDETERMINATE`, `ACCEPTED` and `NONE_ATTEMPTED` all contribute to history, and only a `VOIDED` reservation is excluded; multi-DOI takes the maximum; the released public route's 14-digit behaviour is unchanged for non-provider consumers, and every 17-digit value exceeds every 14-digit value; the floor cannot be lowered by any writer; the floor domain rejects every value but `0` and `99999999999999`; one advance per attempt including a respelled UUID; a floor advance is refused while a blocking permit exists, in both interleavings.

### 25.12 Back-catalogue (T145–T153)

Every combination of §18.10: zero units attempted; all accepted; mixed accepted and none-attempted; mixed accepted and indeterminate; a unit left `RESERVED`; a unit left `AUTHORIZED`; crash after N of M; late accepted report; outer lease expiry with surviving permits; retry eligibility per unit; **no aggregate conceals an ambiguous unit**.

### 25.13 Ledger, G-6 and G-7 (T154–T161)

The recognition corpus (9 cases); duplicate-key rejection with an explicitly configured parser; a semantic array reorder failing despite valid JCS and a correct digest; chronology (a success terminal without its observation terminal rejected; a failed observation write yielding `ATTEMPT_HOLD`; no attempt with two closing records); outcome 16 and Case B; **Case A adversarially** (observation terminal appearing before the hold, a concurrent second closing record, restoration failure, a controlled session still alive, a stale census, a late observation terminal after closure); the authority corpus (wrong digest, unfetchable digest, v4-only grant, G12 drift, audit/ledger digest agreement); G-7 (consumed-plus-failing-condition returns Class 1B before eligibility; unconsumed-with-floor-mismatch returns Class 1A preserving the observed floor; a deadlock-observed chain never reaches mutation; a second advance raises `unique_violation` even when respelled). The 30 positive fixtures must pass all four evidence classes separately and the 27 negatives must each be rejected by the stated rule at the stated step.

### 25.14 Compatibility and authorization (T162–T170)

Released back-catalogue behaviour is not accidentally changed (a full replay of the released `distribution_job` test suite); `claimDistributionJobs` with `kinds: []` returns only back-catalogue jobs even when work-level jobs exist and are due; a `v1.8.0` client cannot claim `WORK_UPSERT` by any input it can form; the migration and the deployment are inert (no job, target, attempt, admission, permit or audit row can exist while the control row is `(false,false)`); **no Crossref network call occurs in any test or in G-6** (asserted by a network-refusing test harness); no Metrics table, migration or query is touched by the candidate diff; no `thoth-dissemination` file is touched; **every mutation is exercised with each of the four principal shapes** (worker-only, superuser-only, both roles, publisher-scoped) and the observed accept/refuse matrix equals §19.4's table exactly, rule 3's replay, repeat and role-removal cases included (T257); **in the built SDL, `reservationToken` is an output field of `CrossrefWriteReservation` only, and an input field of the `finaliseCrossrefWrite`, `reportCrossrefWrite` and `voidCrossrefWriteReservation` inputs only; no other type — in particular no permit, finalisation-result, report or query type — has a field named `reservationToken` or `reservation_token`; and no mutation clears a fenced abandonment** (T170, a required implementation test: no BE-06 SDL can be built before implementation).

### 25.15 Migration (T171–T174)

The two sequences of §23.4; enum labels survive `down M2`; the single-transaction variant fails with "unsafe use of new value"; **an exact-path test asserts the two migration directories are precisely `thoth-api/migrations/20260910_v1.10.0` and `thoth-api/migrations/20260911_v1.10.0`**, that no other directory was added, that every directory under `thoth-api/migrations/` has a distinct Diesel version — derived exactly as `migrations_internals::version_from_string` derives it, never compared by path — and that no directory other than those two has version `20260910` or `20260911` (§23.1). The compatibility window's migration evidence is §25.21's T294–T304 (§23.6).

### 25.16 R52A correction tests (T175–T196), with the falsification already performed

These 22 rows are the tests the ten R52A findings required. Each was prototyped and executed against **PostgreSQL 17.10** on a disposable local instance during the R52A pass. Two rows changed meaning under R52B and were re-executed under R52B's rules: T182 (replay, §25.17 T229) and T183 (write-once fields, §25.17 T240).

| # | Test | Result obtained |
|---|---|---|
| T175 | artifact DOI set grows after reservation (the review's exact example) | `VOIDED / SOURCE_CHANGED_DURING_PREPARATION`; no manifest bound |
| T176 | artifact changes, DOI set does not | `VOIDED / SOURCE_CHANGED_DURING_PREPARATION` |
| T177 | **DOI set changes and changes back**: membership equality passes, witness must catch it | membership comparison passed; witness caught it; `VOIDED` |
| T178 | worker reports a DOI set that is not the reserved set | `VOIDED / ARTIFACT_DOI_SET_MISMATCH` |
| T179 | observed batch id mismatch | `VOIDED / ARTIFACT_BATCH_ID_MISMATCH` |
| T180 | observed timestamp mismatch | `VOIDED / ARTIFACT_TIMESTAMP_MISMATCH` |
| T181 | wrong reservation token | refused; permit unchanged and still `RESERVED` |

Two further R52A requirements are covered by rows already listed: **the public generic recovery-clearance API is absent** (T170's SDL assertion plus a source-containment test that `work_upsert_clear_fenced_abandonment` has exactly one call site, inside `reconcileCrossrefWritePermit`), and **Crossref reconciliation clears recovery only on permitted terminal evidence** (T90, extended to assert that clearance does not occur while any permit on the attempt still blocks).

### 25.17 R52B correction tests (T197–T258), with the falsification already performed

These 62 rows are the tests the R52B findings require.

**How they were run.** Every executed row ran against **PostgreSQL 17.10** on a disposable local instance, during the R52B pass.
* **The harness** reproduces the released BE-04 statement shapes (`distribution_job/crud.rs:368-600`) and every R52B trigger, route and lock order in a disposable schema.
* **Race rows** are two-session interleavings with an explicit pause point. "Waited" is observed in `pg_locks`, never assumed.
* **Rust rows** ran against a scratch copy of the released export server at `master 4fa7eaa9`, with `chrono` 0.4.45.
* **Extraction rows** ran the §16.11 reference implementation.

Three rows are required implementation tests that the harness cannot run, and are marked **required**.
