### 25.19 R52B second review-correction tests (T270–T279), executed in the second review-correction pass

These 10 rows are the tests #848 comment `5624224185` requires. Every row ran on **PostgreSQL 17.10** on a disposable local instance during the second review-correction pass.
* **The race harness** is the R52B pass's (§25.17), with its timestamp functions exactly as that pass's database held them. Finalisation, T2 completion, the outcome report, both voids and reconciliation are replaced by the orderings this document states; the `4010dce1` candidate's finalisation ordering is kept beside them, unchanged, as the negative control. Three stand-ins are added: the released BE-03 coordinator's statement order (`lock_publisher` `FOR UPDATE`, the assignment write, the `PENDING` cancellation, one publisher `UPDATE`, and its cascade over every work row of the publisher), the editorial ownership move with its capture, and the seed unit of §21.2. Its permit table and state-machine trigger are the R52B pass's, which predate R52B-27 and R52B-28; no check here reads the columns those changed.
* **Race rows** are interleavings of separate sessions, each pausing inside its open transaction at a named lock point. Waits are read from `pg_locks` and `pg_blocking_pids`, never assumed; order is read from server-clock traces taken inside each transaction and from a stamp taken after each commit.
* **T277 and T278 are specification-harness evidence.** The principal is a session setting standing in for the resolver's roles. They model the precedence of §19.4 rule 3, and they are **not** T257, which remains a required implementation test.

| # | Test | Finding | Result obtained |
|---|---|---|---|
| T270 | the review's schedule, corrected: a permit bound to P1; the root observed under P2 before any lock; the root moved back to P1 while finalisation waits for `W`; a BE-03 writer disabling P1's `CROSSREF` assignment | R52B-29 | in two sessions, and in three with the writer started before the move: finalisation's first lock was P1, its `publisher_identity`; the writer waited on it at P1, observed in `pg_locks`, and was granted P1 only after finalisation's last in-transaction event; finalisation re-resolved the binding under `W` as P1 and returned `VOIDED_RETRYABLE / SOURCE_CHANGED_DURING_PREPARATION`, because both moves were captured; the writer then committed. 15 of 15 |
| T271 | negative control: the same two schedules under the `4010dce1` candidate's ordering | R52B-29 | finalisation locked P2, the Work's publisher read before any lock, and held it throughout. The P1 writer never waited. In two sessions it took P1 `FOR UPDATE` and committed inside finalisation's open transaction. In three, it wrote the disable while the root was still P2's, so its cascade did not touch the root; the root moved into P1; finalisation read P1's assignment as enabled while holding only P2; and the writer committed after that read and before finalisation committed. Finalisation authorised nothing only because the two moves had moved the witness (A1). 14 of 14 |
| T272 | drift that stays, and an ownership move against finalisation in both orders | R52B-29 | the root moved to P2, and P2 was reconfigured, before `W`: finalisation, holding P1, re-resolved P2 and returned `VOIDED_JOB_RETIRED / BINDING_SUPERSEDED` without reading any assignment, and created no replacement. Finalisation first: the move waited at `W`, finalisation authorised, and the move then committed. The move first: finalisation waited at `W`, then refused `BINDING_SUPERSEDED`. 13 of 13 |
| T273 | a configuration writer against finalisation with no drift, both orders, and an activation change with a replacement | R52B-29 | finalisation first: the writer waited at P1 and committed after `AUTHORIZED`. The writer first, disabling: finalisation waited at P1, then returned `VOIDED_JOB_RETIRED / ASSIGNMENT_DISABLED` with no replacement. The writer first, changing the activation from A1 to an admitted A1b: the job retired `BINDING_SUPERSEDED`, and the replacement was created `PENDING` under the held P1 and A1b. 14 of 14 |
| T274 | reverse ordering: the coordinator of the publisher the root drifted to, while finalisation holds P1 and `W(root)` | R52B-29 | the P2 coordinator's cascade waited on `W(root)`; finalisation returned `VOIDED_JOB_RETIRED / BINDING_SUPERSEDED` and committed; the coordinator then completed. No `40P01`. 4 of 4 |
| T275 | T2 completion with a successor due, against an ownership move and against the P1 writer, both orders each | R52B-29 | completion first: the move waited at `W`, and the successor was created under the held P1. (the|its|a) jobs?`; `creat(e|ed|ing) (the|a|its) job`; `edit`, `event`, `capture`, `editorial` or `trigger` followed within one sentence by `creat…`, `insert…` or `enqueue…`; `enqueue`, `bump-or-create` and `in-editorial`; `fourth creator`, `creation site(s)` and `creator(s)`; and `WORK_UPSERT creator` and `job creator`. In the `4010dce1` candidate they matched 51 times on 35 lines. Three phrases, in §21.2 and §21.3, made an edit or an event create a job, and are rewritten; §21.2's "bump-or-create enqueue" is reworded as the design the seed avoids. Every other match already stated the two-stage mechanism: the materializer's step and the drain, the seed's own steps, the withdrawn in-editorial enqueue of §6.3, generation-row inserts, and the three creation sites. In this document, no match outside §6.3's withdrawn-item table makes an edit, an event or a capture create a job. That search did not treat the seed as a creator to be removed; the third review-correction pass's search does (§25.20).

### 25.20 R52B third review-correction tests (T280–T293), executed in the third review-correction pass

These 14 rows are the tests #848 comment `5632920797` requires. Every row ran on **PostgreSQL 17.10** on a disposable local instance, during the third review-correction pass on 2026-09-11.
* **The race harness** is the second pass's (§25.19), unchanged, with one overlay. The overlay adds the execution gate of §21.1 to the claim, the `WORK_UPSERT` reservation, `WORK_UPSERT` finalisation and `setWorkUpsertExecution`. It replaces the seed with §21.2's and adds §9.3's materialization unit and its drain, §21.3's census statement and admission, and §18.4's control CHECK, which the earlier harness lacked. The `37e44ce3` candidate's ungated claim, reservation and finalisation, and its job-creating seed, are kept beside them unchanged, as negative controls.
* **Race rows** are interleavings of two or three separate sessions, each pausing inside its open transaction at a named point: the pause holding `Q` EXCLUSIVE after its control-row update (`QU`), the claim after its statement (`AFTER`), the reservation and finalisation after their execution read (`E`), or a lock point of §25.17 and §25.19. Waits are read from `pg_locks` and `pg_blocking_pids`, never assumed. Advisory locks are identified by their full key `(classid, objid, objsubid)`; the `CROSSREF` gate is `(1948572002, 960590390, 2)`. Order is read from server-clock traces taken inside each transaction, and from a stamp taken after each commit.
* **The census rows** run the census statement extracted from this document's own text, in a harness built from the document's own table blocks, as §25.18's did.

**Executed in the third review-correction pass: 14 of 14 rows.** The race rows ran 34 cases — 33 interleavings, 12 of them with three sessions, and 1 single-session negative control — with 162 checks and no `40P01`. The first complete run scored 161 of 162. Its one failing check was a harness defect, not a finding: it compared two traces from one session that fell in the same millisecond. That check now compares the session's own trace order, the traces carry microseconds, and the full re-run scored 162 of 162. 17 sequential checks and the census suites' 33 checks passed. T293 re-ran 54 interleavings, 70 sequential checks, 220 matrix checks and 161 checks of §25.18 from this document's own text, with identical results. T170, T203, T257, T258 and the unexecuted parts of T241 remain required implementation tests; nothing here executes them. T290's static check is specification-harness evidence, not T203.

**R52B-31 whole-document search.** The whole document was searched, line by line, for wording that could make the seed, the bootstrap, admission, an editorial event or capture a job creator, or merge the seed and the materializer into one creator: `seed…creat…`, `bootstrap…creat…`, `admission…creat…`, `edit`, `event`, `capture` or `editorial` followed within one clause by `creat…`, `insert…` or `enqueue…`, `creation site 1`, `seed and the materializer`, and `creates the/its/a job`. In the `37e44ce3` candidate these patterns matched 66 times on 32 lines. In the third review-correction candidate they matched 110 times on 43 lines. In this document, re-run in the fourth review-correction pass, they match 112 times on 45 lines, 18 of them in §6.3's withdrawn-item tables or in the historical rows T262–T264 and T279. Every other match was read. Each states that its subject creates, inserts or enqueues nothing; describes the three creation sites, the drain or the helper; describes an edit, capture or a reservation writing generation or child rows, or the migration creating capture triggers; or describes the rejected job-creating seed as such — as the review's finding, the negative control, a stop condition or §32's traceability. None makes the seed, the bootstrap, admission, an event or capture create a job, and none names the seed and the materializer one creator. A stricter check, for any unnegated `seed`, `bootstrap`, `admission`, `capture`, `edit` or `event` followed by `creates the/its/a job`, finds none outside the withdrawn material.

**R52B-32 whole-document search.** The whole document was searched for a pause without its barrier and for the superseded lock order: `control row UPDATE only`, the ten-class order `P → I → W → G → J → A → K`, `never waits`, and every occurrence of `pause`, `execution_enabled` and `setWorkUpsertExecution`. In the `37e44ce3` candidate these matched 1, 5, 2, 15, 11 and 4 times. In this document the ten-class order no longer occurs, and `control row UPDATE only` occurs 1 time, in §6.3's withdrawn items. `never waits` occurs 4 times, each qualified to a row lock or to the claim statement, or quoted as withdrawn. `pause`, `execution_enabled` and `setWorkUpsertExecution` occur 100, 26 and 28 times — unchanged when re-run in the fourth review-correction pass — and each was read: every statement that the pause gates the claim, the reservation or fence passage now rests on the execution gate of §21.1, or is a consequence, test, criterion or rollback row that depends on it.

### 25.21 R52B fourth review-correction tests (T294–T304), executed in the fourth review-correction pass

These 11 rows are the compatibility proof #848 comment `5635363920` requires for R52B-33, and the falsification of the order it preferred. Every row ran on **PostgreSQL 17.10** on disposable local instances, during the fourth review-correction pass on 2026-09-11, with the real binaries.
* **The released binary** is `v1.8.0` (`9ae1e567`), built from a `git archive` of the tag, with its full workspace test suite.
* **The candidate binary** is `feature/publisher-services-v1-10` `395cc16a`, the release line's current base, with `thoth-api/migrations/20260910_v1.10.0/` and `20260911_v1.10.0/` added and `thoth-api/src/db.rs` touched to re-embed them.
* **The candidate migrations** were assembled from this document: every object it states as SQL taken from its SQL blocks verbatim, and every object it states in prose or pseudocode — the capture function and its 16 triggers, the guards, the target-set function, the permit state machine, the seed rows and the migration-time assertions — written to that text. Its migration-time assertions are §23.3's, over the values this document states: its canonicalisation examples (§16.3); the calendar successor across a minute, across a year's end, across 28 February in a leap and in a common year, and into the last representable millisecond; the six non-encodings §17.2 names, the 14-digit floor sentinel among them; the two values past the domain; both overflows; the blocking predicate; and the seed rows. The 40-value canonicalisation corpus and the 13 boundary vectors, which this document counts but does not list, were executed as tests in earlier passes (§25.10, §25.18), and embedding them in the migration is the implementation's. Four literal-text variants were kept beside them as negative controls: §8.4's previous R2, §11.4's previous function, §23.3's previous step order, and the previous `'HAS_CHILD'` literal.
* **The instrumentation** was installed after Migration 2, in its own schema, and is part of neither migration: a trigger on each BE-06 table refusing every write, one on `distribution_job` and `distribution_job_attempt` refusing any BE-06 label or column, and a log of every generation-row write.
* **The released test harness** truncates every table before each test. Migration 2's permit and membership `TRUNCATE` guards refuse that cascade, which no released code path issues outside the tests. For these runs only, the harness's reset was adapted to keep the two permanent seed rows and to truncate under `session_replication_role = replica`, and it was run identically over the released schema. Every test statement ran with every trigger enabled.

| T303 | **evidence of a topology since withdrawn (§6.3).** A parent-side deletion racing a chapter edit whose capture propagates to that parent, D1–D4, on the released schema, the fourth review-correction candidate's schema and the R2 before it | R52B-33 | The edit was held, by a test-only trigger firing in its own session only, after the released trigger had taken `W(chapter)` and before capture; the deletion was then issued, and waits were read from `pg_locks`. **Released schema:** in all four cases — a title edit against a Work, an Imprint and a Publisher deletion, and a `work` edit against a Work deletion — the deletion waited on the edit, and then both committed. **Corrected schema:** in all four, the deletion waited on the edit, capture's R2 then waited on the deletion, and PostgreSQL aborted the deletion with `40P01`; the edit committed with its generation bumps. This is §10.6's residual. **Previous R2:** the two Work deletions failed at once with `23503`, and the Imprint and Publisher deletions after waiting, with no `40P01` |
| T304 | the deployment-path facts | R52B-33 | The `Dockerfile` (`ENTRYPOINT ["/thoth"]`, `CMD ["init"]`) and `thoth-api/src/db.rs` are identical at `v1.8.0`, `395cc16a` and `feature/metrics` `9feddcee`. At `395cc16a` the CLI differs from `v1.8.0` only by the `publisher-services migration-backfill` subcommand, so `init`, `migrate` and `--revert` are unchanged. The production stack at `thoth-pub/infrastructure` `582379fc`: the GraphQL API service is given an image and a version, `ghcr.io/thoth-pub/thoth` and `1.8.0`, and no command parameter; the export API service is given two command parameters. Read with a bounded, field-targeted parse; no other field was recorded |

**Executed in the fourth review-correction pass: 11 of 11 rows.** The preferred order survived falsification: the pre-BE-06 release served, restarted and ran its own code over the migrated schema, and the only BE-06 state it caused was generation rows. Each of the four negatives reproduced its defect, and the corrected text removes it. T303's `40P01` is not a defect of the order but of the capture topology of that candidate; #848 comment `5637606889` raised it as R52B-34, and the Alternative A amendment removes it (§6.1a, §10.6, §25.22, §25.23). T296 executes the schema half of §25.14's replay of the released suite; its source half runs against the implementation. T170, T203, T257, T258 and the unexecuted parts of T241 remain required implementation tests; nothing here executes them.

**R52B-33 whole-document search.** The whole document was searched, line by line, for every term that can carry the withdrawn order, its gate numbers or its rollback. In the `a17b535b` candidate and in this document respectively: `backend release deployed INERT` matched 1 and 1 times; `production migration` 4 and 17; `migration authorization` 1 and 13; `deploy`, in every form, 29 and 68; `gate 9` 0 and 26, `gate 10` 1 and 25 and `gate 11` 2 and 23; `M1` or `Migration 1` 9 and 32; `M2` or `Migration 2` 13 and 56; `down M2` 5 and 9; `down M1` 1 and 1; `init` 0 and 26; `migrate` 0 and 31; `run_migrations` or `run_pending_migrations` 1 and 6; the old release, under any of its names, 2 and 35; `v1.8.0` 12 and 27; `compatib…` 19 and 28; `capture enable` or `enableWorkUpsertCapture` 10 and 11; `seed` 115 and 119; `creation site 1` 5 and 5; `execution_enabled` 26 and 26; `setWorkUpsertExecution` 28 and 28; `20260910` 72 and 83; and `20260911` 65 and 79. Every match in this document was read. `backend release deployed INERT` and `down M1` survive only in §6.3's withdrawn-item table. Every gate number names the gate §27.3 now gives it; the old numbers appear only in that withdrawn item and where §27.3 and §32 state the renumbering. Every `init` names the image's migrate-then-serve command, or a startup this document requires to find nothing pending. Every `migrate` names the `thoth migrate` subcommand — gate 10's one-off run, the runs §25.21 executed, or the `--revert` form §23.5 prohibits — or is the verb, for what a deployed `init` would do. No text makes deployment precede migration, and none makes a migration, a deployment or capture enable create a job. The four new `seed` matches name Migration 2's seed rows and the seed SQL this pass leaves unchanged, and `creation site 1`, `execution_enabled` and `setWorkUpsertExecution` gained none. The R52B-31 and R52B-32 searches of §25.20 were re-run on this document; §25.20 states their counts.

The evidence the CTO decision `5646180203` rests on. Every row ran on PostgreSQL 17.10 on disposable instances, against the statement shapes the built `v1.8.0` binary emits (T306), with the **released schema as the control in every schedule**. The schema under test in T305 and T307–T313 is the withdrawn topology of the fourth review-correction candidate.

The cycle: the deletion held `W(P)` `FOR UPDATE` and the `G(P)` its foreign-key cascade had deleted, and waited for `W(C)` in `work_relation_work_updated_at_with_relations()`; the edit held `W(C)` and requested `W(P)` `FOR KEY SHARE`. T170, T203, T257, T258 and the unexecuted parts of T241 remain required implementation tests, and §25.23 adds the implementation-only rows of §29: the full T205–T293 race suite against the implementation's own functions, the drift guards of §10.5, and the gate-9 re-run.

**331 test rows.**

---
## 26. Acceptance criteria

Numbered and objectively testable. No criterion says "architecture matches specification".

**Capture and generation**
1. Every serialized-value, selection/order and ownership column of §8.2 fires capture, and every column of §8.3 does not.
2. `abstract.locale_code`, `reference.publication_date` and `location.location_platform` each fire capture.
3. A `title.canonical` flip advances the generation.
4. Every ownership move dirties both the OLD and the NEW root in one transaction, for all ten mutable owning foreign keys.
5. A chapter edit advances the parent's generation, and BE-06 writes `updated_at_with_relations` nowhere.
6. Capture takes **no** lock but the rows of its own capture queue, and the transaction's generation rows are taken once, at its completion, ascending by `work_id`, proven by a lock-inspection test (T318, T319, T326).
7. Generations advance while `capture_enabled = false` and while `execution_enabled = false`.
8. The serializer-drift guard fails CI on divergence.

**Materialization and residue**
9. No editorial transaction inserts, updates or locks any `distribution_job`, `distribution_job_target` or `distribution_job_attempt` row.
10. The residue predicate contains no timestamp, interval or cadence, proven by inspection and by the arbitrary-outage test.
11. A source event committed with no worker running is materialized by a later drain, with no upper bound on the delay.
12. Re-running the seed or the drain with no intervening event writes nothing.
13. An obsolete PENDING row is unclaimable, is retired with `BINDING_SUPERSEDED` or `ASSIGNMENT_DISABLED`, and is replaced when the current binding is eligible and admitted.
14. Actionable uniqueness never leaves an unclaimable row blocking its replacement.
15. `WORK_UPSERT` jobs are created only by the helper, from exactly three call sites, proven by static containment.
16. All three creation sites allocate `job_ordinal` and the deduplication key under the same `G` lock, and a `unique_violation` on the deduplication key is never observed.

**Lock order**
17. Every non-editorial work-level path takes `P FOR SHARE → W FOR SHARE → G FOR UPDATE → J → A`, and no BE-06 path row-locks an assignment, admission or control row.
18. The work lock is `FOR SHARE`, and an ownership `UPDATE` is proven to wait behind it.
19. Every implicit RI lock falls on a row already held.
20. The claim never waits on a row lock — its job rows are taken `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` and its attempt rows are new keys — proven by a test in which the claim runs against a locked row and returns immediately. Its one added wait is the execution gate `Q`, and only while a control transition holds or awaits it (§10.7, T287).
21. No BE-06-introduced deadlock exists in any of the concurrency tests of §25.5.
22. **Every Crossref path takes a subsequence of LO-C** — DOI advisory keys, then the floor share, then the permit row, the two `WORK_UPSERT` paths after the execution gate `Q` — and the G-6 edge inventory states the identical order. No second Crossref order appears anywhere in the document, the source or the tests.
23. **DOI advisory locks are acquired by actual advisory key**, deduplicated and sorted ascending as signed `integer`, and a real `hashtext` collision pair whose string order opposes its key order produces no deadlock.
24. The DOI advisory namespace and the execution gate's are two different fixed literals, `1948572001` and `1948572002`, in the two-argument lock space, so neither can collide with the other or with the released one-argument `work_relation` lock (T289).

**Fence, authorization and recovery**
25. No provider effect can begin without an `AUTHORIZED` permit; completion without `fenced_at` is refused.
26. `fenced_at` is set exactly at the `RESERVED → AUTHORIZED` transition and never earlier.
27. An identical finalisation retry by a caller holding the route-derived role and the reservation token returns its recorded result without re-evaluating the binding, fence or artifact checks; the role is checked afresh on every retry.
28. A fenced attempt is never cancelled, deleted or replayed by generic code.
29. Every registered profile declares `ReplaySafe` or `ReplayBlocked` with a proof reference, enforced by the registry test.
30. A `ReplayBlocked` profile's fenced-and-abandoned attempt blocks every later claim for that `(work_identity, profile)` until reconciliation clears it.
31. **No public API clears a fenced abandonment.** `work_upsert_clear_fenced_abandonment` has exactly one call site, inside `reconcileCrossrefWritePermit`, and fires only when no permit on that attempt still blocks.
32. No generic fence mutation is exposed.

**Control, admission, bootstrap**
33. `(false,true)` cannot exist; capture cannot be disabled; the control row cannot be deleted.
34. From Migration 2's commit until capture enable — under the pre-BE-06 release, then under `v1.10.0` — BE-06's only database writes are generation rows (§21.1; T296, T299).
35. Admission exists only for an exact `(profile, publisher, activation)` and only after a passing census; a non-empty census refuses admission.
36. Admission rows cannot be updated, cannot be deleted directly, and are removed only by the publisher cascade.

**Cancellation and resolution**
37. `D` and `H` are derived from BE-04 history; no transition writes the generation row.
38. A terminal job resolves only `gen(job)`; higher generations remain residue and materialize without another edit.
39. Cases C1–C5 behave exactly as §12.2 states, including the refusal in C5.
40. Attempt-budget exhaustion through lease recovery produces the same `H` as an explicit terminal failure.

**Deletion**
41. `distribution_job.work_id` is `ON DELETE SET NULL` and `work_identity` is immutable and non-referential.
42. Ordinary Work deletion preserves job, target, attempt and lineage rows and removes the generation row.
43. PENDING and RUNNING-unfenced jobs are retired `WORK_DELETED` before deletion, and a `RESERVED` permit on the retired attempt survives byte-identical and blocking.
44. A fenced attempt makes Work, Imprint and Publisher deletion fail closed, and the block clears when the outcome is reported or the lease is recovered.
45. Terminal history never prevents Work deletion.
46. A deleted Work never reappears in residue, candidates or a drain, and no actionable row exists with a NULL `work_id`.
47. The deletion units take the publisher set first, revalidate under lock, restart at most three times, and produce no `40P01` in any tested interleaving.
48. Publisher deletion still destroys its job rows, and the specification says so; Crossref permits survive with their identities intact.
49. **No FK cascade of any kind can delete a permit or a membership row**, and a direct `DELETE` of either is refused.

**The artifact and its authorization**
50. The BE-06 prepared path performs no Redis read or write, and the released route's cache behaviour is byte-unchanged.
51. The `<timestamp>` and `doi_batch_id` in the prepared artifact equal the reservation's, verified by parsing the emitted XML.
52. An artifact cached before a change is never used to discharge a generation.
53. **A `RESERVED` permit authorises no provider write**, and the only transition that authorises one is `RESERVED → AUTHORIZED` through `finaliseCrossrefWrite`.
54. **Finalisation voids its own reservation** when the generation witness moved, when re-derived membership differs, when the observed DOI set, batch id or timestamp differs, or when the fence refuses — each with its own distinct `crossref_void_reason` — and returns the outcome of §16.6.
55. **The DOI-set-changed-and-changed-back case is caught**, proving the generation witness is load-bearing and not redundant with membership comparison.
56. Worker-supplied DOI values never determine membership; `dois` is not an input to any reservation mutation.
57. `payload_digest` is bound exactly once, at authorization, and is immutable thereafter.
58. Every Crossref provider write, on all four routes, holds a permit that is `AUTHORIZED` at the moment of the write.
59. DOI membership is queryable and **immutable from the instant the reservation commits**, enforced by both a row trigger and a deferred agreement trigger, so a post-hoc INSERT is refused.
60. All six overlap shapes behave as §16.3 requires.
61. Canonicalisation is **total** for every value the released `Doi` parser accepts, including `www.`, and accepts **nothing** it rejects, over the full corpus.
62. Lower-casing the permit identity is proved compatible with the released `doi_uniq_idx` on `lower(doi)`.
63. `crossref_is_blocking_write_permit` is `CALLED ON NULL INPUT` and blocks `AUTHORIZED + NULL`.
64. The blocking count and DRAIN take no arguments in SQL or in GraphQL.
65. `ACCEPTED` never means "registration complete" anywhere in code, comment or report.
66. The permit state machine is database-enforced: of the 30 ordered pairs of distinct states exactly the 7 edges of §16.2 are permitted, with no backwards transition, no deletion and no truncation.

**Timestamp and floor**
67. Every allocated timestamp is 17 digits, is the exact encoding of a UTC instant, and is strictly greater than both the floor and the DOI history, where history includes every state except `VOIDED`.
68. **Advancement past an existing history value uses calendar arithmetic**, never decimal `+1`, and is correct at millisecond, second, minute, hour, day, month, year and leap-day boundaries.
69. A 17-digit value that is not the exact encoding of an instant is refused, including the values PostgreSQL would silently normalise.
70. Same-millisecond allocation is deterministic and strictly higher; equality never reaches a provider write.
71. The floor's domain is exactly `{0, 99999999999999}`, it cannot be lowered by any writer, and G-7's target is exactly `99999999999999`.
72. A floor advance is refused while any blocking permit exists, in both interleavings with an in-flight reservation.
73. The released public route's 14-digit behaviour is unchanged for non-provider consumers, and a 17-digit timestamp is emitted only by the prepared route, with a caller-supplied, calendar-valid value.

**Back-catalogue, API and reporting**
74. Every Crossref POST inside a back-catalogue attempt holds its own unit permit; the outer attempt holds none.
75. An outer attempt cannot terminally close over a `RESERVED`, `AUTHORIZED` or blocking `INDETERMINATE` unit permit, and no aggregate conceals a mixed outcome.
76. `claimDistributionJobs` is permanently bound to `{PUBLISHER_BACK_CATALOGUE}`; naming `WORK_UPSERT` is refused as a whole.
77. `claimWorkUpsertJobs` requires a non-empty profile list; an empty list is an error, never "all".
78. The generation wire type is one lossless representation used identically in SQL, Rust, SDL, the client contract, inputs, outputs, reports and tests.
79. Reporting is work-level only, superuser only, with `workId: Uuid!` and no `kinds` argument, and distinguishes the source generation, the two resolution generations and the outstanding residue by name.
80. No generic provider-effect field exists on any generic table.
81. **`route` is never a caller input**; four route-specific reservation mutations assign it server-side.
82. **A `DISSEMINATION_WORKER` cannot obtain a `MANUAL_RECOVERY` permit by any input it can form**, and a `SUPERUSER` can complete the whole manual-recovery contract without a worker claim token.
83. For finalisation, reporting and voiding, the required role is derived from the persisted `route` and checked on every invocation, and the observed accept/refuse matrix over the four principal shapes — replay, repeat and role-removal cases included — equals §19.4's table exactly (T257).
84. `reservationToken` is returned only by the four reservation mutations, as `CrossrefWriteReservation.reservationToken`, and accepted only by finalisation, the outcome report and the route-owner void; no permit, finalisation-result, report or query type exposes it (T170).

**Ledger and gates**
85. Recognition is parse-independent and the five malformed sentinelled bodies are counted members.
86. All twelve record schemas, six sub-objects and 23 enum sets are closed, and every numeric field has an exact grammar and domain.
87. The register is immutable and digest-pinned, G12 holds along every chain, and revocation is prospective only.
88. The Case-A sequence is hold → restoration start → restoration terminal → reconciliation, every arrow a contract row, and neither reconciliation kind is ever G-7 eligible.
89. G-7 order is floor lock → census → Class 2 → Class 1B → Class 1A → mutation, and a consumed attempt is never Class 1A.
90. The 30 positive fixtures pass schema, graph, authority and JCS validation separately, and all 27 negatives are rejected by the stated rule at the stated step.
91. No G-6 step contacts Crossref.

**Release discipline**
92. The candidate diff touches no path outside §24.1 and §24.2 and no prohibited path.
93. Migration 1 contains exactly three `ADD VALUE` statements and Migration 2 contains everything else, in §23.3's executable order; both sequences of §23.4 are green (T294).
94. **The two migration directories are exactly `20260910_v1.10.0` and `20260911_v1.10.0`**, asserted by test; no other directory is added; and no two directories under `thoth-api/migrations/` share a Diesel version, derived from the directory name as §23.1 defines it.
95. Every count in §30.2 is reproducible from the delivered source and the delivered document.
96. Merging, deploying and migrating do not enable capture, execution, admission or any provider write.

**R52B corrections**
97. No generic substrate path — administrative cancellation, `WORK_DELETED` retirement, lease recovery, binding supersession, assignment disablement, completion or failure — writes a permit row. The only writers are the Crossref-owned operations of §16 and the referential `SET NULL` of rule S1 (INV-GENERIC-PERMIT).
98. A permit can be inserted only as a clean `RESERVED` row. `RESERVED → AUTHORIZED` re-proves, in the database, the witness, the fence, the absence of an overlapping blocking permit, history and the floor.
99. Every **BE-06-owned** transaction acquires locks as a subsequence of the TLO `P → I → W → G → J → A → Q → K → F → X → M`, apart from acquisitions that cannot wait (§10.1 rule 6); inside a released editorial or deleting transaction BE-06 acquires nothing before completion but its own queue rows, and generation rows only at completion (§10.1 rules 7 and 8). Void and report take `X` only; reconciliation takes `A` then `X`; no path takes `X` before `A`, or `Q` before `P`.
100. Finalisation evaluates request authorization (C0) before any lock, then C1–C4, then group B, then group A. It returns exactly `AUTHORIZED`, `VOIDED_RETRYABLE` or `VOIDED_JOB_RETIRED`, or a group-C refusal that mutates nothing, and an identical retry by an authorized caller replays the recorded result.
101. After `VOIDED_RETRYABLE` on `WORK_UPSERT`, `failDistributionJob(retryable = true)` returns the job to `PENDING`, and the next claim's `claimed_generation` is the current source generation.
102. Every reservation route creates a missing generation row at `0` under `W` and reads the witness under `G FOR UPDATE`. No reservation bumps a generation.
103. At most one permit exists per `WORK_UPSERT` attempt, for all time.
104. On the §25.17 fixtures, `observedDois` extraction under §16.11 returns exactly the registration membership and refuses the three malformed artifacts.
105. Registration membership counts the root DOI only when the root has a landing page, and every digest-feeding ordering uses `COLLATE "C"`.
106. The prepared route accepts exactly the 17-digit values `crossref_ts_decode` accepts. It refuses every other value with HTTP 400 `CROSSREF_TIMESTAMP_NOT_DECODABLE` before any query.
107. `crossref_ts_now()` is the only clock. It is `VOLATILE`, UTC and millisecond-truncated, is based on `clock_timestamp()`, and is evaluated exactly once per reservation, after `K` and `F`.
108. History excludes only `VOIDED` permits. No 17-digit value is ever produced for an instant at or after `10000-01-01T00:00:00.000Z`: `crossref_ts_encode` raises `CROSSREF_TIMESTAMP_OVERFLOW`, so the successor of `99991231235959999`, and an allocation that needs it, fail closed before any write.
109. A `RESERVED` permit can be voided only by:
    * its route owner, with the reservation token alone;
    * a superuser, with a persisted, non-blank authorization reference;
    * its own finalisation.

    No void acts on `AUTHORIZED`, `INDETERMINATE` or a terminal state.
110. Outcome reports are authenticated by the reservation token and need no live claim.
111. Reconciliation resolves `AUTHORIZED` and `INDETERMINATE` — an `INDETERMINATE` permit annotated `RECONCILIATION_IMPOSSIBLE` included — and confirms `ACCEPTED` and `NONE_ATTEMPTED`, so every fenced abandonment can be cleared.
112. Permit, membership, floor and audit rows cannot be deleted or truncated by any ordinary statement.
113. From gate 16 onward, no provider-writing path fetches the released public route. Every Crossref POST — legacy scheduled and manual dispatch included — carries the bytes of a finalised prepared artifact (§28.7).
114. A back-catalogue unit already `ACCEPTED` in the same outer job cannot be reserved again (`CROSSREF_UNIT_ALREADY_DEPOSITED_IN_JOB`).
115. The Diesel versions `20260910` and `20260911` are reserved repository-wide for BE-06. At preflight and at every gate §23.1 lists, no directory other than BE-06's two with either version exists on any covered programme or integration line, `feature/metrics` included, and every such check compares derived Diesel versions, not paths (§23.1, §31.1).

**Release topology (#903)**
116. The BE-06 diff is authored on `feature/publisher-services-v1-10--be-06`, branched from an exact `feature/publisher-services-v1-10` SHA bound by a later authorization, and merged only into `feature/publisher-services-v1-10`. No BE-06 change reaches `develop` except through the Publisher Services final integration of §27.3 gate 6c, after the Metrics `v1.9.0` final integration/release line is complete.

**R52B review corrections (#848 comment `5622203229`)**
117. The census counts an eligible Work with no generation row, or with a `0` row, as uncovered, and a positive row as covered, consulting no job, exactly as §21.3's statement does; admission is refused until the seed has visited every eligible Work, and an interrupted or partial seed is never admitted (T291, T292).
118. At `RESERVED → AUTHORIZED` the history reproof excludes the permit being authorised: a clean `RESERVED` permit authorises, an older overlapping non-`VOIDED` permit at or above its timestamp refuses it, and an older `VOIDED` one never constrains it.
119. No permit column claims the instant bytes were physically submitted. `provider_reported_at` is written once, only by the outcome report, from the server clock, and reconciliation neither writes nor changes it.
120. `RECONCILIATION_IMPOSSIBLE` is a non-terminal annotation that keeps the permit blocking until a later resolution supersedes it. Each reconciliation act's reference and time are written once and never overwritten, and a second annotation is refused.

**R52B second review corrections (#848 comment `5624224185`)**
121. Finalisation locks the permit's immutable `publisher_identity` `FOR SHARE` before `W`, re-resolves the binding under `W`, and reads no assignment, activation, admission, control or eligibility state unless the two are equal; a BE-03 configuration transaction on that publisher is serialised with it at `P` in both orders; T2's successor and the binding-refusal replacement are created only under the held publisher after the same re-resolution; and none of these schedules produces a `40P01`.
122. Finalisation, the outcome report and the route-owner void check the caller's route-derived role on every invocation, before any lock and before the token, state, replay or claim checks: a caller without the role is refused the released `Unauthorised` whatever the permit's state and whatever token it presents, no row changes, and no replay result, void result or landing signal is returned (T257).
123. Editorial capture creates no job. A capture and the seed of the same Work serialise on the Work's generation row, which the seed takes under `W FOR SHARE` and the editorial transaction's flush takes at its completion; the flush only inserts, advances or — for a Work the transaction deleted — removes generation rows, and the seed only inserts, raises from `0` to `1`, or observes them; and the job is created by the materializer after admission, at the captured generation (T290, T325).

**R52B third review corrections (#848 comment `5632920797`)**
124. Once `setWorkUpsertExecution(profile, false)` has returned, no claim of that profile returns an attempt opened on an `execution_enabled = true` read, no `WORK_UPSERT` reservation commits on one, and no attempt becomes fenced or `AUTHORIZED` on one. Every consumer holds the profile's execution gate `Q` in SHARE mode from before its read to its commit, and the transition holds it EXCLUSIVE. Executed in both orders for the claim, the reservation and finalisation, with negative controls (T280–T283).
125. The pause is non-destructive and disables execution only. A fenced attempt's report, completion and T2 successor, capture, generation advancement and materialization neither wait for the gate nor are refused by it. The transition writes one column of one control row. No generic path row-locks a control, admission or assignment row, and no path takes `Q` before `P`. The gate adds no `40P01` against the coordinator, deletion, reservation `K`/`F`/`X`, reconciliation, the voids, the report or G-7 (T284–T289).
126. The bootstrap seed inserts no job, target or attempt and never calls the creation helper, and admission creates no job. After admission the materializer's drain creates exactly one job per residue Work, at its durable generation, and a second drain creates nothing. The helper's call sites remain exactly the three of §11.3 (T290; statically, T203).

**R52B fourth review corrections (#848 comment `5635363920`)**
127. Production migration is executed only at gate 10, after gate 9's authorization and before gate 11's deployment, by the `v1.10.0` release artifact's own `thoth migrate`, run once as a one-off task while the backend still serves the pre-BE-06 release. No deployment system, migration service, sidecar or bootstrap container is added, and `thoth migrate --revert` is never run against a BE-06 database (§23.5, §23.6; T295, T302).
128. Gate 11 deploys the `v1.10.0` backend only onto a database whose Diesel ledger holds `20260910` and `20260911` and whose post-migration exit check is recorded, so that its `init` finds nothing pending (T295, T299, T301).
129. Over the migrated schema, the release in production at gate 9 differs from its own-schema behaviour only in the three ways §23.6 lists: its full test suite differs only in the BE-04 catalog-shape tests, the sequence of §23.6 passes, and the instrumentation sees no BE-06 write but generation rows (T296, T299).
130. The per-profile target-set invariant applies to `WORK_UPSERT` jobs only; every released back-catalogue job commits unchanged (T297).
131. No generation row is written for a Work that no longer exists: the flush resolves root existence in its own snapshot and removes any row it wrote for a root deleted while it waited, and every released Work, Imprint and Publisher deletion commits over the migrated schema (T298 and T299 on the withdrawn topology; T320, T321, T325 and T330 on this one).
132. Every SQL literal of a released enum is its stored label: `relation_type` is compared with `'has-child'` (T294).
133. A pre-BE-06 release is redeployed only after a query has shown that no row carries a Migration 1 label (§23.5; T300).
134. A deletion racing a propagated capture **waits and both commit**, in every schedule of §25.22 and §25.23 — T303's four, the rolled-back deletion, K1, K2, K4, K5, K6s, K6c, D2b, S01–S13 and the three `v1.10.0` deletion units — exactly as on the released schema, with no orphaned generation row and no queue residue (§10.6; T325–T328).

**R52B fifth review corrections (#848 comment `5637606889`; CTO decision `5646180203`)**
135. Exactly one flush applies a transaction's capture obligations, at that transaction's completion. No editorial transaction holds a generation row across any of its statements, and the flush's acquisitions are ascending by `work_id` within one statement (T318, T319, T326).
136. A caller cannot force the flush early. With deferrable constraints made immediate — before capture, between statements, repeatedly, or named on the flush's own constraint — no generation row is written before commit, and the obligations are still applied at completion. With the defence removed, the early flush and the deadlock it enables both reproduce (T318, T319, and their negative controls).
137. Obligations accumulated in a subtransaction that rolls back are discarded with it, and those of a released subtransaction survive, including when the rolled-back subtransaction holds the transaction's first edit (T317).
138. A deleting transaction's flush ends the generation of every Work it deleted **before** it advances any root, and no later step recreates it. A generation row written for a root a concurrent transaction has deleted is removed by its writer's own flush; with that cleanup removed, exactly one orphan appears (T320, T321, and its negative control).
139. No `work_upsert_capture_queue` row survives its transaction, after commit, rollback or a statement error, and every queue insert is matched by a delete in the same transaction (T322, T329).
140. `work_upsert_generation` is written by exactly five paths — the flush, the seed, the materialization unit, T2 completion and the four reservation routes — and the last four hold `W FOR SHARE` first. The containment statement of §8.7 is explicit, the `TRUNCATE` and `session_replication_role` boundaries are stated and executed, and a drift test fails CI if a sixth writer appears (T321, T324; T203 at implementation).
141. The two released facts §10.5's proof depends on are asserted by drift guards that fail CI: the released `work_relation` trigger updates both endpoints, and every capture owner is a Work the released statement that fired the capture has already locked.
142. Over the migrated schema the pre-BE-06 release differs from its own-schema behaviour in exactly the **two** ways §23.6 lists — no `40P01` residual among them — and its full suite differs only in the BE-04 catalog-shape tests (T329, T330).

**Acceptance criteria: 142.**

## 27. Release, rollout, rollback and stop conditions

### 27.1 Release line and branch topology (#903; #848 comment `5619637336`)

Re-evaluated against live state on 2026-09-10 (§1.1):
* `master` is `4fa7eaa9`, tree-identical to `v1.8.0`; no PR targets it; no `v1.9.0` or `v1.10.0` tag or release exists.
* `develop` is `395cc16a`.
* `feature/publisher-services-v1-10` is `395cc16a`, created from that `develop` SHA with no source commit (#903; #765 comment `5619631944`).
* `feature/metrics` (`79c13845`; `9feddcee` on 2026-09-11, §1.1) is the Metrics `v1.9.0` programme line. It owns `20260904_v1.9.0` and `20260905_v1.9.0` (§1.2).

The release model:
* **Release line: `v1.10.0`.** Publisher Services' Thoth release identity (#903). It is a release identity only, and authorizes no merge, release, migration, deployment or activation.
* **Programme integration branch: `feature/publisher-services-v1-10`.** Remaining Publisher Services Thoth slices branch from it and PR-target it — **never `develop`**.
* **BE-06 task branch: `feature/publisher-services-v1-10--be-06`**, with PR target `feature/publisher-services-v1-10`. Neither this document nor #903 authorizes creating it.
* **Implementation base: NONE.** After this specification passes fresh independent CRITICAL review, a later authorization binds an exact, freshly observed SHA of `feature/publisher-services-v1-10` as the base, and re-verifies there every source fact this document cites (§1.1, gate 2).
  * `395cc16a`, the SHA the integration branch was created at, is not a BE-06 base by that fact.
  * No SHA is a BE-06 base until that authorization names it.
* **Metrics isolation.** Metrics owns `v1.9.0` and is the absolute release priority.
  * BE-06 is never a prerequisite, compatibility burden or release-content dependency for any Metrics merge, release, migration, deployment or activation gate.
  * Metrics does not wait for BE-06.
  * BE-06 work never merges, rebases, refreshes or otherwise mutates `feature/metrics`.
  * The only constraint BE-06's authorities place on Metrics is the repository-wide reservation of the two Diesel versions `20260910` and `20260911` (#903 comment `5621256877`; #766 comment `5621260046`; §23.1). Metrics receives no other constraint.
* **No `feature/publisher-services-v1-10 → develop` integration** until the Metrics `v1.9.0` final integration/release line is complete. Only after that does Publisher Services incorporate the then-authoritative `develop` lineage into the integration branch, through a separately scoped, independently reviewed and explicitly authorized compatibility/integration step (gates 6a–6c), before any final integration into `develop`. This document authorizes neither step.
* **Release branch: `master`**, per repository-local doctrine (ADR-0011; #903). `v1.10.0` is released from `develop` to `master` only after the final integration. The release must prove that every `master...develop` delta it ships is production-ready (#848 body).
* The BE-06 source contains **no Metrics change**.
* `thoth-dissemination` releases from **`main`**, its established release branch under ADR-0011, through that repository's own process (§28). #903's topology is repository-local to `thoth-pub/thoth` and does not change it.
* The cross-repository order is unchanged (§27.2; #903, "Invariants").

### 27.2 Compatibility order

The order follows the gate numbering exactly:

```
R52B approved after fresh independent CRITICAL review
 → implementation authorization: an exact feature/publisher-services-v1-10 SHA bound; task branch authorized
 → BE-06 source on feature/publisher-services-v1-10--be-06, independently reviewed
 → merge into feature/publisher-services-v1-10
 → [Metrics v1.9.0 final integration/release line complete — owned by Metrics]
 → compatibility/integration: the then-authoritative develop lineage incorporated, reviewed and authorized
 → final integration feature/publisher-services-v1-10 → develop, separately authorized
 → v1.10.0 release preparation and release, develop → master
 → production migration authorization, against the release then in production (gate 9)
 → production migration: M1 + M2 by the release artifact's own `thoth migrate`, the backend still on the previous release (gate 10)
 → migrated schema and inert control state verified (gate 10's exit check)
 → v1.10.0 backend deployed; its init finds nothing pending (gate 11)
 → capture enable; per-publisher bootstrap, census and admission
 → G-6 rehearsal
 → separate thoth-dissemination task: every Crossref write path moved onto the permit protocol
 → dissemination independently reviewed and released from main
 → Crossref execution enable — the cutover gate
 → G-7 floor advance
 → observation and reconciliation
 → legacy selector retirement
```

BE-06 does not wait for the bracketed Metrics condition before merging into its own integration branch, and does not hasten it.

**Release is not migration authorization; migration authorization is not migration execution; migration execution is not deployment; deployment is not activation.** Merge is none of them. Between gates 10 and 11 the previous release serves on the migrated schema; §23.6 proves that window, and gate 9 proves it again against the release then in production.

### 27.3 The gates

Twenty numbered gates, with three integration sub-gates, 6a–6c, added by #903's topology. The fourth review-correction pass put gates 9–11 into execution order: production migration authorization is gate 9 (was 10), production migration gate 10 (was 11), and backend deployment gate 11 (was 9). Every cross-reference in this document, the historical rows of §32 included, uses that numbering; every other gate keeps its number.

| # | Gate | Stop condition |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | specification approval | any CFX or PPP item unresolved; the release line, integration branch or migration identities of §23.1 and §27.1 contradicted by a later control record; the Diesel-version reservation of §23.1 no longer current, or either reserved version consumed on any covered line |
| 2 | implementation authorization | no exact, freshly observed `feature/publisher-services-v1-10` SHA named as the base; the task branch `feature/publisher-services-v1-10--be-06` not authorized, or another name used; any cited source fact not re-verified at that SHA; the §23.1 Diesel-version scan not run over every covered line, or either reserved version found outside BE-06's two directories |
| 3 | implementation completion | any write outside §24; the §25.15 Diesel-version test not green; either reserved version found outside BE-06's two directories on any covered line |
| 4 | independent exact-head source and migration review | drift between the reviewed head and the merge head; either reserved version found outside BE-06's two directories on any covered line |
| 5 | SHA-bound merge authorization | authorization not naming the exact head, or naming a target other than `feature/publisher-services-v1-10`; either reserved version found outside BE-06's two directories on any covered line |
| 6 | merge into `feature/publisher-services-v1-10` | any Metrics path in the diff; any other target; the §25.15 Diesel-version test not green; either reserved version found outside BE-06's two directories on any covered line |
| 6a | Metrics `v1.9.0` completion — a precondition owned by Metrics | the Metrics `v1.9.0` final integration/release line not complete |
| 6b | compatibility/integration: the then-authoritative `develop` lineage incorporated into `feature/publisher-services-v1-10` | not separately scoped, independently reviewed and explicitly authorized; two directories under `thoth-api/migrations/` sharing a Diesel version, or any directory other than BE-06's two with version `20260910` or `20260911` (§23.1); §23.4's sequences or the §25 suites not green on the integrated tree; `thoth-api/src/schema.rs` not regenerated; any §3 fact BE-06 relies on changed by the incorporated lineage without a reviewed specification amendment |
| 6c | final integration `feature/publisher-services-v1-10 → develop` | gate 6a or 6b not passed; not separately authorized; either reserved version found outside BE-06's two directories on any covered line |
| 7 | release preparation (`v1.10.0`) | before gate 6c; CHANGELOG entry missing or naming the wrong issue; a release label another programme line already uses; any `master...develop` delta in the release not proved production-ready; any directory other than BE-06's two with Diesel version `20260910` or `20260911` in the release tree or on any covered line |
| 8 | release authorization | any directory other than BE-06's two with Diesel version `20260910` or `20260911` in the release tree or on any covered line |
| 9 | production migration authorization | the rollback of each state of §23.5 not agreed; §23.6's differential and sequence not re-run, and green, against the exact release then in production and the exact `v1.10.0` release artifact, on a copy of production's schema; **the schedules of §25.23 (T325–T328) not re-run, and green, on that copy**; the production Diesel ledger already records version `20260910` or `20260911` |
| 10 | production migration: Migration 1 and Migration 2, by the `v1.10.0` release artifact's own `thoth migrate` run once as a one-off task, the backend still serving the previous release | gate 9 not passed; either migration sequence not green in staging; the production Diesel ledger records version `20260910` or `20260911` immediately before the run; any command but `thoth migrate`, `--revert` included; the backend already on `v1.10.0`. **Exit check**, recorded before gate 11: the ledger holds both versions; `work_upsert_control` is exactly `('CROSSREF', false, false)`; the floor is `0`; no BE-06 row other than a generation row exists; the previous release still serves |
| 11 | backend deployment (`v1.10.0`) | gate 10's exit check not recorded; the production Diesel ledger lacks `20260910` or `20260911`, so the deployed `init` would migrate; any BE-06 row other than a generation row exists; any control row not `('CROSSREF', false, false)` |
| 12 | capture enable | any deployed worker still able to claim `WORK_UPSERT` by an empty list; any control row not `(false,false)` |
| 13 | per-publisher bootstrap, census, admission | census non-empty under §21.3's statement, which counts an eligible Work with no generation row, or with a `0` row, as uncovered; residual not recorded and frozen; any `WORK_UPSERT` job created by the seed or by admission |
| 14 | G-6 rehearsal | any stop condition of §27.4 |
| 15 | dissemination integration and release | any §28.7 conformance transcript missing or failing; any write path that fetches the released route for a deposit, POSTs before an `AUTHORIZED` result, skips the digest recheck, or cannot void, report or recover |
| 16 | Crossref execution enable — **the cutover** | gate 15 not live in production from `main`; any Crossref POST in the preceding observation window not linked to an `AUTHORIZED` permit; any publisher in the legacy selector's set without an enabled `CROSSREF` assignment; `manual_disseminate.yml` still able to POST for `Crossref` outside `MANUAL_RECOVERY` |
| 17 | G-7 floor advance | not drained; not eligible; not separately authorized |
| 18 | observation and reconciliation | any blocking permit unresolved, or any abandoned reservation neither finalised nor voided |
| 19 | legacy selector retirement | the exact condition of §15.6 not met |
| 20 | closure | any residue growing without drain, or any unreconciled permit |

**BE-06 merge, migration or deployment must not automatically activate external execution.** Gates 6b and 6c are separately authorized integration acts. Gates 9, 10 and 11 are three separately authorized acts, in that order: authorizing the migration does not execute it, and executing it does not deploy. Gates 12, 13, 16 and 17 are each explicit operator acts.

### 27.4 Stop conditions

Halt on any of the following.

**Ledger and G-6:**
* any Class-2 ledger state; any `CONFLICT_UNRECONCILABLE`;
* any unassignable or malformed recognised record; any digest mismatch; any fork;
* any attempt with two closing records;
* any register digest drift, or an unfetchable snapshot;
* drift in the eleven revalidated identities;
* any controlled backend with `track_counts` false or `session_replication_role ≠ origin`;
* any non-zero Class-4 count; any subscription row; `stats_reset` differing between samples;
* the absolute deadline exceeded;
* any success attempt terminal without a durable bound observation terminal;
* a floor advance attempted while not drained.

**Permits and provider writes:**
* a provider write attempted without an `AUTHORIZED` permit;
* **a claim, reservation or finalisation that read `execution_enabled = true` committing after the `setWorkUpsertExecution(false)` that replaced it had returned** — a breach of the execution gate (§21.1);
* a fenced attempt cancelled, deleted or replayed;
* a permit, membership, floor or audit row deleted or truncated;
* a permit reaching `AUTHORIZED` without a bound payload digest;
* **a generic substrate path writing a permit row;**
* **a Crossref POST of bytes fetched from the released route after gate 16;**
* **a `MANUAL_RECOVERY` reservation before gate 16** — a 17-digit deposit would make every later 14-digit legacy deposit for the same DOI fail at Crossref.

**Migration and deployment:**
* Migration 1 or Migration 2 executed in production by anything but the `v1.10.0` release artifact's own `thoth migrate` at gate 10, or before gate 9;
* a `v1.10.0` backend deployed onto a database whose ledger lacks `20260910` or `20260911`, which its `init` would migrate;
* `thoth migrate --revert` run against a database holding either version;
* a pre-BE-06 release deployed or restarted while any row carries a Migration 1 label;
* between gate 10 and gate 12, any BE-06 row other than a generation row or a transient capture-queue row, or a control row other than `('CROSSREF', false, false)`;
* a `work_upsert_capture_queue` row that outlives its transaction;
* a generation row advanced by an editorial transaction **before** that transaction's completion — an early flush (§8.6);
* a generation row for a Work that does not exist, written by any path other than the five of §8.7, or surviving the commit of the transaction that deleted its Work;
* any BE-06-introduced `40P01`: a schedule of §25.22 or §25.23 that the released schema commits and the migrated schema does not;
* §23.6's re-run at gate 9 showing any difference beyond the three it lists.

**Operations and scope:**
* residue growing across consecutive drains;
* a `WORK_UPSERT` job, target or attempt created by the seed, by admission, or by any path other than the three creation sites (§11.3);
* any write to a prohibited path;
* a BE-06 PR targeting `develop` or `master`, or a `feature/publisher-services-v1-10 → develop` integration before gates 6a and 6b;
* any mutation of `feature/metrics` by BE-06 work;
* any directory other than BE-06's two with Diesel version `20260910` or `20260911` on any covered line — a breach of the repository-wide reservation (§23.1).
