## 28. Cross-repository dependency

BE-06 may inspect but **must not edit** `thoth-dissemination`. The later dissemination task consumes exactly this released API contract. Every seam below is named at `thoth-dissemination main 0be4dfba`, which is content-identical to `develop 9a0c252c` (§3.7), so that repository has nothing to guess.

### 28.1 The one downstream seam, and its callers

Every dissemination Crossref write converges on **`crossrefuploader.py:71-163 CrossrefUploader.upload_to_platform()`** (§3.7). Exactly one call in it changes: the fetch at `:90`, `self.get_formatted_metadata('doideposit::crossref')`, which reaches the released public route through `uploader.py:222-226`. The dissemination task replaces that fetch with the protocol of §28.2, and places the released POST (`:132-143`) and acceptance check (`:153-158`) inside it. No other dissemination code writes to Crossref.

| Entry point | Route | Path to the seam | Credential the path must hold |
|---|---|---|---|
| `cr_bulk_disseminate.yml` (hourly at `:45`) → `bulk_disseminate.yml` → `disseminate.yml` → `disseminator.py --platform Crossref` | `LEGACY_SCHEDULED` | `CrossrefUploader` | a `DISSEMINATION_WORKER` token. **New** for this path: `disseminate.yml` carries no Thoth worker credential for Crossref today (§3.7) |
| `manual_disseminate.yml` (`workflow_dispatch`) → `disseminate.yml` | `MANUAL_RECOVERY`, for `platform: Crossref` | `CrossrefUploader` | a `SUPERUSER` token and an operator authorization reference, supplied per dispatch; otherwise refused before any POST (§28.6) |
| `distribution_job_worker.yml` → `distribution_job_worker.py` → `crossref_worker_runner.py:158-161 _deposit` | `PUBLISHER_BACK_CATALOGUE`, one permit per unit | `CrossrefUploader`, once per unit | the existing `THOTH_WORKER_TOKEN`, plus the outer job's id and claim token. The runner (`crossref_worker_runner.py:95`, `run(work_id, expected_publisher_id, …)`) does not receive either today |
| the work-level worker the dissemination task adds | `WORK_UPSERT` | the same protocol | `THOTH_WORKER_TOKEN`, plus the work-level claim token |

### 28.2 The protocol, identical on every route

Every step names the exact API operation or byte-level action.

| Step | Action | On failure |
|---|---|---|
| **R** reserve | the route's reservation mutation (§19.4). It returns `permitId`, `reservationToken`, `crossrefTimestamp`, `doiBatchId` and `dois` | any refusal: no fetch and no POST; nothing to clean up |
| **S** store | keep `(permitId, reservationToken)` for the rest of the run, durably where the runtime allows. **Never log the token** | — |
| **F** fetch | `GET {export}/specifications/doideposit::crossref/work/{rootWorkId}/prepared/{crossrefTimestamp}`, using `crossrefTimestamp` verbatim | void (step V), then stop |
| **E** extract | from the exact response bytes: `payloadDigest`, the lower-case hex SHA-256; `observedDois` per §16.11; `observedDoiBatchId`, the text of `/doi_batch/head/doi_batch_id`; `observedCrossrefTimestamp`, the text of `/doi_batch/head/timestamp` | extraction refused: void (V), then stop |
| **Z** finalise | `finaliseCrossrefWrite(permitId, reservationToken, claimToken, observedDois, observedDoiBatchId, observedCrossrefTimestamp, payloadDigest)`, with `claimToken` present exactly on the job-linked routes | per the discriminant (§28.3) |
| **D** recheck | recompute the SHA-256 of the bytes about to be sent, and compare it with the bound `payloadDigest`. The released DOI-prefix check (`:92-126`) stays | mismatch: no POST; report `NONE_ATTEMPTED` |
| **P** POST | released `:132-143`, exactly those bytes | — |
| **O** report | `reportCrossrefWrite(permitId, reservationToken, outcome)`, by the rule below | lost response: repeat the report; `CROSSREF_PERMIT_ILLEGAL_TRANSITION` then means the first report landed |
| **J** job | job-linked routes only (§28.3) | — |
| **V** void | `voidCrossrefWriteReservation(permitId, reservationToken, detail)`, valid only while the permit is `RESERVED` | lost response: repeat; `CROSSREF_PERMIT_VOID_REQUIRES_RESERVED` then means the void, or a finalisation, already landed |

**The report rule, exactly:**
* `ACCEPTED` when the response body contains the released success text (`:153-158`).
* `NONE_ATTEMPTED` when provably no request byte was sent: step D failed, or the connection failed before the request was written.
* `INDETERMINATE` in every other case: a timeout, a reset after sending, or any response without the success text. A superuser reconciles it against Crossref's own submission records (§16.8).

This is deliberately conservative. A definite rejection is recorded `INDETERMINATE` and reconciled, never guessed as `NONE_ATTEMPTED`.

### 28.3 The finalisation discriminant, per route, and the exact job calls

The discriminant is §16.6's. What each caller does next:

| Result | `WORK_UPSERT` | `PUBLISHER_BACK_CATALOGUE` (per unit) | `LEGACY_SCHEDULED` | `MANUAL_RECOVERY` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `AUTHORIZED` | D → P → O. After `ACCEPTED`: `completeDistributionJob`. After `NONE_ATTEMPTED` or `INDETERMINATE`: `failDistributionJob`, with the code from the table below | D → P → O; the unit is done. The outer job completes only when no unit permit is `RESERVED`, `AUTHORIZED` or blocking `INDETERMINATE` | D → P → O; the run ends | D → P → O; the dispatch ends |
| `VOIDED_RETRYABLE` | discard the bytes; `failDistributionJob(errorCode = CROSSREF_PERMIT_VOIDED_RETRYABLE, errorDetail = voidReason, retryable = true)`. The next claim projects the current generation | discard; skip the unit and record it in the attempt report | discard; the run ends without a POST | discard; the operator decides |
| `VOIDED_JOB_RETIRED` | discard; **no job call** | never returned on this route | never returned | never returned |
| group-C refusal | nothing mutated. After `CROSSREF_PERMIT_CLAIM_STALE`: void with the reservation token, then stop. After a lost response followed by `CROSSREF_PERMIT_ILLEGAL_TRANSITION`: repeat with the identical digest, which replays. After `Unauthorised`: the credential lacks the route's role; stop, because no retry succeeds until it is restored | same, per unit | same, with no claim | same, as superuser |

**The exact `failDistributionJob.errorCode` values.** These are set by the worker, not raised by the API. Each satisfies the released grammar (§25.1).

| `errorCode` | When | `retryable` |
|---|---|---|
| `CROSSREF_PERMIT_VOIDED_RETRYABLE` | finalisation returned `VOIDED_RETRYABLE`; `errorDetail` carries the `voidReason` | `true` |
| `CROSSREF_ARTIFACT_REFUSED` | step E refused the bytes; the reservation was voided first | `true` |
| `CROSSREF_PREPARED_FETCH_FAILED` | step F failed; the reservation was voided first | `true` |
| `CROSSREF_PROVIDER_NONE_ATTEMPTED` | `NONE_ATTEMPTED` was reported after `AUTHORIZED` | `true` |
| `CROSSREF_PROVIDER_INDETERMINATE` | `INDETERMINATE` was reported | the API accepts either value (§10.8 rows 3 and 5). The released dissemination rule — a code is never retryable once an external write may have started (`distribution_job_worker.py:779`) — selects `false` |
| the API's refusal code, verbatim | a reservation refusal, except `CROSSREF_PERMIT_CLAIM_STALE`, after which no job call is possible | `true` for `CROSSREF_PERMIT_BLOCKED`, `CROSSREF_BINDING_MOVED_RETRY` and `WORK_UPSERT_EXECUTION_NOT_PERMITTED`; `false` otherwise |

The dissemination task adds these codes to its own closed failure taxonomy. The API validates only the released grammar.

### 28.4 The four route transcripts

**`WORK_UPSERT`**

```
materializeWorkUpsertJobs([CROSSREF], 100)             → created ≥ 1
claimWorkUpsertJobs([CROSSREF], 1, 900)                → J, claim C, claimedGeneration g
reserveWorkUpsertCrossrefWrite(J, C)                   → X, token R, T (17 digits), B, dois S
GET …/doideposit::crossref/work/w/prepared/T           → bytes b
extract(b)                                             → h = sha256(b); S′ = S, B′ = B, T′ = T
finaliseCrossrefWrite(X, R, C, S′, B′, T′, h)          → AUTHORIZED            (fenced_at set in the same commit)
sha256(b) = h ; POST b                                 → "Your batch submission was successfully received."
reportCrossrefWrite(X, R, ACCEPTED)                    → ACCEPTED
completeDistributionJob(J, C)                          → SUCCEEDED             (fenced; ACCEPTED at g)
```

* **Source changed during preparation:** finalise returns `VOIDED_RETRYABLE / SOURCE_CHANGED_DURING_PREPARATION`. The worker calls `failDistributionJob(J, C, CROSSREF_PERMIT_VOIDED_RETRYABLE, …, true)` and J returns to `PENDING`. The next claim gets `g′ = S` (T224).
* **Binding moved:** finalise returns `VOIDED_JOB_RETIRED / BINDING_SUPERSEDED`; J is `CANCELLED`; no job call (T226).
* **Crash before finalisation:**
  1. X stays `RESERVED` and blocking.
  2. Lease expiry leaves the attempt `ABANDONED` and **unfenced**, and the claim guard keeps J unclaimable while X blocks.
  3. Cleanup: either a restarted worker calls `voidCrossrefWriteReservation(X, R, …)`, or a superuser calls `voidCrossrefWriteReservationAsSuperuser(X, …, reference)`.
  4. J becomes claimable, and its next attempt reserves afresh (T234, T235).
* **Crash after `AUTHORIZED`:**
  1. The attempt is `ABANDONED` and **fenced**, so the claim is blocked.
  2. A superuser consults Crossref's submission records, then calls `reconcileCrossrefWritePermit(X, ACCEPTED | NONE_ATTEMPTED, RECONCILED, reference)`.
  3. The fenced abandonment is cleared in the same transaction.
  4. The next attempt deposits at a strictly later timestamp (T211, T214).
* **Crash after `ACCEPTED`, before completion:** reconciliation **confirms** the permit, and clearance follows. The job is claimed again and redeposits at a later timestamp (§10.8 row 9).

**`PUBLISHER_BACK_CATALOGUE`**

```
claimDistributionJobs(kinds: [PUBLISHER_BACK_CATALOGUE])   → outer O, claim C          (released)
for each unit root u, one crossref_worker_runner.py subprocess each:
  reserveBackCatalogueCrossrefWrite(O, C, u)          → X_u, R_u, T_u, B_u, S_u
      CROSSREF_UNIT_ALREADY_DEPOSITED_IN_JOB          → skip u: already deposited in this outer job
      CROSSREF_PERMIT_BLOCKED                          → skip u; record it
  F → E → finaliseCrossrefWrite(X_u, R_u, C, …)
      AUTHORIZED → D → P → reportCrossrefWrite(X_u, R_u, outcome)
      VOIDED_RETRYABLE → skip u; record it
completeDistributionJob(O, C)                          → SUCCEEDED, or OUTER_ATTEMPT_HAS_OPEN_PERMITS
```

* **Crash after N of M units:** the unit permits survive in their true states, and lease expiry leaves the outer attempt `ABANDONED`. The next attempt:
  - skips units already `ACCEPTED` in this outer job (T256);
  - retries units whose latest permit is `NONE_ATTEMPTED` or `VOIDED`, or that have none;
  - is blocked on `INDETERMINATE` units until they are reconciled.

  `RESERVED` leftovers are voided by the owner or a superuser.
* **Outer cancellation** leaves every unit permit untouched (T222).

**`LEGACY_SCHEDULED`**

```
cr_bulk_disseminate.yml → CrossrefIDFinder selects w                      (unchanged)
disseminator.py --work w --platform Crossref → CrossrefUploader
reserveLegacyScheduledCrossrefWrite(w)                 → X, R, T, B, S
    CROSSREF_PERMIT_BLOCKED | CROSSREF_PUBLISHER_NOT_COVERED | CROSSREF_PERMIT_EMPTY_DOI_SET → the run fails; no POST
F → E → finaliseCrossrefWrite(X, R, null, …)
    AUTHORIZED → D → P → reportCrossrefWrite(X, R, outcome)
    VOIDED_RETRYABLE → the run ends without a POST
```

* BE-06 does not retry a voided legacy run, and the selector's own selection semantics are unchanged. The durable work-level route covers the Work once it is admitted.
* **Crash:** a GitHub Actions run has no durable store for the token, and must not log it. A crashed run's `RESERVED` permit therefore blocks its DOIs until a superuser voids it; the operator finds it through `crossrefUnresolvedPermits` (§20). Blocking is the fail-safe direction.

**`MANUAL_RECOVERY`**

```
manual_disseminate.yml (workIds, platform: Crossref, authorizationReference; SUPERUSER credential)
for each w:
  reserveManualRecoveryCrossrefWrite(w, authorizationReference)    → X, R, T, B, S
  F → E → finaliseCrossrefWrite(X, R, null, …)                     (SUPERUSER, derived from the route)
  AUTHORIZED → D → P → reportCrossrefWrite(X, R, outcome)          (SUPERUSER)
```

* A dispatch without a superuser credential and a non-blank reference is refused before any reservation.
* **No `MANUAL_RECOVERY` reservation may be made before gate 16** (§27.4). A 17-digit deposit would make every later 14-digit legacy deposit for the same DOI fail at Crossref.

### 28.5 The end-to-end compatibility transcript (R52B-1)

```
t0  released production. The legacy selector picks w; the released route serves a 14-digit document; POST.
    Crossref holds w's DOIs at a 14-digit version v14.
t1  BE-06 migrated (gates 9 and 10), then deployed (gate 11), INERT. No permit exists, and legacy POSTs continue unchanged.
    No work-level job can be claimed (execution disabled), and no MANUAL_RECOVERY reservation is allowed.
t2  capture enabled; the publisher bootstrapped, censused and admitted (gates 12–13).
    Generation rows are ≥ 1. There is still no claim.
t3  G-6 (gate 14). No Crossref contact.
t4  the dissemination release deploys from main (gate 15), after the old release's in-flight disseminate.yml runs finish.
    The legacy selector still picks w, but its provider write is now:
      reserveLegacyScheduledCrossrefWrite(w) → T17 (> floor 0; history empty) → prepared route with T17
      → finalise AUTHORIZED → recheck → POST (17-digit) → Crossref: T17 > v14, an ordinary update → ACCEPTED
    From t4, no dissemination path fetches the released route for a deposit.
t5  Crossref execution enabled (gate 16). A WORK_UPSERT job for w reserves: history = {T17, ACCEPTED} → T′ > T17.
    A legacy selection of w while that permit is RESERVED or AUTHORIZED is refused CROSSREF_PERMIT_BLOCKED,
    and that legacy run ends without a POST.
t6  G-7 (gate 17): floor := 99999999999999. Every later allocation exceeds every 14-digit value, whatever the history.
t7  legacy selector retired (gate 19).
```

**Executed** (§25.17, T255):
* a legacy permit carries a 17-digit timestamp;
* a `WORK_UPSERT` reservation for the same root is refused while the legacy permit is `AUTHORIZED`;
* after the legacy permit is `ACCEPTED`, a `WORK_UPSERT` reservation made with a clock *behind* the legacy deposit receives a strictly greater, calendar-valid timestamp.

**Can a 14-digit provider write occur after universal permits are mandatory? No.**
* **Mechanically,** no authorization exists for anything but a prepared 17-digit artifact (§15.6).
* **By gate,** gate 16 requires the §28.7 conformance evidence for every dissemination write path.
* **At the provider,** a stray 14-digit submission after any 17-digit deposit for the same DOI is refused by Crossref's own timestamp ordering. That is defence in depth, not something R52B relies on.

A straggler from the old release that POSTs after t4 is refused by Crossref in exactly that way, and changes nothing.

### 28.6 `manual_disseminate.yml`

From the dissemination release onward, a dispatch with `platform: Crossref` must do one of two things:
* supply a superuser credential and an operator authorization reference, and use `MANUAL_RECOVERY`; or
* be refused before any POST.

Dispatches for every other platform are unchanged. Today this entry point can POST any listed Work to Crossref with no Thoth authorization at all (§3.7). It is a Crossref provider-write path that R52A did not account for.

### 28.7 Conformance evidence required at gates 15 and 16

For each route — `LEGACY_SCHEDULED`, `MANUAL_RECOVERY`, `PUBLISHER_BACK_CATALOGUE` and `WORK_UPSERT` — the dissemination task hands over a transcript against a test database and a stubbed Crossref endpoint. Each transcript covers:
1. the success path;
2. `VOIDED_RETRYABLE`, and for `WORK_UPSERT` also `VOIDED_JOB_RETIRED`;
3. a group-C refusal (`CROSSREF_PERMIT_CLAIM_STALE` on the job-linked routes);
4. an artifact refused by extraction (§16.11);
5. a digest-recheck failure, reported `NONE_ATTEMPTED`;
6. a POST timeout reported `INDETERMINATE`, then a superuser reconciliation;
7. a crash before finalisation, cleaned up by the owner void and, separately, by the superuser void;
8. a crash after `AUTHORIZED`, resolved by reconciliation, with the clearance observed;
9. a lost finalisation response, retried and replayed.

The task also hands over a static proof that no remaining code path fetches `doideposit::crossref` from the released route and then POSTs.

Gate 16 additionally requires one production observation window in which every Crossref POST is linked to an `AUTHORIZED` permit.

### 28.8 What the dissemination task must not do

* Never POST bytes other than those whose digest it bound.
* Never POST while its permit is `RESERVED`, or after it is closed.
* Never construct a DOI list and expect the API to honour it. Membership is API-derived, and worker values are only a cross-check.
* Never fetch a deposit from the released cached route.
* Never re-finalise a `VOIDED` reservation; reserve again.
* Never treat a group-C refusal as an ordinary retry.
* Never call `failDistributionJob` after `VOIDED_JOB_RETIRED`.
* Never log a reservation token.

### 28.9 Acceptance evidence BE-06 must hand over

* The built `schema.graphql` diff.
* The generated OpenAPI document, showing the prepared route.
* The error-code table of §25.1, and the worker codes of §28.3.
* The §16.11 reference extractor and its fixtures.
* **The BE-06 conformance transcript (§25.17, T258).** A scripted API client drives all four routes end to end, against a test database and a stubbed provider, through every branch of §28.7 that the API can observe.

The BE-06 transcript is the evidence for the contractual half of §16.10 on the API side. The dissemination transcripts of §28.7 are that repository's evidence.

---

## 29. Implementation report requirements

The implementation report must contain the following, with exact evidence and no summarised claims.

**Identity and scope**
* the exact head SHA implemented and reviewed;
* the exact `feature/publisher-services-v1-10` base SHA bound by the implementation authorization, the task branch name and the PR target;
* the migration identities actually used (`20260910_v1.10.0`, `20260911_v1.10.0`), and a scan of every covered line (§23.1) comparing derived Diesel versions, showing `20260910` and `20260911` only in BE-06's two directories;
* the full diff path list with line counts, compared against §24;
* the built `schema.graphql` diff and the OpenAPI diff.

**Concurrency and lock-order evidence**
* `EXPLAIN` output or `pg_locks` transcripts for each §25.5 concurrency test and each of the 39 interleavings of §25.17 (T205–T223), showing which session waited and the final state;
* the advisory-key collision test, with the actual colliding DOI pair and both acquisition orders;
* the §25.19 interleavings run against the implementation: the held publisher through finalisation, with a configuration writer and an ownership move in every order (T270–T274), and T2's successor (T275);
* the §25.20 interleavings run against the implementation, with `pg_locks` transcripts: the pause, enable and a repeated pause against the claim, the `WORK_UPSERT` reservation and finalisation in both orders (T280–T284); a fenced attempt's report, completion and successor through a pause (T285); capture and materialization during a pause (T286); the no-deadlock set (T287, T288); and the seed against capture, followed by census, admission and drain (T290).

**Permit, timestamp and artifact evidence**
* the permit state-machine matrix over all 30 ordered state pairs, and the insertion-guard and authorization-precondition bypass tests;
* the Rust/PostgreSQL timestamp-validity parity result over the §17.2 corpus, and the controlled-clock vectors of §17.3;
* the §16.11 extraction results on the §25.17 fixtures;
* the §25.18 rows run against the implementation: the timestamp domain guard (T259–T261), the authorization reproof (T265), the report time (T266), the reconciliation acts and their consequences (T267, T268) and the state machine (T269).

**Migration and ledger evidence**
* the two migration sequences run end to end, with timings and the observed lock modes on `work` and `distribution_job`;
* the full fixture corpus, with the four evidence classes reported separately per lineage and the regeneration reason recorded (§22.14);
* one complete comment body reproduced in full (§22.15);
* the census over an interrupted seed, and against a concurrent binding change, under generation coverage (T291, T292; T262–T264 are historical);
* §23.6's compatibility window re-run with the implementation's migrations and the release then in production: the released test suite over the migrated schema, differing only in the BE-04 catalog-shape tests, with the instrumentation's counts; the end-to-end sequence with the released server in service; and the enum boundary, the `init` hazard and both binaries' `--revert` (T294–T302);
* the deletion/capture interleavings of T303 against the implementation, with `pg_locks` transcripts;
* **the Alternative A schedules of §25.23 against the implementation**, with `pg_locks` transcripts: T325–T328 — T303's four schedules, the rolled-back deletion, K1, K2, K4, K5, K6s, K6c, D2b, S01–S13 and the three `v1.10.0` deletion units — each shown to commit where the released schema commits;
* **the flush properties, against the implementation's own migration**: T317's savepoint cases; T318 and T319 with their negative controls, showing that a forced `SET CONSTRAINTS … IMMEDIATE` advances nothing early and that removing the defence reproduces both the early flush and the `40P01`; T320, T321 with its negative control, T322 and T324;
* **the drift guards of §10.5** — the released `work_relation` trigger's two endpoints, and every capture owner already locked by the released statement — as tests that fail CI when either fact changes;
* the full T205–T293 race set re-run against the implementation's functions under the Alternative A capture (§25.17, §25.19, §25.20).

**Authorization and cross-repository evidence**
* the §19.4 authorization matrix as actually observed over the four principal shapes, with rule 3's replay, repeat and role-removal cases (T257), of which §25.19's T277 and T278 are the specification;
* the BE-06 conformance transcript of §28.9 (T258);
* the built-SDL token boundary (T170).

**Statements and self-checks**
* a statement that no Crossref network call occurred, with the mechanism that guaranteed it;
* a statement that no Metrics path and no `thoth-dissemination` file was touched;
* the recomputed counts of §30.2;
* an explicit list of anything in R52B that the implementation found to be wrong, with evidence.

### 30.2 Numerical self-check, recomputed for R52B

Every count below is recalculated from **this** document. None is carried forward.

1. **Triggers added: 34.**
   * Capture triggers: **16** (§8.2 has 16 rows).
   * The capture flush: **1**, a deferred constraint trigger on `work_upsert_capture_queue` (§8.6).

### 30.3 CFX-9: the complete generation-correctness argument

For every `(w, profile)` and every committed state, exactly one of four conditions holds, and every transition preserves the disjunction:

**(A) resolved** `S ≤ max(D, H)` · **(B) actionable** a PENDING or RUNNING job exists · **(C) residue** neither, and the Work exists · **(D) ended** the Work is deleted.

| Transition | Effect |
|---|---|
| capture on `w` or on any root of `w` | `S` increases **at that transaction's commit**, in its flush, atomically with the editorial change (§8.6); A→C or B stays B (a RUNNING job becomes stale, a PENDING job stays current) |
| cross-work propagation | identical, on the parent's row |
| ownership move | `S` increases (ownership columns are captured for every registered profile); a PENDING job becomes stale-bound and is C-equivalent until the drain retires and replaces it |
| worker absent for an arbitrary time | nothing changes; C persists; INV-RESIDUE |
| materialization | C→B, or C persists when ineligible or unadmitted |
| seed | a Work with no row enters C at `S = 1`, or a `0` row becomes `1`; nothing else moves, and no job is created |
| admission | nothing about `S`, `D` or `H` moves, and no job is created; the admitted binding's C becomes materializable |
| stale-PENDING rebinding | B→B under the current binding, or B→C when the current binding cannot yet be materialized |
| claim | B stays B; `claimed_generation` is frozen from `S` in the same statement |
| **reservation** | B stays B; nothing about `S`, `D` or `H` moves, and no authorization exists |
| **reservation creating a `0` generation row** (§16.4) | a new `(w, CROSSREF)` enters C with `S = 0`; nothing is materialized unless the Work is eligible, admitted and capture is enabled, and the seed turns `0` into `1` exactly once |
| **reservation voided** (route owner, superuser, or its own finalisation) | B stays B; the DOIs are released; a `WORK_UPSERT` attempt never reserves again (one permit per attempt), so its job is failed retryably or already retired, and the next attempt reserves afresh |
| **finalisation (fence pass)** | B stays B; `fenced_at` is set |
| finalisation group B (binding) | B→B (replacement) or B→C; the finalisation voids its own reservation |
| finalisation group A (`VOIDED_RETRYABLE`) | B stays B; the worker's retryable failure returns the job to `PENDING`, and the next claim projects the current `S` |
| source edit after claim | `S` increases; B stays B and becomes stale; a reservation in flight will fail its witness check and void |
| T2 completion | `D` rises to `claimed_generation`; A if `S ≤ max(D,H)`, else B (successor) or C (residue) |
| terminal FAILED or administrative CANCELLED | `H` rises to `gen(job)`; A or C, never silently A when `S` is higher |
| lease recovery | B stays B; at budget it becomes terminal FAILED, which is the row above |
| `ReplayBlocked` recovery block | B stays B but unclaimable; the obligation is visible in reporting |
| execution pause | no state changes; C and B both persist; every claim, reservation and finalisation that read `execution_enabled = true` has committed before the pause returns (§21.1) |
| capture disabled | impossible after enabling (monotone) |
| Work deletion | anything → D; the generation row is removed and actionable rows are retired; a `RESERVED` permit on them survives, blocking, until a Crossref-owned void; no path can create a new job, because the FK requires an existing Work |
| later materialization | C→B whenever the binding is eligible and admitted |

**Nothing disappears**: every transition either raises `D`/`H` in step with what was actually discharged, or leaves `S` above them, which is precisely condition C, which the drain later converts to B. No transition removes a generation without a corresponding discharge, and the only removal of an obligation is the intentional one at Work deletion. The reservation and authorization transitions are deliberately inert with respect to `S`, `D` and `H`: a permit is provider evidence, never resolution evidence.

### 30.4 Integration consistency, answered

| # | Question | Answer | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Which bytes may each of the four routes POST? | only a prepared, uncached, 17-digit artifact whose SHA-256 a finalisation bound; never the released route | §15.3, §15.6, §16.6, §28.2 |
| 2 | When does a provider write become permissible? | at the commit of `RESERVED → AUTHORIZED` inside `finaliseCrossrefWrite`, which writes `fenced_at` in the same transaction | §11.1, §16.6 |
| 3 | Who may release a reservation? | its route owner, with the reservation token; a superuser, with a persisted authorization reference; or its own finalisation. Nothing else | §16.7 |
| 4 | May a generic substrate path mutate a permit? | no. Outside the Crossref operations, only the referential `SET NULL` of rule S1 touches a permit | §6.2 INV-GENERIC-PERMIT, §16.2, T197–T204 |
| 5 | Where is the permit row in the lock order? | last: `P → I → W → G → J → A → Q → K → F → X → M`. Void and report take `X` alone; reconciliation takes `A`, then `X` | §10.1, §10.5, T205–T223 |
| 6 | What does a `RUNNING` job do after `VOIDED_RETRYABLE`? | `failDistributionJob(retryable = true)` returns it to `PENDING`, and the next claim projects the current source generation | §16.6, §28.3, T224 |
| 7 | What if the root Work has no generation row? | the reservation inserts `0` under `W`, reads the witness under `G FOR UPDATE`, and never bumps | §16.4, T231–T233 |
| 8 | Which DOIs does a deposit register? | exactly those at the three §16.11 paths, which the API's SQL membership equals | §14.3, §16.11, T244 |
| 9 | Can the prepared route emit an invalid timestamp? | no. Its validator equals PostgreSQL's strict decode on 360 039 strings | §15.3, §17.2, T248 |
| 10 | Which clock allocates the timestamp? | `crossref_ts_now()`: `clock_timestamp()` in UTC, truncated to milliseconds, read once, after `K` and `F` | §17.3, T253 |
| 11 | Can an old 14-digit provider write occur after universal permits are mandatory? | **no** | §15.6, §28.5 |
| 12 | Whose configuration authorizes a provider write? | the permit's `publisher_identity`'s, locked `FOR SHARE` before `W` and held through the authorization commit, and read only once the binding re-resolved under `W` equals it | §10.1, §16.6, T270–T274 |
| 13 | Can a replay be obtained without the route-derived role? | **no**: the role is checked on every invocation, before any lock, token, state or replay check | §16.6, §19.4, T277, T278 |
| 14 | Can an editorial transaction, the seed or admission create a job? | **no**: capture and the seed record generation state only, and admission inserts its own row; the materializer, the binding-refusal replacement and the T2 successor are the only creators | §11.3, §21.2, §21.3, T290 |
| 15 | Can an operation act on `execution_enabled = true` after the pause that replaced it has returned? | **no**: every consumer holds the execution gate `Q` SHARE from before its read to its commit, and the pause holds it EXCLUSIVE | §10.7, §16.6, §21.1, T280–T282 |

## 31. Remaining decisions

### 31.1 The migration-identity and release-line decision — resolved; Diesel versions reserved

The decision the blocked R52B candidate of 4,053 lines left open was taken by the CTO on 2026-09-10. It is recorded in #903 (`PS-BRANCH-01`) and #848 comment `5619637336`; #766 comment `5619634367` records the Metrics side, and #765 comment `5619631944` the programme side. It was corrected the same day by #903 comment `5621256877`, #766 comment `5621260046` and #848 comment `5621263642`:
* BE-06 belongs to the Publisher Services `v1.10.0` release line, delivered through `feature/publisher-services-v1-10` (§27.1).
* Its operative identities are `thoth-api/migrations/20260910_v1.10.0/` and `thoth-api/migrations/20260911_v1.10.0/` (§23.1).
* **Their Diesel versions, `20260910` and `20260911`, are reserved repository-wide for BE-06.** No other programme or branch, Metrics included, may create any directory whose name begins `20260910_` or `20260911_`, whatever its suffix, or any other directory with either derived version.
* The interim reservation of `20260910_v1.9.0` and `20260911_v1.9.0` (#848 `5618814472`; #766 `5618817876`) is withdrawn as a BE-06 path reservation. Their later release to the Metrics/shared `v1.9.0` namespace (the #903 issue text; #848 `5619637336`; #766 `5619634367`) is superseded: both names alias BE-06's versions, and are prohibited.
* `20260904_v1.9.0` and `20260905_v1.9.0` remain historical, rejected identities, owned by Metrics.

Of the options the blocked candidate listed, this is option A — new dates after every live line's migrations, with a release label no other line uses — chosen by the control plane, not by this document.

**Verified for the release/topology revision, and again for the Diesel-version reservation correction** (§1.2): both operative paths, and both Diesel versions, are unused on all 75 live remote heads, and no `v1.10.0` tag or release exists.

**Why the reservation is keyed on the Diesel version.** The executed probe of §23.1 showed that `20260910_v1.9.0` beside `20260910_v1.10.0` makes Diesel silently drop BE-06's Migration 1. The reservation therefore covers every directory whose derived version is `20260910` or `20260911`, whatever its name, and every check in §23.1 compares derived versions, not paths. It is controlling authority, and each gate §23.1 lists fails closed on a consumed version (§27.3). It replaces the recommended clarification and follow-up that the release/topology candidate recorded here (§6.3).

**Metrics.** The reservation removes two Diesel version keys from the Metrics migration namespace and nothing else. Metrics keeps the `v1.9.0` line and its priority, and does not wait for BE-06 (§27.1).

**For future allocations,** #903 comment `5621256877` requires every parallel programme branch to treat the Diesel version prefix, not the full directory name, as the collision identity.

### 31.2 Operational rulings needed before their gate, not before approval

1. **G-6 executability in the production topology.** It is unknown, and unknowable from source, whether all of the following can hold **simultaneously** for 1500 s:
   * seven controlled Class-1 backends plus one observer, with a sustained zero Class-4 count and zero subscription rows;
   * `track_counts` on and `session_replication_role = origin`;
   * a stable `pg_conf_load_time()` under B1;
   * the eleven revalidated identities stable between READY and START.

   If any one fails, G-6 is not executable, the rollout stops before it, and G-7 never becomes reachable. This is a measurement, not a design question.
2. **Whether G-7 authorisation may be delegated**, or must be a personal CTO act.
3. **Who publishes the authority register**, and where the canonical snapshot is posted. The model is settled (§22.3); only the publishing party is a ruling.
4. **Acceptance of the total-erasure governance residual** (§22.16).
5. **Whether the destructive rollback of §23.5's fourth row is acceptable** at the capture-enabled stage, or whether an evidence export is mandatory first. Default: export first.
6. **How `manual_disseminate.yml` obtains a superuser credential** for `platform: Crossref` dispatches (§28.6): a standing secret or a per-dispatch credential. This is the dissemination task's decision, under its own review. The API requires only that the caller hold `SUPERUSER` and supply a reference.

**Also before its gate:** the scope and authorization of the compatibility/integration step (§27.3 gate 6b). #903 assigns it to a separate, independently reviewed and explicitly authorized step, after Metrics `v1.9.0` completes.

**Also before gate 9:** who runs gate 10's one-off `thoth migrate` in production, with which database credential, and where its exit check is recorded; and the copy of production's schema on which gate 9 re-runs §23.6. The mechanism itself is fixed (§23.6): the release artifact's own image, command `migrate`, run once, with the backend still on the previous release.

R52A's item on who may void an abandoned reservation is **closed** by §16.7. The route owner voids with the reservation token, a superuser voids with a persisted authorization reference, and nothing else voids.

### 31.3 Pre-existing defects surfaced but not repaired here

Each is a separate task. BE-06 neither depends on nor worsens any of them.

7. The public export cache serves stale parent Crossref payloads, and replays a frozen `<timestamp>` (§3.8 items 1–2).
8. Several released default orderings have no tie-break, so `titles[0]`, the two-abstract truncation and the canonical-PDF `find()` are nondeterministic when rows tie (`model/title/mod.rs:39-46`, `model/abstract/mod.rs:57-64`, `model/location/mod.rs:253-260`, `model/publication/mod.rs:348-352`). BE-06 captures the deciding columns, so a change is never *missed*, but it cannot make the underlying selection deterministic.
9. A withdrawn Work whose imprint has no `crossmark_doi` emits no withdrawal signal at all (`doideposit_crossref.rs:573-583`).
10. The serializer panics, rather than returning an error, when a Work or an emitted chapter has zero title rows (`:212`, `:220`). §14.2 E6 makes that an eligibility precondition, which prevents BE-06 from triggering it but does not fix the panic.
11. **The released serializer can emit a document that registers nothing** — a root with a DOI but no landing page, and no DOI-bearing chapter (§3.8 item 3). BE-06 refuses such a reservation (`CROSSREF_PERMIT_EMPTY_DOI_SET`).
12. **`manual_disseminate.yml` can POST to Crossref today with no Thoth authorization** (§3.7). §28.6 binds it from the dissemination release onward.

### 31.4 Deliberately excluded, requiring separate approval if ever wanted

13. A work-level suppression or mute control (§12.2).
14. Any second work-level execution profile (§7.4).
15. Changing `distribution_job.publisher_id ON DELETE CASCADE` so that job history survives Publisher deletion (§13.6).
16. A 17-digit version floor (§17.4).

## 33. Disposition

**Standalone: yes.** §§4–28 cover, without reference to any superseded document:
* scope, architecture, data model and invariants;
* the total lock order, capture, materialization, lifecycle, fence, cancellation and deletion;
* the Crossref profile, the artifact protocol and its extraction contract, permits, timestamps and routes;
* API, reporting, admission and the ledger;
* migration, budget, tests, criteria, release topology, and the cross-repository contract.

**Frozen architecture preserved, with one CTO-approved amendment.** §6.1 restates all twenty-four rules, unchanged. §6.1a records the one amendment the CTO approved in #848 comment `5646180203`: the capture topology, and nothing else. Every other correction sits inside the boundary. Every correction sits inside the boundary:
* R52B-2 strengthens the frozen job-instance cancellation and deletion rules. Generic paths still cancel, retire and recover exactly as frozen, and additionally never touch profile state.
* R52B-3 keeps LO-5 exactly as frozen rule 8 states it, and totalises only the profile suffix composed after it.
* R52B-1, R52B-4 to R52B-9, R52B-11, R52B-12 and R52B-14 to R52B-22 are Crossref-profile mechanisms or cross-repository contract terms, which frozen rule 12 assigns to the profile.
* R52B-0, R52B-10 and R52B-13 are bookkeeping.
* The release/topology revision changes the release line, the migration identities and the branch topology only. #848 comment `5619637336` states that it leaves the frozen functional architecture unchanged, and no frozen rule is touched.
* The Diesel-version reservation correction changes the migration-identity rule only. #848 comment `5621263642` states that the frozen functional architecture, the `v1.10.0` release line and the topology are unchanged.
* The review-correction pass changes Crossref-profile mechanisms only — the timestamp encoder, one permit trigger rule, permit columns and constraints, the reconciliation acts and the census statement — which frozen rule 12 assigns to the profile. #848 comment `5622203229` records that none of its findings reopens the frozen architecture, and the non-terminal reconciliation model keeps frozen rule 11's replay-blocked composition with an exit for every fenced abandonment.
* The second review-correction pass keeps frozen rule 8's `P FOR SHARE → W FOR SHARE → …` exactly and fixes which `P` finalisation locks — the permit's own publisher — as frozen rule 10 requires of the fence; puts request authorization first in the Crossref-owned operations; and removes wording that contradicted frozen rules 7 and 17. #848 comment `5624224185` records that none of its findings reopens the frozen architecture.
* The third review-correction pass restores frozen rules 7 and 17 exactly: the seed writes generation state and creates no job, and the asynchronous materializer creates the jobs after admission. It makes frozen rules 10 and 13 hold under concurrency with an advisory execution gate. The gate is taken after the generic prefix `P → W → G → J → A` and never before `P`, locks no assignment, admission or control row in the generic path, and makes the pause a barrier without widening what it disables. #848 comment `5632920797` records that neither finding reopens the frozen architecture by itself.
* The fourth review-correction pass changes the production order of migration and deployment, and corrects Migration 2's text where executing it against the released schema showed it wrong. Capture stays unconditional and editorial-only, generation rows stay its only write, the target-set invariant stays per profile, and `work_id` stays `ON DELETE SET NULL`. The one lock-order claim it withdraws is replaced by an executed residual, not by a new rule. #848 comment `5635363920` records that R52B-33 does not reopen the frozen architecture by itself.

* The fifth review-correction pass changes where inside an editorial transaction the generation row is taken: capture records, and one flush at transaction completion applies. Frozen rules 5, 7, 8, 18, 20 and 23 are each still true, and §6.1a states the amendment's exact boundary. It is the one change in this document that required a CTO architecture decision, and it has one: `5646180203`.

**One correction required a CTO architecture decision, and has one** (`5646180203`, Alternative A approved in principle). No other correction changed a frozen rule.

**Everything the review listed as preserved is preserved**, item for item (§6.4).

**Findings.**
* R52B-0 through R52B-22 are closed (§32). Each closure rests on executed evidence or, for three rows, on a named, required implementation test.
* The migration-identity collision that blocked the R52B candidate of 4,053 lines is **resolved** by #903 and #848 comment `5619637336`, and the operative identities are verified free (§1.2).
* The release/topology correction is closed (§32, PS-BRANCH-01).
* The Diesel-version reservation correction is closed (§32, PS-BRANCH-02). The reservation is controlling authority, and every gate §23.1 lists fails closed on a consumed version.
* R52B-23 to R52B-28, and the editorial correction E-1 of #848 comment `5622203229`, are closed (§32). R52B-23 re-closes R52B-12 against the corrected text. T170, the built-SDL token boundary, is a required implementation test.
* R52B-29 and R52B-30 of #848 comment `5624224185` are closed, and #848 comment `5632920797` confirms them at specification level (§32). T257, extended with the replay, repeat and role-removal cases of §19.4 rule 3, remains a required implementation test.
* R52B-31, which #848 comment `5632920797` kept open, is re-closed, and R52B-32 is closed (§32), each on normative text and executed specification-harness evidence (§25.20).
* R52B-34, raised by #848 comment `5637606889`, is closed at specification level (§32) on the CTO's architecture decision and on executed evidence (§25.22, §25.23): five `40P01` classes reproduced and eliminated, ten variants falsified, fifteen validations with three negative controls, and the released suite, sequence G and the migration sequence re-run over the adopted schema. **R52B-33's compatibility proof is re-established** on that schema: the window now differs in two ways, not three, and the `40P01` residual that kept it open is gone (§23.6).
* R52B-33, raised by #848 comment `5635363920`, is closed (§32) on executed evidence (§25.21): the review's preferred order survived falsification. Executing Migration 2 exposed four defects in this document's text, now corrected, and one false no-deadlock claim, now an executed residual (§10.6). §23.6's re-run at gate 9, against the release then in production, is required.

**Delivery facts.** 5 107 lines, about 683 KB. Self-check counts (§30.2):

| Item | Count | Item | Count |
|---|---|---|---|
| triggers | 34 | routes | 4 |
| indexes added | 10 | void reasons | 13 |
| new tables | 9 | TLO classes | 11 |
| new enum types | 6 | finalisation checks | 14 |

**Evidence status.** Neither revision re-ran any of the R52B pass's falsification suites. Between them they change the release line, the migration names and their reservation, and the branch topology; no validator, and no semantic premise of that executed evidence, changed. The integration branch's tree differs from `master` in no file that evidence exercised (§1.1).

The release/topology revision introduced one new premise — Diesel's handling of a shared version — so it executed one targeted probe for it, on a disposable instance against the locked crates, over two scenarios (§23.1). The Diesel-version reservation correction executed nothing new: it changes the conclusion drawn from that probe, not the evidence.

The review-correction pass executed the 11 rows of §25.18 — 190 checks, all passing — on PostgreSQL 17.10 on a disposable instance, against SQL taken verbatim from this document, and ran them again from the delivered text. It re-ran the R52B pass's T237, T239 and T240 against the corrected trigger, as T269, and the PostgreSQL side of T248–T252 against the corrected timestamp functions, as T259–T261. It re-ran no other suite: the changed premises reach no race, extraction, prepared-route or ledger evidence.

The second review-correction pass executed the 10 rows of §25.19 on PostgreSQL 17.10 on a disposable instance: 19 new interleavings — 15 for the held publisher and 4 for the seed, two of them with three sessions — with 89 checks, all passing and none deadlocking; the 39 interleavings and 70 sequential checks of §25.17, re-run against the corrected functions with identical results; the suites of §25.18, re-run from this document's own text, 190 of 190; and 220 specification-harness checks of the request-authorization precedence, including 26 negative controls that reproduced the `4010dce1` candidate's disclosures. The race harness is the R52B pass's, with finalisation, completion, the report, the voids and reconciliation replaced by the orderings this document states, and the BE-03 coordinator, the ownership move and the seed unit added; its permit table and state-machine trigger are the R52B pass's, which predate R52B-27 and R52B-28, and no check here reads the columns those changed.

The third review-correction pass executed the 14 rows of §25.20 on PostgreSQL 17.10 on a disposable instance. It ran 34 race cases — 33 interleavings, 12 of them with three sessions, and 1 single-session negative control — with 162 checks and no `40P01`; the first complete run's one failing check was a same-millisecond trace comparison in the harness, and the corrected re-run passed 162 of 162. It ran 17 sequential checks of the gate and the seed, and the census suites' 33 checks from this document's own statement. It re-ran the 39 interleavings of §25.17, the 15 of §25.19, the 70 sequential checks and the 220 authorization-matrix checks against the gated functions, and the 161 checks of §25.18 whose SQL this pass did not change from this document's own text, with identical results (T293). The race harness is the second pass's with one overlay: the execution gate, the generation-only seed, the materializer, the census and admission, and §18.4's control CHECK. The `37e44ce3` candidate's ungated claim, reservation and finalisation and its job-creating seed are kept as negative controls, and each reproduced its defect.

The fourth review-correction pass, whose capture topology this document withdraws (§6.3), executed the 11 rows of §25.21 on PostgreSQL 17.10 on disposable instances, with the released `v1.8.0` binary and its full workspace test suite, and a candidate binary built from `feature/publisher-services-v1-10` `395cc16a` with Migration 1 and Migration 2 added: the two migrations against the released schema, §23.4's executable sequences and two literal-text negatives; the released suite over the released, the migrated and the two literal-text schemas; the end-to-end sequence with the released server in service, and three negative controls; and 12 deletion/capture interleavings. It re-ran no earlier suite. It changes no permit, timestamp, ledger, gate, census or seed SQL. Its only lock change is capture's `FOR KEY SHARE` on a propagated parent, whose one new wait T303 executes; the earlier race harnesses model capture as a plain generation upsert, which that change does not alter.

The evidence in §25.17 and §32 is the R52B pass's, executed on PostgreSQL 17.10 on a disposable local instance, touching no repository, GitHub, CI, Crossref or Metrics state:
* 70 sequential checks, all passing;
* 39 two-session interleavings over 19 path pairs, with one `40P01`: the deliberate negative control;
* a 360 039-string Rust/PostgreSQL timestamp-validity parity corpus under `chrono` 0.4.45, with 0 disagreements;
* controlled-clock, overflow, UTC, truncation and order-isomorphism checks;
* the prepared-route seam applied to a scratch copy of the released export server, with 10 of 10 tests passing;
* the §16.11 extractor over 9 fixtures and 10 serializer outputs;
* SQL-membership parity over 6 fixture shapes;
* a collation comparison.

Three §25.17 rows remain required implementation tests, **not yet executed**: T203 (static containment), T257 (the authorization matrix) and T258 (the conformance transcript). Floor, audit and baseline permanence remain specified, and **not executed** (T241). T170, the built-SDL token boundary, is likewise required and not executed. T257 now also carries §19.4 rule 3's replay, repeat and role-removal cases; T277 and T278 model its precedence in the database harness and do not execute it.

**Source facts discovered**, none of which contradicts the frozen architecture. Items 1–11 come from the R52B pass, items 12–14 from the release/topology revision, item 15 from the Diesel-version reservation correction, items 16–18 from the review-correction pass, items 19–20 from the second review-correction pass, items 21–23 from the third, and items 24–30 from the fourth.
1. Live `master` is `4fa7eaa9`, not `b582e617`. It removes the one-byte `noop` file again, and its tree is byte-identical to `9ae1e567`. `develop` moved to `395cc16a` through the documentation-only PR #901, which merged ADR-0011.
2. Both of R52A's BE-06 migration paths are used by the Metrics line (`MET-WP1-08`, `MET-WP1-09`), which labels its migrations `v1.9.0`. The line added `20260909_v1.9.0` during the R52B pass (MET-WP2-01A, PR #902).
3. The serializer writes root `doi_data` only when the root has both a DOI and a landing page, so a released document can register nothing.
4. The deposit bytes carry five classes of DOI-shaped values that are not registrations: crossmark policy, crossmark update, funder identifier (with its URL prefix), citation DOI and ROR.
5. `manual_disseminate.yml` is a Crossref write path with no Thoth authorization, and the per-work legacy path carries no Thoth worker credential for Crossref.
6. Every dissemination Crossref write converges on `CrossrefUploader.upload_to_platform()`, whose only fetch is at `crossrefuploader.py:90`.
7. The dissemination worker never marks a code retryable once an external write may have started (`distribution_job_worker.py:779`).
8. `chrono` 0.4.45's `parse_from_str` round-trip accepts second 60 and leading-zero years.
9. ICU `en-US` collation orders `_` before `-` and `.`; `C` and Python agree with each other.
10. From the harness: a PL/pgSQL function body must schema-qualify its callees, or its behaviour depends on `search_path` (§23.3).
11. R52A's `CROSSREF_RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED` had no raising path. R52A's permit FSM would have refused the referential `SET NULL` that Publisher deletion performs.
12. `feature/publisher-services-v1-10` exists at `395cc16a`. The historical `feature/publisher-services/*` refs remain, as #903 requires, and no `feature/publisher-services-v1-10--*` slice branch exists yet.
13. `develop`, and so the integration branch, differs from `master` by 33 commits, including the unreleased MIG-01 backfill. That changes no cited fact except line numbers in `thoth-errors/src/lib.rs`, and no locked crate version (§1.1).
14. `diesel_migrations` resolves to 2.3.2, not the declared 2.2.0, and `paperclip` to 0.9.7, not 0.9.5. Diesel keys migrations by version in a map, so two directories with one version silently collapse to one. The `20260910_v1.9.0` / `20260911_v1.9.0` names alias BE-06's versions, and executed, the dropped migration was BE-06's (§23.1).
15. #903 comment `5621256877`, #766 comment `5621260046` and #848 comment `5621263642` reserve the Diesel versions `20260910` and `20260911` repository-wide for BE-06. Each was the latest comment on its issue at preflight and again before delivery, and no directory on any of the 75 live heads has either version, by name or by derived version.
16. PostgreSQL's `to_char` renders year 10000 with five digits, so the `67683f28` candidate's `crossref_ts_next(99991231235959999)` returned the 18-digit `100000101000000000` and raised nothing. The R52B-pass harness had carried a guard the displayed function lacked (§17.2).
17. Read as literal SQL, the `67683f28` candidate's census pseudocode lets an unvisited eligible Work vanish either way: an inner join drops it, and a left join compares against NULL. Both counted 0 where the corrected statement counted 5 (§21.3).
18. Rule A's history text, read literally, makes every authorization impossible, because the permit being authorised overlaps itself at its own timestamp. The R52B-pass harness excluded it; the text now does too (§16.2).
19. The released BE-03 coordinator issues its one publisher `UPDATE` only for a committed change, and that statement's `AFTER UPDATE` cascade locks every work row of the publisher (`publisher_service_configuration/crud.rs:259-297`). A coordinator whose cascade ran before a Work moved into its publisher does not wait for a transaction that holds that Work, so only a lock on the publisher itself serialises it with finalisation (§25.19, T271).
20. No editorial ownership move takes a publisher lock that `FOR SHARE` excludes, and every publisher change of a Work is a captured event (§8.2). Under the `4010dce1` candidate's ordering, finalisation could therefore hold one publisher while reading another's configuration only in schedules that had also moved the root's generation, and group A voided each such run (§25.19, T271). The corrected ordering does not depend on that.
21. PostgreSQL grants a new advisory SHARE request only when it conflicts with no holder and no waiter queued ahead of it. A claim requesting `Q` behind a waiting `setWorkUpsertExecution` waited, and `pg_blocking_pids` named both the SHARE holder and the queued transition (§25.20, T284). A queued transition therefore holds back later consumers, which is what makes the pause return promptly.
22. `pg_locks` shows a two-argument advisory key with `objsubid = 2`, and a one-argument key with `objsubid = 1` and its two 32-bit halves in `classid` and `objid`. Two backends held `(1948572002, q, 2)` and `(1948572002, q, 1)` EXCLUSIVE at the same moment, and likewise `(1948572001, q, 2)` (§25.20, T289).
23. The earlier specification harnesses omitted §18.4's CHECK `NOT execution_enabled OR capture_enabled`, and one R52B-pass sequential fixture set `(capture = false, execution = true)`, a state the schema forbids. The corrected fixture uses `(false, false)`, with the same result (§25.20, T293).
24. The release image's default command is `init`, which runs every pending migration and then serves, and the production GraphQL service inherits it, while the same production stack already overrides the command of the export API service (§23.6).
25. Diesel's `run_pending_migrations` ignores applied versions it does not embed, so a pre-BE-06 binary starts over the migrated schema. Its `revert_all_migrations` refuses at the first unknown version; the candidate binary's reverts every migration it knows (§23.5; §25.21, T295, T302).
26. The released `relation_type` enum stores `has-child`; `HAS_CHILD` is only its GraphQL name, and as a SQL literal fails at `CREATE FUNCTION` (§8.4).
27. PostgreSQL validates a `LANGUAGE sql` function body at `CREATE FUNCTION`, so a function over BE-06 columns or tables cannot be created before them (§23.3).
28. A referential cascade queues the triggers of the rows it removes to fire at the end of the outer statement, after the deleted row's own `AFTER` triggers. A Work's own capture trigger therefore fires after its generation row has gone, which is how the previous R2 re-inserted it (§8.4; §25.21, T303).
29. The released test harness truncates every table between tests, which Migration 2's permanence guards refuse; no released code path outside the tests issues a `TRUNCATE` (§25.21).
30. The released `DbEnum` mapping refuses an unknown label rather than skipping the row, so one `WORK_UPSERT` row makes the released read of `distribution_job` fail (§23.6; §25.21, T300).

**Whether any correction required reopening the frozen architecture: one did, and the CTO decided it.** #848 comment `5646180203` approves Alternative A in principle, as a narrow amendment to the capture topology (§6.1a). No other frozen rule is changed, and this document requests no further architecture decision.

**Repository and GitHub state: unchanged.** In the R52B pass, in both revisions, and in all four review-correction passes alike:
* no repository file, branch, tag, commit, push, PR, issue or comment, CI run, migration, release, deployment, provider contact or Metrics state was created or changed;
* nothing was posted to `#848`, `#903`, `#765` or `#766`;
* the BE-06 task branch was not created;
* the fifth review-correction pass created, migrated and dropped only local disposable PostgreSQL databases, and built only local scratch binaries; it posted nothing and changed no repository, GitHub, CI, provider or Metrics state;
* the fourth review-correction pass read one file of the private deployment repository `thoth-pub/infrastructure`, read-only and field-bounded, recorded only the fields §23.6 names, and changed nothing there.
